madam president, colleagues, it is by chance that i stand before this distinguished chamber in the days when our european union is under the shaking influence of the brutal terrorist act in paris. an act that was hailed by the chairman of the largest liberal party in bulgaria, mr. lutvi mestan, with the assessment that any civilizational difference is not only not a reason for terrorism, but is a reason for an even more convinced common aspiration to diversity and multiculturalism. against this background, i present to you the development of an essentially anachronistic problem of intolerance in the republic of bulgaria. i say development because i already had the opportunity to highlight it during the plenary session of 12 november 2014. i have in my hands the act of finding an administrative violation of the central election commission. my offense is that during the last election campaign, along with the official bulgarian language, i used a few sentences in my mother tongue. i objected to the competent bulgarian authorities, and i object to you. the administrative act handed over to me is in flagrant breach of article 10 of the european convention on human rights and fundamental freedoms, article 9 of the framework convention for the protection of national minorities and articles 11 and 22 of the charter of fundamental rights of the european union. madam president, in closing, i raise this issue not because i don't love my country, but because i want it to reach the high democratic standards of a united europe.
mr president, mr prime minister, mr president of the european commission, at the end of this presidency, i believe we can congratulate you on a successful institutional transition between two commissions of a different democratic scale. but this transition has increased your political responsibility. that is why i would like to return to four priorities. first of all, i would like to welcome the adoption of the conclusions on the management of migration flows and the launch of the khartoum process with the countries of the horn of africa. this is a sign of greater synergy between migration policy and external policies. this is the way forward for a genuine migration diplomacy. i am, however, much more reserved about operation triton and its means of achieving the objective of a policy of solidarity and efficiency. as regards the area of freedom, security and justice, i note that the doors of the schengen area have still remained closed to bulgaria and romania, despite the respect of the technical criteria. the political deadlock must be broken. thirdly, we saw what happened with south stream. energy policy must be conceived today as an instrument of regional integration which will enable the member states, including bulgaria, to continue to support the european project while opening up the prospects of mutual benefits with the countries of the southern and eastern mediterranean. i congratulate the italian presidency on the interministerial conference held in rome last november with the euromed countries. finally, i regret that this presidency has not made progress on the only credible legislative dossier on gender equality: the directive on gender balance among company directors. it is a missed opportunity and i hope that in the future it will not be the case.
madam president, i supported the rapporteur's proposal because i think it is sensible and contains new provisions which are definitely needed. the question of banning gmos in the member states is a fundamental one. in bulgaria, there is a strong suspicion against the cultivation of gmos and also against the marketing of goods containing gmos. while this report does not contain provisions regulating the placing on the market of products containing gmos, it fulfils one main objective, which is to give member states the necessary freedom to prohibit or restrict the cultivation and distribution of gmos in their national territories. i believe that the citizens of the member states should have the right, through their governing bodies, to decide whether and how gmos are to be grown on their territory. it's part of national sovereignty. as far as bulgaria is concerned, the bulk of bulgarian society is against the cultivation of gmos and i share that opinion and those concerns. i take this opportunity to call on the european parliament, the commission and the council to continue the discussion on the issue of the placing on the market of gmos or products containing gmos. it is necessary to allow member states to determine for themselves whether and how products containing gmos are to be produced and marketed on their territory.
in writing. today, the european parliament took an important step towards resolving the issue of the cultivation of genetically modified food in the european union. the commission has also proposed that the eu should be able to take action to prevent the spread of the disease, and that the eu should be able to take action to prevent the spread of the disease. the amended legislation brings additional legal clarity by allowing countries such as bulgaria to ban the cultivation of gmos on specific grounds related to socio-economic reasons, specific environmental objectives, etc. the role of the european food safety authority (efsa) is maintained, which has the right to give an opinion on the safety of a given gmo product on the basis of in-depth scientific research. each member state is now free to decide for itself whether to follow the opinion of the efsa or to define a regime which is in line with its national specificities. it is important to note that the texts also require a high level of guarantee from countries that grow gmos on their territory, so as to ensure the protection of the production of neighbouring countries. once again, the ep has demonstrated that it ensures the highest possible nutritional standards to protect the interests and health of its consumers.

madam president, the flow of migrants from third countries via turkey to bulgaria and greece is constantly increasing and poses serious risks to the external land borders of the european union. i've been paying attention for a long time, commissioner, because this situation could be even more serious than the mediterranean. the bulgarian-turkish border is a difficult-to-access region and an ideal environment for smuggling and trafficking in human beings. over 45 rescue operations have been carried out by the bulgarian government, over 1500 police officers guard the border around the clock, there are already casualties on both sides. ninety percent of illegal immigrants entered with the help of traffickers, and over 170 smugglers were detained in 2014 alone. the tragedy of the victims has become a lucrative business, organized even on the internet. there's a high risk of infiltration by terrorists who pay good money for their services. bulgaria is third in the number of requests for protection by syrian nationals after germany and sweden, and at the same time receives one of the lowest budgets from the migration fund. at the same time, there is no political solidarity the country is making tremendous efforts to secure the european union's border, while renouncing its membership in schengen.
in writing. the situation in the mediterranean region, triggered by the unprecedented wave of migration, is extremely serious. it's time to make the distinction again between refugees and illegal migrants, because a large proportion of migrants are not refugees, but economic migrants. the reasons forcing these people to seek the social benefits of the eu are rooted in the failure of their own states, in the destruction of the secular character of much of the north african coast, in the rise and invasion of radical islamism. and it's also the fault of those states and economic interests that organized the so-called arab spring, which destroyed the state and gave way to extremist islamist factions like isis, al qaeda, boko haram, al-shabaab and the mujahideen. the fight against migration must be fought against the causes of migration, where they originate. the role of the neo-ottoman government of turkey, which supports and organises traffickers and organisers of human trafficking channels, should not be forgotten. more european solidarity and financial assistance is also needed for countries at the frontier of migration pressure, including bulgaria, which currently bears a disproportionate financial and organisational burden. european financial assistance is needed to complete the construction of the protective structure along the entire bulgarian-turkish border.
mr president, madam commissioner, madam secretary of state, since the draft directive was proposed, the legislative pressure has been working. companies sought and found women who were competent and qualified to fill these seats, 18.6%. paradoxically, despite the strength of the evidence, we stomp on it. it is more than a year since parliament proposed a basis for negotiations. two presidencies later, the council has still not reached agreement. it is therefore now urgent for the member states to move forward on this matter on the basis of three key arguments. yes, commissioner, women make up 60% of graduates in the european union, but their role in the labour market does not reflect the skills available. the objectives of the europe 2020 strategy can never be achieved without breaking the glass ceiling. it is urgent to abandon the logic of the burden on industry and to draw on the t secondly, parliament has already given a pragmatic answer to those who are allergic to quotas. quotas are not an end in themselves, they are a means of ensuring that recruitment procedures are transparent and non-discriminatory. thirdly, it is a question of instilling positive dynamics, starting with the boards of directors and counting on a snowball effect at other hierarchical levels to reduce vertical segmentation of the labour market. today we all wish for greater equality between men and women in the union. it is high time that these wishes were translated into concrete action. at european level, there is little room for manoeuvre. where there are opportunities, it is politically irresponsible, vis-à-vis our fellow citizens, not to seize them.
mr president, commissioner, the european union has a single labour market which should ensure equal rights and equal opportunities for women and men, including senior management positions in companies, but this is not the case everywhere, even though in a number of member states there are national regulations on the matter. the draft directive provides an opportunity to overcome inequalities, it is a policy of equality in action, not promises that do not lead to results. although a small step forward in terms of its scope of impact, the directive will ensure a fairer distribution of women and men in management positions in the european single market. we must be consistent in our words and actions and bring it to the european level. i wish you success, mrs. yurova.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, we are all aware of the data that show how much corruption and organised crime cost european economies and european citizens. but there is something far more important. something that can't be measured is trust. the acute perception of corruption and organized crime erodes trust in institutions, and democracy is that political regime which is maximally dependent on the trust of its citizens in themselves. the fight against corruption and organised crime therefore has another dimension - it is a fight for the quality and future of democracy in the european union. so this battle must be fought and won with pan-european institutions, and i am not just talking about the european public prosecutor's office, which is being delayed, but also about the urgent need for a european intelligence service with a very strong and well-developed financial intelligence unit, which will not only increase trust but also create a sense of security, because we are all aware of the sinister link between organised crime and terrorism. as a bulgarian mep, i have to admit that bulgaria is one of the countries with the highest corruption index and low trust in the judicial system. so i want to wish the new reform government success in judicial reform.
in writing organised crime and corruption are global crimes that require close cooperation between law enforcement agencies not only in europe but also internationally to combat them. only in this way will their transnational nature be limited and it will be shown that there is no stable and legal state where their perpetrators can hide. i believe that the european union's attention in the fight against organised crime should be focused on three areas. the first is to combat financial fraud with european funds by setting up a european public prosecutor's office. the european funds are not intended to finance criminal activities but to guarantee the well-being of european citizens. it also requires enhanced police cooperation through more joint teams, cross-border operations and more timely data exchange. this is the only way to effectively combat organised crime and corruption in europe, because no country can tackle these crimes alone. last but not least, stricter controls on financial flows related to criminal activities are needed. i welcome the agreement reached between the european parliament and the council on the anti-money laundering directive and the financial transactions regulation and urge its swift implementation in the member states.
in writting. – a competitive europe also means europe with secure and reliable energy supplies. bulgaria, similarly to latvia, has a particularly strong interest in energy diversification, which is a key aspect of a functioning energy union. we have suggested the creation of a gas distribution hub on our territory in order to ensure better energy supply for the region. a newly-created high level working group for cross-border diversification projects will sit in bulgaria next month, with which we hope to create a new momentum for better synchronisation of the european energy markets. the completion of such an ambitious plan requires putting it high on the council agenda and i strongly urge the latvian presidency to introduce policy instruments and solidarity mechanisms in support of this action. another important step for achieving energy diversification would be the creation of a common mechanism for negotiating gas contracts with external suppliers for the whole of the eu. such a mechanism would give the europe a stronger, more united bargaining position and would prevent suppliers from using the fragmentation of the eu’s energy policy and markets to enhance monopolistic practices. since political willingness for decisive action in this regard is currently missing, i look forward to seeing the latvian presidency stepping up its work in this regard.

mr president, madam high representative, egypt is one of the countries which can play a decisive role in stabilizing the mediterranean region and in many other matters, as you have so well said. i will emphasize five elements. first of all, i would stress the implementation of the constitutional provisions on fundamental rights and freedoms. we know that it is not enough to list rights and principles. we need concrete action, not just electoral promises. but how can we make sure that the egyptian authorities actually respect the constitution? secondly, freedom of the press and of expression. stability and security are not guaranteed by a closed press. on the contrary, media freedom will allow the emergence of an inclusive and democratic pluralistic society, as it is one of the instruments of dialogue within society. it is this dialogue that will overcome the polarization of egyptian society. thirdly, i believe that interreligious dialogue could do much to reduce tensions and promote pluralism. i encourage the egyptian government and the european union to support initiatives that promote mutual understanding and religious tolerance. fourthly, the role of women. without the participation of women, the democratic transition can only be partial. their rights must be guaranteed, violence against women must be combated and their participation in political decision-making must be encouraged. here again we need concrete initiatives with results, with the participation of men and with the participation of the union. finally, the fight against trafficking in human beings. this is a major challenge egypt is facing, one we can cooperate more closely on. we have the instruments. now we need to get them up and running. there are many challenges. our support must be sufficient to finally strengthen the institutions and the democratic society of egypt, without which, as we know, no lasting stability in the country is possible.
mr. chairman, madam high representative, colleagues, today we are discussing the situation in egypt, exactly one year after the adoption of the country's new constitution. the constitutional referendum was an important first step towards a comprehensive political process and the european union stood behind it. but unfortunately, since the presidential elections in june, the political situation in the country has remained unstable, and the methods of power are too strong and disproportionate to the factor that caused them. the isolation of a certain section of egyptian society from the current political process in the country is eroding the statehood. the issuing of death sentences, human rights violations, pressure on civil society organisations and the lack of freedom of expression, which has led to the arrest of students and journalists, have a destabilising effect on the democratic process and the socio-economic environment. alongside the negative trends in domestic politics, we must take into account egypt's active regional role. it has successfully mediated an end to the 2014 war between israel and gaza, is working on a dialogue in libya, has given new impetus to talks with ethiopia and sudan on the controversial renaissance project, and in october hosted an international donor conference for the reconstruction of gaza. that is why we need to engage in dialogue and actively cooperate with all representatives of egyptian society.

in writing. i voted abstention on the report on the european ombudsman's activity report, as i do not think that anything particularly positive can be said about the ombudsman's activities over the past period. the spirit of the ombudsman's report is administrative-command style. of the 100 complaints, only 15 are considered relevant. the admissibility rule is a formal criterion which does not guarantee that the ombudsman has correctly assessed whether there is a bad administrative practice. the formal approach to the examination of appeals leads to a refusal to give a ruling on the petition. the low number of complaints and the number of citizens who have asked for help show that the institution is unknown to the citizens, they do not expect it to solve the problems. a low number of complaints does not mean less bad administrative practices. i do not share the ombudsman's view that frontex should set up a mechanism and rule on individual complaints from migrants. this is a matter for the member states' administrations. frontex cannot usurp this competence, the agency coordinates and assists but does not directly manage the process. the ombudsman's proposal is legally inadmissible. given that the present ombudsman has been in office since september 2013, these observations are findings and recommendations, not a censure.
in writing. in the european parliament (ep) resolution on the situation in ukraine voted today, 15 january 2015, the leading european political groups succeeded in uniting around a common position of solidarity with the country and its citizens. resolution strongly condemns all acts of terrorism and criminal behaviour by separatists and other illegal forces in eastern ukraine. the aggressive expansionist policy of russia, which poses a threat not only to the integrity and independence of ukraine, but also to the european union as a whole, has been the subject of sharp criticism. i am particularly pleased that the resolution supports the current sanctions regime of the union and stands firmly for its continuation should russia continue to destabilise eastern ukraine. the ep's letter makes clear that the eu-ukraine association agreement is not the end-all-be-all of their bilateral relations and recalls the possibility, given the country's stated european perspective, of applying for accession to the union. i welcome the strong signal of support for ukraine's european aspirations reflected in the full text of the resolution. i wish for a smooth and successful implementation of the necessary reforms that the country faces.

in writing. i supported the proposed resolution on the case of the two italian marines because i believe that the rule of law, human rights and the rights of suspects must be respected and protected. i believe that this case allows the eu to show its capacity and its capacity to act in relation to the protection of the rights of european citizens in third countries, because it is our citizens who most need to feel the support of the union, to see that it defends their interests both in europe and beyond. i am of the opinion that the high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy should take the necessary action in the defence of the two italian marines in order to achieve a swift and satisfactory resolution of the case, ensuring an independent investigation and a fair trial.
in writing. i support the programme put forward by president juncker's team and the epp motion for a resolution, because i believe that the measures proposed by the commission will help europe recover more quickly from the crisis. the new european commission is charged with the high expectations of european citizens in relation to overcoming the significant economic and social challenges facing europe. i am convinced that good and adequate legislation, combined with effective enforcement, can help create jobs, increase growth and competitiveness. in the age of new technologies and communications, europe's digital internal market is key to its economic development. last but not least, events at the union's eastern border over the past year have demonstrated the need for new measures to ensure the eu's energy security. the creation of a european energy union would help diversify and increase the energy sustainability of a number of european countries, including bulgaria. today in the chamber we have witnessed a sharp contrast between the ambitions of the commission and the political will of the ep. i hope that the epp and cd groups will be able to overcome their differences on important european issues and ensure support for the work of the european commission in its current mandate.
without a doubt, islamic fanaticism and radicalism is the greatest challenge facing western civilization right now. undoubtedly, this religion is seen as politics, and its inspirers have and are pursuing a single political goal - to take over the whole world in the name of their point of view. we have two choices, either to resist or to lose this fight. but this fight is not fought with declarations, mr. chairman and colleagues. this fight can only be fought with a strong state, with borders, with intelligence, with national security. what we can and must do as the european parliament is to use our political instruments. and one political instrument, commissioner, is to impose sanctions on a state that supports terrorism, for example turkey; a state that benefits from european funds; a state that organises human trafficking; a state that funds, organises and treats isis or isis fighters, or whatever you want to call them; a state that threatens neighbours like bulgaria and greece by transferring dozens of illegal migrants. we can and we must do that and those sanctions must be demanded by you.
in writing. at a time when we are talking about cross-border crime, radicalization, international terrorist networks, security cannot be limited to the nation state. european countries must therefore unite to ensure that the community's existing instruments for cooperation in this field are applied optimally and, if necessary, to introduce new ones. the rights and freedoms of citizens are the foundation of the european union. but when the right to life is threatened, all other rights and freedoms become rather illusory. i don't think we have much room for discussion right now. today we see that in the past mistakes were made precisely in the direction of providing more security for our citizens. i am absolutely in favour of a common name register of passenger registration data; a proposal which was rejected by the votes of the left and the liberals in the previous term of office of the european parliament. there should be cooperation with isps, especially when it comes to frankly radical sites recruiting members of terrorist organizations. last but not least, alongside immediate action, let us not forget that the best way to counter extremism is to promote democratic values through civic education. it is the only way to ensure that we do not allow people with a propensity for violence and terrorism to grow up within the european union.
in writing. following the terrorist attacks in france, a number of proposals were discussed to improve cooperation at european and international level in the fight against terrorism. the truth is that this will not be the only attack of this kind in europe and all member states, including bulgaria, must be prepared to prevent many others. bulgaria's proximity to the conflict in syria, as well as the fact that hundreds of europeans are crossing through bulgarian territory to join the islamic state, is reason enough for bulgarian services to work even harder to prevent terrorist attacks. they should actively participate in european networks for cooperation in the fight against terrorism and continue to exercise increased controls on the bulgarian-turkish border. at the same time, other member states should provide more information on people suspected of crossing bulgaria into turkey on their way to syria, so that bulgarian authorities can have a full picture of the flow of passengers across our southern border. i believe that the french citizen detained on suspicion of terrorism is a positive example of european cooperation. the fight against terrorism is a continuous process that requires constant efforts, and it is our duty to win it in the name of european and bulgarian security.
in writing. ladies and gentlemen, i am pleased that the promises made by jean-claude juncker at the start of his new mandate are now coming to fruition with the presentation of the legislative proposal to create a european fund for strategic investments, which provides for an additional €315 billion in private and public investment across the european union. i would like to point out that the european union is still moving at two speeds, and even at three speeds, because many of the poorer regions of the european union, instead of catching up, are significantly lagging behind others. living standards and economies in new member states such as bulgaria are still far from those of the western member states, and eu cohesion policy is not producing any visible results. i would like to remind you again that it is the uneven distribution of economic growth in the eu that has led to an increase in internal migration and the depopulation of entire regions. more intensive and more focused action is needed, particularly in the poorest regions, and i hope that the new proposals from the european commission and the additional measures envisaged to support the european economy will be proportionately allocated to the needs of the regions and the people. if these 315 billion euros are invested mainly in the old member states, i would not be surprised if in a year or two there is even more mass migration in this direction.
in writing. – the success of the investment initiative hinges on the ability to identify and select those risky projects that would have not been realised in the absence of the european fund for strategic investments (efsi), and where the ‘leverage effect’ can achieve its full potential in terms of attracting private financing. one of the main challenges for the governance structures will be to ensure additionality and avoid the crowding-out of private investment. according to the provisions of the proposed regulation, an independent investment committee will be charged with the task of examining potential operations and approving support for them irrespective of the geographic location of the projects concerned. although the text does not provide for any geographical pre-allocations of investment, it would be important to consider devising a mechanism to guarantee the balanced distribution of the projects, taking account of the differing economic conditions and investment environment across the member states. it is important to emphasise that projects in poorer and peripheral regions could, ceteris paribus, provide higher social returns and european added value, thus helping to safeguard the proper functioning of the single market. i call on the commission to assess carefully the governance structure of the fund so as to guarantee the independence of the investment committee.
in writing. ladies and gentlemen, 2015 is a crucial year for reaching a new international agreement to limit greenhouse gas emissions and climate change. the expiring kyoto protocol had a major flaw: the largest emitters (the us, china, india, russia) were not officially involved in global efforts to save the climate. so in the run-up to the 2015 paris climate conference, it's worth taking all reasonable steps to get formal commitments from both these countries and all the economically powerful developing countries. encouraging signals from china and the united states bring hope for the success of the conference. the eu is certainly leading not only in emission reduction performance but also in transparent and ambitious plans for the next decades to 2050. but it must not be forgotten that european industry and citizens are paying the price, losing competitiveness, jobs and incomes. this requires that the paris 2015 roadmap be at least equally focused on both succeeding in obtaining realistic and comparable commitments from the world's major polluters and protecting eu interests from undue burdens on industry, smes and citizens. it's not gonna work if the eu keeps footing the bill.

member of the commission. - mr president, i want to thank the committee on legal affairs and especially its chairman, mr svoboda, for their question, because the answer will allow me to maybe explain more and put a decision in context. you asked about the setting up of a group of experts on the enforcement of intellectual property rights. so maybe just one short remark putting the decision into context. markets and technologies are constantly changing, and we need to keep up with them. we need to keep on finding new solutions to breaches of ipr, and we need to be targeted. we need to develop new enforcement tools. it is for this reason – and also because member states cannot go alone with the issue – that in july last year an expert group was set up. it was a core part of our ten-point plan to improve ipr enforcement, and the member states welcomed it. let me turn to the specifics of your question. first you asked about the tasks of the group and how they add value to the work of the observatory. second, you asked about the financial implications and whether there will be duplication. the expert group, it has to be stressed, has a strict mission. it is to advise and assist the commission on policy initiatives. its members are the national authorities responsible for national ip policies, and it is to exchange experience and good practice. it will not gather data or conduct studies itself. instead it will rely on work done at national, eu and international level, including, of course, the work done by the observatory. this is completely different, as we see it, from the role of the observatory. the observatory is there to facilitate and support the practical enforcement work. that includes improving understanding and raising awareness of the impact of ipr infringements, providing training, promoting enforcement tools and technology, and helping national enforcement authorities to work together. the observatory supports national authorities, the private sector and eu institutions. part of this is data collection; the data collected and reported by the observatory supports the work of the commission and the member states. so the expert group focuses – just to repeat – on policy, best practices and monitoring, and the observatory focuses on the day-to-day enforcement and the collection of data. they do not duplicate. on the contrary, they are complementary. so the group will not result in unnecessary expenditure. third, you wanted confirmation that you could send experts to an expert group meeting, and the answer is yes, absolutely you can – you will be very welcome. we apply the framework document on relations between the european parliament and the european commission, but equally important, i think, is that you should be part of this body and participate in this work. we have to work together on ipr enforcement. it would be really helpful if you would follow the group actively. i will also listen to your questions and remarks, because i think that it will help our future work. i hope that reassures you and answers your questions.
on behalf of the ecr group. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, it is important to stress the importance of protecting intellectual property and the strongly negative impact of infringements of this property on the economy as a whole, on the workforce, on the incentive for innovation and creativity. let us not even begin to talk about the grey economy, which benefits from the lack of and non-compliance with the necessary legislation in this area. of course, we need good enforcement of intellectual property rights, but not at the expense of the taxpayer, not by wasting public funds, which in low-income countries would mean the livelihood of hundreds of families. is the creation of this group really necessary, given that the observatory is already working on this and one of its working groups is entirely concerned with the enforcement and protection of intellectual property rights? the observatory even has its own observers in the european parliament, including myself, and the expert group doesn't even foresee that. do you not think it is at least more practical for the directly elected to have oversight of the work of this expert group so that we can report and account to our colleagues in the european parliament and to our constituents about the work done in this area? i had the honour and pleasure of attending the observatory's plenary session last october and getting the necessary information about their work, for which i am of course grateful and believe in their efforts for future useful work. the legal affairs committee also frequently invites them to meetings, where they provide information on the activities and developments in the field. i ask again, is there a need for further waste of funds and how can this need be explained by the creation of a new expert group of this kind?

member of the commission. - madam president, honourable members, child abductions are another heart-breaking topic of today’s session here and i know that this matter is of special interest to many of you concerned by specific cases that desperate parents bring to your attention. like you, i want justice to be served and, within the limits of my competences, children in the eu to be better protected. the 1980 hague convention on the civil aspects of international child abduction is an important international legal instrument. its aim is to provide a system of cooperation between the central authorities and a rapid procedure for the return of an abducted child to the country of the child’s habitual residence. altogether, 93 countries, as ms hautala has said here, are already parties to this convention. all eu member states have joined the child abduction convention. the eu is not a party to the convention but has incorporated its principles in binding european legislation, which is the brussels ii regulation. it is in the eu’s interest to maintain a common legal framework with third states in order to solve international child abduction cases and guarantee legal certainty and speedy proceedings for eu citizens involved in these kinds of sensitive disputes. in december 2011, taking the view that the question of the international abduction of children fell within the exclusive external competence of the eu, the commission adopted proposals for accession acceptance for eight third-country states. contrary to the commission’s and parliament’s views, within the council most of the representatives of the member states considered the council to be under no legal obligation to adopt those proposals. as a result, the council did not adopt the proposals. last october the european court of justice confirmed that the exclusive competence of the european union encompasses the acceptance of the accession of a third state to the 1980 hague convention. it is now time to finalise this process without further delay. the council needs to take a decision on each of the proposals in unanimity. the negotiations are quite challenging but we are dealing with serious matters of child protection. we therefore have a duty to find a solution to this. i will use my contacts with member states to advance this process as much as possible. i would like to thank the rapporteur ms hautala and the committee on legal affairs (juri) for their constant support for the commission’s initiative. we share the same determination to protect children and resolve abduction cases quickly. i am convinced that a robust eu presence in the external field is of paramount important in this regard.
on behalf of the ecr group. madam president, ladies and gentlemen, madam commissioner, it is imperative to extend international cooperation in the field of child protection. every society must take greater care and a set of measures to protect the rights of persons who, by reason of circumstances, physiology or the law, have a limited capacity (minors or minors). we therefore welcome the accession of a number of european union member states to the 1980 hague convention. this is an important step and it will certainly be appreciated in the field of international law. i would like to note that crimes against children unfortunately remain at the same high levels. some of the abductions are actually the initial stage of the compound crime of trafficking. it is no secret that thousands of children in europe are victims of trafficking and criminal exploitation. there are criminal gangs, mostly minority gangs, that deal with trafficking children, selling them, exploiting them as beggars, prostitutes, pickpockets, and so on. in this sense, i think that we must now, as an institution, respond to these challenges and put an end to this dirty business.
madam president, the need for measures to improve effective cooperation between national judicial authorities is one of the problems we need to work on in order to make the application of the hague convention more effective and to minimise problems in its application. the european commission's report of april 2014 reveals some important shortcomings of the legal framework, namely: the lack of a single and comprehensive rule on jurisdiction; the enforcement of judgments given in other member states often proves difficult due to differences between procedural standards, such as those regarding the hearing of the child; cooperation between central authorities of the states also needs to be improved. all these problems were manifested in a single case which was presented to the committee on petitions only recently. ladies and gentlemen, we are here today to discuss the forcible removal of a child from one parent without the consent of the other. we condemn these practices. but what do we do when it comes to forcibly removing a child and giving that child up for adoption without the consent of the parents? an act not committed by traffickers but by a court in a member state of the european union. my dear colleagues, i assure you, it is possible and it is becoming common practice in the british courts. two weeks ago, a similar case was brought before the house committee on petitions, megan's case. in addition to the parental rights being withdrawn from both parents, the grandmother of the child was also denied parental rights in clear violation of her procedural rules. where is the child interest that we're all talking about in this particular case, and how long are we going to be silent witnesses to this vicious practice in the british courts?

mr president, there is an important council tomorrow and it is natural to have a heated and extensive debate. on the security issue and counter-terrorism, i would like to underline the fact that we still have a divided european security area: until bulgaria and romania are part of schengen, this undermines our common security. secondly, on greece, we do not need arm twisting or a clash between two views. europe is based on mutual respect and constructive dialogue, and that dialogue will take into consideration not only the results of the elections but also the undertakings that have been given and the unity and cohesion of the eurozone. it is clear that europe needs a new deal for greece, a new perspective, and hope for the people. finally, on the talks in minsk, we need to be constructive because this bloodshed has to be stopped. we can no longer afford it: there are 1.5 million refugees, internal and external, among them people who are suffering. there are between 300 000 and 500 000 bulgarians living in ukraine, and i call on the european union and the bulgarian government to extend humanitarian support to them.
in writing. ladies and gentlemen, i voted in favour of the report by mrs hautala on gabon's accession to the 1980 hague convention on the civil aspects of international child abduction because i believe that the statistics on the increasing number of abductions speak for themselves of the urgent need for cooperation. according to the data currently available, in 2008 the number of applications for return of children submitted within the eu alone on the basis of the 1980 hague convention was 990. this is 35% more than in 2003. unfortunately, the crime rate against children is also high. i cannot help but point out that kidnapping is the first step to human trafficking and exploitation for criminal purposes. i welcome the accession of gabon to the 1980 hague convention and call on all parties to that international instrument to take measures to ensure its practical application.

mr. president, i would like to president, ladies and gentlemen, i supported the resolution on the crossborder exchange of information on road accidents because i believe that the policy of reducing road casualties must be supported and must be followed consistently. the traffic offence fine is also preventive in nature. so it makes sense to make such an exchange. yes, certainly privacy is an important civil right that must also be strictly protected, but no less important is the preservation of the lives of our fellow countrymen and citizens. that's why i think that any traffic violations committed by foreigners must be prosecuted to the fullest extent of the law and that's why this data can be transferred. i am aware that transposing this information into national legislation will require time and a huge amount of work. i therefore consider that granting the kingdom of denmark, ireland and the united kingdom a two-year transitional period is a sufficient, normal and realistic period. in conclusion, i would like to stress that i support the policy of preventing road accidents.
in writing. mr president mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the report by ms sander is important not only because it reflects a judgment of the court of justice of the european union on the legal basis of directive 2011/82/ec on the facilitation of the cross-border exchange of information on road safety related traffic offences and the opinion of the european data protection supervisor on the exchange of information between member states, but also because it generally improves the text of the directive. i believe that the proposed amendments thus contribute to the effective implementation of the directive by ensuring a better level of protection of the personal data of european citizens.
in writing. with today's vote, the european parliament has demonstrated a consistent policy to tackle road safety - an issue that affects the health and lives of every citizen. the goal is clear - to reduce road fatalities. the court of justice of the eu's decision of may 2014 to repeal (in relation to the legal basis) the previous directive on the cross-border exchange of information on road traffic offences led to the need to re-adopt the text with some amendments. i hope the new directive will lead to more safety on the roads. moreover, it will apply in all member states of the union, subject to the requirements of personal data protection. it is essential to ensure that there is a european tool to deal with cross-border traffic offences. this is about tackling the most common examples of speeding, not wearing a seatbelt, running a red light, driving drunk, etc. these breaches will be investigated and sanctioned, regardless of the member state in which they are committed. i look forward with great interest and hope to the results of the texts adopted today and to the commission's future assessment of the application of the directive.
in writing. ladies and gentlemen, i support the adoption of the new directive as soon as possible to avoid the risk of a legislative vacuum due to the annulment by the court of justice of the european union of directive 2011/82 on the facilitation of the cross-border exchange of information on road safety related traffic offences. despite its short duration, the repealed directive has had a strong deterrent effect on foreign drivers of road vehicles, regardless of the country of registration within the european union. thanks to the new rules, any infringement committed abroad can be notified through the mechanism for the exchange of information between the competent authorities of the member states and lead to the effective application of the sanctions imposed. the european parliament has always maintained that its priority is to improve road safety and reduce the number of road fatalities. the figures to date continue to shock us with their scale - more than 26 000 people die on european roads every year, and many more are injured. in road warfare, children are particularly vulnerable, unfortunately even on the pavement. i therefore strongly support the european commission's efforts to ensure a high level of protection for all road users in the european union through the proposed legislation.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i did not support this resolution because i did not see it as a clear condemnation and a demand for punishment of the guilty. undoubtedly the use of torture, of kidnapping, of violating civil rights is everything that this room denies, and everything that this room has to fight against. not to mention, when there are confessions extracted by torture, imagine what kind of fight against terrorism is being talked about. then you get a distortion of information, then you get a redundant effort, and it's all going in the wrong direction. yes, civil rights should be respected. yes, no one can afford to justify breaking the law by breaking the law. and that would compromise the whole idea of justice, would compromise the whole idea of building a better and safer world. therefore, the guilty persons must be investigated and effectively prosecuted in order to restore faith in justice and faith in the rightness of the law and its supremacy.
mr. chairman, ladies and gentlemen, i voted in favor of this resolution because islamic terrorism represents perhaps the most serious threat to western civilization in the last century. what is happening on the southern borders of europe, the uncontrolled invasion of illegal migrants, the inability to secure the borders of the states are actually the way in which terrorism enters our territory. that is why an effective system of cooperation between the intelligence services of the european countries must be established, and why the military operation to destroy islamic terrorism where it is currently gaining strength must be expanded and developed - in iraq, in syria, in nigeria, along the entire northern coast of the african continent. because, again, the greatest threat to our civilization, our values, our way of life right now is armed islamic terrorism, led by al qaeda, islamic state, boko haram and other islamist organizations.
in writing. ladies and gentlemen, i supported the proposed resolution to renew the mandate of the internet governance forum, as it has proven to be a successful democratic platform for the exchange of good practice, working mainly to hold discussions on internet governance. the internet is playing an increasingly important role in people's lives and daily lives, while promoting innovation, trade, growth, democracy and cultural diversity. i agree that parliament, together with the member states and the commission, should continue to participate in future meetings of the internet governance forum with a substantial delegation in order to contribute to the effective formulation of a coherent and comprehensive approach of the european union. i believe that the un general assembly should renew the mandate of the internet governance forum, and uphold the multi-stakeholder model of internet governance, so that, among other things, we can improve internet access around the world.
mr. president, i would like to president, ladies and gentlemen, i have supported the motion because i believe that the arguments put forward by the rapporteur and the observations and recommendations made are correct. the report on the country of origin labelling of meat in processed foods is of great importance in the range of legal measures that need to be taken to protect the interests and health of consumers. every consumer has the right to know what he eats, where it is produced and who is responsible for any irregularities. the same applies to the control authorities, who will thus be able to monitor and control the supply and trade of meat and meat products. it should be noted that the introduction of mandatory labelling will not harm meat processing establishments. on the contrary, it would lead to a better product, better marketing and increased competition. the truth is that we have a problem right now with illegal imports and dumping of processed meat. it's bad for our businesses. if we shine a light on this sector, it will be to the benefit of meat processing companies and member states. this measure will have a tangible effect on fraud in the food trade and will reduce the share of the grey economy in the sector. in conclusion, the most important thing we should be guided by is to protect the right of consumers to know what they are buying and what they are eating.
in writing. - it's extremely important to know the origin of all the products we consume. consumers expect to be properly informed about what they are buying and their trust should be justified. however, i am not convinced that mandatory labelling requirements will provide the necessary security. i supported the position on voluntary labelling because i think that further research is needed on how mandatory labelling would affect everyone from the retailer to the mass producer. it is desirable to avoid excessive costs and administrative burdens - we need to ensure that mandatory labelling does not create an additional burden for smes in the sector. voluntary labeling gives you time to adapt. in addition, the country of origin or place of origin labelling for meat and meat products does not in itself prevent fraud. strict control, prevention and fight against food fraud must be an integral part of eu policies. that's what's going to restore the confidence of european consumers.

in writing. in its communication to the european parliament and the council of 11.3.2014, the commission in its previous composition acknowledged that the existing mechanisms and procedures proved inadequate to ensure an effective and timely response to threats to the rule of law and fundamental human rights. it has been argued that a coherent protection of the rule of law in all member states is necessary to address situations where there is no specific breach of an existing provision, no 'clear risk of a serious breach' or 'a genuine serious and persistent breach' as required by article 7 of the eu treaty, but where threats to the rule of law are systemic in nature and threaten the political, institutional or legal order, its constitutional structure, the separation of powers or the judiciary, or where national guarantees to protect the rule of law are not sufficiently addressed effectively. i believe that we should do everything possible to ensure that the present commission recognises the application of the rule of law as a priority and continues to work towards the monitoring of respect for eu values in all member states on an equal footing.
in writing. ladies and gentlemen, on 28 january the european commission adopted the next progress report on bulgaria's progress on the cooperation and verification mechanism. once again, bulgaria and romania are being singled out for problems with organized crime and corruption. but such problems exist in other member states as well. an example of this is the commission's february 2014 report on the state of play of the fight against corruption in the european union. i believe that the publication of such reports would show the overall picture in the union, thus avoiding a focus on certain countries. another example is the proposal for a european-wide rule of law mechanism to monitor the respect of the rule of law and make recommendations to member states. in december 2014, this mechanism was replaced by a dialogue, once a year, based on the principle of objectivity, non-discrimination and equal treatment of all member states. i believe that these two instruments could replace the cooperation and verification mechanism of bulgaria, which has proven ineffective and represents a double standard applied to countries like bulgaria in terms of fighting organised crime and corruption. in this context, it is high time that the cooperation and verification mechanism for bulgaria was removed.
madam president, commissioner, we were looking forward to seeing mr avramopoulos here with you this evening, who in just a few months' time is due to propose a new holistic policy to tackle europe's biggest problem and challenge - migration - as illegal crossings of external borders have almost doubled in just one year. the greatest pressure is in the mediterranean, but the situation is no less tense on the land border between bulgaria and turkey. in december alone, 1,500 new immigrants have tried to enter our country. illegal immigration from kosovo to hungary is also on the rise. there is a risk of a new severe humanitarian crisis following the events in ukraine. these statistics have serious economic, social and political implications. the people are feeling the powerlessness of both national and european institutions. you asked, commissioner, what our proposals were. the review of existing instruments shows that the risk analyses of the european agencies and member states, coordination and cooperation are not sufficient. rather, the process should start with increasing the capacity of frontex and end with the creation of a european border response corps to respond in 'hot spots' where pressure is greatest. the operations must be carried out under the command of frontex, and the corps must be composed of border guards from the individual member states, and of course the whole process must be supervised by the european parliament.
madam president, commissioner, frontex's work has a number of instruments for protecting europe's borders: the sis-ii system, the vis system, the eurosur programme is being developed, and the pilot project under the 'smart borders' package is about to start. to protect europe, we need above all good cooperation. it would be more effective to introduce an obligation under the direction of frontex to exchange the data collected between the member states' law enforcement authorities and between them, frontex and europol, with a particular focus on the countries entrusted with the responsibility of guarding europe's external borders. and since obligations correspond to rights, and responsibility goes hand in hand with solidarity, i would like to remind you that bulgaria and romania fulfil their obligations to protect europe's borders and security, but despite meeting the admission requirements, they remain outside the schengen area.
madam president, commissioner, the tragedies that occur in the mediterranean almost daily show us that it is time to act decisively. i would like to raise four points. firstly, frontex does not have its own technical equipment and is dependent on the goodwill of the member states. a partial solution to this problem would be to systematically register all assets co-financed by the internal security fund in the frontex equipment pool. moreover, since the end of operation mare nostrum, the question of patrols in international waters, where shipwrecks are most frequent, remains unanswered. what answer do you propose to this dilemma? secondly, cooperation between the different european agencies easo and frontex, but also eurojust and europol should be strengthened, particularly as regards data sharing. this is the only way to strengthen mutual trust between member states and the different agencies and to gradually achieve a genuine integrated border management system. as regards easo, its action is mainly in the field of assistance and training. however, this assistance is provided at the request of the member states and on a voluntary basis. how can we determine priorities and needs in the light of the shortcomings we see in the various member states, for example by involving the european parliament more actively? finally, we must ask ourselves what is happening with the actions carried out within the framework of the mediterranean task force. what can we learn from the work of the task force? what are the practical, viable solutions and best practices to be adopted? we must have the courage and the political will to also set up a genuine migration diplomacy, which also requires a stronger role for the agencies, which can become genuine interlocutors and credible partners for third countries.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, mr timmermans, i have to say that the dublin regulations and agreements are extremely unreasonable and unfair because they place the burden on the border states. so italy, greece and bulgaria are now bearing the brunt of a huge refugee flow, a huge migration wave. last year alone, more than 14,000 illegal immigrants entered bulgaria legally. nobody knows how many people are in here illegally. the bulgarian economy can no longer afford to withstand this enormous migration pressure. and here it is your role and frontex's role to find a fairer distribution of this burden and to organise the burden of illegal immigration in a fairer and more responsible way. and here comes the next problem: the problem is with the role of turkey, which organizes the traffic on the territory of italy, on the territory of greece and on the territory of bulgaria. i think you should consider sanctions against turkey, commissioner.

in writing. ladies and gentlemen, i fully support the creation of the special committee on tax regimes. the aim of the new parliamentary committee is to investigate the scandal that has shaken the reputation of juncker and the european union as a whole. the other essential task facing the new commission is to examine the tax regimes of all member states in order to detect and prevent abuses of tax benefits. the juncker scandal has subsided, but speculation has neither been debunked nor confirmed. we must not forget the essence of this scandal, namely that tax breaks and their abuse threaten the overall functioning of the single market, as it is one of the foundations of the european union. the registration of companies in a tax haven for the purpose of avoiding taxation in another member state should be prevented, prohibited and sanctioned. this is not european solidarity, this is not free markets, this is the draining of resources and revenues from the state and the robbing of the people. that is why i believe that as an institution directly elected by the european citizens, it is our duty to impose parliamentary control and to investigate such cases until they are finally resolved. this is not about the reputation of one man or one government, this is about the reputation of the european union itself.
the idea of a common energy policy is certainly useful and should be implemented. however, i am more concerned about how the interests of the member states will be represented in this common energy policy. i'll give you an example: the political wrangling, the disputes and the disagreements around the south stream project have left the bulgarian economy without this potential resource and also potentially probably harmed the economies of romania, croatia, austria and other countries. to give you another example: the potentially useful green energy in my country, bulgaria, has become the reason for the electricity bills to be very high and unbearable for ordinary people. therefore, mr commissioner, this energy policy, in addition to providing europe with various sources of energy, must also look at those that are potentially possible in europe itself, such as the development of nuclear energy, which for bulgaria, for my country, is one of the good opportunities for secure and cheap energy.
in writing. the need for an effective energy strategy to build the energy union is more evident than ever. to overcome the existing over-reliance on external energy suppliers and their high prices, we need a sustainable single european energy system and a functioning internal energy market. the key to energy security is precisely to overcome existing market fragmentation, develop a common functioning energy market and extensive cooperation at local, regional and european level. to this end, we need to concentrate our efforts on developing standards for energy investment, on promoting the improvement of energy infrastructure and the use of renewable energy sources, on improving energy efficiency by reducing energy consumption, on addressing energy transmission challenges and on eliminating regulated pricing that limits consumers' freedom to negotiate prices with their suppliers. however, i believe that the focus of our efforts should also be on protecting consumer rights, overcoming energy poverty and ensuring an open and competitive market for the benefit of small and medium-sized enterprises, as they are the backbone of the european economy.
in writing. i welcome the strategic framework for the energy union presented today by vice-president šefčovič. the first voices about the need for such a union were heard last year, and its implementation has since become a top priority for the commission. the analogy that is needed is for a decision of the same importance to the european union as the creation of the european coal and steel community. moreover, there is talk of a fifth european freedom - the free movement of energy. today we have an ambitious project with the ultimate goal of energy independence, affordable electricity and a competitive economy within the eu. against the background of the objectives thus set, i regret to note that bulgaria is explicitly mentioned as one of the six countries in europe that are entirely dependent on a single supplier, and therefore much more vulnerable. it is enough to recall the suspension of gas supplies from russia in 2009, when the bulgarian economy lost over 250 million euros. it was in bulgaria that we witnessed a gross attempt to circumvent european rules under russian dictation - that of the construction of the "south stream", in violation of the provisions of the third energy package. i hope that the member states will find a way to show more solidarity in their energy policy and hence more common security.
in writing. – i welcome the commission’s framework strategy for a resilient energy union with a forward-looking climate change policy. i believe the strategy presents a holistic approach to the multi-facetted demands we european socialists and democrats make to a modern energy policy that equally delivers on climate, economic development and external security considerations. we will work constructively with the commission in order to create a comprehensive energy union. it will lower energy prices for citizens and industry, which will improve the european economy’s international competitiveness, will provide a reliable framework for investments and will contribute to jobs and growth. also, the energy union will make markets less dependent on third states’ energy supply and supply disruptions. such a union will be crucial to meet the eu’s 2030 climate target of 40% reductions of greenhouse gas emissions and makes europe a credible leader in the paris climate change negotiations this december.
in writing. ladies and gentlemen, i congratulate the european commission on the rapid development of the strategic vision for a european energy union. i consider this idea to be the biggest project on the road to eu energy integration since the 1951 coal and steel treaty. europe does not have enough energy resources of its own. we will probably be dependent on imports for a long time and hundreds of billions of euros will continue to flow outside the union to buy oil and gas. it is time to come together for greater energy security, to achieve, through trust and genuine solidarity within the union, the provision of energy to citizens and industry at affordable prices and costs. it is time to solve the problem of the countries of south-eastern and central europe: no longer dependent on a single supplier and every winter afraid that the continuing disputes and conflicts along the route will interrupt supplies once again. the ambitious target also requires ambitious solutions for the rapid physical interconnection of member states' energy systems, without which neither the completion nor the functioning of a single energy market is possible. the success of the energy union depends on citizens feeling its benefits in an unambiguous way. only such an energy union focus will ensure a successful move towards an energy-united europe.
and i'll be speaking on counterterrorism measures. we are lacking, mr president of the commission, in a thorough analysis of the means and instruments we have used so far, especially on the eve of the adoption of the new security strategy. we also lack an analysis of the interaction between member states and the interaction of member states with the european agencies europol and eurojust. but whatever new tools we propose, they are doomed to fail unless there is solidarity and understanding for a common fight and resistance against terrorism. a united effort, not each for himself, is the way to success. the schengen system must not be abolished, but strengthened and developed. in this context, the land borders of bulgaria and greece with turkey need special attention and european assistance. it is possible that radical elements and foreign fighters may attempt to cross turkish territory and from there to syria through these borders, following the tightening of controls at airports. the situation is complicated by increasing migratory pressure and canal-building. it is not justified that bulgaria should be a buffer zone guaranteeing the security of the other countries in the european union and be kept out of schengen.
in writing. two weeks ago, eu leaders encouraged meps to adopt the eu passenger name record (pnr) directive, which will be the basis for the eu's new data protection law. such a document would represent a major step towards ensuring better protection of the freedoms and security of european citizens, and i welcome its establishment. the council's discussions of 12 april have demonstrated the need for concrete measures in this direction, once again demonstrating that the institutions of the new mandate are committed to a targeted and practical approach. as an external border of the eu, bulgaria takes the protection of the security of europe and its citizens as its important task. the gerb party therefore proposed an amendment to the resolution on the protection of freedoms and security, which was welcomed by the epp group. according to this proposal, the eurodac system should be used for police purposes. this would enable the authorities of a particular country to work with a database that could help investigations and prevent future crimes. i call on european leaders and my colleagues in the ep to stand behind the implementation of this and other decisive reforms that will raise the level of protection in europe at a time when threats to the security of our citizens are increasing.
there were a lot of different topics discussed. i would like to focus on a more specific issue: bank lending. the ecb's annual report shows an improvement in financial stability, and with that, it is time to turn our attention to longer-term measures to reduce the fragmentation of financial markets and facilitate lending. these measures are essential for the development of business in europe, which is highly dependent on bank lending. alternative sources of financing are insufficient in view of the less developed capital markets, and we must therefore seek to develop the potential of banks. the commission notes that the commission has not provided any information on the level of the measures in force. this is due to the bank debt recorded in the ecb's sectoral stress tests. in early september last year, the ecb reacted to the downturn in activity and took steps to stimulate new lending. so i welcome this authority's decision to provide four-year loans to banks, on the condition that they increase lending to businesses. i believe this measure will act as a powerful tool to facilitate new credit flows to the real economy. i would like to call on the ecb to use all the instruments at its disposal to fulfil its mandate to maintain price stability by stimulating demand and supporting the investments that, together with the necessary structural reforms, are needed to achieve growth in the economy.
on behalf of the ppe group. madam president, madam commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i should like to begin by thanking my colleague, mr tarabella, for his excellent work as rapporteur, both in substance and form. successful cooperation is all the more important as this report allows us, once a year, to take stock of the progress made, but also of the challenges that remain to be met. i hope that tomorrow we can all, as an institution, vote in favour of this very good report. in 2013, we will note among other things the following progress: we have seen that the legislative pressure is working, with the draft women on boards directive. since the directive has been on the table, there are more women on the boards. the commission has taken stock of the implementation of the 2006 equal treatment in employment directive and has proposed recommendations. finally, the fundamental rights agency carried out the european study on violence against women. we have a database now. head to the action! so we are seeing positive dynamics, but the challenges remain great. in this report we propose new ideas. firstly, the participation of women in the science sector and in the information and communication sector. we also need to focus on using the digital strategy as a lever to promote gender equality. secondly, to encourage women's entrepreneurship to realise their full economic potential, also noting that this can contribute to the renewal of the structures and functioning of the labour market in favour of a better reconciliation between working life and family life. beyond the european social fund, the proposal to consider the use of european funds as a lever to improve women's participation in the economic and political decision-making process. and finally, the eternal struggle against gender stereotypes, especially the role of education and the media. i hope that these proposals will receive particular attention from the other institutions and the member states. i would add that, in order to achieve real progress on gender equality, it is crucial that action is taken at the most appropriate level. the competences of the member states must also be respected. that is the point of my group's amendments, but i am counting on the will of all of us to vote in favour of equality between men and women.
