answer is "blue cardboard". the stereotypes i'm referring to are the stereotypes that actually prevent women from realizing their potential. stereotypes that still divide male and female roles in our society. stereotypes still refer to school, where girls are taught that there are professions for them and others for men. that's the kind of stereotype i'm referring to. it is clear that today it is necessary to address this in order to achieve equality between men and women.
i want to congratulate all the colleagues in this room who worked on this report, and i want to turn to my colleague, mr. tarabela, and say to him, "mark, you've done a great job because you've presented not just a photographic picture of this problem, but you're looking for and finding solutions". i would add one more thing, however, because we are not talking about third world countries, but about member states. we are talking about black labor, the gray economy, which in countries like bulgaria, for example, reaches 32%, and in the tourism sector even exceeds 60%. the vast majority of those forced to work in this way are women - without employment contracts, without maternity rights, without hospital leave to raise children, without any basic assistance. we need legislation to criminalize employers who use social dumping and exploit women's labour. and another thing, commissioner, today's discussion would sound rather fake if you didn't help unblock the maternity directive and put it back on the agenda of the european institutions.
in writing. mr president, the european parliament, the commission, the council, the member states and all of us as eu citizens must work towards achieving gender equality not just as political declarations but also in the daily practical activities we carry out. the gender equality strategy 2010-2015 has failed to achieve its objectives. the economic gap between women and men is widening, and there is still a significant gap in their participation in the labour market - in their pay, in their pension payments. to achieve the objectives of the europe 2020 strategy, we need to pool our efforts to make progress on a horizontal equality policy. it is necessary for all member states to effectively implement the directive on the implementation of the principle of equal opportunities and equal treatment of men and women in matters of employment and occupation and to update the 1992 maternity leave directive. the adoption of the directive on women on boards would have a positive impact. progress on gender equality policy remains insufficient. i believe that women's equality will contribute to achieving sustainable growth in europe and i call for the systematic and consistent integration of the principle of gender equality in all our activities, structures, policies and priorities.
i'd like to commend mr. messersmith for the tremendous amount of work he's put in. the report is extremely accurate and objective. on the positive side, mr messersmith pays great attention to protecting small and medium-sized enterprises from competition from large companies and cartels. yet these smes employ over 65% of all the people employed in the european union and over 55% of the community's gross value added. it has been rightly noted that in most cases smes are disadvantaged compared to large consortia. of course, in such work some omissions and inaccuracies may be allowed, but they are not essential. the commission has to pay attention to the issues of different interest rates on loans not only for companies but also for individuals. in bulgaria, interest and loan servicing costs can reach up to 50%, while in countries such as germany they are no more than 15%. as regards social dumping and cabotage, i believe that these issues will be resolved when europe stops moving at two speeds and the division between small and large is abolished. and once again, congratulations to mr. messersmith on the job he's done.
mr president, i would like to ask the commissioner to refer to the third question that our chairman, mr cramer, has addressed to her, namely which measures would the new commission envisage undertaking in order to avoid a further deterioration of the european hauliers situation. i emphasise the new commission because the point that was made was that this was a commission working as a team – not in silos, not each commissioner for himself – so, when a decision is taken by the union, it has to look at all the consequences of this decision, namely the grave losses that the transport sector is suffering and the indirect disruption to the transport market throughout europe. it is no good just to tell us to go back home and look for state aid from our governments.
i support the idea of making such proposals in connection with various environmental initiatives and considerations, but they raise more questions than they answer than they permit. for example, i cannot support the proposals made to amend the maximum size and mass limits in international traffic. i think some of the proposals are too hasty and others are virtually unworkable. first, the reduction of the lower overload limit, after which the freight vehicle is stopped, is unrealistically low. it's being proposed to reduce it to 2%, which is impossible. on the other hand, the idea of reducing the distance for road transport in combined transport from 300 to 150 km in one direction is impractical because there are sections and cases where this is not possible. i would also like to point out that i do not accept the amendments' contention that 'intermodal' and 'combined' transport are completely identical concepts. it is also appropriate to clarify the term 'stop-and-go' as it is obviously a temporary stop-and-go rather than a permanent stop-and-go. it is necessary to specify explicitly that this would lead to problems in the transposition of those rules in some of the member states. in conclusion, i think we should not be concerned with these details but with the social dumping that non-eu countries are inflicting on our sector.
the report that has been proposed raises a lot of questions. long-term investment is an important tool for steering the european economy towards sustainable and inclusive growth, which should be in line with the objectives of the europe 2020 agenda. the european long-term investment funds should provide financing for various infrastructure projects, for unlisted companies or for listed smes for an extended period of time through equity or debt instruments. by financing these projects, long-term investment funds should contribute to the financing of the union's economy and the implementation of its policies. but european long-term investment funds will also invest in illiquid assets that are difficult to buy and sell. in addition, undertakings need to be sure that they will be able to dispose of the funds invested in them for the entire period they have declared to investors and that the investors themselves will not be able to withdraw their funds before the specified end date of the investment. rather, attention should be paid to the short-term financing opportunities of smes.
in writing. sustainably overcoming the consequences of the economic and financial crisis in europe is unthinkable without investment. financing opportunities, while available, are often insufficient or targeted at short-term objectives. the long-term financing of the real economy in europe is therefore a priority and must be guaranteed. the european long-term investment funds (eltifs) are the instrument that can ensure the smart, sustainable and inclusive growth of the european economy by mobilising and channelling private funds for long-term investment. the eifs are part of the vision of creating a single capital market, in line with the investment plan for europe. they can provide a steady stream of income for pension funds, insurance companies, professional and retail investors who are willing to invest at least eur 10 000 in one or more eltifs for the long-term to implement projects with a positive impact on the european economy. the implementation of this new investment facility will allow the financing of long-term projects in the fields of transport and social infrastructure (housing or hospitals), sustainable energy, education, research, small and medium-sized enterprises. the successful implementation of the efsi will help to overcome the lack of investment in projects of eu relevance and contribute to the sustainable growth of the european economy.
in writing. ‒ i voted against the application to mobilise the european globalisation adjustment fund because i believe that this mechanism is badly designed and a waste of money. with this money we help firms that are close to closing or even bankruptcy, but not with the aim of returning to the market and creating new jobs, but to help the laid-off workers to be reintegrated into the labour market. i fully support the idea of the reintegration of workers into the labour market, but this should be the responsibility of the social services in the country where these workers paid their social security and taxes. why should all european taxpayers pay for this, for this social policy? for the 708 redundant workers in belgium, the fund will pay €981956 and for the 615 redundant workers in poland €115205. on top of that, this money will be paid years after the application is filed, and i don't think the laid-off workers will wait 3, 4 or 5 years to start looking for a new job. the longer a person is unemployed, the harder it is to find a job. that's why i think that before we can continue to mobilize funds, we need to completely rethink and reconstruct this mechanism.
in writing. ‒ i think that the approach taken by the commission and the parliament on these issues is completely wrong. in fact, as can be seen from both the procedure and the regulatory bases, the problem is that the union is losing markets and becoming increasingly uncompetitive internationally. the internal market suffers from this imbalance and europe is being invaded by products that are killing our own production. but i don't see any serious steps to address these challenges. the egf is an anachronism because the aid granted will neither solve the problems nor compensate the workers who have lost their jobs. we need investment to increase jobs and increase growth, not giving away handouts and welfare. the current model is flawed. i would add that my position as a member of the edf group is that this fund is a flawed mechanism from the outset and i am opposed in principle to such ineffective instruments. we need investment to create growth, jobs and skills, not welfare.
in writing. - only months ago we were discussing possible measures to develop the steel industry, and today we are already wondering how to save what has already become unsalvable. i would stress that the egf is an instrument which does not help to improve the situation, but rather tries to compensate to some extent for the deterioration of the situation resulting from the global transformations in the economy. i would stress that the absence of an adequate growth policy, which in turn leads to the creation of new jobs and, consequently, to the improvement of workers' skills and, therefore, their wages, cannot be replaced by the egf. the collapse of the steel industry and the metalworking industry in general is a fact, but it is mainly due to the fact that we are constantly losing markets and positions. the reason for this is that we are imposing rules that make us uncompetitive. as a result, our businesses are losing money, our workers are losing their jobs, and we are dealing with this: should we give them 100 euros per person or not? forgive me, but this is not a growth-oriented approach to employment.

in writing. ‒ the egf is an inefficient and conceptually flawed instrument. as previous reports have shown, it has a compensatory mechanism. its effect is more than doubtful it leads neither to an increase in production nor to employment. our fundamental problem in both the steel industry and heavy machinery manufacturing is the strong pressure of imports from third countries. i'm aware of the situation because years ago bulgaria was in the same situation. as a result, our production was destroyed. we have become too liberal with the dumping that is being done on our market by suppliers from china, turkey and other asian countries. they're the root of the problem, they're killing our businesses. it's worth thinking about. in summary, our goal should be how to protect our own market, how to increase growth in economies, how to increase employment, how to enter new markets.
in writing. ‒ i welcome today's overwhelming vote on a regulation that sets a ceiling on fees for credit card transactions. in recent years, more and more european union citizens have been choosing to pay by card rather than by cash. the new legislation thus voted will harmonise the payment conditions in the territory of all eu member states, including bulgaria. the maximum fee for debit card payments will henceforth be 0.2% and for credit card transactions 0.3%. this is extremely good news for the growth and development of the eu economy, as it reduces costs for consumers and traders. initial estimates suggest that the ceiling voted could save eu businesses around €6 billion a year. it also introduces a requirement for more transparency in the charging of non-cash payments. the effect of all this will be to stimulate competition. we have an example of european legislation at a supranational level, entirely at the service of citizens, which achieves a degree of harmonisation that individual member states could not achieve one by one. this is the european union at its most positive - good for consumers and good for business!
in writing. ‒ i supported the report on the introduction of clear rules for more transparency and limits on the fees that banks charge merchants and buyers for debit and credit card transactions, as i believe that such a measure would significantly reduce the costs of cross-border and national card payments. i believe that the standardisation of card payment conditions in the european union will have a positive impact on competition, as it will allow merchants and consumers to choose the card payment schemes that offer them the best terms. this requires the introduction of a ceiling on fees for cross-border and domestic payments. under today's proposal, the agreed limit for cross-border debit card transactions will be 0.2% of the transaction value, with the same ceiling to apply after 5 years for domestic payments. for smaller national debit card transactions, member states will be able to set a maximum fixed fee of €0.05 per transaction. it is also envisaged that they may impose a lower fee for domestic credit card payments. i am confident that today's decision will lead to lower costs for both merchants and buyers, who are currently often obliged to accept all cards on the terms set by the banks that issued them.
in writing. ‒ i cannot agree with one text in the report which says that you see, the products that are exported to eastern europe lead to job losses in belgium, france, spain. the situation in the eastern european member states is no better. i remind you that we are in a free market and competition and such texts are unacceptable. it is correct to say that we are being crushed by imports from third countries - china, turkey, india, etc. why are we silent about this and why are no measures taken to limit this trend? instead, we're dealing with the allocation of funds for social programs, and it's clear that they're not going to bring growth to economies, or improve production, or create new jobs. the egf is a weak instrument and neither i nor my group support it in principle. on the contrary, efforts are needed to increase growth, access new markets outside the eu and increase employment.
i did not support this report because i fear the interference and regulation of any external factors in family relations. my personal belief, the belief of my political force is that family issues, issues of relationships within families should be traditional and should be as they have been for years. in my country, bulgaria, the family is a traditional relationship between a man and a woman and i strongly object to any supranational decision, whether by the commission or the parliament, to take a stand and to intervene in these family relations. no one denies the right to self-determination, no one denies the right of every individual to make decisions about his own life and health. but family relations, the issues of marriage, the issue of family should be national relations and should be relations that are regulated by traditions, as they have been for centuries.
in writing. ‒ progress on equality between women and men in the european union is a topic of paramount importance, especially since the financial crisis. there is a positive trend in gender equality, but the employment rate for women remains rather unsatisfactory. more efforts are needed to achieve the european objectives in this area. that is why i supported the tarabela report. gender equality is a permanent commitment of the union, and moreover, it is a fundamental right. on the other hand, inequality directly affects our society - it hinders employment, development, economic growth. unfortunately, in the 21st century, factors such as domestic violence and gender stereotypes still prevent women from reaching their potential. my personal belief is that more civic education and equal access to information are key factors in this regard. i believe it is our duty as meps to create the conditions for a better representation of women in business, universities and research centres. change does not happen automatically, more efforts are needed at all levels to enable both women and men to participate fully in economic life.

in writing. ‒ competition policy is one of the main pillars of building a functioning single market and has always been at the heart of the european project. the gradual establishment of a european single market without internal borders has brought economic benefits to europe and its trading partners. but in a rapidly changing competitive environment, new and new challenges are emerging that europe must address. the report on eu competition policy presented to us by the commission addresses the key challenges in this area and clearly shows that competition policy is moving in the right direction towards the enlargement and development of the internal market. the report recognises the crucial importance of the implementation of competition policy and the guarantee of a level playing field within the single market. i call on the commission to continue to monitor the independence, impartiality and objectivity of competition policy procedures, as they give consumers and businesses confidence in europe's development towards sustainable growth.
in writing. ‒ an internal market in which goods, services, capital and people can move freely and in which european citizens can develop their businesses freely is impossible without eu competition policy. we need european-level regulation against monopoly practices, agreements between companies to fix prices and share markets, illegal state aid and so on. competition policy works well against abuse, and as a result we get more innovation and benefits for end users: lower prices, better quality and protection. i support the european parliament's resolution on the annual report on eu competition policy. the text was adopted in the plenary by a large majority: 526 votes in favour to 108 against. this is a clear political message from parliament on one of the union's strongest exclusive competence policies. i would point out that the report highlights important trends in the energy sector, namely that the european gas market should develop towards building an energy union with fair and stable prices, improving the diversification of its energy sources and access to strategic infrastructures. bulgaria has a unique opportunity to benefit from the full coincidence of its national interest with a clear pan-european political line.
in writing. ‒ i voted against this motion for a resolution because i am against the policy pursued by the ecb. i'm against her being in a threesome. i'm against it because it doesn't pursue policy driven by economic or financial decisions or arguments, but has become a political tool that is far removed from its recognized role. i am against it because the policy of the ecb has not only not led to an increase in gdp, but on the contrary - during this reporting period we are not seeing growth, but a decline. i am against it because despite the policies of the ecb, unemployment is not falling, but on the contrary, we are seeing an increase not only in general unemployment but also in youth unemployment. at the same time, we're saying that it's a problem that we're trying with all the levers we can to solve. i'm also against it because this policy, coupled with the fall in oil prices, will lead all our countries to deflation, from which we will emerge with great difficulty and effort. the report is very well written, but the reality outside is different. and the ecb can be accused of being sympathetic to the problem, of being part of the problem.
on behalf of the alde group.– madam president, we support in general the report by the rapporteur mr fernandes on the guidelines for the 2016 budget. the priorities are correctly presented as they have been discussed within the budget committee. launching the guidelines for the preparation of budget 2016 we should have two things in mind. firstly, the eu member state economies are already in the 8th year of fiscal consolidation with visible lack of resources to bridge the gap of missing investments. secondly, 2016 will serve as a benchmark of post—electoral multiannual financial framework divisions. the main directions alongside on which the budget should be developed are creation of favourable conditions for economic growth, an increase of employment, which will lead to economic social and territorial cohesion with any member state left aside. in this respect, special emphasis should be put on policies towards growth, competitiveness and employment with the particular highlight on the role of small and medium enterprises and mid-cap companies and on ensuring the proper distribution of european funds. in 2016, the new investment plan proposed by the commission, aiming at mobilising eur 315 billion to stimulate public and private capital investment in europe, shall also be fully operational. while it is expected that the eu budget will fund the biggest part of the guarantee for this european fund for strategic investment, i would like to stress that this eu contribution must not be made at the expense of existing eu multiannual programmes. last, but not least, the 2016 budget has to be provided with a sufficient level of payment appropriations so as to bring real results to the european citizens, to enable the budget to match eu political priorities and to allow the commission to meet its legal obligations. the alde group insists on the conclusion of a structural plan for payment before the presentation of the commission of its draft budget 2016, as jointly agreed among the parliament, council and the commission during the negotiations on budget 2015.
the rapporteur. it is important to recognise that each member state makes its proportionate contribution to the budget and therefore has an equal interest in the most reliable protection of the union's financial interests. the report of the committee on budgetary control, which is before you, was adopted unanimously. however, in the course of its discussion, two different understandings of what adequate protection of the eu budget entails have emerged. it is traditionally considered that the focus should be on restrictions and penalties in relation to the infringements found. but the point is that there are two different types of breaches - one is the result of corruption, fraud and criminal activity. but far more frequent and more widespread are the violations resulting from errors, ignorance of the rules, or overly complex regulations. sanctions therefore need to be combined with active support from member states to both prevent intentional fraud and to decisively limit instances of unintentional infringements. the figures in the european commission's 2013 report are sufficiently indicative of a total of €2 billion and 140 million of financial irregularities, the largest part 840 billion is the result of unintentional deviations from the rules, while the damage from intentional abuses amounts to no more than €309 million. in this context, i would also like to draw your attention to the sensitive issue of interruptions and suspensions of payments in the field of cohesion policy. the text now contained in the report calls on the european commission to follow its policy on the implementation of such measures closely. in this formulation, the wording is half-hearted, because it does not contain the essential meaning of the preventive role of these measures as a means of preventing and preventing infringements. that is both the spirit and the letter of article 325 of the treaty on the functioning of the european union, which gives priority to the objecting party to measures to protect the financial interests of the european union. it is essential that member states report to the european commission on cases of fraud and unintentional infringements detected. this information helps to identify where problems are and to seek solutions so that similar problems do not occur in the future. however, information on fraud and irregularities detected should not be used to discredit countries. on the contrary, countries should be incentivised to report, encouraged to detect and prevent fraud and errors, to punish and correct them, to address shortcomings and gaps in their control systems. therefore, if it is found that some countries are experiencing difficulties, the responsible european institutions and other countries should assist them through good practices, exchange of experience and information, coordination and training. this is the best way to protect the financial interests of the union in accordance with the requirements and mechanisms of sound financial management and in response to the expectations and requirements of european citizens. in conclusion, madam president, i would like to thank in particular those who worked with me on this report, namely my colleague diana popova, the socialist and democratic party secretariat assistant, mrs kudruca, mr philippe, and the chair of the secretariat of the committee on budgetary control, mrs carmen de castillo, for their efforts and for the smooth running of the process.
as a representative of a member state which is undergoing a monitoring report in the field of justice and home affairs, i am particularly sensitive to the subject of the prosecution service and the european public prosecutor's office and corruption. the bulgarian prosecutor's office has not received support from several bulgarian governments, mr. pirinsky, who have shown a high tolerance for corruption. so far, olaf has detected fraud with the funds, but it does not have the powers and competence to protect the european union and its citizens. opponents of the idea of a european public prosecutor's office should be aware that by refusing to create one, the conditions are created for tolerating crime and fraud, whereby the european union is in effect an accomplice to crimes against itself. the influence of the governments of the 28 member states should be reduced in the future european central prosecutor's office. this requires the appointment of a representative of the central prosecutor's office in each member state.
vice-president of the commission. – madam president, i would like to thank the members for all the comments that have been made. we have a huge obligation to our people to use their money wisely and to account for every penny they give to us. the facts are that fraud actually has gone down a bit on the expenditure side: it stands at 0.19% versus 0.21% the year before. but 0.19% is too much and we have to continue relentlessly to push this evil out of the territory of our european project. there was a comment that our accounts have not been signed. our accounts are signed. every year for the last seven years, the court of auditors has signed off the eu accounts but we still have an unacceptably high level of errors. the errors, at 4.7%, far exceed the tolerance of 2% that the court has established and the court has also told us very clearly that we have to simplify the rules. many of you spoke about this. simplifying the rules is necessary not only because then we will have fewer errors but also because then we can have true transparency and people can find out and trace the money. if it is complicated then we cannot do that. we know that transparency is our best friend in fighting fraud and corruption. so i just want to stress that this is the reason we look to parliament as a partner to help us in simplification. we now have rules. unfortunately, in the mff we managed to start from simpler rules and actually make them more complicated in the end but, within the parameters of the mff, let us try to have the foundation for transparency that can help us to use money better and to be more accountable. i completely agree with those who have spoken about the significance of also addressing the revenue side. on the revenue side the trend is in the opposite direction. fraud has gone up a little to 0.29%. obviously we have to take this very seriously because this is foregone revenue for our countries and it is a crime not to be tolerated. i fully subscribe to the notion that prevention is better than cure. preventing fraud and preventing errors is much better than having to respond to them. but we also need to act when wrongdoing or fraud takes place. i believe that if we have a european public prosecutor’s office with the right mandate, which is also able to tackle vat fraud, it will make us more effective, not less effective. this is a notion that i have heard from many here. that, of course, would help us to protect the interests of the european union. and what is in the interests of the european union? it is to protect our taxpayers; and for that we have to be credible in our own activities. this is why olaf has asked the supervisory committee to look into the credibility of data. it is very important that we trust our own office on the data that it provides. that is actually something i personally would follow up. this is not for me to act upon – it is for the supervisory committee – but i am very interested in a good outcome. this is a conversation we will continue. it is an action which should never be stopped and in the end we will be judged by our deeds and not by our words.
rapporteur.– madam president, i believe that the purpose of this exercise is to establish working and effective cooperation between parliament and the commission in truly tackling fraud and irregularities, because it is my belief that the commission in the person of mrs georgieva today has given a very clear commitment to achieving these goals and i think that our overall objective should be to get every necessary support to that effort. i agree with madam gräßle when she says that we have to pay all due account of statistics and i would like to invite colleagues to consider the following data. out of the eur 1.7 billion in expenditure that is subject to irregularities, these represent 1.34% of overall expenditure, which means that over 98% of expenditure has been disbursed correctly. as to the own resources recuperation, eur 380 million – you can find it in the report – are subject to irregularities, which is 1.86% of total traditional own resources. furthermore, in 2013 the volume of irregularities committed decreased by 30% over 2012, and fraud by 21%. i am talking about the values. i admit that the number of irregularities has grown, which requires all our attention, but i do believe, colleagues, that whipping ourselves up into a hypercritical stance is not the best way of protecting the financial interests of the union.
in writing. ladies and gentlemen, the annual report on the protection of the european union's financial interests - the fight against fraud is an important component of the fight against corruption and the misuse of european funds at european level. in reality, it is part of a package of reports and legislative proposals from recent years, including the proposal to establish a european public prosecutor's office and the directive on fraud involving european funds. however, all these proposals and analyses will not lead to an effective fight against corruption and fraud unless they are backed up by concrete measures at european level and in the member states. i therefore call for the creation of a european strategy to combat corruption, which also covers fraud involving european funds. the strategy aims to set a framework for interaction between the different instruments and policies applied at european and national level and to outline guidelines for how member states and european institutions can combat these criminal activities. i believe that the strategy will also provide a clear picture of the state of play in the fight against corruption and the misuse of european funds across the eu, which in turn will remove the unjustified focus on bulgaria and show that other countries are facing the same problems.
on behalf of the ppe group. i would like to thank the rapporteur, charles tannock, for his work on the report and for his good cooperation. montenegro is one of the countries showing progress in a number of areas and has recently opened new negotiating chapters. the work on chapters 23 and 24 remains key, and reforms in this area will determine the dynamics of future negotiations. in the past year, a number of decisions have been taken on the appointment of a prosecutor general, the fight against corruption and the effective functioning of judges and prosecutors. black forest has a vision and a plan of action. we now expect concrete measures to implement the legal framework, which will be important for the further integration of the country. ensuring the rule of law is of key importance for the european future of montenegro. montenegro is also a good example of civil society involvement in preparing for the adoption of european union law. at the same time, i expect the authorities to make more efforts to improve the media environment and to detect and stop crimes against journalists. podgorica must not repeat the mistakes of other countries where media concentration and business dependence lead to the distortion of information and the meaninglessness of democracy. as for the other countries of the western balkans, it is very important for montenegro to break with the shadows of the communist past and to walk the path of historical reconciliation and good neighbourly relations, which is the only possible path for european integration. finally, i would like to warmly congratulate montenegro on its solidarity with the european union's decisions on the common foreign and security policy, in particular despite the backlash against the crisis in ukraine, the sanctions and the fight against the islamic state and terrorism.
the fact is that montenegro is the closest to european union membership among the western balkan countries. the progress the country has made in the past year is remarkable. 16 negotiating chapters are already open, including chapters 23 and 24 on fundamental rights and justice, and chapter 31 on foreign and defence policy. montenegro's foreign policy is fully aligned with the european union's foreign and defence policy. of course, the progress made brings additional responsibilities. montenegro must continue with the reform process. the government and the opposition need to improve dialogue with each other on national issues such as the rule of law, the fight against corruption and media freedom. while welcoming the efforts of the european commission, i would like to state that the european project will not be civilisationally complete if the western balkans are not part of it. full integration is achieved through the membership of all countries of the region in nato and the european union.
member of the commission.– madam president, let me conclude by reiterating my appreciation to the parliament and especially the rapporteur mr tannock, for the work on montenegro. your resolution captures the basic elements of our policy towards that country and the acknowledgment of the achievements that have been possible, but it also sends a clear message on what needs to be done now. this year, montenegro should continue working on rule of law issues and focus on delivering results. results should include direct recourse in the fight against corruption and organised crime, as several speakers have said. at the same time, we expect montenegro to continue working on meeting the opening benchmarks set in challenging areas such as competition, the environment, climate change and food safety. the opening benchmarks provide clear guidance on what needs to be done. for the chapters that have already been opened, we will work with montenegro in helping the country meet the closing benchmarks. we have a challenging programme ahead of us and the support of this house will be instrumental in completing it. next month our joint parliamentary committee will meet again in podgorica. this will be a good opportunity to take stock of what has been achieved in montenegro since the last meeting in december and to present the challenges ahead. let us be clear, it is up to each candidate country to ensure that there is progress in the reforms necessary for its accession to the union. it is up to each candidate country to ensure that these reforms bring results that help improve the lives of its citizens. the commission can offer, and does offer, its technical know—how, its expertise and, of course, financial assistance in meeting the challenges of the accession process. strong political will and determination will be needed from montenegro to make progress. this will be required not only of the government but of the whole political system. montenegro’s cross-party consensus on eu accession is a major asset in this endeavour. i was reassured about this during my last visit to that country, when i had the opportunity to meet the european affairs committee of the montenegro parliament. this was further evidence that montenegro and the whole of montenegrin society is trying to meet all the challenges in order to make significant progress when it comes to the future accession of the country. once again, and to stress it again and again, the announcement of president juncker that there will be no enlargement during this period does not mean we are not following up on all the necessary negotiations. it is more about the process than about negotiations, and what we have discussed and what you have discussed today on montenegro, and what we will discuss imminently with other countries, is proof that it is about the process and less about the negotiations. it is about helping countries catch up, to meet european standards and to be ready and mature enough to join the european union at a given moment.
president-in-office of the council.– mr president, our debate today showed general support for the european parliament’s resolution on the commission’s progress report. we seem to agree on the point that the work with potential accession countries should continue towards democratic reforms and closer european integration. i am pleased that the speakers, like the council, praised the efforts by montenegro to align with the common foreign and security policy. the council also attaches particular importance to the area of the rule of law and fundamental rights and has urged montenegro to tackle the issues of concern identified by the commission, especially the independence of the judiciary, the fight against corruption and organised crime. the council thoroughly assessed the action plans which montenegro prepared in order to meet the opening benchmarks for the chapters on the judiciary and fundamental rights and on justice, freedom and security. they were of good quality and allowed us to get to the next stage and open the negotiations in these two key chapters. the action plans were revised in february 2015 and re—worked to calibrate and adjust them, with implications for the interim benchmarks set for these chapters. we also agreed that freedom of speech and media independence is one of the fundamental values of the european agenda. it is essential that all attacks on media representatives be properly investigated in order to ensure freedom of expression and citizens’ trust in public institutions. as a presidency, we are continuing the work on accession negotiations with montenegro and are looking forward to opening new chapters during our presidency. so far, the accession negotiations process with montenegro has been quite dynamic. nevertheless, in order to ensure the further opening of new chapters, montenegro needs to deliver tangible results in the rule of law area. the council, as already mentioned, now expects montenegro to keep up the momentum and intensify its efforts to move forward with the reforms and their effective implementation. we look forward to further cooperation with the european parliament and the commission on this subject.
president-in-office of the council.– madam president, let me first thank the rapporteur, ivo vajgl, for his work. like the parliament in its draft resolution, the council, in december 2014, welcomed the fact that the eu agenda remained the strategic priority of the former yugoslav republic of macedonia. but we note that it will require strong political will and commitment on the part of politicians from the former yugoslav republic of macedonia to continue the implementation of the eu—related reforms. the council broadly shares the view that the country has achieved a high level of alignment with the legislative acquis. however, i would point out that the council conclusions of december 2014 emphasised serious concerns about an increasing politicisation of state institutions and growing shortcomings in relation to the independence of the judiciary and to media freedoms. failure to deliver on these issues has damaged the sustainability of reforms. we will continue to emphasise that the rule of law, fundamental rights and media freedom are at the heart of the enlargement process and that it is essential that candidate countries take decisive, rapid action to address the challenges. as shown by the latest developments, political crises between government and opposition parties are recurrent. the council shares the view expressed in parliament’s draft resolution that a more constructive and inclusive political dialogue is needed. i would urge both the government and the opposition in the former yugoslav republic of macedonia to demonstrate political responsibility and engage in a constructive dialogue in order to resolve all the issues that remain open and to accelerate the reform process in line with european standards. concerning the inter—ethnic situation, greater trust between the communities needs to be built. the ohrid framework agreement continues to be an essential element for democracy and rule of law in the former yugoslav republic of macedonia. we would remind you that the review of the ohrid framework agreement needs to be completed rapidly and its recommendations need to be implemented. the council reiterates that maintaining good neighbourly relations remains an essential part of european integration. this includes a negotiated and mutually accepted solution to the name issue, under the auspices of the united nations. we call for progress towards bringing the longstanding discussions on this issue to a definitive conclusion without delay. in the light of the overall importance of maintaining good neighbourly relations, it is essential to continue high—level contacts between the former yugoslav republic of macedonia and bulgaria, and to translate them into concrete action and results. finally, let me add that the council broadly shares the commission’s assessment that the political criteria continue to be sufficiently met, on the basis of the cumulative progress achieved. the council also took note of the commission’s recommendation, reflected in parliament’s draft resolution, that accession negotiations should be opened. with a view to a possible decision by the european council to open such negotiations, the council will return to the issue at some time this year, on the basis of an update by the commission on the implementation of reforms. this will take into account the high—level accession dialogue and the tangible steps taken to promote good neighbourly relations and to reach a negotiated and mutually accepted solution to the name issue. progress in all these areas will be conducive to reaching a positive decision among the eu member states.
member of the commission.– madam president, honourable members, i would like to acknowledge the report which has been prepared by your rapporteur ivo vajgl, which to a large extent corresponds to the findings of our 2014 progress report on the country. i welcome the supportive stance the european parliament has taken towards the country’s efforts in the accession process. in our progress report we repeated our recommendation to open accession negotiations but raised our serious concerns about selective justice, problems with the freedom of expression, politicisation and the lack of constructive political dialogue in the country. the current political crisis has further exacerbated all these issues. i will come to this in a second. as the draft resolution points out, the level of alignment with the eu acquis remains high, but decisive action is required from skopje to address backsliding in several key areas. i share your concerns that delays in setting a date for the start of accession negotiations have added to the frustration of the population in the country and also affected the sustainability of previous reforms as well as the leverage of the union as such. as regards the current political events in the country, i visited skopje in february and expressed my very serious concerns to all political leaders. all of them must step back from the brink. they are all playing with the future of their country. in my frequent and on—going contacts with the political leadership and the opposition, i continue to urge political actors to engage in constructive dialogue within the parliament, focusing on the strategic priorities of the country and all its citizens. they have no time to lose. i also reiterated that investigations are to be conducted by the proper authorities, notably the judiciary, in a manner that is totally independent, transparent and rapid with full regard for due process and the presumption of innocence. in this respect, i welcome any initiative taken by european political party groups and individual meps to help overcome the crisis and welcome their willingness to engage further. the commission will work closely with the meps in this respect. i would like to reiterate that the commission stands ready to provide support, while fully insisting on the separation of powers. the commission could support efforts to improve checks and balances and address failures, for instance through the assessment on continued concerns about the independence of justice. meanwhile, irrespective of the origins of the wiretapping, which needs to be investigated, any potential wrongdoing revealed, along with its disturbing content, must be investigated and appropriate consequences must be taken, especially if it fundamentally impinges on the rule of law and separation of powers. at a time when public trust in institutions is low, all public figures must act responsibly and take accountability for their actions in their own interest. turning to the issue of good neighbourly relations, i agree with you that it is an essential part of the country’s progress towards the european union. while the country has good relations with its western balkan neighbours, relations with greece and bulgaria remain affected. it is of the utmost importance that there are continued efforts to improve the overall climate for cooperation and trust. good neighbourly relations remain an essential part of the enlargement process and the eu’s own history shows that working out compromises on contested issues is the best way to enhance regional cooperation and move forward. i would also like to use this opportunity to highlight the importance of maintaining good inter—ethnic relations. they remain fundamental for the very functioning of the country and its eu perspective. to build on the positive lessons learned so far, the review of the ohrid framework agreement is important and should be soon converted into policy recommendations. finally, let me stress that renewed efforts from all sides are needed to improve relations and trust between parties, and these need to be friendly. therefore, i urge all parties to respect the rule of law and the independence of the judiciary and freedom of the press.
on behalf of the ecr group. our western neighbour's perspective is undoubtedly european, but what i ask and insist you pay attention to is the following. the government in skopje must be made very clear that european membership means european responsibilities. these european responsibilities mean only one thing: respect for the rights and legitimate interests of all ethnic communities in the territory of the state, including the bulgarian one. i would like to draw your attention to the fact that there is still no good-neighbourly agreement between our two countries, and it is key because it requires a few simple things: the cessation of hate speech by skopje, the cessation of the distortion of history by skopje, the recognition of the rights and legitimate interests of all communities, including the bulgarian community, to be able to use their native language, to be able to associate in public organizations and their political organizations. all these issues are key to the continuation of the european perspective of our western neighbour, whom we regard as a brotherly country.
madam president, discussing the progress report on the republic of macedonia, we can indeed say that there has been progress on a number of issues. however, the socialist family is deeply concerned about the political climate in that country. ever since the last elections in spring 2014, which the office of the organisation for security and cooperation in europe (osce) and the commission heavily criticised, the opposition has been out of parliament. the problem is not just about the current, very sharp and dangerous crisis: the problem is that we have seen this before, in december 2012. then, with mediation assistance by the european institutions, which involved many members of the european parliament, the crisis was overcome, but not for good and not in a sustainable way, because it has now been reproduced in an even worse manner. this time it is not only about political dialogue or its absence; it is a matter of upholding basic democratic standards, which are extremely important for the negotiations. we have to ask – when we speak about the allegations against the leader of the opposition – whether we can really trust the judicial system, which was so severely criticised by the commission. it was the commission which pointed out that this was a selective judiciary, and that it is the basic problem. we can assist and facilitate dialogue but the truth is that the majority party – the government – should lead, and continue with a normal dialogue in the country and with the political process. otherwise the prospects for negotiating macedonia’s accession to the european union could be endangered. lastly, i would also remind our macedonian friends that good neighbourly relations are really important, and that they should show goodwill in order to achieve concrete results and to deliver.
the authorities in the former yugoslav republic of macedonia must mature in european values, which make it unacceptable to make policies based on ethnic opposition and the identity of the population. in order for europe to pursue an active accession policy towards fyrom, the governments of this country must satisfy the claims of the old members of the european union, among which are bulgaria and greece. we demand a transparent census of the population in the republic, which will reflect the bulgarian minority, which in all probability will be the majority. i would like to remind you that the last census was in 2002, when only 1417 people were declared as bulgarians, while 50,000 citizens had applied for bulgarian citizenship. we insist on the abolition of any interpretation of the authorities in burm towards the bulgarian history, which is objectively part of the history of the territory of this country. the government of this republic steals identity from bulgarian, greek and even, notice, egyptian history.
i will start with this: the citizens of the republic of macedonia need not strong leaders but strong and transparent institutions and deserve a european future now, not later. unfortunately, the republic of macedonia is currently in a serious crisis. there are no steps to improve the political climate in the country, it is even worse. i have the impression that the government and prime minister gruevski personally are deliberately moving the country away from its european path, manipulating citizens by maintaining a dependent media environment that helps them win elections. this calls into question basic democratic principles. skopje also fails to take the necessary actions to improve the situation of citizens who openly declare their bulgarian ethnic identity, as well as to sign a treaty of good neighborliness with bulgaria. i have already called on the authorities to heed the recommendations of the european parliament and to break with the shadows of the past and the practices inherited from communism. skopje must take a decisive step to improve relations with its neighbours, and i stress, this is also a regional problem. and i'm going to share with you a litmus test of my own for want of good neighborliness. for 7 years bulgaria has been trying to get permission to restore some military cemeteries and monuments, such as the one where the communists blew up the grave of colonel kavarnaliyev. no reply, although according to the legislation of the republic of macedonia we should receive such a reply. it is irresponsible to educate young people in the balkans in hatred and distrust of their loved ones by manipulating history with methods from the totalitarian past. this is not the way to reconciliation and a european future.
in writing. the political situation in the republic of macedonia has deteriorated significantly in recent months, and this is a major obstacle to the country's european and civilizational path. the shocking abuses, the mass wiretapping scandals and the lack of political dialogue threaten the rule of law and even increase the use of hate speech. macedonia is an important factor for stability in the region and the european project will not be completed as intended without the membership of the western balkan countries. in this context, i welcome commissioner hahn's decision to visit the republic of macedonia and to involve the european union in the resolution of the political conflict. the country needs our help and we must immediately propose a political team, on the model successfully applied in albania, to bring all political groups back to the negotiating table and to help find a constructive dialogue. the immediate launch of a public debate is necessary to restart the reform process in the country. political stability is needed to ensure sustainable cooperation, which will accelerate the european agenda of the republic of macedonia and lead to the opening of negotiating chapters for membership.
on behalf of the ecr group. mr. mcallister, without a doubt your report is extremely balanced and reflects objectively the development of the situation in serbia. and there's certainly progress, there's a willingness to talk, but there's also a lot of problems. issues not only related to internal affairs in serbia itself, such as freedom of speech, such as freedom of the media or the state of justice. another substantial problem is the bilateral relations between serbia and some of the neighbouring countries. there are still unfinished relations with croatia, there are still laws that in theory have been adopted in the republic of serbia, but are not applied in practice, and this is the example of the non-application of the obligation to teach in bulgarian in the western bulgarian suburbs. commissioner hahn, these are questions that need to be asked and serbia needs to make its decisions on them if it is to develop along the european path.
serbia started negotiations a year ago and has shown a willingness to follow the path of european integration. the key now is for this willingness to be expressed in concrete action and political will for reform. it is important that serbia shares in solidarity the common european foreign and security policy, as colleagues have mentioned, and that it does not succumb to pressure from third countries. the european union and the european commission must therefore help serbia. the country has a solid legislative framework in the field of minority rights, but the application of the law is not always uniform and impartial. the bulgarian minority still has serious difficulties in learning their mother tongue. there are all kinds of political obstacles to this, bureaucratic and financial excuses are being sought why textbooks cannot be translated into bulgarian. at the same time, i call again on serbia, as the report says, to begin to reconcile its own history, to open the files of the communist secret services of the uda and the kos and to make the relevant archives available to the former yugoslav republics. it is also very important that serbia's economic and integration policy includes the border economic backward areas where many of the minority groups live.
i would like to start by congratulating my esteemed mrs lunacek on the serious work we have done together. it was a real challenge for me to be her shadow speaker on this report. the report reflects the situation in kosovo objectively, clearly outlines the problems and outlines the results achieved. it is impossible not to mention the problems, it is impossible not to mention the suspicions of corruption linked to one of the european institutions, it is impossible not to mention, of course, the high level of organised crime linked to drug trafficking and other serious criminal offences. what concerns me most, however, is the possibility and availability of safe haven bases for fighters who travel from europe to the islamic state and who threaten in some way, by returning, our continent as well. this too must be considered.
the year 2014 can be noted as a significant year for kosovo's european integration. the stabilisation and association agreement has been initiated and we expect it to become a reality this spring. the country held elections that were described as democratic and transparent by all international observers. despite a difficult political dialogue between the main political parties, which lasted six months, a government was formed. it is now of the utmost importance that kosovo continues with the key reforms that will help improve the economic environment. the professionalism and transparency of the judiciary, the fight against organised crime and the high levels of corruption remain highly problematic. economic reforms are needed to combat unemployment effectively. in this context, i believe it is timely for the council to adopt a decision and sign a framework agreement on kosovo's participation in european union programmes. this will help the country's economic growth and strengthen cooperation with the european union in various sectoral policies.
a question asked by raising a blue card. you talked about foreign policy, but you didn't mention anything about the islamic state. do you not think that the main issue for the european council should be the crimes of the islamic state, the investigation of these crimes and of the countries that support them, such as the republic of turkey?
the european union continues to face a major challenge rapid economic recovery and growth, and addressing high levels of unemployment, especially among young people. this challenge requires coordinated action and responses at all levels, and the european semester in recent years has produced many positive results that have helped us to overcome the crisis more quickly. today, the determination to put europe back on the path to growth is stronger than ever. our efforts must be directed towards all three pillars of our economic policy and above all towards stimulating investment. the measures in this direction are not limited to the juncker plan, the changes are much larger. they are linked to improving the overall investment and business ecosystem in the european union. stimulating investment, whether public or private, alongside the untapped potential of the single market, especially in the area of network industries, will help to improve competitiveness, job creation and growth. the essence of the european semester is coordination and synchronisation. they can be effective if we have robust rules that apply equally to small and large member states. it is particularly important that member states actively promote investment in line with their fiscal space and pursue structural reforms, especially where negative demographic trends and growth are evident.
in writing. ‒ i welcome the new amendments as they provide for a structural improvement in the objectives and general functions of the general fisheries commission. the proposed measures are vital to strengthen the supervisory and control mechanisms also affecting and protecting bulgarian waters and fish stocks. the new sanctions and dispute settlement mechanisms will provide the union with an effective tool to tackle issues such as overfishing, which threatens many native species, and unregulated and illegal activities that have a negative impact on the environment. the european parliament's proposals will strengthen policy in this area and help to make the management of fish stocks in the mediterranean and associated waters more adaptive, responsible and modern.
in writing. ‒ i supported the report on the protection of the eu's financial interests because i believe that in a time of crisis and its resolution it is extremely important to reduce the number of abuses. the reduction of abuses will ensure effective and sustainable development within the community, which is a very important objective at this time. the report noted that it was more important for member states not to cover up fraud cases in order to be transparent with the institutions. agriculture is one of the main sectors in bulgaria and access to european funds is vital for producers, but unfortunately there is a high number of fraud and abuses both at national and european level. it is important to reduce the number of such fraudulent practices and to increase the efficiency of the funds allocated by making them more accessible to small producers, as in bulgaria a large part of the funds allocated is absorbed by large entrepreneurs. i also note the problems of smuggling, which is damaging both to bulgarian producers and the budget, and to our partners in the union. once again, i support this report and congratulate the rapporteur on his good work.
i supported the proposed report because it gives a fair assessment of montenegro's progress towards joining the european union. of course, it has its weaknesses. they're related to some degree to freedom of speech, to media ownership. there are of course issues that should also be clarified with the republic of croatia, but overall montenegro is the model and the first in the balkans in the practical application of european standards. not to mention that montenegro has absolute unanimity and absolute agreement with the common european policy regarding the crisis in ukraine, regarding the anti-terrorist legislation related to the islamic state. and of course it has to do with its legislation for individuals on its territory who would be engaged in illegal combat related to the islamic state. that is why i supported this report, which gives a fair assessment of the progress made in montenegro. i wish them luck.
i also supported this report with the clear awareness that we are sending an extremely important message to the government in skopje, namely that bilateral relations must be resolved, that the treaty of good neighbourliness and friendship between bulgaria and macedonia must be concluded, that the practice of hate speech against bulgaria and bulgarian in the media in skopje must be stopped, that the rights of all citizens in the republic of macedonia to unite on their national symbol and principle and to use their native language must be recognized. for the first time in a european parliament document, macedonian citizens with bulgarian national consciousness are mentioned. this is extremely important to me and i hope that this message is clearly heard by the government in skopje. this is the only obligation and guarantee of this government to be able to continue on its path of european integration, knowing that it must use all european rules and practices.
i also supported this report, but we must send a clear message to the belgrade government that on its path towards european integration it must respect european rules, it must respect the rights of every national minority on its territory, recognized by the republic of serbia itself, the rights of every national minority to be educated in its own language, to have textbooks in its own language, to speak its own language. we are talking about the bulgarian national minority in the western bulgarian peripheries, which is recognized by serbia, but until now absolute obstacles and serious problems have been made before this minority can learn its native bulgarian language and use bulgarian textbooks. this must be our clear message to serbia on the otherwise acceptable, otherwise legitimate, otherwise normal path to its european integration.
i also supported this report because the rapporteur, mrs lunacek, had given a completely accurate account of the events in kosovo. of course, kosovo has a lot of problems with organized crime, with drug trafficking, with the difficult economic situation, with many people being forced to leave kosovo. of course, in that report we also recommended the protection of the rights of self-determination and the use of the mother tongue of national minorities, including the goran, and that is the path that this country should follow if it wants to be part of the european family despite the problems. i appreciate the efforts of mrs. lunacek. they're heading in the right direction. that is why i supported this report.
in writing. ‒ ladies and gentlemen, i voted against mr rosati's report because it is of paramount importance to me and my colleagues from the european conservatives and reformists to protect the sovereignty of the member states and to oppose the additional conferral of competences on the european institutions. i fully support the idea of coordination between the economic, social and financial policies of the member states, but this is, above all, about national budgets and the role played by the european semester. initially, we hoped that the european semester would allow member states to be consulted on their budgetary policies, but it turned out that the european commission had become a controlling body that could oblige member states to pursue one or another budgetary policy. this is completely contrary to our views, since, on the one hand, the national budget is a fundamental element of any sovereign state, and on the other hand, it is unacceptable for an undemocratically elected institution to take decisions of this nature that directly affect the member states and their citizens. these matters should remain entirely within the competence of the member states.
in writing. ‒ ladies and gentlemen, i supported this report because its adoption is imperative because the european council, back in 2012, recognised the need to improve the governance of the single market. the proposals set out in this report are aimed at improving the implementation and performance of the single market, while ensuring full respect for the principles of subsidiarity and proportionality. this is reflected in the involvement of national parliaments in the coordination of single market priorities. it is of particular importance to me and to the group of european conservatives and reformists that the principles of subsidiarity and proportionality are strongly established in all areas and policies, as they alone are the guarantor of national sovereignty in the current form of the union, in which the european institutions increasingly usurp the competences of national legislatures.
in writing. ‒ the eu single market has entered its 23rd year. it has transformed the way europeans live, learn, work, travel, and do business. only in the last three years has a report on the management of the single market within the european semester been included in the annual growth review. i note this to justify my dissatisfaction with the fragile progress on the same recommendations for these three years, aimed at increasing the degree of integration of the single market. otherwise, objectivity requires that we emphasise that it is the path taken towards deeper integration of the eu's single market that is the expression of new political thinking, the key to increasing the eu's competitiveness and a responsible attitude towards a changing world. the action of the member states is paramount to achieving this, and it is therefore essential to build the single market as a pillar of the european semester.
in writing. ‒ i supported the resolution because the issue of sexual abuse of children on the internet is not up for discussion and we must oppose it in every way. this issue is becoming increasingly important in the context of the growing digital society with almost unlimited access to information, which on the one hand has an inclusive effect on the elderly, but on the other hand can pose a serious threat to the youngest members of our society, who are most vulnerable to harmful and inaccurate information. unfortunately, there are too many cases that reveal the imperfections of the current safeguards and it is therefore imperative to put this issue on the agenda. there is a huge need for additional protection, and it is our shared responsibility to work towards creating a secure digital space in which future generations have free access to information.
in writing. ‒ with the development and widespread use of high-speed internet and the transformation of social networking sites into a social and economic phenomenon that has changed the face of communication and information exchange, the youngest and most vulnerable members of our society are exposed to specific risks such as cybercrime, the dissemination of violent content, cyberbullying, the misuse of personal information and data. the resolution on combating sexual violence against children online is of particular importance as it highlights this serious problem and the need to develop a comprehensive approach and a broad strategy to combat violence and exploitation of children online and to protect the best interests of the child.
mr president, there is absolutely no doubt that the dastardly murder of boris nemtsov is one of those killings that have the potential to change the course of events, if not of history. i would invite colleagues to look back to the morning after the murder and the message sent by a no less well—known figure, sobchak, at the murder scene when she said ‘let us all stop’. what she meant was: let us all back down. i think she struck a note in russian society which woke up to the understanding that a very dangerous line had been crossed, and that each and every citizen in russia has to think about what is going to happen. war has been mentioned many times in this debate. we have to think very carefully as to our input, and in which direction events will evolve.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to congratulate and thank mr brok for his excellent report, which offers both a genuine strategic vision of the union's foreign and security policy and concrete measures to improve it. i would like to stress three elements. first of all, i would like to thank you, mrs mogherini, for your great contribution to raising the profile of our action. let's keep it that way. and in this, the special representatives also have a key role to play. we need to give them a higher value for their work. at the operational level too, we must continue to seek to provide ourselves with adequate means to enhance this visibility. secondly, the coherence between our internal and external policies. strengthening ourselves externally will bring benefits internally. let us consider some examples, particularly on sensitive subjects such as migration. coherence must also be ensured both at council level and in third countries between union delegations and embassies. it's a matter of credibility. finally, cooperation with regional organisations. working with the african union, the arab league and the gulf countries gives us the chance to better fight terrorism, to better cooperate, to the benefit of all citizens.
mr panzeri has obviously done a lot of work on this report, but we in the group of the european conservatives and reformists have some remarks to make. we do not agree with the inclusion of topics related to development, trade, fiscal policy, climate, refugee policy and asylum. we also think that the report crosses the line with its criticism of the member states of the european union, for example lithuania, croatia and slovakia, in terms of their domestic policies, in terms of the family. we believe that each country has the right to decide through its own internal referendums such important issues in its own traditions as the family. separately, i personally think that more attention could have been paid to the victims of the islamic state and the complicity of the countries that support it, such as turkey.
madam president, madam mogherini, the resolution of the libyan crisis is clearly a priority, indeed an emergency, because we are all directly affected and that is why we need a strategy that is up to the challenge. first, we must continue to support the negotiating process under the auspices of the united nations, with a view to a political solution to the conflict. but we also need to strengthen diplomatic efforts and find concrete levers that allow us to move towards the union of all parties against the expansion of the islamic state. in this context, a few observations. let us make sure that all efforts are in the same direction and that the countries in the region also have sufficient leadership. secondly, perhaps we should think of a plan b which could also be a lever in the discussions that are taking place. what would we do if the talks came to nothing? let us have the courage to ask that question too! thirdly, i shall stress the issue of reconciliation. we must reach agreement at national level and, above all, promote reconciliation and unity at local level. good luck to the mayors and local elected officials! and i'd like to draw attention to the women. the full participation of libyan women must be ensured so that they contribute to sustainable peace and security for future generations. and yes, the european union can make a difference in dealing with the consequences of the crisis. to deal with the humanitarian situation, to give full support to the countries that are hosting refugees: that is what we are asked to do, because in the end, to anticipate is to be better prepared, and that is where the european union can make a difference.
terrorism is the greatest threat to the world today. it threatens all citizens, regardless of their ethnic or religious affiliation. i welcome the diplomatic efforts initiated with the league of arab states to act in a comprehensive and coordinated manner against the threat of terrorism and extremist violence. madam minister, the fight against terrorism must ensure full respect for international human rights standards. sharing intelligence with third parties and creating tools to curb the financing of terrorist organisations is essential. i see the proposal initiated to create a 'security officer' post in the european union delegations as an opportunity for better coordination. another instrument is the strengthening of security cooperation in the renewed european neighbourhood policy. alongside the creation of a european strategy to combat international terrorism, we need to deepen intercultural and interethnic dialogue among all countries. this dialogue must take place both within and outside the european union. i believe deeply that freedom is the backbone of security, and that diversity and variety are the foundation of any freedom.
in writing. extreme liberalism and illegal migration have allowed a clash of civilizations to take place in the heart of europe. on one side, the european christian civilization, and on the other, those determined to impose their salafist order and rules. the increase in extremist attacks and the growing anti-semitism are a direct result of the spread and preaching of radical salafi islam. the best example of this is the paris attacks, where radical islamists born and raised in france shot dead 17 people, four of them jews. europe today has no islamophobia, but diversity and freedom of expression. movements like pegida are europeans' response to attempts to change the face and spirit of europe. europe is founded and built on christian values and only by preserving them can it survive.
