the act of kidnapping hundreds of children is unacceptable, no matter what end of the world we're talking about. the events in south sudan have been a hardship for the local population. however, europe must play an active role in finding ways to address this and other similar challenges together with international organisations, because conflicts around the world are increasing every year and more and more minors are either suffering or being drawn into them. the situation in the country is particularly critical. schools there are used for military purposes, and youngsters are taken and trained as soldiers. according to international organizations, more than 400,000 children have been forced out of school in recent months because of the conflict there. that's nearly half a million children who become at-risk. i am of the firm opinion that the european commission should help to mobilise resources for the long-term reintegration of children who have been involved in conflicts, and this should be done in coordination with the un special envoy for children in armed conflict, unicef and other agencies.
i supported the joint resolution on child sexual abuse online because i believe that the increasingly digitised world we live in does not provide sufficient protection for adolescents from the dangers that exist online. it is clear that children are starting to use the internet at an earlier age and this requires us to ensure that they do not feel uncomfortable and that they are in a safe and secure environment. we can only achieve this through partnership and collaboration between institutions, ngos, the media and business representatives. we need to support educational and awareness-raising initiatives, both for minors and for their parents, guardians and teachers. i call on the commission and the governments of the member states to engage with representatives of alternative payment systems for more effective cooperation with law enforcement authorities to achieve better identification of payment processes related to the commercial distribution of child abuse material.
in writing. i did not support the motion for a resolution because i believe that the eu's policy on russia over the past year has not produced any constructive results, but on the contrary has caused difficulties for millions of european citizens and cannot continue in its present form. the murder of boris nemtsov is undoubtedly an act which i condemn and i believe that the relevant authorities of the russian federation should make the necessary efforts to clarify the circumstances surrounding this murder. at the same time, however, i do not think it is within the european parliament's competence to assess the russian approach, which is done through this text. instead, we should look for ways to melt the ice. with such texts we build new walls in europe. i believe that such acts only worsen the already strained relations between the eu and the russian federation. the union must offer solutions to the difficulties faced by european citizens. a number of our citizens and industries, such as agriculture and tourism, have been affected, especially for countries traditionally known in the russian market, such as my country, bulgaria. i believe that the text of the resolution in this regard is unnecessarily provocative and one-sided and does not reflect the lack of adequate policies on the part of the eu regarding compensation for its citizens and member states for policies that are decided in brussels.
in writing. ‒ ladies and gentlemen, i voted abstention on the report by mrs brock on the annual report of the high representative of the european union for foreign affairs and security policy to the european parliament. i believe that the report has its strengths, such as the threat posed by the islamic state, the situation in ukraine and the serious terrorist threats faced by many eu member states. on the other hand, however, i cannot help but note that the report draws general conclusions which in no way contribute to the development of the common security and defence policy of the european union and its member states.
in writing. ‒ i supported the resolution on the memorandum of cooperation between the eu and the league of arab states because, as a representative in the european parliament and a member of the foreign affairs committee, it is extremely important to me to find strategic partners in the fight against terrorism, and in particular against the islamic state. the resolution includes important elements such as the need to take measures to limit uncontrolled migration and the lack of accurate data on the number of european citizens who go to be trained as terrorists or to take an active part in military action on the side of terrorist organisations. i therefore believe that the european parliament should call on the european council to develop a cooperation mechanism to enable the tracking of migration and the reduction of arms trafficking. an important partner in our fight against the terrorist group islamic state is the member of the league of arab states, jordan. my personal belief is that the fight against the islamic state must be fought not just by air strikes, but by ground military action.
in writing. i voted for the resolution because it stresses the essential points. first, our cooperation must be translated into concrete action. a multidimensional approach is needed, which takes into account, beyond security, cultural, religious and socioeconomic aspects. promoting the exchange of good practice, as well as developing and disseminating a common counter-narrative to religious extremism, are key actions. in addition, we insist on the development of a common early warning and crisis prevention mechanism. intensified political dialogue must make it possible to avoid getting bogged down in crises that we are unable to resolve and to promote the role of mediator of certain countries and cohesion towards a peaceful and democratic development of the region. finally, the sources of funding and recruitment of terrorist groups must be cut off. the eu has to increase its political pressure and build up its capacity on the ground, particularly at the borders.
in writing. i am of the opinion that the approach taken by the european council on eu policy towards russia over the past year is not producing the desired results and needs to be changed. at a time when our citizens, and especially those employed in agriculture and tourism, are expecting us to adopt decisions and policies to support and protect them, the european council is dragging us into a new cycle of sanctions extensions. they affect not only the relationship at the level of governance, but also millions of citizens and entire economic sectors, especially in europe. farmers in my country, bulgaria, took the news with fear because they remember what happened after the imposition of similar sanctions last year - they were in merciless competition with thousands of other european farmers for the severely inadequate compensation, which ran out in days. it is our responsibility to dispel that fear and give them the confidence that better times will come to them very soon. solidarity is becoming increasingly alien to the european union, and the gap between the decisions taken by some european politicians and the real problems and needs of the average european is widening. the solutions must be sought for the benefit of the ordinary citizen and put into practice as soon as possible.
in writing.– the annual tax report is an important exercise as it addresses issues such as tax fraud, tax evasion and tax havens, which pose a threat to both the nature of the single market and the image of the eu. as an integral part of the european single market, the digital single market is also highly dependent on the tax legislative framework the eu is shaping. this framework should enable the core aim of the digital single market – establishing a single market that gives equal conditions for production and trade for any european over the internet. i thus welcome the report, which calls for treating e-products equally with their physical alternatives with regards to taxation issues. currently, e-commerce presents a major challenge for tax administrations, given the often multi-jurisdictional nature of the transactions and the potential anonymity of the parties. the eu should therefore push for policies that make it easier to do e-commerce. the proposal in the annual tax report that invites the commission to come up with a clear legislative framework to ensure equality between electronic and physical products is a good first step, yet there is a further need to review all eu tax policy from a digital market perspective.
(ecr) is used. madam president, mr commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, ukraine's economy is undoubtedly in a very difficult situation, the country is in a severe economic crisis and its currency is depreciating. from this point of view, this loan may be a lifesaver and necessary, but i would ask both the rapporteur and the commissioner to monitor very closely the expenditure of these funds so that they go where they are needed and not into the hands of those who are still fighting each other in the streets of ukrainian cities. and i would ask for one more thing: that this loan be linked to a serious commitment by the ukrainian government to reform, especially in the area of preserving the human rights of national minorities, which in the state of ukraine are actually more than even the indigenous population. i remind you again, the number of the bulgarian minority there alone is more than half a million.
in writing. ladies and gentlemen, the decision to grant macro-financial assistance is of the utmost importance for the implementation of political and economic reforms in ukraine. our relations with ukraine are developing within the framework of the european neighbourhood policy and we must support the government's efforts to reform. the military conflict, which has led to the violation of sovereignty and territorial integrity, has had a detrimental impact on the ukrainian economy. the so-called 'minister of foreign affairs' of the so-called 'donetsk people's republic'. as a result, the country's economy has shrunk by more than 20% in the last year, and direct budgetary expenditure related to the conflict has further impaired ukraine's balance of payments and liquidity. in this context, i consider the granting of financial assistance to be a fully timely measure, because the country's needs for external financing are significantly higher than initially determined. for its part, the ukrainian authorities should reaffirm their commitment to reforms in line with the framework provided for in the association agreement and present a clear action plan.
(ecr) is used. why should we make german companies into monopolies? minimum rates of pay in transport must take account of the legal wage rates in each individual member state of the european union. if employers of truck drivers from the poorest country in europe - bulgaria - were obliged to pay minimum rates of french or german standards, it would be a killer for the bulgarian transport industry. centralized wage setting is absolutely not possible across the european union because of the huge differences in the standard of pay. it is in the interest of national economies that minimum rates should be set by national legislation and that the remuneration of workers in transit should not be subject to centralised regulations by the european commission. i would remind you, commissioner, that the fundamental rule of the european union is the free movement of goods and people.
in writing. the german proposal sounds very good, but the truth is that this measure is also being introduced to restrict access to foreign companies in germany. according to the rules adopted by berlin, all wage-earners, even those who are passing through germany, must be paid 8.5 euros per hour. thus, bulgarian hauliers would be obliged to pay similar wages plus travel expenses. that makes them uncompetitive and they'll just refuse to work in germany. this idea of germany to restrict carriers from bulgaria, poland, lithuania and other european countries is intended to benefit german carriers. one day the big member states talk about free markets and competition, and the next day they adopt the exact opposite measures in their national legislation. incidentally, france also applies this approach and tries to discriminate against carriers from small countries. france is trying to introduce rules that require drivers to sleep in hotels, which disrupts their routes and increases the cost of transport. all these seemingly insignificant measures are aimed at restricting small countries. it's a form of protectionism. however, when it is applied by germany and france, it is supposedly legal, and if we decide to do it, we will be sanctioned. that's the double standard we're fighting against.
in writing. the german minimum wage act in force since 1 january 2015 in the transport sector has raised a number of concerns about its compliance with eu law, in particular with the internal market provisions and the posting of workers directive. the introduction of a minimum wage of eur 8,50 per hour worked in germany is a protectionist measure of its own, which has a number of negative consequences: additional administrative costs for transport operators, increased transport costs to find alternative routes and higher prices for the end user. the new rules are particularly unfavourable to small and medium-sized international carriers. according to the international road transport union (iru), the implementation of the law is associated with an increase in their administrative costs by an average of eur 65 000 per year, which may force many of them to leave the sector. bulgarian carriers are particularly concerned about the expected significant additional administrative burden that the new law will impose on foreign transport operators, as well as the lack of clarity on its practical implementation and the risk of heavy financial penalties. i therefore welcome the commission's detailed study of the german law and the german government's imposition of a moratorium on its application to transit transport by foreign carriers.

the revision of the multiannual financial framework to delay the implementation of key projects is an important and necessary step and, as this debate shows, is the subject of consensus in both the european parliament and the commission. the carry-over of unused commitment appropriations to the next financial period is key to the launch of the new programming period and is, in this sense, closely linked to the european union's efforts to create a more competitive and prosperous economy. as we know, the latest agreement on the multiannual financial framework for the period 2014-2020 delayed the adoption of the legal acts of the european structural investment funds for that period. these delays have a negative impact on the economies of the member states and are associated with significant losses. the structural funds are the union's most important investment policy and their effective preparation and use of european resources will generate growth and jobs. all european countries must have equal access to finance and support from the union's institutions. i therefore welcome the commitment made at the beginning of the debate by commissioner georgieva that the commission will work more closely with member states and regions, which will facilitate not only a rapid start of programmes but also their successful implementation thanks to the full and efficient use of all resources. the regions and the member states cannot afford to waste any more time. economic development requires immediate action and real support.
mr president, the revision of the mff has been made necessary because of three major delays: in adopting respectively the mff and the regulations concerning the eu funds, and in the approval of operational programmes. in practice therefore, in the course of 2015, member states will need to commit the amounts which were previously envisaged for two years – 2014 and 2015. furthermore, 2015 is the last year for absorption of the funds from the previous programming period. all this means exceptional pressure on the responsible authorities to comply with the relevant requirements for proper commitment of resources. hence there might be a significant increase in the number of infringements for which member states could easily be faulted. really effective protection of the financial interest of the union begins with the provision of enough time for the proper preparation and implementation of the relevant budgetary rules and regulations.
in writing. ladies and gentlemen, the european structural and investment funds are the union's most important investment policy and it is of paramount importance that all resources are used in a timely manner and as efficiently as possible to bring about growth and job creation. as the representative of the poorest country in the european union, bulgaria, it is of paramount importance to me that all the operational programmes function properly, as i have been closely watching entire regions being depopulated because they have no prospects, no economic growth, no education, no health care. that is why i consider this proposal to amend the multiannual financial framework to be of paramount importance, since by carrying over appropriations by one year, delays in payments for many operational programmes will be avoided, which would have negative consequences for regions and cities and therefore for european citizens. i would hereby ask the commission to bear in mind article 174 of the treaty on the functioning of the european union, which obliges the eu to develop and implement its own initiatives leading to the strengthening of its economic, social and territorial cohesion, and to pay particular attention to rural areas, areas affected by industrial transition and regions affected by serious and persistent adverse natural or demographic conditions.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i do not take the floor to talk about history, much less to debate historical subjects, because i do not think that is what is expected from the european parliament's rostrum. and i confess i don't feel competent, not because i haven't read any book about the events that provoked the draft resolution we're discussing, but because i'm convinced that having read or heard something on a subject may be enough to form some personal opinion, but it still can't give you the self-confidence to turn that opinion into a general one. ladies and gentlemen, controversial historical events must not be transformed into modern political conflicts. historical facts, when they are facts, will not cease to be facts, whether one denies or accepts them. i plead against the self-serving political use of historical facts, against the self-serving digging into the past, against the choosing of confrontational themes from it. turkey and armenia are mature enough and advanced enough in their democracy to resolve this issue through dialogue between themselves. the debate in the european parliament today is unlikely to contribute to that. no, they would make it difficult. to conclude, i was born in the balkans, a region that is marked by severe conflict, but also by a unique ethnic tolerance. mr. chairman, the great balkan poet and nobel prize winner ivo andrić in his work writes about the bridges we must walk together towards the future, not return to the past. a question asked by raising a blue card. mr. kuciuk, as a representative of a party whose name has the words "rights and freedoms", do you not think that crimes against humanity and genocide should be condemned and those who committed them should bear their political responsibility? the armenian genocide and the destruction of the thracian bulgarians is an undeniable historical fact. you can read it and ask any expert on the subject. my question is: do you think genocide should be condemned and denied and those who committed it should be held accountable?
answering a question asked by raising a blue card. mr president, the blue card procedure involves the holding of a dialogue. i don't think that my colleague angel jambazky is having a conversation with me. apparently he didn't hear my call for a tolerant and dialogic world. so, mr. jambazki, i won't be able to accommodate you with my duplicate.
what i hold in my hands is a memorial - a symbol of homage, adoration and respect for the memory of one and a half million armenians and hundreds of thousands of victims of other nationalities of the genocide committed by the young turk government. this monstrous crime against humanity began in 1913, when the regular turkish army and the bashibozuk ravaged hundreds of bulgarian villages in thrace, killing and driving hundreds of thousands of bulgarians from their homelands. the genocide continued in 1915 and 1916, when the young turk government ordered, organized, and carried out the physical extermination of more than one and a half million armenians within the borders of the ottoman empire. the aim of the turkish authorities is to kill and expel all nationalities other than turkish. this monstrous genocide must be condemned in the strongest terms. the turkish government, the successor to the ottoman empire, must be forced to admit its guilt, to take political responsibility and to compensate the heirs of the victims. i strongly condemn the turkish genocide of the armenians, bulgarians and other peoples of the empire. that is why i have proposed, signed and support the european parliament resolution condemning the turkish atrocities and the armenian genocide. i end with the motto of the armenian community, dedicated to the centenary of the genocide: "i remember and condemn, i remember and demand, i live and remember, i live and demand".
mr president, here in the european parliament today we mark a sad anniversary - 100 years since the armenian genocide, which claimed over one and a half million human lives and caused immeasurable suffering and suffering to the ancient armenian people. different interpretations of the past continue to create contradictions and divide nations and peoples. the overcoming of these divisions can only be achieved through historical reconciliation and on the basis of the recognition of objective truth and the facts of history. today we pay tribute to those who died during the armenian genocide. but let us also remember the other ethnic groups within the ottoman empire, subjected to centuries of persecution, among which is the bulgarian population, which especially in the second half of the 19th century became a victim of the bataclan massacre, the bloody pogrom against the april uprising and many other such events that shocked and horrified the european public of that time. history should be a source of wisdom and unity, and i hope that the republic of turkey will find the moral strength and courage to walk with armenia on the path of cooperation in the interests of the citizens of both countries and the prospect of european integration. we must not allow the dark shadows of the past to linger over our present and our future. a tribute to the memory of the victims of the armenian genocide and all the victims of crimes against humanity!
mr president, mrs georgieva, colleagues, with today's resolution the european parliament is sending modern turkey three messages of particular interest to turkey itself. first, unconditional recognition of responsibility for the armenian genocide will be conclusive proof that today's turkey unequivocally breaks with the tragic legacy of the ottoman empire and does not share any neo-ottoman tendencies today. secondly, it will also be a clear sign that the turkish leadership is capable of a modern european consciousness and an unambiguous ability to take responsibility for the darkest pages of its country's history not as self-deprecation but on the contrary as a testimony to the maturity and wisdom of a modern turkey. and thirdly, it is precisely such recognition that is the right path to historic, future-oriented reconciliation between turkey and armenia, a truly necessary development as an extremely important positive contribution in the current extremely acute geopolitical reality.
madam president, mrs georgieva, it is imperative that the european commission sets a convincing example of non-discrimination and equal treatment in employment and the exercise of the right to work by introducing clear and binding rules for the employment of people with permanent disabilities on a quota basis in the european institutions. the 2000 directive introduced a general framework for non-discrimination in employment. it is essential that this measure, together with the other founding documents of the european union dealing with the equal rights of persons with disabilities, be put into practice. in this context, the european commission should also consider the barriers in state aid regimes that limit the possibilities of protecting people with disabilities against discrimination and unequal treatment in employment and the exercise of labour rights.
in writing. ladies and gentlemen, fair competition of knowledge and skills is essential when recruiting staff for new jobs. but people with special needs are in most cases disadvantaged in this selection, even if they have a strong aptitude for the particular job. these people face and overcome all kinds of difficulties every day, and they face and fight all kinds of discrimination. their integration into the social and working environment continues to be one of the greatest challenges across the union, even though the un convention on the rights of persons with disabilities was one of the first conventions ratified by the eu. progress is too slow and barely noticeable. so i think it's time for more radical measures, to adopt the affirmative action approach to people with special needs. the introduction of mandatory quotas for the employment of people with disabilities in the european institutions is one such measure. moreover, 21 of the 28 member states have such quotas in place. the european institutions are lagging behind eu countries instead of setting an example. quotas do not solve everything, but good practices for positive discrimination based on the skills and abilities of these people will gradually be established and can be followed by member states.
mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to raise a matter with you, namely the protection of nature, and in particular of forests as part of the environment, by giving you a specific example. i have shocking video footage showing the naked cutting of a sixty-year-old pine forest over a bulgarian village of banja, razlog municipality, blagoevgrad region. even more shocking is the fact that this forest is being destroyed to build a landfill in its place, and with european funds. i wonder what the european commission and the european taxpayer think about the fact that european money from their taxes is being used to destroy a 60-year-old pine forest and build a landfill. i think that is unacceptable, and i think that spending european money on the destruction of nature and the forest is, to put it mildly, unreasonable. i think that the european commission should carefully investigate the facts, circumstances and documents surrounding the spending of european funds, in particular for the construction of this landfill in the village of banja, municipality of razlog, blagoevgrad region.
in writing. ‒ i voted against the application to mobilise the european globalisation adjustment fund because i believe that this mechanism is wrongly constructed and its meaning is that millions of euros have been drained without any effective result. in the case of mory-ducros sas, a french company operating in the 'road and pipeline transport' sector, i do not see any reason for retraining the workers, as these modes of transport are about to expand their influence within the european union. eur 6 052 200 will be allocated to 2 804 redundant workers. this financial resource is huge and i think the effect will be minimal. with this money, we're not going to create new jobs, but instead we're going to try to do the work of social services in the country where these workers have been paying their social security and taxes. on top of that, this money will be paid years after the application is filed, and i don't think the laid-off workers will wait 3, 4 or 5 years to start looking for a new job. the longer a person is unemployed, the harder it is to find a job. that's why i think that before we can continue to mobilize funds, we need to completely rethink and reconstruct this mechanism.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of the report by mrs ulvsko, which approves the council's proposal to set up a social protection committee. the establishment of this committee is clearly stated in article 160 of the treaty on the functioning of the european union. the purpose of the committee, as laid down in the treaty, is to promote cooperation in the field of social protection policy between the eu member states and between them and the commission. it also monitors the social situation in the eu and the development of social protection policies in the member states. the social protection committee also produces reports on social inclusion, health, long-term care and pensions. i believe that the establishment of this committee is essential, in particular for the poorer eu member states, who need guidance and support to deal with the social crisis they are facing.
in writing. ‒ i support the creation of a social protection committee because i strongly believe that the search for a common approach and, above all, common rules guaranteeing social protection is of the utmost importance for all eu citizens. a few days ago in my country, bulgaria, two women died in a work accident. the conditions under which these pick-up workers from the peripheral region were transported show that the worker's right to decent working conditions and pay is being violated. in the interests of social justice and the protection of our citizens, i believe that we must seek pan-european mechanisms to guarantee the protection of these rights. labour exploitation and social blackmail must cease to be tolerated in any eu member state. i very much hope that the social protection committee, with its establishment, will be able to make clear and effective recommendations to the commission for guaranteed protection for every one of our citizens, regardless of the member state, region or economic sector of employment.
in writing. ‒ i supported the report by ms ulvsko for a council decision to set up an employment committee. this committee will help coordinate the employment policies of the member states. the employment problem, and particularly that of young people, is one of the main challenges facing not only individual member states but the union as a whole. i believe that action at european level is needed to tackle it, because its scale makes it impossible for countries to tackle it alone. in many areas of eastern europe, entire regions are being depopulated and people are being driven out of work by labour migration. this process must be stopped because it poses a serious threat to the very existence of the union.
in writing. ‒ mr president, ladies and gentlemen, in the face of the economic crisis, the high levels of unemployment and especially youth unemployment, the vote in favour of the decision to set up an employment committee is of the utmost importance. the committee will monitor the overall development of employment issues and policies, and work to develop cooperation, exchange of good practices and macroeconomic dialogue between member states. i believe that the creation of an employment committee will stimulate the improvement of conditions in the european labour market and economic growth in general.
in writing. ‒ i support the creation of the employment committee, although i believe that such an organisation should have been set up earlier, at the very first symptoms and effects of the economic crisis that has been going on for several years now and which has also had very severe social consequences throughout the european union. i believe that this committee should take ambitious action in the social field, particularly in the policies affecting european youth. as one of the biggest victims of this economic and financial crisis, many young people, especially in europe's smaller economies, have lost hope of realisation and of support from the union. i also think that the employment committee should pay particular attention to a social initiative that still seems to be underestimated: volunteering. i believe that by recognising it as a work experience and by increasing its legal status across the european union, we will enable civil society and entrepreneurship to help tackle unemployment. the severe floods last year in my country, bulgaria, showed that volunteering is a social investment, so i call on the newly established committee to treat this issue as a priority.
in writing. ‒ i supported the proposal for a council regulation amending regulation (ec, euratom) no 1311/2013 laying down the multiannual financial framework for the years 2014-2020 because, as the representative of the poorest country in the european union, bulgaria, the proper functioning of all operational programmes is of paramount importance to me. the bulgarian citizens hoped that with bulgaria's accession to the eu, our country would experience economic growth and an increase in living standards. unfortunately, this process has been hampered by a wide variety of factors, such as the economic crisis, demographic problems and, not least, the internal migration process within the union itself. all of this has led to the depopulation of entire regions in my country, because they have no prospects, no economic growth, no education, no health care. in addition to my support for the report, i call on the commission to review the principles for granting projects in the member states and to apply genuine article 174 of the treaty on the functioning of the european union. last but not least, the commission must work with the member states and regions not only for the rapid adoption of all other programmes but also for the careful implementation of the programmes themselves in order to achieve full and efficient use of all resources.
in writing. ‒ the multiannual financial framework (mff) ensures consistency in the development of eu spending. it sets annual ceilings (maximum amounts) that the eu can spend in the different areas of its activities. the current revision of the mff for the period 2014-2020 aims to transfer the unused 2014 appropriations, or eur 21,1 billion, to the following years. the role of the european parliament is key here, as without its approval the council cannot adopt an act amending the mff. the late adoption of a large proportion of operational programmes (47%, or 300 programmes) has made it necessary to revise the mff in order to avoid further delays and losses. this is a technical amendment in nature and was supported by a large majority in parliament (591 votes in favour, against only 24 against). the conclusion drawn from the political analysis of this situation is that more speed and flexibility of procedures are needed - a matter of the utmost importance which has yet to be discussed.
in writing. ‒ mr president president, colleagues, i supported the first revision of the multiannual financial framework (mff 2014-2020) as a necessary technical change, but also as a clear political signal to citizens that the commitments made for the financing and implementation of the programmes for convergence, the common agricultural policy and regional development are being respected. the transfer of eur 21 billion of unused funds from 2014 to 2015 will allow 300 programmes already approved in different member states not to be cancelled due to the more than six-month delay in the approval of the mff and the serious delay in the approval of new operational programmes, which implies turning 2015 into a year-zero for the absorption of euro funds. i support the need for flexibility in the mff to allow for changes like today's because this is necessary to avoid interruptions and problems in the implementation of eu projects and programmes and to prevent the loss of large amounts of eu funds, as would have happened to the 21 billion euro in question without the amendment adopted today. today, as the european economy recovers from the crisis, it is more than ever necessary to make full use of the opportunities for job creation and the revival of investment, which in many member states comes mainly from the european funds. let us not allow bureaucratic procedures to choke the european programmes.
mr. chairman, ladies and gentlemen, i have proposed, signed and supported this resolution for several reasons. first of all, our deepest sympathy and condolences to the heirs of those one and a half million armenians who died, victims of the armenian genocide. not only for the memory of these victims, but for all the other victims of the young turk genocide within the ottoman empire, which lasted from 1913 to 1921, in the mad, man-hating attempt of the young turk government to create a unified nation by killing all other peoples and nationalities within the borders of the ottoman empire. secondly, the present turkish state must be forced to acknowledge the historical guilt of its predecessors, condemn this act, assume political, if necessary financial responsibility and compensate the victims of armenian, bulgarian and all other nationalities. thirdly, the behaviour of the turkish political elite today is scandalous. they deny and try to dismiss all guilt. not only must genocide be recognized, but its denial must be prosecuted.
in writing. ladies and gentlemen, i voted against the motion for a resolution not because i deny the need for a common assessment of these events, but because i believe that the path to achieving, to implementing a common assessment could be made more difficult by the adoption of such a position by the ep. controversial historical events must not be transformed into modern political conflicts. historical debates are in the field of historical science, not in the field of politics. let's leave that to the academies of science and the universities. parliament is a highly politicized body and not the most appropriate medium for establishing historical truth. we as politicians have no right to dictate the content and direction of thought.
in writing. ‒ every bulgarian knows about the sufferings of the armenians in the ottoman empire. he knows from our native history and literature, from the stories of the representatives of the armenian communities scattered throughout bulgaria. they came as refugees to bulgaria, found their second home, preserved and passed on the memory of the massacre of the armenians because that's how we bulgarians have called their hard fate for a hundred years. and so i have no doubt that the concept of genocide, which emerged after the second world war, is fully in keeping with the facts and the depth of their tragedy. even though a hundred years have passed since those terrible events, we must show that we have not forgotten those one and a half million innocent victims. europe knows best that only through truth can peace be brought about between peoples. european citizens have shown the whole world how this is done. that is why the armenian genocide must be recognized by all european states. i am convinced that the national assembly of the republic of bulgaria will very soon follow the example of the european parliament and adopt a declaration recognising the armenian genocide. i'm proud to have been part of today's truly overwhelming majority.
in writing. ‒ i did not support the resolution on the international roma day anti-roma sentiment in europe and eu recognition of the roma genocide commemoration day during the second world war, as i believe it lacks important points of relevance to the current situation in europe. i don't think the famous roma integration happened anywhere in europe. on the contrary, there are a number of examples of exactly how western european countries do not apply it. in addition, there is a lot of evidence of blatant misuse of funding for roma integration. there is a clear double standard between the official documents that are adopted, the programmes that are created and the behaviour of a number of member states. i also think that the linking of international roma day with so-called anti-roma sentiments is at least a bad compilation of two issues that in their genesis have little in common. i stress that our efforts should be directed not towards the creation of artificial standards and privileges based on origin, as is the spirit of the resolution in question, but towards practical solutions with measurable results. the truth is that integration of the kind that is being preached is not and will not be. after so many years of integration, the current approach has proven to be completely untenable.
mr president, tomorrow’s vote on the deployment of the ecall in-vehicle system is not only the conclusion of more than six years of hard work on the issue by the european parliament. tomorrow’s vote also represents an urgent call on the commission to propose legislative measures as soon as possible to ensure that the ecall system will be mandatory in all european countries by 2018. but most importantly, tomorrow’s vote is a decisive step towards the consolidation of a system that can save human lives. it is among the best examples of how and why the european union brings value to its citizens. as with most recent developments, here too digital technologies play a key enabling role. when talking about digital technologies and interconnectedness we always talk about protecting personal data. in this respect, the report puts a special emphasis on the issue of data protection, clarifying that information will only be used at the moment of a serious accident and not for the continuous tracking of the vehicle. i would like to use this opportunity to call upon car manufacturers in all member states to make the benefits of the ecall system available for vehicles across europe.
the emergency summit following the recent tragedy in the mediterranean has not made bold decisions. some member states have given up quotas for resettling refugees, refusing to participate in rescue operations. do we have a clear picture of what is happening in the countries on the european union's external borders, which are taking in thousands of refugees every month and where resources are being depleted? the only measure adopted was an increase in funding for triton. europe cannot act as a fire brigade after every new tragedy. we need a long-term policy agreed by brussels and the 28 member states. if today's focus is on the mediterranean, tomorrow's events could test the land border between bulgaria and turkey, which is already a tricky one. over 5,000 illegal immigrants entered in the first three months of this year alone, 60% more seeking protection. it is not enough to save people, as the situation in reception centres and refugee camps borders on a humanitarian catastrophe. dublin 3 should be changed and teams with frontex should be formed for rescue operations and border protection.

i can only give a positive assessment of the report on the new european union forest strategy. i fully share the general thesis of the report that we need a systematic, planned and new approach to forest management. i also want to emphasize that i fully support the view that forestry policy should be a national priority and that it is not necessary or right to introduce community regulation. i agree that on some issues that indirectly affect the forestry sector, we also need common measures at european union level investments in the forestry sector, more jobs, support for the sector that can provide more added value. but here's the thing, not everything in this sector is up to par. i'll give you a couple of examples. european money is being used to illegally clear a forest in the course of a european project in the village of banja, razlog municipality, blagoevgrad region. this is unacceptable. in addition, there is a so-called 'timber mafia' in europe, which is involved in the illegal harvesting of timber that is exported to member states or third countries. we can no longer turn a blind eye to this and other similar ugly phenomena. as regards financing, much more investment is needed, both from european funds and public funds from member states for forests.
in writing. the amendment of the fuel quality directive and the renewable sources directive is an important element of the series of measures proposed by the commission in the fight against global climate change. i welcome the steps taken by the european commission with regard to the european union's biofuels policy and believe that so-called 'second generation' biofuels will contribute to reducing greenhouse gas emissions, protecting the environment and the health of the citizens of the european union. i think that the amendment of these directives is a good example of the transposition of political priorities into concrete measures.
in writing. i welcome the final agreement reached on the amendment of the fuel quality and renewable energy directives, as it offers a realistic approach that will allow investment in renewable fuels in transport to continue. the 7% cap for first generation biofuels will allow the preservation of investments already made in these biofuels and at the same time, by introducing a benchmark, albeit non-binding, for new generation biofuels of 0,5%, will encourage investments in second generation biofuels. i'd like to remind you that some of our colleagues are not entirely consistent. seven years ago, they were pushing for the use of biofuels from food products in europe and defending the production of ethanol or biodiesel. then they criticized us for not supporting these biofuels enough, and now the opposite - they criticize us for not banning them fast enough. i was strongly against drastic measures to phase out first generation fuels, because that would have been a way of punishing those european entrepreneurs who took the initiative for biofuels and, in compliance with all european requirements, opened up businesses and invested in their production. what our investors lack most is the guarantee that there will be consistency in european legislation. the adoption of this agreement will ensure that to a large extent.

mr president, i supported this directive because i believe that a clean environment is a prerequisite for good public health. i think that plastic bag pollution is a concern and that this problem needs a solution. at the same time, i am of the opinion that the text as it stands, with its proposal to impose additional taxes and charges in a situation of de facto economic stagnation, contains economic and social risks. such measures may seem like a quick-fix solution, but the economic and social impact is not negligible. i fear that this directive will be used as a pretext to impose disproportionately high charges on consumers, which in my view is a prerequisite for a dangerous effect - a drastic reduction in demand for this type of production, which in turn poses a danger to those working in the sector. i call on the commission and the governments of the member states to come forward with quality proposals in this regard and more ambitious measures to demonstrate the benefits of using bags made of other materials.
in writing. ‒ i abstained on the report on reducing the consumption of lightweight plastic shopping bags because i believe, as the european commission has mentioned, that the proposal contains specific texts which are beyond the competence of a legislative body. i and my political group fully support the long-term objectives contained in the commission's proposal. however, they should be clear, consistent and enforceable to reduce the use of plastic carrier bags, and not overly restrictive - and potentially ineffective - requirements such as those set out in this agreement.
in writing. ‒ i supported the second reading final agreement on the amendment of the directive to restrict the use of plastic bags with a wall thickness of less than 0.05 mm, because i hope that it will put an end to their excessive waste and unregulated disposal in the european union. the fact is that plastic shopping bags are among the most polluting substances in the environment and they are a significant problem, especially for our aquatic ecosystem. it is estimated that over 8 billion plastic bags are thrown away each year. the new legislative measure, which is significantly improved in all respects compared to the commission's original proposal, obliges member states to ensure one of two conditions: that the average annual consumption does not exceed 90 plastic carrier bags per capita by 2019, or alternatively that they are no longer offered free of charge in the retail network by 2018. i believe that such measures have the potential to limit the negative impact of nylon envelopes on the environment and will lead to their more efficient and sustainable use. however, their successful implementation requires public acceptance, which is linked to raising awareness among citizens about the motives and nature of the changes approved today, and to promoting the use of biodegradable and compostable envelopes.

in writing. the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon was a step towards stronger protection of the financial interests of the european union. with its subsequent proposals for a european public prosecutor's office and a directive to combat fraud with european funds by criminal means, the commission has put better fund management at the heart of the effective implementation of its policies. actively fighting fraud with european funds will allow more people to access eu financial support, end bad practices such as vat fraud and improve the union's image as a driver of growth and prosperity in the member states. it is therefore necessary for the council to adopt as soon as possible the regulation on the european public prosecutor's office and the directive on combating fraud involving european funds. these two files should cover the maximum number of financial crimes involving the european funds and ensure the independence of the investigations. the proposals will not lead to a conflict between the european approach and national enforcement, but will strengthen cooperation and trust between member states. the adoption of the european public prosecutor's office regulation and the anti-fraud directive on european funds will be a clear signal that the eu is willing to protect its financial interests and to ensure that funds reach those who really need them, namely european citizens.

the rapporteur on the opinion of the committee on regional development. mr president, i am taking part in this debate today on the commission's 2013 discharge in my capacity as chair of the committee on regional development and also as rapporteur for the procedure before it. i congratulate mrs gresle as rapporteur on the precise, detailed and focused report and i would like to say that i am very pleased that some of the regi proposals have found their way into the final version of the report. the commission's focus on a culture of results is key to us results to account first and foremost for the efficiency, effectiveness and impact of cohesion policy. i am glad to see such understanding on the part of the court of auditors and the european commission. i would point out that the final measures to stop payments may have undesirable consequences.
mr president, we support the envisaged proposal for the parliament resolution on the discharge in respect of the eu agencies for 2013. in the report regarding the implementation of financial management and control, the main aspects of agencies’ functioning in 2013 are covered. talking about the agencies and their activities we should be aware that their main tasks must not be to spend a percentage of the funds but to spend in an optimal way to implement their missions and to provide added value for european citizens. based on this and the recent migration issue, i would like to draw your attention to the case of the frontex agency. the agency’s mission is to promote, coordinate and develop european border management in line with the eu charter on fundamental rights. in this regard i would like at the end of each financial year to know to what extent the agency has been effective and has contributed to the coordination and implementation of joint operation at external borders, and analysed intelligence on the situation at the external borders, providing rapid response capabilities and assisting member states in joint return operations. these issues have become more important in recent years in connection with the change in the overall eu strategy on resources and the worsened security environment and problems concerning migrants to the eu. for me, as a representative of bulgaria, one of the countries with external borders upon which there is a dramatically increased migration pressure, two main aspects are essential – growth in the agency’s budget and an improved strategy that fits distribution management. it is a relationship like this between the amount of money spent and the actual results achieved in the implementation of agenciesʼ missions that should guide the discharge at the end of the financial year.
mr. chairman, i was only eight years old when bulgaria went through one of the worst crises in its modern history. few of the government systems and mechanisms then worked, people faced challenge after challenge and problem after problem. but since then, a long way has been done, and many lessons have been learned. it's been exactly eight years since bulgaria was a member of the european union, and if i look back at the past programming period, i see results. i see some progress. and it's the results and the successes that should be the subject of our debate today, because many of the numbers are pointing in that direction. according to the european commission, bulgaria absorbs almost 75% of the agreed funds. on average, 80% are those for the union, and the bulgarian percentage is growing. the rural development programme has also been rightly criticised. but a year after that report, hundreds of people are the beneficiaries of the aid. almost seven million have been paid out to beneficiaries by the european fisheries fund alone. the control and verification systems for eu funds in the country are functioning. every report from now on will be proof of my words. last but not least, i would like to thank mrs gresle for her high level of professionalism and diligence in her work on the report.
a question asked by raising a blue card. the 30% increase in pre-financing for operational programmes aimed at promoting youth employment is undoubtedly important. but improving employment is a symbiosis between reform and investment. they cannot achieve this goal on their own. how do i see progress going? there are large differences between member states and between the use of the same means and the achievement of specific results. the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the new rules and will continue to monitor the implementation of the new rules. this effort must be two-pronged. firstly, making the most of this pre-financing and secondly, reforming education. how do you see these reforms in your member state and in others?
mr president, as i said a moment ago, an increase of 30% in the advance payments for operational programmes to promote youth employment is undoubtedly a very important step and i welcome the commission's efforts. but i want to encourage her to do even more to raise awareness about this initiative and the programme and how young people can benefit from it. i also realise that the role of member states and local authorities is crucial to achieving sustainable employment for young people abroad. i therefore sincerely hope that member states and local authorities will also make efforts to increase their capacity and, alongside the investments they will receive from pre-financing, to implement reforms in education and make their employment services even more effective. i believe that only by accumulating all these measures can we ultimately make sure that young people from outside find better jobs faster and easier in the future.
mr president, the creation of a website where anonymous reports can be made to the committee on tax rulings and other measures of a similar nature or impact is a rather risky undertaking that will not produce the desired results. the arguments against this proposal are many. first, this possibility could lead to a lot of abuse and settling scores. the verification of the accuracy of the information will require efforts and competence that the european parliament does not have. secondly, in view of the negative opinion given by the legal service of the european parliament, we cannot guarantee the anonymity of the reports submitted. as meps, we do not have the same legal protection as investigative journalists have in relation to sources of information. third, the creation of 'secure clouds' could set a dangerous precedent that could be applied to other legislative committees of the european parliament. what are we gonna do then? i support the decision already taken in the committee on taxation and the position expressed by the legal service of the european parliament that such a page is not necessary.
thank you, mr chairman. the daily tragedies in the mediterranean are a test for all of us. hundreds have lost their lives at sea in the past month, trying to find refuge from the horrors of war. it is clear, as this parliament has repeatedly said, that europe needs a unified, holistic approach to migration. first, we need to step up rescue operations to avoid further casualties. secondly, it is important to share responsibility between member states. europe must find the right approach to asylum seekers, because overburdening the reception systems of several countries will only lead to a violation of asylum seekers' rights and to a failure to tackle their integration. i therefore believe that the commission and the member states must jointly take responsibility and provide for the possibility of allocating refugees on the basis of factors such as gross domestic product, population size and the number of refugees already admitted.
mr president, commissioner, mr president-in-office of the council, it is time to act. we expect concrete proposals and a common policy. it is not enough to be alarmed by the seriousness of the situation in the mediterranean or on the bulgarian-turkish border. we do not want an ad hoc policy, but a policy which is effective, not only in the mediterranean and at the bulgarian-turkish border, but also at our external borders, in the east, because of the crisis in ukraine. we want redistribution and relocation quotas. we must be united, and that means revising the dublin regulation. we demand concrete action to break up the trafficking networks and fair sentences for these criminals. we are asking for legal immigration routes and humanitarian visas. we're asking for more help with the shelters. finally, we must give migrants a chance, because saving them is not enough. these people have to live after the rescue, too. they must be able to work and have access to health care and education.
in writing. i see a consistent practice of the eu institutions acting ex post facto in crises. we lack the foresight and the foresight before events happen. in other words, we have no readiness and no skills for preventive action and measures. we usually follow events as both thinking and acting. what is happening in the mediterranean is not of today, yesterday or 1-2-5-10 years ago. but we're just now opening our eyes and realizing that we have a great problem with illegal immigration, with human trafficking. and it's not just italy. it's the same in spain and greece. the same is true in bulgaria, whose land border is literally being stormed by migrants from the middle east and africa. i ask the question, how long will we wait? how long are we gonna pretend like this is okay? those member states are literally collapsing because all those migrants are blowing up our social systems, blowing up public order. our current policy on this issue has failed. it's time to impose strict border controls, to revise the liberal understanding that anyone who comes to europe can stay. i urge you to look at the problem from this angle. it's time to go.
in writing. mr president, in the last month we have witnessed the cruel tragedies that have unfolded in the mediterranean, turning it into europe's 'dead sea'. i fully agree with mr schulz: 'it is time to act with concrete steps! talking about a shared value system does not imply abdicating our obligation to share responsibility. schengen created common european borders, but we expect common european problems to be solved by individual countries. we need to change, we need to do more to prevent this humanitarian catastrophe. we need to work together to save lives, and we need to work together to define our border policy. it is clear that we need a european migration policy, because there is no national solution to the complex problem, but only a common european approach. the dublin regulation, with all its amendments, has proved to be a failure and we must take measures to distribute refugees proportionately and in solidarity among the member states. in addition to fighting traffickers and improving the capacity of frontex, we must not forget the efforts we must make to stabilise libya and the region.
in writing. i would like to express my disapproval of the council's further failure to create a pan-european solidarity mechanism, under which the flow of migrants should be redistributed proportionately between member states. it is not fair that some member states should act as a buffer and have to bear all the pressure without the institutional support and sympathy of the other eu member states. cooperation with regions that are sources of radicalisation is important and part of the solution, but it is not the only solution. the growing instability at europe's borders is creating national security concerns for frontline member states - particularly from southern europe. my country, bulgaria, is one of the two eu member states that have a land link to the middle east conflict regions. i therefore call for measures to strengthen the presence at the maritime borders and for adequate instruments to protect the land borders. i find it unacceptable that the council can quickly decide to impose political and economic sanctions, driven by geopolitical competition and ambition, but that on a matter which affects the security of millions of european citizens, especially in southern europe, there has been no effective solution for years.
in writing. ‒ i voted against the discharge for the implementation of the general budget of the european union for the financial year 2013 of the commission and the executive agencies because i believe that the european commission and the member states are not managing the eu-funded agricultural and regional funds and employment policies effectively enough. i refer to the report by my colleague, mr giorgi pirinski, which points out the lack of appropriate measures to tackle fraud, and to the report by the court of auditors. in the context of the recovery from the financial crisis, i believe that the commission should focus on the control and efficiency of spending.
in writing. ‒ i supported the ep report on the discharge to the commission for the implementation of the 2013 eu budget, which proposes concrete measures to improve the financial management and control systems of european funds. the report notes a trend towards an increase in the rate of error in the management of european funds, with the cap and rural development areas remaining the most sensitive. it should be noted that not all errors are fraudulent and misuse of european funds, and some are due to an administrative or technical omission. therefore, when identifying errors, it is important to look for the root causes that gave rise to them, with a view to their effective correction. in this context, i welcome commissioner hogan's commitment to make simplification of the cap a top priority. achieving a less bureaucratic cap will lead to a lower error rate in the field of agriculture. in focusing on the new cap, we should not overlook the successful completion and disbursement of the old cap projects. i am confident that the commission will take the necessary action to ensure a smooth transition between the old and the new programming period by extending the eligibility period for expenditure by 6 months to allow sufficient time for beneficiaries to absorb the funds already granted.
s&ds) i welcome the decision to pre-finance operational programmes under the youth employment initiative. i believe that this action is particularly timely, as the youth unemployment rate at eu level is over 20% and the proportion of young people not in employment, education or training in the european union is over 10%. in bulgaria, this statistic is 22% or twice the european union average. one of the main challenges to the implementation of the youth guarantee at national level is the inadequate mechanisms to encourage registration at employment offices. another major problem is the lack of diversified employment policies to identify and provide the skills needed. i believe that the key to ensuring sustainable youth employment is investing in quality education and in building transversal skills such as entrepreneurship, digital literacy and critical thinking. education is the cause and solution to youth unemployment.
in writing. ‒ the youth employment initiative (yei) funding contains a €3.2 billion specific allocation from a specific eu budget line, combined with at least €3.2 billion from the esf allocation to member states. the commission proposal sets a higher pre-financing rate for 3.2 billion ipa specific appropriations to ensure rapid mobilisation. our young people need support and such funding is absolutely essential. bulgaria is also one of the countries that will benefit from this initiative.
in writing. - this week. i voted 'no' to the commission's proposal to increase the amount of pre-financing under the youth employment initiative to be paid this year. i am aware of the serious difficulties member states face in starting up programmes due to a lack of available funds, and i am also aware that it is the countries with the highest levels of youth unemployment that are also experiencing the greatest financial difficulties. i voted this way because i believe that tackling the problem of youth unemployment requires immediate and targeted action. the release of €1 billion to provide employment for 650 000 young people already this year will undoubtedly help member states' efforts to combat youth unemployment. these funds are intended for 20 member states with regions where youth unemployment exceeds 25%, including my home country, bulgaria. the rapid implementation of programmes funded under the youth employment initiative will help to achieve our overall objective of accelerating the transition of young people from education to employment and helping their sustainable integration into the labour market.
in writing. ‒ i take this opportunity to remind parliament, the commission and the council that we should carefully weigh the pros and cons of opening the european market to goods from third countries. indeed, the positive effect of such measures is felt above all for competition and consumers. on the other hand, i cannot help but point out that there are countries and economic circles which are openly abusing our rules for the sake of profit. it is noted that dumping and circumvention practices continue to exist in relation to imports from third countries. in addition to opening up the market and liberalizing import rules, it is therefore necessary to adopt measures to punish unfair practices by third parties. i would stress that too much liberalisation is also damaging to our producers, so a more balanced approach, including ad hoc or temporary sanctions mechanisms, is not without meaning. i urge the commission to pay attention to this matter.
in writing. ‒ the european parliament, the council and the commission decided by interinstitutional agreement of 20 december 1994 that the accelerated procedure could be used for the rapid adoption of codified acts. the new regulation will replace the various acts. the content of the acts codified shall be preserved in full and shall be limited to their consolidation, with only the formal amendments necessary for the codification itself. that's what my "for" is for. for the sake of transparency, this procedure goes through the ep.
in writing. ‒ the stabilisation and association agreement with albania entered into force by council regulation (ec) no 1616/2006 of 24 october 2006, but has been substantially amended in recent years, which has led to the need to codify the text. this is an obligation imposed by the commission as far back as 1987, when it stipulated that all texts should be codified no later than the tenth amendment. this legislative act does not itself introduce further amendments but merely formally replaces and consolidates all the previous acts while preserving their substance. i have supported this report because i believe that the obligation to codify texts is extremely important, as it simplifies european legislation and makes it more accessible and understandable to all.
in writing. ‒ mr president, i welcome the progress that the western balkan countries continue to make in meeting the criteria for membership of the european union and i sincerely hope that we will soon be able to welcome them all to a truly united europe without borders. i call on albania to continue to work towards the democratic values at the heart of the european project, paying particular attention to respect for fundamental human rights and freedoms, legislative guarantee of minority rights, gender equality and freedom of the media and speech, in line with european standards.
in writing. ‒ the stabilisation and association agreement and the interim agreement on trade and trade-related matters with bosnia and herzegovina entered into force by council regulation 594/2008 of 16 june 2008, but have been substantially amended in recent years, which has led to the need to codify the text. this is an obligation imposed by the commission as far back as 1987, when it stipulated that all texts should be codified no later than the tenth amendment. this legislative act does not itself introduce further amendments but merely formally replaces and consolidates all the previous acts while preserving their substance. i have supported this report because i believe that the obligation to codify texts is extremely important, as it simplifies european legislation and makes it more accessible and understandable to all.
in writing. ‒ i welcome the report on the implementation of the stabilisation and association agreement between the eu and bosnia and herzegovina, adopted by a clear majority. however, it should be noted that little progress has been made in bilateral relations since the signing of the agreement in 2008. over the past year, the council has agreed on a renewed approach to bih, which is also a step in the right direction for restoring the country's integration processes. however, despite the commitment made, the lack of common political will still appears in the bih progress report as a serious obstacle. the lack of political compromise, inefficient institutions and severe social opposition mean that a serious rethinking of the way the federation itself is run is needed. the most serious problems bosnia is currently facing include the severe socio-economic damage caused by the 2014 floods and the protests in the country. during the parliamentary debate, representatives of the institutions repeatedly reaffirmed the union's commitment to the country, but did not fail to stress that bih should also make the appropriate efforts. the resumption of the integration process and the progress of bih on its path towards european accession is of key importance for the security of bulgaria as well as for the stability of the entire western balkans.
in writing. ‒ ladies and gentlemen, while i supported the report on macedonia, i would like to share my concerns about its future in the context of the severe political crisis that has engulfed the country. it is disturbing that there is this sharp, extreme, even dramatic opposition between the ruling and the opposition. the revelations of abuses and crimes by members of the civil service and administration are of concern. the situation is grave and i believe that we cannot talk about any reform in macedonia unless we stop these harmful practices that we all know about. i believe that parliament, the council and the commission must insist clearly and unambiguously on the adoption of a common political plan, a national consensus and a common reform programme. if necessary, let us be the mediator for this dialogue and let the government and the opposition be invited to a tripartite format here in the ep. the future of macedonia is important to the union and we must give a clear signal of this. it should be clear to every citizen of the republic of macedonia that we offer aid in exchange for reforms. after all, macedonia's future is in the european family, but that cannot happen without some effort on the part of skopje. i urge a much more active policy on this issue, because its neglect could lead to serious social clashes in the country.
in writing. ‒ i voted against the commission's discharge because i think that the commission does not exercise sufficient control over the spending of european funds. it is also important to me that european funds are not wasted, but that they create more jobs and stimulate the economies of the member states and especially the less developed regions, which is extremely important for bulgaria. the use of european funds and access to them must be facilitated, but the control over and the effect of the money allocated must be more serious in order to have a real result and revival of the bulgarian economy, creation of jobs and raising the living standard of the bulgarian.

in writing. ‒ i voted against the discharge for the implementation of the general budget of the european union for the financial year 2013 of the european agency for the cooperation of energy regulators because my position and the position of my group is that the agency needs to be supervised and controlled in terms of the expenditure and the results it achieves. i refer to the report by my colleague, mr giorgi pirinski, which states that there are no adequate measures to tackle fraud, and to the report by the court of auditors. in the wake of the financial crisis, i believe that agencies should be more closely monitored and the funds allocated to their existence should be realistically justified. the european union maintains an oversized and cumbersome administration that fails to deal with the problems it faces quickly and effectively. that is why i am voting against the discharge report.
