in writing. ‒ ladies and gentlemen, there is no doubt that the open market is one of the essential elements, one of the foundations of the union, and we should strive not only to preserve what has been achieved, but also to improve the conditions in which it exists. we were discussing mr. morten's report on competition a few months ago. agreements on imports from third countries are of course part of the big competition issue. i come back to it because the report raised a very important issue - dumping and social dumping. in the case of trade with third countries, and with those which do not share our ideas of the market, we must take account of these two phenomena. it's no secret that there are parties that abuse our rules and our trust. therefore, without hindering competition and access to the free market, it seems to me logical to consider some form of safeguard mechanism when we find that third parties are de facto taking advantage of our market by violating other of our principles. what those mechanisms are is a matter of debate, but it's worth thinking about in that direction. i think that this should not be overlooked and that the commission should also raise this issue.
in writing. ‒ i support this move as a commitment that ep funds will be used more efficiently. in particular, i support and emphasise strengthening the powers of parliamentary committees in order to increase their efficiency and their delegated powers, which play a key role in the proper functioning of the institutions, and to consider improving the overall situation to support our staff for the future. i believe the budget proposal is well balanced and can be put to the secretary-general in this form.
thank you, mr. chairman. i supported this report because as a bulgarian representative i support the creation of a strong european public prosecutor's office, especially in view of the fact that bulgaria is an external border of the european union. the parties shall cooperate in the implementation of the provisions of this agreement and in the implementation of the relevant provisions of the treaty on european union, in particular those relating to the protection of individuals with regard to the processing of personal data and the free movement of such data. the european chief prosecutor shall be appointed by the european parliament and the council on the basis of a proposal from the commission. i support the idea that, once appointed, the deputy prosecutors general should continue to perform their function as european prosecutors, because otherwise this would create additional procedures for the nomination and appointment of new prosecutors, which would lead to administrative burdens. i believe that the european commission should clearly define the scope and powers of the european public prosecutor's office, as well as the competences of eurojust, europol and olaf, in order to avoid legal uncertainty.
in writing. ‒ european taxpayers lose over €500 million a year as a result of misuse of european funds. in cases like this, where countries are unable to cope, it is the union's responsibility to intervene. i supported the creation of a european public prosecutor's office because i am convinced that such a structure, with an open and transparent selection of independent prosecutors, would save european citizens hundreds of millions of euros. let's not kid ourselves. this body will not replace the national prosecutor's office! the european public prosecutor's office will intervene when the state is unable to cope, and only in respect of abuses involving european funds. the creation of teams of prosecutors from different member states will ensure the independence of the investigation and prevent the concealment of abuses. the eppo will also be able to provide the necessary information to the national prosecutor's office in order to carry out the investigation. while there are concerns for a country like the uk that such a structure would reduce the effectiveness of the fight against fraud involving european funds, there is no doubt that the european public prosecutor's office would have an entirely positive effect in bulgaria. today, as europe is making a tremendous effort to boost economic growth and increase investment, tackling fraud with european funds is crucial.

thank you, madam chairman. the chairman of our committee, mrs costa, is absolutely right when she says: 'barbarism has no language and no religion.' unfortunately, in recent months, alongside the barbaric killings, we have also witnessed the unprecedented destruction of archaeological sites, statues and cultural monuments. determined to erase the region's history from the face of the earth, terrorist groups are destroying a cultural heritage that dates back more than three thousand years. these cases have rightly provoked the secretary-general of unesco, mrs bokova, to declare them a cultural cleansing and a war crime that the world must punish, and the european union must support the organization in its fight to preserve our heritage. it is imperative that we immediately confront this assault on our collective human memory, because it is not only a cultural tragedy, but also a security threat. i call on the council to set up additional units within eurojust and europol to combat the illicit trafficking of cultural property and to coordinate better with national authorities. the european union also needs to stand firmly behind unesco's 'united for heritage' campaign for global support for the protection of cultural heritage.
on behalf of the alde group.– madam president, i would like to thank our rapporteur, mr fleckenstein, for a very fruitful cooperation and his balanced view on albania. in the last year albania achieved significant progress, and the report clearly recognises the country’s role in regional stability as the only one of the western balkan states that is a full nato member. albania has constructive and stable relations with its neighbours and its foreign policy is in full compliance with the eu’s foreign and defence policy. in domestic policies albania has made some progress in meeting the political criteria for eu integration. nevertheless, for the further course of reforms the government and opposition should ensure implementation of the political debate in the parliament, in order to consolidate and expand their achievements so far. as regards the economic criteria, albania has made some progress in its pursuit of the functioning market economy, but it has to accelerate structural reforms in order to cope with the competitive pressure on the common eu market. as a result of the low competitiveness of the economy, unemployment is high and the grey sector is still large. the report clearly highlights the challenges that albania is facing and some of the positive steps that the government has taken to deal with them. despite the reform of the judicial system, including the engagement of the venice commission, fundamental reform is still needed to increase the independence of the judiciary. the country has demonstrated political will to fight corruption which remains the major problem along with the organised crime that erodes statehood. there is a serious need to strengthen the de-politicisation of the state administration. on that point, we should congratulate the government on its efforts and the progress it has achieved in the reform of public administration. in conclusion, i would like to emphasise that unification of albania and kosovo is only possible within the eu, because the european integration perspective remains the only viable option for all countries in the region.
thank you, madam chairman. i congratulate albania on the progress it has made over the past year, as evidenced by the granting of candidate country status to the european union. but this is again under the pretext that more progress must be made on the five key areas identified by the commission reform of public administration, strengthening the judiciary, fighting corruption and organised crime and, last but not least, strengthening fundamental rights and freedoms before the start of genuine negotiations. i am glad that the report pays particular attention to these areas, because they are the foundation of any properly functioning legal system. from the bulgarian point of view, this report contains a highly important recommendation to albania, namely to adopt a comprehensive law on ethnic minorities, by which to overcome the existing legal gaps in line with the recommendations of the consultative committee of the council of europe framework convention for the protection of national minorities. this is of extreme importance for bulgaria, since according to data from our state agency for bulgarians abroad, between 40 and 50 thousand citizens of bulgarian origin live in albania. only by guaranteeing the rights and freedoms of all citizens will albania show that it is ready for membership of the european union. in conclusion, i sincerely hope that the commission has learned from its mistakes and is closely monitoring the process of adopting the reforms, but above all their implementation, so that we do not witness yet another deja vu, namely that the applicant country has fulfilled all the criteria on paper, while in practice none of them are being applied.
on behalf of the ecr group. thank you, madam president. first of all, i would like to thank the rapporteur, christian dan preda, the shadow rapporteurs and all the colleagues who contributed to the drafting of this comprehensive resolution. the situation in bosnia and herzegovina is extremely sensitive and it is not surprising that the commission has criticised the lack of substantial progress in the past year. the main reason for this is the current complex state structure, which delays the reform process. in pushing for a change in the country's constitution, we must not forget that the structure of bosnia and herzegovina was designed to reduce the risk of inter-ethnic conflict. the attitude of the state on foreign policy issues is worrying, as is its attitude to the policy of russia, ukraine and the crimean peninsula. the other thing that worries me is the insistence on a common curriculum across the country. this proposal is quite hypocritical, because on the one hand we demand that the state respect the rights of minorities, and on the other hand we allow the majority to impose its own interpretation of history on smaller communities. furthermore, it is not clear whether this general curriculum provides for the possibility for smaller communities to learn in their mother tongue. in conclusion, i would like to make it clear once again that bosnia and herzegovina is a very complex country and we must weigh very carefully all the recommendations we make in our resolutions.
on behalf of the ecr group. on behalf of the group of the european conservatives and reformists, i would like to thank the rapporteur for his work and express our support for the report. as rapporteur for the opinion of the committee on foreign affairs, i am particularly concerned about the introduction of the human rights clause in the autonomous trade regulation, which gives the western balkan countries preferential, unlimited and duty-free access to the european union market for certain products. the new clause provides for the possibility of suspending preferences in the event of a serious and systematic breach of the fundamental principles of human rights, democracy and the rule of law. i hope that we are all united in this house in the belief that respect for democratic principles, the rule of law, human rights and the protection of minorities are absolutely necessary to make progress in the process of joining and integrating into the european family. that is why i am pleased that, through this human rights clause, the european parliament will have another instrument with which to defend the values of the european union and to influence countries wishing to join the european union to adopt and implement the necessary reforms. in writing. it is imperative to help people with visual impairments adapt to the normal rhythm of life as much as possible. specific action in this direction is to facilitate their access to books and printed works in formats suitable for them and to works protected by intellectual property rights. to this end, it is necessary to accelerate the ratification by the european union of the marrakesh treaty, which regulates copyright exceptions for printed works intended specifically for visually impaired persons. the issue is a priority for the bulgarian government as well as for the governments of the other eu member states. i believe that efforts in this direction are therefore expected in both the political and civil sectors. these are social and humanitarian efforts. addressing the discrimination suffered by our citizens with visual impairments is of key importance both at national and european level. so i ask the european commission: what is its vision - is there already a roadmap for ratifying the marrakesh treaty, how should the eu legal framework for international treaties be changed and in what timeframe will ratification be achieved?
in writing. ‒ the alcohol strategy, which was established in 2006, has been a success and has produced positive results in terms of the development of similar strategies in the member states. after it expired in 2012, calls for its renewal were numerous. the new strategy is expected to run from 2016 to 2022. i support the position that this strategy should remain advisory in nature as it has been so far, while encouraging member states to develop national ones rather than requiring and forcing parties to take concrete measures.
in writing. ‒ in most eu member states, alcohol consumption is a serious public health problem that causes widespread socioeconomic damage. alcohol abuse is responsible for the premature deaths of some 3.3 million people worldwide each year. in addition, in 2010 in europe alone, the direct and indirect costs of alcohol abuse amounted to €155.8 billion. with today's resolution, my colleagues and i are calling on the european commission to create a new alcohol strategy for 2016-2022. it should include: collecting reliable data to monitor, improve prevention and treatment and reduce accidents caused by drunk driving. the resolution also calls on the commission to submit a proposal for mandatory disclosure of ingredients and calories on the labelling of alcoholic beverages, as well as labels containing warnings to pregnant women and drunk driving. parliament also proposed to increase measures to protect young people, for example by increasing the dissemination of information campaigns on the dangers of underage drinking and strictly enforcing the laws on the age limit for alcohol consumption.
in writing. i strongly support the call for a new eu strategy on alcohol for 2016-2022, because the lack of a vision and action plan will only exacerbate already worrying trends in our society. despite the efforts of recent years, the statistics remain shocking. the age at which not only minors but also those who are already minors systematically abuse alcohol continues to decline. the european union must take a stand and propose europe-wide measures to combat this trend. alcohol tourism is not a phenomenon due to the tradition of the destination country. this is a common problem and a solution must be sought at european union level. european schools need adequate educational programmes that not only present the implications of this critical information but also encourage healthy lifestyles. healthy lifestyle classes and more physical activity will help both in the fight against teenage alcohol consumption and the other major problem of young europeans obesity. i am firmly of the opinion that the target of information campaigns should not be only adolescents. in europe, where the proportion of the population aging is increasing, public health care starts in the foetal stage, so i strongly support the proposal for more information campaigns also among pregnant women. the harm that alcohol consumption causes to the foetus has lasting and severe consequences for the individual and for public health in general.
in writing. ‒ i welcome this joint resolution step as it is very important for us to support the proper implementation of the bangladesh sustainability pact. the obvious human rights violation must be corrected and i welcome the efforts to help by providing a control process that provides the right guidance.
in writing. i would like to express my condolences to the families of the more than 1200 workers who have died in the textile factories in bangladesh over the past two years. however, by supporting the joint resolution, i also express my strong disapproval of the policies of a number of global brands and textile chains which have for years ignored the working conditions in which their products are made. instead of discussing mechanisms such as the now well-known isds system, the european commission should put mandatory corporate social responsibility clauses in front of its partners in the international agreements it negotiates. this requirement should also apply to all european companies. i find it unacceptable that european chains with annual profits of over 1 billion euros have decided to contribute a minimal amount to the donor fund for the victims and victims in bangladesh. europe must stop pursuing this policy of pursuing only economic interests by exporting production to countries with minimal production costs. this attitude leads to the closure of textile capacities in the eu member states and increases unemployment in them. it also condemns these third countries to endless poverty and makes the european consumer an unwitting accomplice in this modern enslavement. it's time this stopped.
in writing. ‒ i am once again very concerned about the half-hearted way in which the mediterranean problem is being dealt with and viewed. we can all see that the situation is escalating and there is no sign or symptom that things are going to calm down. in africa and the middle east, we have conflicts that will unleash new and new waves of illegal immigrants. the more illegal immigrants, the more boats, the more accidents. what worries me is that the question of what to do is being answered with measures that would have been appropriate 10 or 15 years ago. now they're just a non-functional or partial solution to a general question. i therefore reiterate my call for a total revision of community legislation on migration and asylum. i also urge the countries on the front line - bulgaria, greece, italy and spain - to provide immediate financial, frontex and technical assistance. i call for, rather than endlessly chewing on measures that do not work, support to be provided within weeks to months. this is solidarity, not procrastination.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of the resolution on migration because it outlines the main policies of the european union and the necessary steps to be taken as soon as possible to overcome the crisis of migrants and refugees at europe's borders. in the last few years, europe, and in particular the union's external borders such as bulgaria, italy, greece and malta, has been subject to unprecedented migratory pressures and a wave of refugees. the current dublin rules have proved inadequate to the situation: only a few member states are obliged to deal with the influx of people, while most are protected by shared responsibility. however, a genuine european response to the migration crisis must be based on solidarity and mutual assistance between member states. i therefore welcome the inclusion in the ep resolution and in the council conclusions of last week of an emergency mechanism for the relocation of refugees and asylum seekers within the union. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competent authority' to cover all the activities of the european parliament. this is the first and most difficult step towards revising the dublin system. i believe that the positive experience of these measures will not only help countries like bulgaria, but also improve the lives of asylum seekers.
in writing. ‒ i supported this report as it contains some extremely important points and decisions of the european parliament. firstly, the adoption of the report introduces a so-called 'human rights clause' in the autonomous trade measures regulation, which grants the western balkan countries preferential, unlimited and duty-free access to the eu market for certain products. the new clause provides for the possibility of suspending preferences in the event of a serious and systematic breach of the fundamental principles of human rights, democracy and the rule of law. secondly, the report proposes to end the exceptional trade measures in respect of bosnia and herzegovina, as, since the accession of croatia to the eu, the country has not brought its legislation into line with european law, leading to trade imbalances and harming the eu. thirdly, the report proposes to replace the implementing acts with delegated acts giving the european parliament greater powers. this means that in the event of violations of the human rights clause, the european parliament will have the power to suspend the trade preferences of the relevant party.
in writing. ‒ what is happening in kenya is a chrismatistic example of the danger facing every christian or representative of any other religion in a region where extreme islamism is rampant. we have repeatedly raised the issue of al-shabaab and other terrorist organizations in africa. obviously, this issue is directly related to the global issue of peace in africa, and the problems of illegal immigrants. people are fleeing conflict zones. and they come here to europe, looking for a better life. al-shabaab is an organization that should not be underestimated. we should therefore regard our position and our policy, including our actions, as a key element of the internal and external security of the union. in particular, this group, along with others like it, is of course a threat to the union. a threat that must be neutralized as soon as possible. i am saying that we must make efforts to isolate such structures and, if not to completely destroy them, at least to weaken them considerably so that they do not pose a threat to europe.
in writing. i supported the joint resolution because i believe that barbaric and tendentious religious acts in kenya should not go unpunished. 148 students were killed in cold blood and nearly 80 others were injured. 148 people who were willing and prepared to work for the future of their country! it is unacceptable that the responsible kenyan authorities have failed to respond to these attacks despite the preliminary information that such an attack could take place. i strongly believe that the european commission should propose effective schemes to combat money laundering and the indirect financing of terrorist organisations, because structures such as al-shabaab are financed through such schemes. the role of the european union, especially in this part of the world which is becoming a source of increasing instability and radicalisation, must be strengthened. instability in our neighbouring regions will lead to new waves of immigration, especially to the southern eu member states, including my own country, bulgaria. for years now, the union has been unable to cope effectively with the waves of immigration. it is time for effective action, not just promises and strategies on paper.

in writing. ‒ the situation in the maldives is very worrying and is progressively deteriorating. the first democratically elected president, mr. mohamed nasheed, was sentenced to 13 years in prison on politically motivated terrorism charges. despite calls by the un and the eu for a fair and transparent trial, it has in no way met national and international standards of justice. on top of this, civil society organisations and human rights defenders are increasingly facing harassment, threats and attacks, including the maldives human rights commission, which was charged by the supreme court with treason and undermining the constitutional foundations for submitting a report on the human rights situation in the maldives for the purpose of the universal periodic review of the un human rights council. the european parliament resolution strongly condemns the events in the maldives and calls for an immediate end to political interference in the maldivian judicial system and for its depoliticisation. reforms are urgently needed to ensure the independence and impartiality of the maldivian judiciary in order to restore confidence in its functioning both domestically and internationally.
in writing. ‒ i supported the resolution on albania's progress in 2014 as i believe it is comprehensive and represents very well the progress achieved, as evidenced by the granting of eu candidate status. the resolution also focuses on the five key areas identified by the commission: reform of public administration, strengthening the judiciary, fighting corruption and organised crime, and, last but not least, strengthening fundamental rights and freedoms. i welcome the commission's intention to link the opening of the actual negotiations to progress in these areas, as they are the foundation of any properly functioning rule of law. from the bulgarian point of view, this report contains a highly important recommendation to albania, namely to adopt a comprehensive minority law to overcome the existing legal gaps in line with the recommendations of the consultative committee of the council of europe framework convention for the protection of national minorities. this is of extreme importance for bulgaria, as according to the state agency for bulgarians abroad, there are about 40 thousand citizens of bulgarian origin living in albania. only by guaranteeing the rights and freedoms of all citizens will albania show that it is ready for eu membership.
in writing. i supported the resolution on albania because i believe that, like every country in europe, albania deserves a clear sign on its path towards european integration. however, i believe that provocative statements like that of mr. rama in early april are an obstacle to this path. the peace process in the balkans is a long and complex process and requires many compromises. such statements only constitute a greater obstacle to laying the foundations for the full integration of the entire region into european structures. at the same time, however, i would like to express my disapproval of paragraph 29 and more specifically the encouragement of the imposition of sanctions against russia. this is a geopolitical choice that europe has made, but the consequences for countries that are not fully integrated into the european single market are much more dangerous and, instead of helping the citizens of the candidate countries, they pose a threat to their prosperity and potential. in response to the sanctions imposed by albania, russia added the country to the list of banned countries - a measure that has affected ordinary farmers. they must not bear the consequences of the geopolitical decisions made in brussels.
in writing. ‒ i supported the 2014 progress report of albania as it gives an objective assessment of what has been achieved and the need for further efforts to make progress in the five key priority areas. the government of tirana should continue on the path of reforms at a steady pace and focus on fulfilling the conditions for membership. political dialogue and the proper functioning of the institutions are an important part of the political criteria for accession and a prerequisite for the proper conduct of the negotiation process. the crucial element is constructive dialogue and consensus between the government and the opposition. i express my expectation for a fair approach to national minorities and recognition of the rights of persons of bulgarian ethnic origin. bulgaria has always expressed its consistent support for albania's european integration. in june 2014, our country approved the granting of eu candidate status to albania. the bulgarian government has proposed the conclusion of a memorandum of cooperation in the field of european integration. albania should make further efforts to implement strategic balkan connectivity infrastructure projects, including the pan-european transport corridor 8. the positive role of albania as the rotating chair of the see cooperation process shows the potential for the country to become a factor of stability and a constructive partner in the western balkans region.
in writing. ‒ i welcome the report on the progress of bosnia and herzegovina in 2014, and as a shadow rapporteur i can conclude that thanks to the work of mr preda and the other shadow rapporteurs, we have been able to achieve a really moderate and comprehensive resolution that presents very well the problems facing the country. many of the country's problems stem from the complex state structure, which is the main reason the process of adopting reforms has been so slow. in pushing for a change in the country's constitution, we must not forget that the structure of bosnia and herzegovina was designed precisely to reduce the risk of inter-ethnic conflict. any change in the structure of the state must be properly coordinated with absolutely all the communities in the country, so that we do not witness another civil war in europe. as much as we may criticize bosnia and herzegovina, i personally believe that the country's path of development is towards eu membership. that is why, with our work and constructive criticism, we must give a clear signal that we welcome the country's efforts on the road to european integration.
in writing. ‒ overall, the annual report of the european investment bank (eib) shows quite a few positive features, such as the emphasis on the fact that strengthening eib financing instruments should not be a substitute for national economic policy and structural reforms in the member states, or that smes are the backbone of the european economy and should in principle be a target for investment. i also agree with the argument for greater efficiency, less regulation and more flexibility in the allocation of eib funds. however, there are some things that are touched upon in this report that i disagree with less - the eib cannot be responsible for creating a new tax policy, it is entirely a matter of national sovereignty. that's why i abstained in the final vote.
in writing. ‒ expo 2015 in milan, from 1 may to 30 october, will serve as a platform for the european union and its institutions to present their policies to combat the food crisis, promote the fight against food waste, promote healthy eating and more. i supported the motion on the subject. its scope is rather broad, aiming to show what can be achieved at expo 2015.
in writing. i supported the resolution because i believe that europe must speak with a united and strong voice in tackling the challenges of food production and redistribution and in combating food waste. we need to encourage positive initiatives to meet the challenges facing the sector. nearly 900 million people worldwide suffer from hunger or malnutrition, while nearly 1.4 billion are overweight. these statistics signal the need for clear and specific policies to address these two problems, with a common root - the lack of sufficiently clear government policies in food production and redistribution. and with the world's population projected to reach nearly 9 billion by 2050, which in turn will generate a 70% increase in the need for food supplies over the same period, the need for such policies is becoming more urgent. i believe that the european union, as a leader in upholding high standards in agriculture, should set a strong example, especially in those areas related to unfair trade practices or the circular economy. i join the call to the european commission for an ambitious international agreement to include food and food policy as measures to mitigate the effects of climate change.
in writing. ‒ boko haram is currently believed to control more than 50,000 square kilometres of northeastern nigeria. there is an escalation of violence - including the use of children as suicide bombers - threatening the security of west africa as a whole. alongside the fight against boko haram, nigeria faces numerous other challenges, including widespread corruption, poverty, and a huge gap in economic inequality. all things considered, the nigerian elections are a momentous moment in nigeria's turbulent history, as they mark the first peaceful transition of power since independence from britain in 1960. i think we should send a good signal about what's happening in nigeria, and that's why i supported the resolution.
in writing. i supported this joint resolution because i believe that the boko haram phenomenon represents a direct threat to security not only for central but also for northern africa and hence for the european union. as a result of boko haram's actions, over 3 million citizens have been affected by its actions and over 1.5 million have been forced to flee their homes. over 300,000 nigerians have fled to neighbouring countries, some risking their lives by taking migrant routes to the eu in the hope of better economic and safe living conditions. i am deeply concerned by boko haram's pledge of allegiance to the islamic state, because beyond a common ideology, i fear that there will come a moment of common action. actions that have so far been directed at the local population, among whom are a number of christians. i fear that the attacks may be redirected to european union citizens. as a supporter of the joint resolution, i also call on the european commission to investigate the financing of boko haram and to pay attention to the trade in resources and goods that could be from the areas where the organization operates.
trust in our markets has been hurt by scandals and allegations of manipulation of the metrics. this must be stopped, and confidence restored, and more transparency given to the financial system. i therefore welcome this proposal and congratulate the colleagues who worked on the report for producing a more balanced text that overcomes the problematic elements and can secure broad support. most eu member states currently do not have a regulatory framework for the production of benchmarks at national level, which allows for the creation of a harmonised european framework for criteria. the introduction of such a framework will improve the protection of consumers of financial services, enhance trust and thus also contribute to improving financial stability at national and european level. however, it is also important to look at the impact of the new rules on the financial market as a whole, for example if an initiative forces input providers to change their business models significantly and there is a risk that they will reduce the number of services offered. it is therefore important to ensure that the new rules on benchmark input providers do not hinder innovation and do not create unnecessary burdens for business. overall, i support the report, which will increase the confidence of european citizens in the european union's financial system and ensure greater transparency.
mr. president, i would like to thank you for your service. president, mrs georgieva, every year the european union loses more than 10 billion euros from the illegal import of cigarettes. strong safeguards against this illegal importation must be sought, because it involves unlicensed products. for this reason, such products also pose a potential danger to the health of citizens. illegal imports also carry social risks. it presupposes unregulated employment relationships and puts workers in this grey sector at even greater risk. the nearly ten million cigarettes that are brought into my country, bulgaria, every day and for which no taxes are paid, cause disproportionately high economic damage. according to estimates by various organizations, my country loses at least 450 million leva a year from excise and vat - an amount that exceeds the total cost of monthly child benefits and is ten times more than the total cost of school transportation and free textbooks. in the interest of ending this practice, i believe that renewing agreements with the big tobacco companies will create stronger mechanisms for more socially responsible control and transparency.
mr president, commissioner, there has been a lot of criticism of the tobacco agreements in this chamber today, and it is clear that they are not perfect, but they are also necessary to protect the interests of producers and to increase control over the implementation of these agreements. bulgaria is known to be a traditional producer of tobacco and tobacco products. tobacco alone employs about 5.5% of the agricultural workforce and accounts for 13.8% of agricultural exports. it is estimated that tobacco provides jobs and income for some 35 000 farms. the sector is extremely important for a number of regions in bulgaria and is the only livelihood in some municipalities. therefore, any restrictive measures against producers and the sector would have serious negative effects. at the same time, smuggling and fake producers cause serious financial losses to bulgarian producers. i therefore support the idea that the commission should consider tightening controls on compliance with these agreements and try to stop smuggling.

in writing. the information and communication technology (ict) sector is growing at a very fast rate, creating 120 000 new jobs a year. at the same time, there is a shortage of ict specialists in europe. i believe that the first obstacle to the realisation of a european digital single market is the need to match skills with the real needs of the labour market. in order to ensure that citizens benefit fully from the opportunities offered by interconnected electronic services, it is necessary to stimulate the development of digital skills, which significantly increases the chances of young people finding a job. statistics show that 40% of the eu workforce does not have adequate digital skills and 14% does not have any at all. the results of the digital economy and society index (desi) are also alarming. in bulgaria, for example, only 1.3% of bulgarians aged 20 to 29 have a degree in science, technology, engineering and mathematics. only 54% of them use the internet regularly and have a fixed broadband connection. the commission shall be empowered to adopt delegated acts in accordance with article 26 to supplement this regulation by laying down detailed rules for the application of this regulation.

in writing. ‒ it is a sobering fact that around 10% of patients in the eu suffer from disabilities caused by healthcare. the main reason for this is thought to be the savings imposed by the member states. according to a world bank report, the bulgarian citizen pays approximately three times more for health than normal, only 51 percent is borne by the state. ensuring a high level of human health protection is achieved at national level, through national policies, but cooperation between member states and the exchange of good practices can only be useful. bulgaria tends to use a lot of antibiotics and access to them is relatively easy. as the report points out, this leads to drug resistance and ultimately does not help, but harms. patient safety policies and programs are needed. let's put health first!
in writing. ‒ smes are the backbone of the european union's economy and are important drivers of economic growth and job creation. there is a very broad consensus that 'green growth' provides a wide range of benefits. the report is trade-oriented and calls on the commission to avoid creating unnecessary red tape for smes in this area in order to be able to stimulate their funding, r & d and innovation more effectively. the acquisition and dissemination of accurate knowledge, which can only contribute to growth, also needs to be given careful consideration.
in writing. ‒ i supported the report on green growth of smes, as i believe that one of the eu's main competitive advantages is the capacity of european smes to grow and innovate sustainably. the potential for green growth lies in the efficient use of resources to reduce the environmental footprint. to this end, the eu and the member states should promote measures related to energy efficiency, renewable energy, waste management and emission reduction. environmental sustainability in the activities of smes can be developed through instruments such as eco-design, the single european patent in the field of green technologies, eco-innovation in rural areas, etc. green growth of smes is directly linked to the provision of an adequate set of financial instruments and access to european funds and programmes such as cosme and horizon 2020. given that 90% of enterprises in the eu are micro-enterprises, this funding should be available to them as well as to start-up innovative companies. developing entrepreneurial skills in young people is key to the future of the green economy. the erasmus+ and erasmus for young entrepreneurs programmes offer a very good opportunity for students and young people to develop entrepreneurial skills by funding traineeships and exchanges of experience.
in writing. ladies and gentlemen, the current level of insecurity at the eu's borders and in its immediate neighbourhood is unprecedented since the late 1990s. with the high number of persistent and emerging security challenges, it is clear that no member state can cope alone and this requires a strengthened csdp. the union and the member states need to adapt urgently to new challenges by making effective use of existing csdp instruments and by cooperating in the field of security and defence with other international institutions, in particular the un, nato and the osce. of course, we cannot ignore the fact that the financial crisis has had a serious impact on national defence budgets, but member states need to take more responsibility for collective security and work to strengthen the european defence industry and the european defence and industrial base. more resources should be invested in defence and we should focus on those areas of crisis management where the eu can really add value. member states need to be more prepared for eu military operations. make an appropriate contribution of the resources and capabilities they possess to enhance cooperation and coordination, in particular in the areas of counter-terrorism, organised crime, cyber defence and migration.
on behalf of the ppe group. mr. chairman, according to the un, money laundering costs about 2.7% of global gdp, or about $1.6 trillion a year. the real cost is much higher, because organised crime and terrorism, which are financed by money laundering, affect the lives and destinies of millions of people around the world. the fourth legislative package of the european union on combating money laundering is therefore of the utmost importance. it will bring more transparency to an increasingly complex financial system which, without this package, would provide a multitude of opportunities for fraud. agreements reached on a central register of beneficiaries of a company, on the inclusion of tax crimes in the scope of the package, on stricter controls on financial transfers, on the obligation of intermediary payment intermediaries to cooperate with the competent authorities are important innovations in european legislation. they will allow for the clarification of the ownership of companies and more transparency in business relationships, which will lead to a strengthening of the functioning of the single market of the european union. at the same time, it will facilitate the fight against organised crime and terrorism, and more specifically the tracking of their financing, which is relevant in the current context of the terrorist threat to europe and was reaffirmed as a priority in the european security agenda presented at the end of april. ladies and gentlemen, i believe that the fourth anti-money laundering package takes into account current and future threats to european security and provides adequate tools. (the president declared the common position approved as amended)
mr president, for our economy to prosper, we need two ‘t’s in place – trust and transparency. in europe each year, the volume of laundered money reaches eur 600 billion. this is double the funds we need for the juncker plan. this is money that we could now be investing in innovation, in education and in research. with these two proposals today, we want to reverse this negative trend through improving traceability in the transfer of funds. this is how we will improve the integrity, proper functioning, reputation and stability of the financial system. the new measures on due diligence are very much needed. they will ensure a better knowledge of customers and a better understanding of the nature of their business. the creation of central registers for information on beneficial ownership will enhance transparency and improve the trust of our citizens in the financial system. data privacy of subjects on the register, though, must be respected. these are rules we cannot do without at european level. money laundering and terrorist financing cannot be tackled by individual states. together we stand strong in fighting financial fraud. this type of legislation creates trust: trust in our financial system; trust that we put our money to good use; and trust that europeans can thrive and develop in a fair environment that rewards initiative and prevents unlawful practices.
three legislative packages have been adopted so far in the fight against money laundering. unfortunately, the results are far below our expectations. crime is flexible and always one step ahead of us. something has to change. we need a new, holistic policy with binding laws and a common approach to fight corruption, organised crime and money laundering. europe must be a leader in the fight against tax havens, not turn a blind eye to them. the european commission is currently monitoring two countries - bulgaria and romania - for progress in the fight against crime, but this mechanism is exhausted and not working. commissioner malstrom's corruption report shows the enormity of the scale of corruption in all countries. we need common european legislation, the approximation of criminal standards in the member states, so that cross-border criminal networks do not take advantage of the differences and loopholes in national laws. we expect this policy to be the main part of the new security strategy by 2020.
on behalf of the ecr group. mr president, it is undeniable that the severe economic crisis has caused difficulties for economic operators in the member states. large corporations are struggling, small and medium-sized enterprises are in difficulty, and many citizens are facing the literal impossibility of covering even their basic expenses. it is therefore logical and entirely normal to discuss the possibility of improving and standardising insolvency proceedings. of course, we cannot ignore the fact that the legal systems of the member states regulate this procedure differently, but a common framework can nevertheless be introduced. this would be beneficial for the common market and for increasing the level of competition and would have a favourable effect. i agree with the thesis of some of my colleagues that it is right and necessary to consider the introduction of the status of 'bankruptcy of the family and the individual'. it cannot and should not be that a man should be a slave to his creditors permanently and forever. this practice has long been renounced in the member states. unfortunately, current practice shows otherwise. i therefore support the idea of community regulation of the bankruptcy of natural persons. it has its legal and, of course, its life logic. many may not like this idea, but it would certainly be fair and would benefit the citizens of the member states.
in writing. fellas, everyone deserves a second chance. this is especially true for entrepreneurs. the fear of bankruptcy in the eu must be overcome. i welcome the extension of the scope of the regulation and believe that focusing on national procedures that provide for company restructuring in the pre-bankruptcy phase and lead to the recovery of businesses, as well as giving entrepreneurs a second chance, are key to having a viable eu economy. i believe that requiring member states to publish information on cross-border insolvency cases in a publicly accessible electronic register will improve information for the creditors and courts involved and prevent the creation of parallel insolvency proceedings. the new regulation will avoid incentives for parties to transfer assets or litigation from one member state to another in order to obtain a more favourable legal position to the detriment of the insolvency creditors. it will bring more clarity to the provisions on jurisdiction to open insolvency proceedings, on the recognition and enforcement of judgments in such proceedings, on the applicable law and on the coordination of insolvency proceedings. only in this way will cross-border insolvency proceedings operate efficiently and effectively and help to achieve one of our most important priorities: promoting growth in europe.
mr president, despite the numerous calls by the european parliament in recent months to open negotiations with the council on the maternity leave directive, we are once again at an impasse today. it is high time that the council and the member states took responsibility for the apparent failure to improve the situation of women giving birth and becoming mothers in europe. i believe that now is the time for the commission to play its role as a balancer and help to achieve a compromise between the positions of the co-legislators. do you consider it normal that the european union's legislation in this area has not been changed in the last 20 years? does it respond to the social progress of our societies and can we afford to sacrifice european social standards in the name of better administrative governance? the council's position does not only compromise the safety and health of pregnant women and women who are about to give birth. the withdrawal of the directive must be seen in the context of an ageing population in europe. the demographic crisis has brought to the fore the need to stimulate fertility, which will also be neglected if we delay indefinitely the adoption of this text.
in writing. the proposal to revise the maternity leave directive has been under discussion since 2010 and has reached a deadlock in the negotiation process. the impact assessment of the proposal indicates that prolonging motherhood and strengthening the rights of women returning from motherhood will contribute to ensuring equality between women and men in terms of labour market opportunities and work-life balance. i think the proposed amendments would be a small step towards equality, given that working women continue to receive lower pay and pensions. i also believe that we cannot afford to seek to reduce the administrative burden at the expense of social justice. we cannot afford to make european women choose between work and children, especially in an ageing europe. i therefore hope that the maternity leave directive will be amended in line with the standards set by the international labour organisation of the united nations.
mr president, commissioner, your proposal is a huge step forward, particularly in terms of binding quotas of refugees for all member states. this is only the beginning and we are waiting for a specific programme to implement these measures. it is not enough to rescue and receive refugees on european soil, we must also respond to their integration into our societies to allay the fears of our citizens. however, some member states want to block the measures proposed by the commission even before this first debate. these reactions are a cause for serious concern. the council's responsibility is enormous. we can't run from problems when they're right in front of our door. we need to show solidarity. if we do not take urgent action, we risk deepening the humanitarian crisis at our external borders and giving a helping hand to smugglers. the dublin regulation should be revised to take account of the interests of all countries in europe. the commission should use all the levers to get the council to accept the urgent application of the proposed measures.
in writing. illegal immigration is a central problem for the union and there is hardly a member state where this issue is not on the agenda. i have raised the question here many times what to do with this wave of illegal immigrants. i will say again that as long as the islamic state exists, as long as al-shabaab exists, as long as the middle east and africa are shaken by conflict, this wave will not subside. that's why all the talk about quotas for illegal immigrants, and their subsequent distribution in europe, is a palliative measure. the solution to the problem is not in quotas. i think it's a mistake to introduce them. we treat the symptom, not the disease. we're talking about quotas, how we're going to allocate 20,000 people among 28 member states. and when that quota runs out? - then what? bulgaria, italy, spain, greece will suffer again. in italy alone, more than 50,000 people will enter, in bulgaria alone, 30,000 people will enter this year. what are we going to do with them? the problem must be solved by pacifying the areas from which the illegals come. every economic migrant should be sent back to his country. we're being offered a liberal model that will only redistribute the wave. no, thank you. i will not support such a policy. it's against the interests of my bulgaria.
in writing. ‒ i supported mr steer's report on the trade, development and cooperation agreement (tdca) with south africa, as it gives the council the right to conclude an additional protocol with south africa with a view to croatia's accession to the eu. the additional protocol is intended solely to make only minor technical and linguistic adaptations to the trce which are necessary with the accession of croatia to the eu. however, this protocol is necessary, in particular, to enable the republic of croatia to become a contracting party to the trsa.
in writing. ‒ i supported the proposal to amend the directive on money laundering for the purpose of financing international terrorism, as i believe it is of the utmost importance. the current european legislation in this area is outdated and does not comply with international standards, which currently puts us on the losing side in the fight against international terrorism. we all know very well that terrorist organizations are always one step ahead, both in the use of new technologies and in finding holes in our legislation. the main response to the threat posed by terrorist organisations is to halt or partially reduce the cash flows financing terrorist activities. it is also directly linked to money from the production and trafficking of illegal drugs, human trafficking and other activities inherent in organised crime. the link between organized crime and terrorism is straightforward. restricting financial flows to terrorist organisations and groups will lead to a reduction in terrorist activity. terrorism is international in scope and that is why we need to take decisive common action and constantly adapt the regulatory framework to be able to at least partially limit the threat posed by illegal organisations such as traffickers and terrorists.

in writing. ‒ in its previous mandate, the commission proposed a voluntary certification regime for european companies importing so-called 'conflict minerals'. today i supported the proposal for more ambitious measures and mandatory certification of all eu importers of 'conflict minerals' to ensure that trade in minerals does not fuel conflict and contribute to human rights abuses. i believe that mandatory certification will help to break the link between the trade in minerals and the financing of the activities of terrorist organisations in the relevant conflict zones. the concerns about the effect of the mandatory certification scheme on smes are justified, but i would remind you that parliament has called on the commission to support smes in obtaining this certification by covering their costs of the procedure through the so-called eu programme for the competitiveness of enterprises and small and medium-sized enterprises (cosme). the adoption of the draft law in a more binding version is in itself a historic step forward, but it is important to note that the text will only be finally finalised after the conclusion of informal negotiations with the other european institutions. i am convinced that economic interest should not be put above values.

in writing. ‒ the maternity leave directive does not comply with the principles of subsidiarity and in this respect i cannot support this resolution calling for its revision. the member states have the competence in this area and should have the final say and the right to regulate legislation in this respect themselves. in my country, maternity leave is paid for two years. the exchange of good practice is necessary, but european legislation is not.
in writing. i believe that the negotiations on the maternity leave directive, or rather the lack of negotiations, are an example that should not be repeated. this is a piece of legislation that was approved by this parliament five years ago to better protect mothers and pregnant women at work. that is why i voted in favour of this resolution. this text thus contributes to the achievement of the major objectives of the europe 2020 strategy and contributes to equality between women and men; it promotes economic recovery in europe by improving the integration of women into the labour market and reducing the inequalities they face. and yet, this text was slowly buried and almost removed permanently. parliament has repeatedly demonstrated its willingness to negotiate, but it cannot do so without the council, as a co-legislator, or without the help and political will of the european commission. we're being told to bring back a new proposal if it fails. what will be its position in the absence of an official position? a positive outcome is still possible, but for that the three institutions must play their full part.
in writing. ‒ the problem of people with settlements in the european union is not to be underestimated. bulgaria ranks among the last in the union in terms of the quality of services offered to people with such functional deficits. according to the last census in 2011, there are half a million. eurostat shows that twice as many bulgarians with disabilities as the eu average have dropped out of school prematurely. not to mention the social exclusion and poverty they have to fight. the 2011 convention on the rights of persons with disabilities is a positive step and its implementation should be monitored.
in writing. ‒ ladies and gentlemen,people with disabilities are among the most vulnerable groups in our society. some 80 million people in the european union have some form of disability and their integration, especially into the labour market, is one of the greatest challenges. despite all the existing programmes, initiatives and strategies at eu level, there is still much to be done in europe to achieve equal treatment and to remove the barriers that still exist for people with disabilities. the resolution adopted today is a guarantee of our commitment to place at the heart of european policies full respect for the un convention on the rights of persons with disabilities, with a view to eliminating all forms of discrimination against them and their full participation in economic and social life. this document reflects our desire to be more involved and involved in developing targeted policies to improve the conditions for independent living, in line with the convention, as ratification by member states does not mean implementation. turning it into action is our shared responsibility - that of the european institutions and the member states. this can only be achieved through a harmonised approach and in continuous dialogue and close cooperation with organisations representing people with disabilities.

madam president, i would like to congratulate the rapporteur for her huge efforts on the preparation of the draft motion for a resolution and the intention to provide a suitable framework for eu-turkey relations. unfortunately, the last version has been modified and the whole philosophy of the motion has been changed. there is a disproportionate emphasis on the negative elements, without referring to the positive steps undertaken by the turkish government. such an approach could increase the euroscepticism within turkish society and threaten the positive trend of public approval for eu membership. instead of tolerating positive attitudes towards the eu within society, we could achieve a negative effect. analysis of the official visit of the delegation of the committee on foreign affairs (afet) to ankara has not been taken into consideration. there is no clearly stated position on opening chapters, especially chapters 23 and 24, which could provide the turkish government with impetus for implementation of reforms. noting that turkey plays a key role in the region, in regard to the refugees from the middle east it should be stressed that sustainment of almost two million syrian refugees costs nearly usd 6 billion, while financial and political support from the international community is very limited. turkey has a strategic role as a partner of the eu and a member of nato and its geopolitical importance in addressing security and stability in the region is crucial. the cooperation between the eu and turkey is of mutual interest, so we need each other in order to overcome the security challenges more than ever.
madam president, i am deeply convinced that the european union must continue to actively support anyone in turkey who wants democracy and pro-european integration in the country. it is strategically important for us to have a reliable ally, neighbor and partner in the republic of turkey. regional and global challenges underline the clear need to strengthen cooperation on priority issues such as energy security and diversification of supply, stability in the middle east and tackling irregular migration, which is particularly relevant in the context of the discussion of a new eu solidarity migration policy. the eu accession negotiation process for turkey has been open-ended from the outset, but it represents a strong impetus for reform and democratic processes in the country and an opportunity to realise the full potential of our strategic partnership. i welcome the resumption of active dialogue in recent months, which is vital to building mutual trust and which i hope will give a new impetus to reforms in the country. overcoming the critical moments in key areas such as respect for fundamental rights, the rule of law, and resolving the problems with neighbours, which are the values on which the european union is founded, are conditions without which the process of european integration cannot continue. the ratification and entry into force of the readmission agreement is a step in the right direction, but it is important to ensure its full and effective application. bulgaria and turkey share a land border which is also an external border of the european union. we attach particular importance to full cooperation with the turkish authorities in strengthening border security, combating trafficking in persons and goods, energy infrastructure and the economy. member of the commission, on behalf of the vice-president of the commission/high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy.– mr president, thank you for this opportunity to make a statement on behalf of vice-president federica mogherini on the situation in the former yugoslav republic of macedonia, which is very fluid. you are all familiar with the tragic events in kumanovo in the north of the country close to kosovo and serbia. we have expressed our condolences to the families and friends of those killed and injured. we understand that these events were predominantly criminally and not inter-ethnically motivated. the commission therefore welcomes the fact that all parties, exceptionally, came together in a multi-ethnic way to condemn the activities. one of the parties also played an important role in helping to negotiate a solution without further bloodshed. let me now turn to the long-term political crisis from which those events cannot be a distraction. in response to the eu’s and others’ calls for political accountability for what is being revealed in the wiretaps, which the opposition are making public, we note the resignation last week of two ministers and the head of state security. we have already said that this is a concrete step towards political accountability and that we hope that this development will contribute to restoring faith in state institutions. in this context we have also followed the two sets of demonstrations that were held in skopje on sunday and monday. it is important that people were able to freely express their views on both occasions. this was done in a peaceful manner. a calm and appropriate handling from all sides will remain important. from the outset of the political crisis, the european union has called for the restoration of normal political dialogue between the main government and opposition parties. i welcome that three of your colleagues responded to my colleague johannes hahn’s proposal to facilitate dialogue between these two parties. as you will be aware, three distinguished members of this parliament, mr vajgl, mr howitt and mr kukan, together with commissioner hahn, met here in strasbourg yesterday and in the early morning hours of today with the prime minister of the former yugoslav republic of macedonia, mr gruevski and the leader of the opposition, mr zaev. this was an important meeting. it is clear that both sides are taking these facilitation efforts very seriously. in-depth discussions on divergent viewpoints took place and further dialogue will be needed. the commission commends the confirmation of the commitment of the parties to the eu integration process and democratic principles as well as their agreement to put the interests of the country first. we urge all sides to continue their engagement in this spirit; we welcome in this respect their readiness to meet again under the umbrella of this facilitation effort. also, we welcome the fact that the four main party leaders met in skopje on 14 and 18 may and that they have agreed to meet again on 26 may in an attempt to find a way forward, because the citizens of this country deserve better than a political stalemate. a stalemate that drags on simply does not respond to the aspirations of the population. we call on the leaders of the country to take their responsibilities seriously and put the interests of the state above those of their parties. in parallel, the european union has been calling on the relevant authorities to investigate all allegations of potential wrongdoing, including those being made public in the wiretaps, in a transparent and independent manner. the commission has also sent a team of senior legal experts to look into some of the systemic issues on which the commission had previously expressed concern. arising from the wiretaps, these systemic issues need to be addressed to get this candidate country back on the right track as it deserves. in conclusion: our collective approach to this crisis is a good example of how various eu institutions and services can work together using our various tools to deliver a clear european message. in all of this, we are also working very closely with our international partners on the ground to improve the situation.

mr president, there used to be times when macedonia was among the forerunners for eu accession in the western balkans. however, today we are facing a very deep political crisis and we should be aware that it is not just a momentous situation; it is about very fundamental issues which are enshrined in the european idea. we should ask ourselves how we got here and how the european union underestimated what was going on in the republic of macedonia for such a long time as a way of government, establishing quite an authoritarian regime which is subduing the media and the economy and putting pressure on the judiciary. this is a very serious problem. i was there on sunday at the civil rally which gathered in skopje. i was not there to teach or preach; i was there to listen, and i saw people of different ethnic origins, of different political affiliations and people from ngos and from the media. they were standing together for democratic values. as one of the speakers from the students’ movement said, it is not about power, but about how the state will be functioning, and this should not be repeated. this is what we have to take into consideration in our attitude towards the situation in macedonia, because the crisis is deep and the eu has responsibility.
mr. president, i would like to mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the leader of the bulgarian liberal party movement for rights and freedoms, mr. lutvi mestan, wrote a letter to all liberal leaders in europe at the beginning of the year, saying that it is time for the european union to open the doors to the western balkans. the situation in macedonia proves the wisdom of this thesis and provokes us to insist on a proactive union policy for its speedier integration as a guarantee for the preservation of its territorial integrity and peace within it. the leadership of the state must make efforts to resolve the political crisis and macedonia should move quickly towards european values and standards, towards guaranteeing the rights and freedoms, including those of the bulgarian community. a good example is the bulgarian ethnic model, which ensured a peaceful transition from totalitarianism to democracy in our country. i hope that macedonia and the european union will meet on the field of democracy and respect for the rights of all citizens.
mr. president, the political elite in macedonia must make every effort to find a solution to the current crisis and not seek confrontation and conflict, both domestically and externally. unfortunately, in the last nine years, prime minister gruevski has pushed the country further away from its european path, which we here have always supported and will continue to support. but we did not see the will to improve democratic practices and guarantee equality for all citizens, regardless of their national identity. on the contrary, the country's relations with its neighbours deteriorated as a result of the unwillingness to accept the outstretched hand and to put behind it the vicious practices of the totalitarian communist yugoslav past such as the manipulation of historical facts at the expense of the aggressive imposition of a newly formed macedonian identity. the european union has an interest in a stable and democratic macedonia, in which freedom of the media and the separation of powers are guaranteed and the individual and collective rights and freedoms of macedonian citizens are respected. the question remains of the country's geopolitical choice. macedonia does not support sanctions against the russian federation, lavrov recently took the liberty of saying that macedonia is a victim of the west and bulgaria and albania want to share it, it remains unclear macedonia's attitude towards the crisis in ukraine. macedonian citizens deserve a transparent and accountable government that ensures rapid admission to the european union and it is unacceptable to become hostages of the political survival of one or another party leader who tries to personify the country!
