mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the problem in macedonia is not just a problem of the state of macedonia. this is a problem of the balkans, and not least of the european union itself. the long-term forced isolation of macedonia is one of the main reasons for its current crisis. we should not absolutize the criterion that the parties have previously resolved all their bilateral issues. absolutizing means telling all the balkan countries that their membership in the european union is impossible. so, mr president, i would like to say that there are issues in the balkans that can be resolved not before but with membership of the european union itself. the integration of the western balkans is not because of their political elites, it's not even because of their prime ministers, but because of the civil societies of these young democracies. the european union must not allow the western balkans to stand in the seemingly neutral field between east and west. last but not least, the integration of the western balkans is the most reliable prevention against the radicalisation of these young societies. that is why we, the bulgarian liberals, will prepare a meeting in september to discuss a new integration strategy for all the countries in the region.
in writing. during today's meeting in strasbourg, we discussed the situation in the republic of macedonia with our colleagues from the european parliament. the protests and riots that erupted there a few days ago are also the reason for the presence in the european parliament of the macedonian prime minister nikola gruevski and the leader of the opposition zoran zaev. the most important and positive news is that when there is a crisis in macedonia, the european union is the mediator. it is also very important that despite their differences, both politicians clearly define the country's eu membership as a priority. the conclusions of this and previous hearings are that this problem is not new and is quite serious. in order to prevent the deepening of the crisis in the country, macedonia needs to be integrated more quickly. without the start of eu membership negotiations, the country's democracy will be in danger. it should also be noted that the most irritated by the whole situation is russia. it is once again trying to interfere in the politics of a balkan country that shares the values of a united europe, wants to be a member of the european union and is seeking to develop democratically.
madam president, the question under discussion is really one: what is the council's attitude to the implementation of the youth guarantee, which has been so much hyped for several years now? the council has not yet adopted a decision on the matter, but it is not yet possible to conclude that the council has not taken a decision on the matter. will the council undertake to present an assessment of the implementation of this guarantee six months after it enters into force under the amended regulation and what will the council do if this guarantee is clearly not working? these are the questions we want answered, it is the duty of the council, at each of its meetings as the ministers for labour and social affairs, to put the question of how the guarantee is being implemented first.
madam chair, i will use my 60 seconds today not to point out the problems, but to propose solutions. if we are going to deal with youth issues, we better do it with a youth approach and in the language of the youth. experience has taught us that it doesn't matter how much money we spend on employment, what matters in the end is how many young people find jobs. let's take a step forward, let's move from subsidized employment to fostering youth entrepreneurship, let's awaken the entrepreneurial spirit in every young person out there, let's make the employees in the employment offices not just clerks, let's take off their ties and go out in the field to those who need them. let's use smartphones to fight youth unemployment, let's have employment agencies have apps so they can be close to young people. last but not least, young people hate queues, they hate standing in line at the counter, they hate red tape and paperwork. let's get rid of this bureaucracy, let's give the young a chance and they will turn europe upside down.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, efforts to overcome the problem of the alarmingly high levels of youth unemployment must be global, targeted, oriented towards effective action. i appreciate the actions taken so far to increase youth employment, in particular the youth employment initiative, but despite the efforts made in this direction, as of february 2015, nearly 5 million young people were unemployed in the european union. recognising the serious difficulties member states face in launching operational programmes, i was among those members of the european parliament who supported the european commission's proposal to increase the amount of their pre-financing. i know that simply providing additional funds will not solve the problem. i would therefore like to ask the council: what action do you intend to take with regard to the use of the so-called funds and how do you intend to adequately report on the results achieved?
madam president, although more than a year has passed since the implementation of the youth employment initiative, the results at local level are far from what was expected. i believe that we must not only make effective use of the financial instruments available, but also embark on the much-needed educational reforms. the lasting solution to this problem is through educational changes that offer diverse forms of informal learning, emphasise practical experience and stimulate the entrepreneurial spirit and innovative thinking of young people. last but not least, i believe that youth employment schemes should be adapted to the specific problems of the member states. in bulgaria, for example, one of the most serious challenges continues to be the difficult transition from school to employment. in addition, in the context of the demographic crisis, the emigration of highly qualified professionals continues to increase in my country. the council must not only ensure that the programme is implemented swiftly and efficiently, but also be aware of the problems and difficulties faced by the member states and take action.
in writing. we in the epp see europe as the project of the working people. because only genuine employment can lead to the other things we are striving for - social inclusion, political engagement and professional activity. however, the future of europe as a workers' project is under serious threat, given statistics that show that 15.4% of young people in europe between the ages of 15 and 29 are out of work or education. in the face of the risk that in some countries we will have a lost future generation that may fall off the social network, the commission and the ep have approved funding under the youth employment initiative of €1 billion for 2015 alone. i come from a country where the proportion of people who are not employed by education, retraining or work is one of the highest in europe. i would therefore like to point out that this decision is particularly important and that it frees up the national governments to use this fund. i hope they all spend these funds wisely, with a clear plan and look for real effect. after a year, we all have to give an account of what we've accomplished. to make sure that in 20 years, there will be someone to drive europe's economy.
in writing. ladies and gentlemen, the european union has been working towards a single energy market for two decades. three energy legislative packages have been adopted, but we are still far from this market because member states have almost complete sovereignty in energy policy. the single energy market is not possible without free cross-border flows of energy subject to regulatory rules common to all member states. a strong pan-european agency for the cooperation of energy regulators is therefore needed, with the necessary human and administrative resources to manage the single market and to ensure that the rules for the functioning of the energy market are uniform across the countries. the acer has responsibilities not only to ensure the regulatory completion of the internal energy market with the relevant rules and regulations in cross-border energy exchanges, but also to coordinate the activities of national regulators in this regard. the new european energy union, which was launched with a strong political drive to achieve greater energy security, requires significantly greater coordination and cooperation between member states for the interconnection of energy systems and for open and free energy trade between them. only then will we have competition, lower prices for consumers and security of supply for all. i therefore support the extension of the mandate of the aser expert panel.
in writing. ‒ i voted against the report on the implementation of the common security and defence policy because i believe that defence policy should remain a matter of national sovereignty. i agree with several of the points made in the report, namely that the economic crisis has led to a serious reduction in defence budgets. this threatens the union's strategic autonomy and creates a lack of military capability in its member states, which forces us to rely increasingly on others. the very idea of a common army worries me because i believe that the union should help to develop the military capabilities of each member state, not to take away what little sovereignty it has. it is not the union but each member state that needs a strong and autonomous army, because it is the guarantor of the country's sovereignty and territorial integrity and an instrument for defending national interests.
in writing. ‒ i support the resolution on the implementation of the common security and defence policy adopted by a minimal majority. the unprecedented level of instability at the eu's borders requires the union and its member states to take greater responsibility for ensuring their security and defence. at present, the eu does not have the resources to respond effectively to the growing security threats, not only at european level but also at global level. it cannot be ignored that at the level of operations conducted under the common foreign and security policy (cfsp), structural deficits and a long and inflexible decision-making process are at the forefront. as regards union capabilities, unfortunately, minimal progress is reported on the pooling and sharing of capabilities projects. the potential of member states to use the cfsp tools more effectively, to improve coordination between internal and external security measures and to work towards progress in the area of pooling and sharing capabilities is undeniable. this is why they must play an active role in taking concrete action, because the international context itself now requires flexibility and readiness to act not only in the region but also on the global stage.
in writing. i voted against the report on the financing of the common security and defence policy because i believe that defence and national security are a priority for the member states, not a general priority for the union. at a time when we are surrounded by conflicts - in the southeast, the islamic state and the looming conflict between iran and saudi arabia; in the south of europe, in north africa, we have disintegrated states, numerous terrorist groups and an influx of refugees; and in the north-east, the military conflict in ukraine - we must pursue a more secure and responsible policy. i believe that national security is a national state's priority and that it must decide for itself how much and how it will use it to ensure its national integrity and sovereignty. my view is that the osce should not overlap its functions with those of nato.
in writing. ‒ i voted against the report on the impact of changes in european defence markets on europe's security and defence capabilities because i believe that defence and national security are a priority for member states, not a general priority for the union. at a time when we are surrounded by conflicts, in the southeast by the islamic state and the looming conflict between iran and saudi arabia, in the south of europe in north africa we have disintegrated states, numerous terrorist groups and an influx of refugees, and in the northeast by the military conflict on the territory of ukraine, we must conduct an increasingly serious and more responsible policy on defence and national security.
mr president, first of all i would like to congratulate rapporteur marc tarabella on the report and the compromises that were made in it. i would like to call on member states to pay attention to the fact that the problem of obesity is becoming more acute in europe. finding answers to this problem does not simply lie in increasing medical costs; it requires educational measures that explain to students the benefits of a healthy lifestyle. it is vital that growing organisms consume quality products, because this is an investment in the health of future european citizens. i would like to draw particular attention to the use of organic products under the new common scheme for the supply of agricultural products in educational establishments. these products have a 40% higher dry matter content and have characteristics closest to the natural qualities of the products. i call on the member states of the european union to ensure that the necessary information on the quality of organic and local products reaches the widest possible range of growers.
in writing.– i welcome the commission’s new aid scheme. the original idea of ensuring access for children to natural products must be enhanced. the number of educational establishments and students benefiting from the scheme should be increased. this can be achieved if the member states increase their commitment in the implementation of the scheme. there are over 22 million children in the european union with excessive weight and almost 5 million with obesity problems. in bulgaria alone the share of pupils with obesity problems has increased from less than 10 % in 1998 to 21% among boys and 15% among girls in 2011. the existing ‘school milk’ and ‘school fruit’ schemes have been a step in the right direction. in my country children aged 7-10 consume on average only 40% of the recommended daily intake of fruit and vegetables. 89% of parents of children that have been benefiting from the scheme explicitly state their desire for it to be continued.
in writing. with today's decision, the european parliament is sending a clear signal to the council that it supports the adoption of the school schemes regulation as soon as possible in order to reduce the administrative burden of implementing them and make them more effective. the adoption of uniform rules for both schemes and the abolition of the co-financing requirement will lead to a fairer distribution of funds between member states and will increase the interest in the 'school milk' scheme. we are calling for an increase of €20 million in the annual school milk budget as an additional measure to support the dairy sector, local and small dairy farmers who are most affected by the loss of milk quotas. i welcome the extension of the range of school products with honey, yogurt and cheese, which are traditional for our country and have a proven positive effect on children's health. in addition to contributing to a healthy and balanced diet, the inclusion of these products in the daily menu of young people will increase their knowledge of the origin of food and stimulate local and organic producers by providing a market for their products. that is why i support the participation of producer organisations in school schemes as a means of stimulating short supply chains and children's access to higher quality and seasonal fruit and vegetables.
madam president, we have recently witnessed a serious and disturbing trend. the speed at which new applications appear on our mobile devices is inversely proportional to the speed at which the european union adopts legislation on many important issues. once again, a key dossier such as the network and information security directive is delayed in council discussions, even though the european parliament adopted its first opinion on it during its previous mandate. in brussels and beyond, everyone knows that the digital economy is a priority for both the european commission and the parliament. the benefits of the digital single market, however, are rendered meaningless in the context of an insecure online space. there's one topic that comes before economics and growth. this is the issue of our security - an issue on which we need to work together more than ever and be concrete and decisive. the new legislation on network security will raise the level of protection on the internet, in particular by strengthening cooperation between individual eu member states. we cannot afford to be the target of attacks, whether online or offline. our security is a global issue that does not concern the citizens, businesses and institutions of just one country. the security of europe depends on all of us and is only possible if we combine our efforts in this area in a targeted way. for this reason, the speed with which we make decisions and operate is of great importance. it's even more important to think preemptively, to prevent future instances of cyber espionage, attacks against cyber domains and possible cyber wars. it's the only way we're going to protect our citizens, our businesses, and our communities.
in writing. first of all, i would like to express my solidarity with the french journalists of tv 5 monde. the cyber attack on a french tv channel on 8 april highlighted our vulnerability to high-tech cybercrime and the growing potential threats to european media. like the charlie hebdo terrorist attack in january of this year, the cyberattack on a major international media outlet by the islamic state is actually an assault on the foundations of our democratic societies. this is a deliberate attack on freedom of expression, democracy, the rule of law and respect for fundamental human rights. all values that are at the heart of the european project. immediate and coordinated action by member states is needed to ensure effective protection against isil cyber terrorism. the new european security programme is a step in this direction. but that's not enough. it is necessary to ensure the full implementation of existing legislation at national level. i believe that the creation of adequate cybersecurity policies should also be a priority in the european counter-terrorism strategy.
the new digital single market strategy has been met with mixed feelings. one of the controversial points in it is the way vat is levied. i think the one-stop-shop option will really help reduce the administrative burden and make it easier for businesses. the situation is far more complicated when it comes to vat on books. the gaps in european legislation are most visible here, as evidenced by the recent ruling of the court of justice of the european union on the application of a reduced vat rate to e-books in france and luxembourg. i consider this discrimination to be unacceptable and unjustified and call on the commission to review the overall legislative framework as soon as possible. my country, for example, is one of the few that doesn't take advantage of the opportunity to reduce the tax on books. at present, it is 20% and one of the highest in europe. i believe that the european union should be a model and a corrective for ensuring easy access to culture for its citizens.
in writing. ‒ more than 22 million children in the eu are overweight, according to the latest figures, while the consumption of fruit and vegetables among adolescents is declining. in other words, the problem of obesity is becoming more and more widespread. that's not a statistic you can just walk away from. i believe that the diet of our children should be a priority, because nutrition has a powerful role in fighting certain diseases. the key point here is that the aid scheme for the supply of fruit and vegetables, bananas and milk to educational establishments should be motivating for member states and should have less administrative burden. we need to use schools to act responsibly and instill good eating habits in our youngest citizens. we must not forget that this is an investment in the health of future generations. the other big benefit of this scheme is that it gives an introduction to farming, so the youngsters will understand where the products they consume come from and will get to know the supply chain, which is educational. i believe that this scheme is a way of giving the eu a priority and i support it.
mr president, implementing the requirements set out in the regulation on the financing, management and monitoring of the common agricultural policy and establishing rules for direct payments to farmers under support schemes is an important element in the process of drawing up the 2016 eu budget. according to the regulations, in order to support the agricultural sector in case of major crises affecting agricultural production or distribution, the reserve for crises which is proposed by the commission to be included in the 2016 draft budget is eur 441.6 million. the percentage of the financial discipline adjustment rate – approximately 1.39%, which we hope is calculated correctly – is based on the presumption that it will be applied only to the amount of direct payments for farmers which exceed eur 2 000 and does not include three member states. in this regard, the financial discipline adjustment rate means less money for the farmers and, in this context, it will be a good step to do the maximum for the simplification of the application forms for small farmers. the above-mentioned sum will be included in the 2016 draft budget, with the budget heading ‘reserve for crises’. regarding the correct implementation of the requirements, we support the report for fixing the adjustment rate for direct payments in respect of the 2015 calendar.
thank you, madam chairman, i want to congratulate maria noehn on a job well done. for the first time in a long time, we have a clear and unambiguous document, with a bold vision for policy, with bold decisions that we must defend this week with our vote. but in this debate, we have to be honest enough. there is very little progress in the area of equality between women and men. good strategies and proposals are declarative, we wear them like a beautiful brooch on the lapel, but we don't implement them. we can't convince anyone of our concern as long as the maternity directive continues to travel between institutions. we can't convince anyone, while one in three women has been the victim of violence, and we continue to argue about which year to dedicate to fighting violence. we can't convince anyone while women are getting much lower wages, much lower pensions, much fewer in the workforce. no strategy will work as long as we close our eyes to the fact that more and more women are working in the black economy, without contracts and basic protection. no one will believe us if we talk about sovereignty and subsidiarity, when in fact we deny women rights. today, efforts are focused on rescue operations for thousands of refugees at the european union's external borders. but there have been reports of the exploitation of thousands of refugee women who work for meager wages to save their families. there have also been reports of secondary trafficking of women and girls already on european territory. if we don't make adequate decisions today, tomorrow will be too late. the feminization of poverty is the definitive history of the work of both the european institutions and the member states. and that is why it is important to support the report on noah's ark as a good starting point for bold decisions.
thank you, madam president, the latest proposal of the european commission for the distribution of illegal migrants in other eu member states is scandalous and it directly affects and threatens the interests of bulgaria. bulgaria has accepted and granted status to 2.5 times more illegal migrants than italy and 2.7 times more than greece in the past year, and the current new proposal to redistribute new migrants is unfair, unsolidarist and contrary to the national interest. the state can't afford to take in any more illegal immigrants. the treaty on european union, article 73, should also be invoked in this case and this burden should be borne in solidarity between the member states, because to do otherwise would be unfair and would mean a vicious and non-solidarity solution to this question. i urge the european commission to reconsider its proposal and declare bulgaria a state of emergency.
thank you, mr president, intellectual property has never been more crucial than it has been in recent years, not only in terms of innovation and technological progress, but also in terms of the overall increase in digital awareness among consumers. however, there are structural obstacles in the mechanisms for the implementation and enforcement of intellectual property regimes. member states face different challenges resulting from a variety of factors, especially in countries with weak intellectual property regimes, which have fallen victim to illicit trade in goods and services due to a lack of means to combat the spread of infringements. in the european union, we have to keep in mind that a strong legal framework for intellectual property is very important. to be able to function properly, it needs a strong and coherent toolbox in each of the member states. my country is among the worst affected. the proximity to major eastern markets is a prerequisite for a large influx of counterfeit goods. the analyses suggest that we should actively seek to support ip mechanisms through targeted coordinated surveillance projects and as a basis for good practice.
thank you, mr president, today, during the debate on mr landsbergis' report, the question of the european union's relations with russia has been raised again. the report clearly reflects our deeply damaged relationship and calls into question the basic principles of security in europe. however, we must ask ourselves: 'can the european union envisage a return to the previous relationship with russia?' and 'what action plan should the union put forward to return to the path of dialogue?' blacklisting european politicians and members of this parliament does not help to achieve this progress. moreover, it predisposes the conflict in ukraine to becoming frozen, and our experience with georgia shows that this is a serious obstacle to the development and stability of the countries concerned and to their rapprochement with the european union. the use of energy as a major foreign policy tool is a prerequisite for the crisis to spread and the effects to be felt in other parts of the region. in this context, i support the rapid establishment of a strong european energy union.
thank you, mr chairman. "united we stand" is the motto of bulgaria and that is exactly what we need today more than ever in the european union. europe must remain united in the face of the challenges that lie ahead from the east and the south. russia, instead of being our natural partner, sees us as an adversary. unfortunately, the ruling administration in the kremlin has not only retained the soviet union mentality, but is also trying to forcibly re-establish the union under a new name. but this new soviet administration is missing an important detail, which is that such a union should be voluntary and attractive to its citizens. and the evidence is that democracy is attractive. many young people from all over the world want to immigrate to the western democracies and the united states. moreover, many of the families of the ruling russian administration, including the children of mr. putin and mr. lavrov, have chosen to live in the western world. the current model of the kremlin's governance is not attractive. at the same time, the european union cannot stand by and not respond to the aggressive propaganda machine. i would also like to call for the reinstatement of nabucco and support the report by mrs landsbergis.
in writing. the geopolitical situation in which we find ourselves requires eu member states to be united. but to keep the peace around us, that's not enough. we need partners. such a partner should be russia, whose recent actions point in a very different direction. the unjustified ban list of european diplomats denied access to russian territory was another step towards isolating the current kremlin rulers. the break was another avenue for dialogue. instead of pulling out of eastern ukraine, russia is challenging europe and risking a more serious confrontation. worst predictions have been confirmed that the goals of today's rulers in moscow are far from european values. failure to comply with the minsk agreements will not help to reduce or remove sanctions against russia.
in writing. ‒ the creation of a crisis fund to cover losses to farmers is a good idea, especially to respond to force majeure circumstances, as is now the case with export bans to russia. in bulgaria, this has affected a number of sectors, including livestock and fruit production. such a mechanism should exist. the concern is that the provision of the financial resource provides for a correction of the basic payments by the indicated percentage of 1,393041. this is a short-sighted decision because it actually reduces subsidies as a kind of guarantee for the payment of aid for a future uncertain event. in this sense, we can consider this mechanism as a kind of insurance or guarantee premium. however, the idea of the mechanism described is quite different and this resource should be separated from the additional payments and provided separately, rather than deducted from the subsidies to producers. there are more flexible options.
thank you, madam president, i supported the report on the european union strategy for equality between women and men post-2015 because i am convinced that the inclusion of women in decision-making processes at all levels is essential for the implementation of gender equality policy. although half of europe's population is made up of women, our policy does not reflect this fact. most meps 63.1% and 75% of the members of the national parliaments are men. as the representation of women depends to a large extent on the political parties in so far as they determine the lists of candidates for the respective elections, i call on the european parliament to take a joint initiative by appealing to national parties to help women acquire the necessary political skills and to include them in the electoral lists.
in writing. ‒ i voted against the report on the eu strategy for equality between women and men after 2015 because i believe that the foundation of a healthy society is the traditional family. i find it unacceptable that the commission should be called upon to enforce and change legislation in favour of the lgbt community, which is a minority. against the background of the demographic disaster in bulgaria and in europe in general, where the population is aging, i think that the main topic should be support for women who give birth, and not so much their so-called inclusion in the labor market. we need a strong, healthy family with a model of more than two children.
in writing. – i support this report because it is very important that we do not just protect the internal market against counterfeit goods. we must also ensure protection against illicit goods coming from third countries and the effect a weak intellectual property rights (ipr) protection system might have on the eu. it is crucial that we provide assistance to third states in terms of consultancy and training on improving enforcement measures and maintaining high standards. only a strong system with adequate protection measures will help us in the fight against illicit trade, child labour, financing of terrorist activities, etc. therefore, i welcome the proposals on the overall situation concerning ipr in third countries and the enforcement thereof.
in writing. – i greatly welcome the action plan initiative. it is essential to have a strong enforcement mechanism throughout the eu, and especially in countries dealing with combating counterfeit trade intensively on a daily basis whilst lacking the proper support and resources. bulgaria is one of those countries and we see that the consequences include not only damage to our and the european economy, but also consumer insecurity, child labour, issues of security, etc. therefore, the ec proposal is a valuable step forward to strengthening local resources and combating counterfeit goods and intellectual property rights (ipr) infringements more strategically and efficiently. after all, a legal framework is only effective if it is properly enforced.
rapporteur for the opinion of the imco committee. madam president, as rapporteur for the imco opinion on this subject, i have made clear my position on the need to overcome the heavy dependence on external energy suppliers and the high prices they impose in order to achieve a functioning internal energy market. one of the key elements for achieving these objectives is the improvement of energy infrastructure. therefore, as the bulgarian representative, i would like to draw your attention to the importance of integrating the south-east european region into the european gas market through the construction of a gas distribution centre in bulgaria and interconnections with its neighbouring countries. this will ensure natural gas flows from a variety of sources, including the mediterranean and the black sea. i believe that the implementation of such a project would be a real expression of the policy we are discussing today.
madam president, today’s debate is important on several levels. first of all, it is important politically, because it comes at a time when we are faced with various geopolitical challenges. secondly, it is important economically, because the european union’s prosperity and security hinges on a stable and abundant supply of energy. thirdly, it is important technically, because it requires the member states to successfully implement complex infrastructure projects. let me tell you the story of the country i know best, which has been active on all these levels in order to contribute to long-term energy security. bulgaria is still dependent on russian energy supplies but did not give in to recent pressures and instead took a step forward by working together with the commission and the member states in creating the high level group on central and south eastern europe gas connectivity (cesec). the aim of this group is to support cross-border diversification projects in the region, starting with a regional hub in bulgaria. this regional approach is the basis to developing key infrastructure better and faster, bringing success to the improvement in our energy security and, finally, achieving a diversification of suppliers. as i said before, and i would like to state it one more time, to me, european energy security means working together and staying connected – both politically, economically and technically.
in writing. the crisis in ukraine and the lack of a long-term solution for the region put in question the provision of reliable energy supplies. diversifying energy supplies is more important than ever, because the eu's prosperity and security require rational and highly efficient use of energy. the commission's natural gas stress tests have shown the vulnerability of the eu to dependence, and high energy costs are forcing industry to leave the eu, hampering europe's economic growth. in this context, i believe it is necessary to pay particular attention to the most vulnerable member states and to promote the exploitation of local conventional oil and gas fields in full compliance with the acquis of eu law. we need to focus on infrastructure development, the integration of sustainable energy sources and support for new projects. the creation of a single energy market requires above all political independence, and this can only be achieved with an affordable and sustainable energy supply.
in writing. more and more often, when we talk about energy security, we have to recall the precedents of the interruption of gas supplies from russia. as a result of the second such gas supply interruption in 2009, the losses to the bulgarian economy amounted to over eur 250 million. the crisis in ukraine and the european commission's stress tests have sent a clear signal that policies need to be updated to ensure europe's energy security. the european energy security strategy proposes a package of short- and long-term measures aimed at enhancing the eu's capacity to deal with a significant interruption of natural gas supplies during the winter season; strengthening emergency response and solidarity mechanisms, including coordination of risk assessments and emergency action plans, and protection of strategic infrastructure; reducing energy consumption; building a well-functioning and fully integrated internal market; diversifying external supplies and related infrastructure; improving the coordination of national energy policies and establishing common positions on external energy policy. i am convinced that only coordinated action will be able to counter the potential risks to energy security in the union.
in writing. energy security is a major challenge for the eu today in the context of the crisis in ukraine, which has created a new geopolitical fuel situation, especially for the countries of central and south-eastern europe. energy security therefore involves reducing dependence on russian gas imports to at least a manageable level. this can be achieved by improving energy efficiency, building a fully integrated internal energy market and its single regulation, decarbonising the energy mix, building on reliable and emission-free nuclear energy combined with renewable energy sources and increasing local energy production. the eu should support any sustainable local energy source that can contribute to our energy security. a key prerequisite for achieving this objective is the strengthening of regional cooperation between member states, including energy community countries, with a view to better coordinating national energy policies, and full convergence in shaping pan-european positions in energy negotiations with third countries. i strongly disagree with the drastic changes by some political groups to ban fossil fuels and nuclear power and to favor only energy efficiency and renewable energy. we can only prevent parliament from taking a decision on one of the most important european priorities for the union's energy security.
on behalf of the alde group. thank you, madam chairman. the outline of clear project objectives and the proportionality of the proposed measures to the need for a legislative solution remain among the most important issues in the 'smart borders' package. there are many ambiguities with regard to the legislative proposals currently in existence. it was pointed out that their objectives included dealing with the increasing flow of passengers, collecting statistics, tracking illegal residents and providing a higher level of security for european citizens. however, at the same time, law enforcement authorities in the member states continue to lack full cooperation and exchange of information to prevent and combat international crime. furthermore, the technical examination of the legislative proposals indicated that facial recognition as a stand-alone geometric recognition for the identification of passengers is also considered as a possibility. it is also the subject of an analysis during the trial phase which is currently underway. it is true that additional methods such as fingerprinting provide greater accuracy, but the large number of fingerprints slows down the process. longer storage leads to additional costs, and too short storage does not facilitate the management of passenger traffic. the question arises as to whether it is justified in the light of the additional resources that member states will have to invest and in the light of the inviolability of the person. secondly, as shadow rapporteur on the report on the amendments to the schengen code, i thank you, mr president, the current situation of increased terrorist threat to europe and of illegal migration pressure at the external borders requires clear and concrete measures to guarantee european security while not violating fundamental human rights. the 'intelligent borders' proposal is one such measure. border guards and law enforcement agencies of the european union need to know who, when and how crosses european borders. it is the only way to reduce illegal migration, to fight human trafficking, to prevent terrorist attacks in european cities. at the same time, the european economy will be supported by facilitating the travel of third-country nationals who frequently come to the european union for business or tourism. i believe i will have the support of many of my colleagues in saying that the 'smart borders' package should not be implemented at any cost but should be backed by reasonable financial means. at a time when the eu must set an example of careful spending of european funds, it should not pay for an overpriced project. the security of personal data should also not be compromised, especially in the context of the judgment of the court of justice of the european union on the retention of personal data. i therefore call on the european commission to make a careful analysis of the most financially efficient 'smart borders' project, as well as the legal basis and compatibility of the package with existing european legislation on the protection of personal data. in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of the doha amendment to the kyoto protocol because i am convinced that only by the joint efforts of all parties will we not only be able to make progress in the fight against climate change, but also send a signal to the international community, and in particular to the united states, canada, russia and japan, which have not ratified the amendment. by voting in favour, the european parliament reaffirmed the commitments of the member states and iceland to reduce greenhouse gas emissions by 20% compared to 1990 levels by 2020. for our children to have a future, we must act now!
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of iceland's participation in the joint implementation of the member states' obligations under the kyoto protocol for the second period from 2013 to 2020, because, although iceland has formally withdrawn its application for eu membership, it has expressed its willingness to jointly implement our efforts to actively combat climate change. together, we agree to reduce greenhouse gas emissions by 20% below 1990 levels over the next five years. this will send a strong signal internationally to the other parties to the kyoto protocol and an example of how different countries can come together and collectively fulfil their commitments to combat climate change.
in writing. ‒ this week, the european parliament made an unconditional commitment to take ambitious action on climate change. by adopting the doha amendment to the kyoto protocol, the european union and iceland firmly state their commitment to reduce their emissions by an average of 20% below 1990 levels for the period 2013 to 2020. i am convinced that global climate change presents an unprecedented challenge to the international community, which must be met by responsible transnational action. i therefore believe that the european union's request to achieve further emission reductions by 2020, subject to a similar commitment by other developed countries, is a very important call and a guarantee for concrete action. i believe that with this decision the european parliament is once again demonstrating not only the european union's willingness to tackle global climate change, but also a call for global leadership and partnership in this area. and most importantly, the european union is thereby setting a high bar for the negotiations at the un climate change conference in paris at the end of 2015, where world leaders will discuss their commitments to keep global warming below 2 degrees.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of croatia's accession to the convention on mutual assistance in criminal matters between the member states of the eu. i am firmly convinced that cooperation between eu member states in criminal matters is of the utmost importance. the free movement of people within the eu is a fundamental right, but it sometimes allows criminals to escape to another member state. it is therefore important that croatia, as a new eu member, is also part of the mutual assistance convention, so that in such situations there is quick communication between law enforcement agencies in different countries and the reaction is timely.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of the report on the accession of croatia to the convention against corruption, in which officials of the european communities or officials of eu member states participate. although this report is largely technical, by voting 'yes' i would like to express my support for equality before the law of all citizens of all eu member states. croatia is ranked 61st out of 174 countries in the transparency without borders corruption perceptions index. obviously, this is a big problem for the newest eu member. in this regard, whether it is an official or another ordinary citizen, the law regarding corruption should apply to all. i hope that by supporting this report, we will send a clear message to both croatia and bulgaria and the other eu members about how important it is to fight corruption not only at the highest levels of society, but in general.
in writing. ‒ accession to the convention on the fight against corruption, in which officials of the european communities or officials of eu member states participate, is important not only for croatia but also for the eu as a whole, as it concerns a matter of common interest improving judicial cooperation in the fight against corruption. corruption is a problem that affects long-term economic growth, sustainable development and equality in the union, and ultimately the interests of european taxpayers. given the importance of the convention as a common eu tool to tackle the problem, i am pleased that the report on croatia's accession to the convention was voted with such a convincing majority.
mr president, i voted 'abstention' on the report on the state of relations between the european union and russia because i consider that the report is incomplete and does not reflect all the issues and aspects of the issue. for example, we are witnessing russia's aggressive policy in the balkans. an example of this is the relationship with serbia and macedonia and their close relationship with the kremlin. serbia has systematically violated the decisions of the union, failing to comply with them in solidarity, as a state with the status of a negotiating party should do. as regards the republic of macedonia, this report does not contain any comment on the statements of the russian foreign minister. the report also omits the topic of energy and energy security and does not analyse the project 'russian stream'. in my view, this project must be given a clear assessment, and it must be borne in mind that the rapprochement between ankara and moscow is a serious threat to the union's foreign policy interests, and in particular to those of my country. the positive aspect of the report is that it touches on the conflict in ukraine, the barring of opposition parties from participating in the upcoming elections, the persecution of political opponents and the restriction of media freedom.
in writing. ‒ in supporting the report, i would like to remind you that the restrictive measures imposed are not against the russian people, but against the undemocratic model of governance. in this context, i believe it is necessary for the commission and the council to develop appropriate strategic communication tools and to work towards promoting a genuine and independent civil society in russia. we need to encourage cooperation between students and researchers to improve mutual understanding. last but not least, i call on the commission and the council to take immediate action against the externally funded european populist, fascist and xenophobic parties which aim to undermine democratic values and the rule of law in the eu.
in writing. ‒ the european union has always regarded the russian federation as a strategic partner and has sought constructive dialogue in its relations with it over the years. but we cannot consider a partner state that violates democratic principles, international law, freedom of speech, that calls into question the basic principles of security in europe and that attacks a sovereign and independent state. the harshness of the sanctions, as well as the eu's efforts to strengthen the independence of its gas supplies from russia, are a clear sign that europe is decisively changing the direction of its relations with the russian federation. as a representative of bulgaria, where russian propaganda and attempts at political and economic influence are so strong, i strongly support the sharp tone and the hardening of the eu's position towards the kremlin. with its actions in recent months, with its refusal to comply with the minsk agreements, with the use of jet fighters close to the airspace of nato and eu member states, russia has closed the door to a partnership with europe. i supported the resolution on the state of eu-russia relations because it provides clear evidence that we will not compromise on our principles and values.
in writing. i did not support the resolution on eu-russia relations because i find calls for the extension of sanctions by 'further extension of retaliatory measures' unacceptable. this 'war of sanctions' is only deepening the crisis and exacerbating relations between the union and the russian federation. europeans suffering from these circumstances are hundreds of times more numerous than those who are defined as having been affected by the crisis. it's not the european politicians, but the thousands of farmers all over europe, and the tourism industry in countries with a tradition in this respect with russia. it is almost a year since the russian federation's counter-embargo, but there is still no clear eu commitment to european farmers. in a situation of economic crisis in almost all of europe, it is unacceptable to expect these farmers to look for and find alternatives to this traditional market on their own. the illiberal politics of self-regulating markets and bold measures continue to shake the eu and make things worse, and create hardship for thousands of europeans.
i voted against the report on the european energy security strategy because it says that the commission should have the right to intervene as a negotiating party in the conclusion of all energy contracts by national states. bulgaria has twice suffered economic consequences and losses from purely political decisions such as the important and strategic projects related to the belene npp and the south stream project. the report also calls into question the safety and use of nuclear energy. it has been proven that this energy is the cheapest ever invented and its price increase is nothing more than an artificially created phenomenon. i should note here that after the unclear closure of units one, two, three and four of the kozloduy npp, bulgaria is left with only two working reactors, which, if not renewed, will be endangered as an energy center in the balkans and we will become an exporter of electricity to an importer from a non-european country turkey. i believe that this is contrary to the energy security of the union and i oppose this report.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of the resolution on the 2014 annual report of the supervisory board of the european anti-fraud office. olaf has been heavily criticised for its poor cooperation with its supervisory board, which has failed to monitor olaf's independence and to perform its functions properly, due to the refusal to grant access to the necessary documents. i believe that as the european anti-fraud office, olaf should be monitored, but also that it should be given the freedom to carry out its duties.
in writing. ‒ i abstained on the report on the situation in hungary, as i consider it incomplete and does not reflect prime minister orbán's position sufficiently well. the report calls on the commission to activate the first phase of the eu rule of law framework and to submit a legislative proposal to establish an eu mechanism on democracy, the rule of law and fundamental rights. i think that is not necessary and that the rule of law should be upheld by national governments without pressure from various euro-bureaucrats. i support the view expressed on 1 may 2015 by prime minister orban that the decision to re-introduce the death penalty should be the exclusive competence of the member states. i believe that it is up to the member states to define their own rules at national level.
in writing. ‒ i support the resolution because the european union is founded on the values of respect for human dignity, freedom, equality, the rule of law and human rights. these values are universally valid for each member state and any attempt to undermine them calls into question the democratic principles on which the union is founded. the actions of the hungarian government in recent months should serve as a red light that freedom and democracy are being fought for every day. in this context, i call on the commission to carry out a thorough monitoring of the state of democracy, the rule of law and fundamental rights in hungary. in conclusion, within the eu, it is necessary to create a special mechanism for the respect of democracy and human rights.
i did not support the resolution for the following reasons. first of all, i believe that the republic of turkey is not a european state, neither politically, nor culturally, nor historically, nor civilisationally. i do not think that the political leadership in turkey has any intention of becoming part of the european union and the european cultural and political tradition. secondly, i have not seen any texts that condemn turkey for refusing to participate in the operations against the islamic state, but on the contrary supporting the islamic state. i didn't see any texts that accused turkey of buying oil from isis for nothing. i didn't see any texts that raised the issue of turkish stream. another thing turkey benefits from import and export relief but does not comply with the union's policy on sanctions. this country only benefits from its european partnership, but never fully meets its obligations. these privileges must be terminated. turkey is also helping the wave of illegal migrants to bulgaria and greece. this cannot happen without the official ankara's consent. turkish companies and drivers are dumping our companies on social. that's not in the resolution, either. that is why i cannot support such a text. i think we are being soft on this neighbor of ours who is demonstrating no respect for our interests and who, in my opinion, has no place in the european union.
in writing. ‒ i voted for the report because i believe that turkey is the european union's most important regional partner and we should support them on their european path. eu-turkey cooperation in foreign policy and counter-terrorism is a shining example of the common values we share, and the high turnout in the recent elections underlines the country's level of democracy. however, the inclusion of the armenian genocide in the report has had a destructive effect on our relations, as the purposive use of historical facts has led to modern political conflicts.
in writing. ‒ one of the key issues for the future of european integration is that of turkey's accession to the european union. i supported the european parliament's resolution on the commission's 2014 progress report on turkey, which clearly articulated many of the important elements of this issue. the accession negotiations with turkey are a 'long process with uncertain outcome, based on fair and rigorous demands and a commitment to reform'. the copenhagen criteria are clear, as is the obligation to maintain good neighbourly relations and to ensure the successful democratization of the country. in this context, i welcome the active civil society in turkey and the results of the recent parliamentary elections. this election was about a very important process that could lead to the possibility of a heavy constitutional change and in practice turn turkey into a presidential republic with a strong authoritarian element. the turkish voters have not allowed the formation of an all-powerful majority and now turkey will have to be governed by a coalition. turkey is a strategic partner of the european union. i hope that the country will not 'tired' in the negotiation process (the start of formal negotiations was given on 3 october 2005) and will bring to a conclusion the necessary reforms to develop the full potential of our relations.
mr president, in april 2007 the european commission launched its cooperation initiative in the black sea region through the black sea interaction project. the region is a growing market with great potential for development and an important hub for energy and transport flows. the black sea is a key euro-atlantic component of strategic importance for the european union and its member states, in particular when it comes to ensuring their security. the document focuses on economic and trade cooperation, while cooperation in the field of security and defence is not mentioned enough. today, the current format seems to be outdated. the geopolitical balance of the black sea basin has been upset and the existing conflicts in the region have been escalating since the annexation of crimea. in this context, i think it is timely for the european external action service and mrs mogherini to revise the european maritime security strategy and in particular the european union's black sea strategy. the new european union strategy must be developed in full coordination with our nato partners.
blue-card question.– you mention bulgaria, the baltic states and romania joining the european union and nato. those were legitimate decisions on the part of these countries. so you are against the legitimate decisions of the authorities of these countries to join the union, which is where they want to be? or how am i to understand your statement?
mr president, it is clear that the sovereignty of national borders and territorial integrity must be respected and that the annexation of crimea should be condemned as a violation of international law. but the report is not just about relations between the european union and russia. he was the president of the council of ministers of the republic of crimea from 1 january 2014 to 31 december 2014. this report omits something very important namely the policy of the republic of turkey and the events that may result from it. while we are so fairly condemning the policies of president putin, we must not forget that the policies of his colleague president erdogan are absolutely the same and are two sides of the same coin. when we talk about insecurity in the black sea basin, we should also be talking about the behavior of the turkish state, its support for the islamic state, the possible new wave of refugees that could be targeted and assisted by the republic of turkey. otherwise, denying and condemning only one act of annexation and aggression would be a double standard. i therefore also turn to my fellow rapporteur: when we talk about the black sea basin, we must also take account of the anti-european policy of the republic of turkey.
mr president, clearly the black sea has turned into a focal point of confrontation, involving strategic national security interests of the highest importance for the countries of the region. in order to overcome this situation, we are offered two approaches: armaments and oneupmanship, which the rapporteur seems to be proposing. this is a road to nowhere. what is needed is to take seriously each side’s security concerns and to examine ways of diplomatically overcoming those concerns. i strongly support urging the commission to become involved in a new black sea strategy building on cooperation.

madam president, the republic of bulgaria is the oldest sovereign state that has an outlet to the black sea. the bulgarians have been linked to this region historically and economically for centuries. it is in the interest of bulgaria and the european union that the black sea basin be declared a zone free from military presence and military actions, in which provocations are not carried out, and which is not the object of speculation by parties to a military conflict. i am firmly convinced that the european union must play the role of guarantor of peace in the black sea, stimulate social and economic development in the border areas and promote their convergence. any military presence would have a detrimental effect on tourism, fishing and shipping. i urge you to do what you can to limit militarism at the expense of active diplomacy. the annexation of crimea was a gross violation of international law, but the nabucco project would be a serious and good response to russian aggression. i call for active dialogue without military arguments.
i support the resolution on the recent revelations of high-level corruption at fifa, because i believe that this international scandal, which is linked to one of the most popular sports in europe and the world, will have a damaging effect on the reputation of football. fifa has been the subject of accusations of corruption for years. but the arrest of 14 officials on charges of money laundering, racketeering and bribery this time went beyond football and into the political spectrum, even beyond it. this is about people's lives and health. over 1,200 people have died building qatar's stadiums, a fact that fifa continues to turn a blind eye to. in brazil, there were a number of protests against the hosting of the world cup, and yet we are talking about a country where football is a religion. it is vital that an independent body is set up to monitor compliance with ethical standards. fifa must regain the trust of the people, and this is through the introduction of the mandate of the members of the executive committee and the transparency of the salaries of the senior management. first, i call on the commission, together with the member states, to make the best use of the framework set out in the european union work plan for sport, focusing on actions and concrete measures directly affecting good governance. secondly, i would like to draw your attention to the fact that to date only nine member states have signed the council of europe convention on the manipulation of sports competitions. the worst part is that the corruption scandal will inevitably affect not only professional football organizations, but also amateur clubs. it is professional and amateur sport that is an important vehicle for the message of peace and respect for human rights worldwide. preserving their integrity is vital.
mr. president, azerbaijan is a country with a rich history and a distinctive national culture. for many years it was part of the soviet union, and today it is seeking its own identity and its own political presence. azerbaijan's ambitions to play a serious and visible role in europe are confirmed not only by baku's aspiration to partner with the european union and even deepen integration processes, but are also illustrated by its bright bid to host the first european summer olympic games. for this initiative, azerbaijan has invested 10 billion dollars, over 10,000 volunteers have joined the organization. of course, europe has its principles, rules and criteria and we should not close our eyes to any deviation from the principles of democracy, the rule of law and respect for human rights. but this should not make us irreconcilable opponents, and we should not treat this situation with any condemnation or as a last resort, mr president, but rather as a partnership to solve democratic problems.
mr president, let us look at the question posed: sport and politics. the azerbaijani authorities are being questioned precisely because they want to gain political credit – but is it also right to invite what is really a propaganda attack against the azerbaijani government on the subject of human rights precisely at the time of these games? these are olympic games. historically, when olympic games took place, hostilities were ceased. it was an occasion to try to come together, and i venture to say that if there were not this preparation for such an onslaught, there would not have been this harsh regime, which we cannot accept, of course. but we have to question what brought it about in this particular case.
