mr president, first of all, very warm congratulations to our two vice-presidents today, mr katainen and ms georgieva, for their strong leadership and commitment that led us through this process – and that is why we are here today – as well as to my colleagues mr karas and mr fernandes and many others, who worked tirelessly to match this very tight schedule. on very key and important projects such as this, citizens expect us to deliver, and this is what we did in the most efficient way possible. but if we only worked in such a swift and efficient way on other projects such as the digital single market or the energy union, europe would probably blossom not only through springtime but throughout the entire year. yet, as commissioner georgieva mentioned earlier, the tough part of the job is ahead of us now, so we need to get efsi to function in an efficient and cohesive manner. what i would like to see – and what we need to help with – is business ideas becoming quality operational projects with high returns, by providing the necessary assistance. this means that technical and advisory support should be readily available to get projects off the ground, especially in states with less experience and expertise. this is why i very much welcome – and i think it is a very good thing – to have the european investment advisory hub. but charging business for the use of its services might create a barrier for those in greater need of assistance. with the political will and the right incentives for projects to be realised, this investment plan can be … (the president cut off the speaker)
in writing. ladies and gentlemen, the proposal to create a european fund for strategic investments (efsi) comes at a crucial moment when the eu is struggling to emerge from the economic crisis, which has resulted in a 15% drop in investment in the union. i was very concerned by the commission's initial proposal, as it said that the approval of projects financed by this fund would depend entirely on their economic return and support for economic growth without any geographical distribution. my concerns were that with this type of criteria, efsi-funded projects would concentrate in the better developed regions of the eu, further widening the gap with the less developed regions, such as those in bulgaria. the commission has also been asked to provide a report on the implementation of the efsi, which is a key instrument for the implementation of the european union's external policy. the ep's proposals radically change the commission's original text, namely that they give a chance to less developed regions, such as those in bulgaria, to apply for efsi funding projects.
in writing. the economic crisis has left a deep impression on the confidence of citizens and businesses in europe's ability to create the right conditions for growth and jobs. we have passed many legislative measures in recent years and they are already producing positive results, but the psychological impact of the crisis continues to hamper europe's return to a sustainable economic growth path. with the adoption of the juncker plan today, we are sending a strong signal that the union's institutions are committed to the problems of its citizens and are working to solve them. as politicians, we have a responsibility to create the right environment and to ensure that vital investments are directed towards those projects in the member states that will improve the living standards of citizens and create a better future for young people. i believe that the allocation of the investments to be mobilised through the european fund for strategic investments should be geographically balanced and should not lead to a deepening of regional disparities. at the moment, it is also of enormous geopolitical importance, given the instability and security challenges in the eastern part of the continent. the fund also provides an additional guarantee to counter attempts to divide member states through various external economic, energy, cultural projects and financial incentives from third countries.
in writing.– i see the european fund for strategic investments as a key instrument for risk finance for smes and a driver for the future competitiveness of the european economy. though i am satisfied with what we were able to achieve so far – namely: less to be taken from the existing programmes, cef and horizon 2020; for countries which are still progressing in the r&d area and finalising their modern infrastructure, eur 1 billion less cuts will give them an equal chance to prosper; the steering board would not be politically influenced, and member states and third parties as contributors in the guarantee fund will not intervene in the decisions; the principle of additionally has been supported, which means that similar projects may not be cofinanced with other eu funding opportunities; and technical assistance is provided – nevertheless i would like to stress that many projects have already been announced as approved for financing, before the final agreement being reached. i think this practice should be abandoned because it confuses the beneficiaries and it is not fair towards them. finally, i put my hopes that the efsi will give young entrepreneurs the courage to realise their brave and innovative ideas in the european and global markets.
in writing.– to achieve credibility of the stability and growth pact (sgp), it is essential to apply its rules across the member states in a transparent, fair and timely manner. admittedly, it is not advisable to resort to a one-size-fits-all approach across the eu when implementing the guidelines of the pact. however, the commission has to ensure that the balance between flexibility and the application of rules is maintained. exceptions must be granted only when based on evident facts and their unbiased interpretation, and political bargaining must be avoided at all costs. we cannot accept political statements as a proof of genuine reform. the economic and monetary union – especially in the light of the five presidents report – should thus avoid introducing further flexibility into the existing system, while focusing on successful implementation of the rules laid out in the stability and growth pact instead. furthermore, sufficient attention must be paid to the country-specific recommendations provided in the framework of the european semester. we need political will and clear guidelines in order to achieve the ambitious objectives we have set ourselves.
madam president, as we discuss the commission’s action plan for fairer corporate taxation in the european union, we can see that this is not some dry or technical matter. it is actually a very important day-to-day issue which we need to see from three different perspectives. these three different perspectives can be in terms of continuity, efficiency and competitiveness. speaking of continuity, the action plan on corporate taxation is part and parcel of a growth agenda which president juncker has been implementing with determination since november last year. it is no coincidence that taxation falls within plenary debates such as the european fund for strategic investments (efsi) and the stability and growth pact. looking at the bigger picture, it reminds us that taxation rules can be combined and complemented by other initiatives at eu level to bring the best possible results. importantly, initiatives such as the digital agenda and the use of new technologies can aid the transparent collection and control of tax contributions. just to give a quick example: in estonia, 95% of tax declarations are filled in electronically and the process takes about five minutes. it is so much easier for businesses and saves so much time which is not spent on queuing but actually on doing business. this brings me to efficiency, which requires clear uniform rules. as in most areas of taxation, it is best to keep things simple. the more exceptions and specific regulations there are, the more loopholes can be created. these loopholes can be exploited by the most skilled players and are often to the disadvantage of smes. this brings me to my last point: competitiveness. with continuity and efficiency, we can also support competitiveness for our companies, which will do business in a much more clearly-regulated environment. this is crucial for smes. europe needs highly efficient and productive enterprises that can withstand global competition.
dear colleagues, in recent days, tensions have increased in bulgarian society, caused by the boom in ethnic domestic crime. on the borders of the roma slums, clashes and confrontations are increasingly occurring, caused by the arrogant and aggressive behaviour of some of their inhabitants. bulgaria is on the brink of social and ethnic conflict. part of the fault for not removing these illegal structures in the neighborhoods is their protection by european institutions and colleagues who protect them and prevent the equal and equal application of the laws to all bulgarian citizens. this is a clear discrimination against the majority of bulgarian citizens, especially taxpayers, and it is an injustice that creates strong social tension. similar problems with the borders of illegal slums exist in france, belgium, the czech republic, slovakia and other european countries, which shows that the european approach and model of integration is flawed, wrong and ineffective. on the other hand, the authorities in paris, ghent and elsewhere are clearly unable to deal with the integration of the inhabitants of the illegal roma slums in the way they recommend, and then they try to return them back to bulgaria. i find this behavior hypocritical, un-european and dishonest. they are european citizens in the sense of the teu, they have the right to free movement and i would very much like to see successful examples of integration at european level. i urge a review of the european commission's policy on so-called integration, decades and other failed ventures, an end to the indiscriminate funding of theft foundations and ngos and a return to the model of personal responsibility, civic duty and equal respect for the law.
in writing. ‒ i supported the proposal to create a european fund for strategic investments (efsi) as the commission proposal was substantially amended in the interest of my country, bulgaria. i was very concerned by the commission's original text, as it said that the approval of projects financed by this fund would depend entirely on their economic return and support for economic growth without any geographical distribution. my concerns were that with this type of criteria, efsi-funded projects would concentrate in the better developed regions of the eu, further widening the gap with the less developed regions, such as those in bulgaria. the commission has also been asked to provide a report on the implementation of the efsi, which is a key instrument for the implementation of the european union's external policy.
in writing. i believe that the european fund for strategic investments should be used fairly and geographically balanced to reach as many european citizens as possible. by concentrating resources in already highly developed areas and lacking funding in poorer ones, the gaps not only between member states but also between their regions are widening. in this context, i strongly hope that: firstly, there will be a geographical balance in the allocation of projects; secondly, that funds will be allocated to rural, remote and disadvantaged areas; thirdly, that the funds will also be used to tackle high unemployment, especially in rural and remote areas where it is a very acute problem; fourthly, that priority will be given to projects that create long-term quality jobs; i believe that this fund should help to address worrying trends such as the depopulation and isolation that are affecting disadvantaged regions and areas, most of which are rural.
in writing.– the capital markets union (cmu) is a classic project meant for all 28 member states; an initiative aimed to overcome dividing lines and boost the growth of the real economy in europe. i welcome the commission’s efforts to build a true single market for capital, which would make it easier to channel available savings into the productive economy, enhance the funding sources for smes and break down barriers for investment across borders. growth has to be funded without building debt and by avoiding a focus on one type of creditor. currently, european companies rely on banks for three quarters of their overall debt financing, especially in states with less developed capital markets. capital markets activity in some member states needs to increase more than twice to achieve the average for the eu. this process can be triggered by finalising the cmu, which will enable more intensive flows of capital and will reduce discriminatory factors at play in access to financing, like the origin of the borrowers and the lenders or the business location. these developments will be especially beneficial for member states with less deep and diverse capital markets, and i call on member states to implement the recommended policies as swiftly as possible once the cmu strategy has been published.
in writing. ladies and gentlemen, the report on the prospects for the eu dairy industry is extremely important for countries like bulgaria, where the dairy industry provides many jobs, especially in the least developed regions of the country. i would like to express my gratitude to the rapporteur, mr nicholson, with whom i had the opportunity to discuss the problems of bulgarian milk producers and processors and the position of the bulgarian government on this dossier. i am pleased that, as a result of the discussions and negotiations on the dossier, the proposal has paid particular attention to the impact of the russian embargo and to helping the most affected countries, such as bulgaria. the second main point in the proposal is that the current quota decisions should be maintained in order to avoid further market instability. the abolition of quotas risks further concentrating milk production in favour of the largest dairy farms and to the detriment of the smallest, such as producers in bulgaria. i fully support mr nicholson's work and wish him success in the future negotiations.
in writing. ladies and gentlemen, the situation in the milk sector in a number of member states is worrying - the purchase prices of raw milk and cheese continue to fall. since january of this year, milk purchase prices in bulgaria have been at their lowest levels in five years. the commission has also received comments from the united kingdom and the united states on the application of the measures in force. i welcome the report, as it proposes a number of measures to strengthen the bargaining position of milk producers and their ability to withstand market crises (strengthening contractual relations, encouraging sectoral association, risk management tools). price volatility as a consequence of the abolition of quotas requires additional action to safeguard the livelihoods of the most vulnerable, namely small producers, producers in mountain and less-favoured areas. i therefore call for the funds from the surcharge to be returned to the sector, providing temporary emergency support for eu dairy farmers if the situation continues to deteriorate. the russian embargo has shown the disruption that the loss of a certain foreign market can cause. the challenge now is to optimise domestic consumption, which can be encouraged through various promotional measures, guarantee schemes, additional payments for the products concerned (e.g. cheese) and the school milk scheme.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, i rise to speak in the one-minute procedure to introduce the now publicised case of the bullet mosque in karlov, republic of bulgaria. under the pretext of "let's reveal the scientific truth", the local authorities of the municipality of karlovó are trying to oppose institutions, leading historians and the public. being aware that in the early stages of the excavation a muslim necropolis from the early 19th century was discovered, we insist that scientific digging should not be an excuse for desecrating these graves. the feverish excavation around a building, the subject of a fierce legal dispute, suggests that the procedure for granting permission to carry out the excavation has been completed. i speak from the rostrum of the european parliament to the bulgarian minister of culture. mr. minister, i ask that you conduct a due diligence on compliance with the procedure, with the results of which being made public. in addition, in the case of the discovery of artifacts related to the muslim religious and secular tradition, the muslim confession of the republic of bulgaria should be notified. in closing, i sincerely hope you will not give me further cause for comment on this and other similar cases. madam president, commissioner, first of all the agreement must be mutually beneficial for all, especially for small and mediumsized businesses. it represents a historic opportunity for us to set the high standards on the global stage. i think energy should be part of this agreement. the european union really needs to diversify its energy supply. unilateral visa restrictions for citizens of bulgaria, romania, poland, cyprus and croatia are discriminatory and should be abolished. many myths and false claims are being spread, even though we still don't have a text agreed upon between the two sides. it is necessary to dispel this speculation and ensure that environmental protection standards, the health of european citizens and the right of states to regulate in the public interest are not compromised. it is deplorable that this has been used to set populists, nationalists and communists against each other ideologically, and to lead to a complete distortion of the debate on the potential benefits and risks of the agreement. i am convinced that the european parliament will only support the future agreement if it is in the interests of the citizens and their voice is heard.
in writing. big goals in history require big steps and big vision. steps and vision have led to things like the european union. what we need is the next big thing in economics. the transatlantic trade and investment partnership (ttip) is this big thing, far more than just a trade deal. it's an agreement between the two strongest democracies in the world, between the two largest consumer markets and financial systems. the ctbt is the gateway to investment, jobs and opportunities for europe's small and medium-sized businesses. to get there, we need to dispel some myths and tell some truths. there is no room for backroom deals - member states will have the final say. there can be no compromise on online security - the protection of personal data is a fundamental right in the eu. only member states can decide whether their health systems will be public or private. the cipt is designed for small and medium-sized enterprises, which account for 99% of companies in europe. a new market will be opened up for young entrepreneurs, non-tariff barriers (bureaucracy) will be eased, e-commerce will be strengthened. this is no time for tpp populism. populism leads to situations like greece. it is time for sound economic decisions, for a vision and for the future of europe.
in writing. ‒ i supported the appointment of ms bettina michelle jacobsen as a member of the court of justice as a representative of the danish government, as her professional experience and competence are of an exceptionally high level.
in writing. ‒ i support the adoption of this report because it concerns merely a simple and straightforward codification of existing texts. in my opinion, streamlining the european legal framework is very important in light of our commitment to the rules of subsidiarity and proportionality and improved understanding for citizens.
in writing. ‒ there is nothing more normal than for budget surpluses to be spent in the accounting year concerned on urgent payments or other commitments or, if this is not possible or there is simply not enough time, to be carried over to the next budget period and used there. that is why i will support this report, especially when it mentions my country's name and that more funds will be mobilised for it under the solidarity fund. i won't go into detail here about why my country needs these funds, but i will ask everyone here: please spend this money wisely, properly and so that it can create more added value for the community.
in writing. ‒ the 'solidarity fund' is a mechanism to repair damage caused by force majeure, by force majeure. this is about disasters and accidents. the mechanism may not be very fair, as i have pointed out on more than one occasion. the mechanism may be benefiting the richer countries at the expense of us, the poorer ones. but when we talk about overcoming the damage of natural disasters, of accidents that affect the population of entire regions, we have to set an example of solidarity. that's why in situations like this, we should help each other first, and then argue about methods and approaches here.
in writing. ‒ today i voted in favour of mobilising the european union solidarity fund for bulgaria, romania and italy and providing assistance to these countries to deal with the consequences of last year's floods. bulgaria is one of the countries that has been hit particularly hard by the floods of the past year. the commission notes that the measures provided for in this decision are in accordance with the opinion of the standing committee on plants, animals, food and feed, the disaster has shown that in such cases member states are in urgent need of assistance. unfortunately, the procedure for mobilising the 'solidarity' fund can only be launched if an application is made by the national authorities within 12 weeks of the disaster. i believe that the mechanisms for granting the aid so necessary should be simplified and the procedures shortened. people can't and shouldn't wait more than a year for money to come to them. thank you for your attention.
in writing. ‒ although i have repeatedly pointed out the weaknesses of the methodology used to determine the assistance from the solidarity fund, it is a mechanism that shows genuine solidarity between member states. i can argue a lot about why some people get a certain amount and others a smaller amount, but that is a whole other debate. i'd like to take this opportunity to remind everyone that there is no such thing as natural disaster insurance. that is why we must spare no effort to help the affected regions, to help the people who live there. it is our duty and a sign of true solidarity. the other is a small grocery bill that i don't even want to comment on.
in writing. in the summer of 2014, a number of european countries were hit by floods. one of the countries most affected, along with romania and italy, was bulgaria. in my country, they have taken the lives of dozens of citizens, destroyed basic infrastructure and left many people homeless. i therefore welcome the release of eur 1.98 million from the eu solidarity fund for flood damage in the misia region. this financial assistance will undoubtedly be useful in repairing the damage caused to public and private infrastructure, including the affected farms. however, i believe that the granting of these funds is only a temporary solution to the problem. in order to avoid similar disasters in the future, it is necessary to focus on the need for preventive measures. i believe that the culture of prevention should be taught in schools. to this end, we need a clear commitment at institutional level and public engagement. the preparation of a flood risk assessment is essential if we are to avoid such tragedies in the future and be able to protect european citizens and their property.
in writing. ‒ i voted with 'abstention' on the report on achieving multimodal integrated ticketing in europe for a number of reasons. in theory, the idea of a single ticketing system in europe and the creation of an online platform for travel planning accessible to all for free sounds great, but de facto, unrealistic targets are set that would cost the livelihood of dozens of small carriers. the report sets out the idea that by 2020 every form of public transport will be equipped with an intelligent system that monitors the movement of the vehicle in real time. but you can imagine what a computer system like that would cost by our standards and what would happen to the small carriers that are already in the industry, not to mention those that are just starting to get in. we will find ourselves in a monopoly situation where the entire industry is held by a few large firms and the smaller players will not have the opportunity to be competitive because of the overwhelming demands of the multimodal integrated system in question. these were the main reasons why i abstained.
in writing. ‒ one of the challenges facing the european transport sector is to find an easier way to plan trips and combine different modes of transport. i support the ambitious aim of introducing the possibility of buying a single ticket valid for all modes of transport within the european union. the resolution adopted by parliament calls on the commission and the member states to support efforts to achieve this goal through a clear framework, minimum rules and timetable. the ease and convenience of purchasing tickets is key to achieving a single european transport area, will make it easier for consumers and will ultimately attract more passengers. in another week when everyone's attention is focused on solving the greek problem, i welcome good european ideas like this one. transport is a key sector of the economy, and many jobs depend on it. i am convinced that the european union will give us the right tools to tackle the challenges of the increasingly mobile society we live in today. many of us no longer remember that it was impossible to cross borders with only an id card. now that's a fact. i believe that the modernisation of the ticketing system is a good initiative, an example of how the european institutions can and do serve the citizens.
in writing. ‒ i voted for the report on draft amending budget no 1/2015 of the european fund for strategic investments (efsi) because i support the idea of creating an additional mechanism to promote investment in our country. the economic crisis has led to a sharp decline in investment across europe, which requires a common and coordinated effort at european level to achieve sustainable economic growth in europe. in the situation with countries such as greece, italy, spain and portugal, it is important to focus on fiscal responsibility. however, this is not enough and other mechanisms should be sought to stimulate those member states, including bulgaria, which follow a strict fiscal policy and have a low deficit. the investment plan for europe is at the heart of a long-term development strategy. bulgaria is participating with 100 million euros, but our country has control over what projects these funds go to, and can give priority to those of national importance. although we may not see the effects of this new strategy immediately, in the long run the idea is to increase private investment in our economy, and thereby more and better-paying jobs and a higher standard of living.
in writing. – i voted in favour of the european fund for strategic investments (efsi) report, because it is an important step towards long-term growth, job creation and tackling poverty. efsi, which is at the heart of the european investment plan, will enhance growth, particularly in the areas of research and development, innovation and education. the fund will be a key element in countries such as bulgaria that are less developed and with less expertise. in particular, the european investment advisory hub will help these countries develop their potential and allow them to achieve further economic growth. furthermore, it will take a vital role in our ambition to stimulate the small and medium-sized enterprises within the eu as it will finance projects with a higher risk profile that will lead to more private investments. i welcome the report imposing stronger reporting requirements and increased accountability to the ep.
in writing. - i agree with the report in principle. i have some remarks to make about the wording. there is no doubt that much more money should be allocated to migration policy. i agree that the southern coastal states are facing unprecedented pressure. i agree that this is a trend, not an isolated phenomenon. but i don't see a word of it coming from bulgaria, and we're also under unprecedented pressure. it's time to think about bulgaria. it's good to have additional funding. the situation is extraordinary. i would like to point out that there are no refugees from ukraine in this size, moreover bulgaria is ready to accept its compatriots from ukraine. i just want to stress that it's time to say honestly here how many more migrants from asia and africa we can take in. i think we've crossed the threshold of what's possible a long time ago. isn't it time we gave a clear sign that this can't go on?
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of an additional €69.6 million to deal with the migration pressure towards europe, because the crisis of recent years has shown that stronger measures are needed at european level to overcome this pressure. the funds released are not enough to meet the objectives set - increasing the capacity of naval operations triton and poseidon, which save lives on the high seas, and supporting member states through the asylum and migration fund and the internal security fund - but they are only the first step towards a rethinking of european migration policy. bulgaria will be entitled to receive part of the money provided to implement and comply with european legislation on the reception of refugees - to ensure sufficient capacity for their accommodation, the rapid processing of asylum applications, and their integration into society. the sad truth is that the conflicts in europe's neighbourhood will not be resolved anytime soon. so countries like bulgaria, the eu's external borders, must be prepared for new waves of refugees, which are already becoming a daily reality. the appropriations allocated will allow for this preparation to begin.
madam president, the measures proposed by the european commission in the new reality facing the dairy sector are extremely inadequate and far from meeting the challenges facing a large number of european milk processors. a few days ago, russia issued a decree extending the import ban for another year. this news was received with concern and disappointment by the bulgarian dairy farmers. disappointment because the intervention mechanisms proposed by the european commission last year proved inadequate to their needs. this is not due to a reluctance to participate in the schemes, but to the european commission's refusal to allow white and yellow cheeses into these mechanisms. in supporting this report, i call on the commission to put in place adequate mechanisms in the short term and to extend the list of products eligible for support. the free market in the sector has led to the free fall of more than one or two dairy processing industries in the european union. i strongly believe that the union must intervene with adequate emergency measures.
in writing. ‒ there is a big risk for dairy farmers in a small country like bulgaria after the abolition of milk quotas. it's a well-known fact that only in our yogurt is the bacterium lactobacillus bulgaricus, which makes it delicious, healthy and sought after around the world. i therefore hope that the eu will be able to use all the opportunities for economic expansion so that we do not lose such valuable products in the absence of milk quotas.
in writing. ‒ countries’ societies and economic structures, and the dynamism thereof, benefit from environments which allow interaction between the public and private sectors; public-private partnerships (ppps) are a long-term tool used in government policies at international, national, regional and local level, there is no internationally recognised definition and comprehensive regulatory framework for them; we need to stimulate decent job creation, competitiveness and productivity inside the eu and in third countries; both smes and larger companies alike can provide unique private-sector know-how, experience and good practices, as well as networks involving public authorities in non-eu countries, effectively helping to deliver on sustainable development policies.
madam president, i strongly support paragraph 18 of the report on fruit and vegetables and call in particular on the european commission to restore financial support for the investments of the newly created fruit and vegetable producer organisations. these organisations are a powerful mechanism in the hands of producers when negotiating with other actors in the supply chain, but they are still unpopular, especially in newly acceded member states, such as my country bulgaria. the developments over the past year in relation to the russian embargo have clearly shown that the sector is sensitive and the measures envisaged by the european commission have proved to be grossly inadequate. i therefore call on the commission to commit adequate resources and direct aid to producers, rather than leaving millions of farmers without prospects and livelihoods. i also call on the commission to launch information campaigns aimed at presenting the attractiveness of the sector to young people as a livelihood opportunity. the current worrying trends of ageing and depopulation of rural, mountain and remote regions are a pressing problem and the commission needs to put in place practical efforts and make concrete proposals to overcome this trend.

in writing. ‒ i supported the ep's motion for a resolution on the fruit and vegetables sector after the 2007 reform, as i believe that the association in the sector is the right step, which will provide producers with a better bargaining position with the trade chains and make them more competitive on the domestic and external markets, guaranteeing high quality of production. the extension of the russian embargo has contributed to the vulnerability of the sector. it is therefore of paramount importance to make producer organisations more attractive by reducing the administrative burden and creating additional incentives for farmers to associate. information campaigns on the benefits of association are needed, especially in countries where the degree of association is very low or almost non-existent, as is the case in some of the new member states, including bulgaria. the association in the sector will increase their strength in negotiating with the trade chains, which is a prerequisite for tackling unfair trade practices. producer organisations can give young farmers a strong start, especially in the first years of their activity when they are building their market position. last but not least, the association of producers will stimulate short supply chains in favour of the final consumer, who will be able to consume locally produced seasonal fruit and vegetables.

on behalf of the alde group.– mr president, first of all i would like to congratulate the rapporteur, josé manuel fernandes, on his comprehensive report because it represents a good basis for the trilogue. the general assessment of the presented budget for 2016 reveals that it abides by the previously-set priorities – employment, enterprise and entrepreneurship. while commitments are going down by 5.2%, real payments are calculated to increase by 1.6%. thus we expect to melt down the huge pile of backlogs and to reach a sustainable level by the end of 2016. in this regard, we appreciate the cooperation between the committee on budgets and commissioner georgieva in drafting the budget and in implementing the commission’s pledges at the beginning of this mandate. the budget supports the political priorities: the energy union; the digital single market; the european programme for education, training, sport and youth; increasing competitiveness; support for farmers; and, of course payments to efsi. the eu budget also responds to new developments in europe’s neighbourhood and beyond. while an increased budget will be available to tackle today’s immigration challenges, we should be very careful, because the increasing pressure of migration flows means that the level of the ceiling set in the mff could be outdated. at the same time, we should be aware that the above—mentioned increase in payment appropriations, taking into account inflation, reveals that there is almost no increase in real terms. this means that the overall weight of the eu budget remains more or less the same. furthermore, we must ask ourselves: is it appropriate to reduce, by 4%, the financial resources for economic, social and territorial cohesion when there is a growing division between regions and states within the union? generally, we support the main goals of the budget to boost innovation, create jobs, deal more effectively with migration and strengthen the global role of the european union. i believe that we will get the best positive result from the trilogue process as a whole.
mr. chairman, mrs. vice president, people outside this building are rarely interested in the budget sections, the interest rates, the percentages or the numbers in it. but what they are really interested in is the results. and i believe this is one of the first budgets to be based on results. today, we face four major challenges: the situation in greece, ukraine, north africa and the fragile economy. all four are duly reflected in this budget, for which i thank you, as well as mr fernandez's work. but there is something that i personally want to thank as a young person, actually the youngest in this parliament, and that is that erasmus+ has 30% more than last year, and horizon 2020 has 10 billion more than last year. i want us to continue in this direction and reflect the principle that we should be small for the small things and big for the big things. i wish we had more budgets like this. congratulations again.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, perhaps symbolically today, ten years after the london bombings, we are having this conversation in the european parliament building. and while security should be and is a priority of sovereign nation states, the european commission has its obligations under the european security agenda. without a doubt, the greatest threat facing the european peoples today is islamist terror. the lack of reliable protection at the southern borders threatens the security of citizens of the european union. to the south of us is a growing menace, which has already borne its poisonous fruit in madrid, london, paris, lyon, copenhagen and many other european cities. the islamic terrorism that is growing in the south of us, supported, among others, by the government of the republic of turkey, threatens not only security, it threatens the identity, self-consciousness, culture and security of all european peoples. the european programme should be directed towards strengthening the southern borders of the european union. it is necessary to build and complete a protective structure on the bulgarian-turkish border. the trafficking of migrants, including islamic state fighters, across the black sea, which is inevitably being created, must be prevented in its infancy. the exchange of intelligence and cooperation within the european union needs to be strengthened in order to prevent the movement of terrorists within the borders of this european union. i should like to remind you that the perpetrators of the terrorist attacks in paris, london and madrid were citizens of their own countries, that is, european citizens. it is time for the european commission not only to declare security a priority, not only to hold continuous meetings, but also to put the measures listed in practice.
madam president, the challenges we have faced over the past year have made it clear that the threats to the internal security of the european union have become more complex and unconventional. they are increasingly difficult to predict and their management requires a common integrated approach by all member states, with a clear division of tasks between the european and national levels. the main reasons for high crime are: the increase in inequality, poverty, racial and xenophobic hatred, and these cannot be solved by security measures. they need to be considered in a broader context, and this includes social and employment policy as well as education. but also taking measures to reintegrate the most disadvantaged parts of our societies. in conclusion, i welcome the european security agenda for 2015-2020 proposed by the commission and the priorities set out therein. but i also want to stress that we must be prepared to face new challenges in the near future.
in writing. the european security agenda, adopted at the end of april, is an important step in ensuring european security over the next few years. it responds to ever-changing threats and offers flexible measures and solutions for a more secure europe. at the same time, the success of the programme depends mainly on how member states implement the proposed measures and how cooperation with the european institutions and the relevant agencies proceeds. it is therefore essential that the existing information exchange tools such as the schengen information system and the criminal records exchange system are used to the full by the relevant services. it is important to strengthen the coordinating role of agencies such as europol, especially in the fight against terrorism and organised crime. the commission should also be able to provide the necessary information to the member states and the european parliament on the progress of the criminal investigation. in order to ensure the implementation of the security programme, i call on the european commission to draw up an action plan outlining what concrete steps will be taken when to implement the proposed measures. the european action plan should be complemented by plans from each member state, and synergies between actions at european and national level should be sought. it's the only way to make the strategy a reality.
in writing. according to eurostat data for may 2015, 23 348 million european citizens are unemployed. unemployment in the eu is close to 11%, with millions living on minimal financial resources. at the same time, there is a shortage of labour and skilled workers in a number of industries. i believe that against this background of the labour market, the european commission should develop a strategic vision and a forward-looking analysis of the needs of the european economy and labour market. we need to know what they will look like in the long term, so that we can focus our efforts on retraining and training specialists for precisely those sectors where they are really needed, and meet the demand and supply of the labour market, in order to improve overall economic growth and the living standards of european citizens.
in writing. ladies and gentlemen, development aid is intended to reduce poverty while promoting human rights, strengthening democracy, good governance and the rule of law. the union contributes almost 60% of global official aid, but reducing poverty and inequality can be achieved by mobilising additional funds. in order to achieve these priorities, we must not forget the private sector as a key actor and its potential to contribute to effective and long-term development. in this regard, i support the council conclusion of 12 december 2014 on the need to strengthen the role of the private sector in the implementation of the future sustainable development goals. in conclusion, development policy cannot achieve its objectives on its own, but must be integrated into a wider framework that combines both foreign and trade policy and other external policy instruments. only then will development finance produce a long-term and sustainable result.
in writing. a few days ago, the russian federation decided to extend the embargo on imports of agricultural products from the eu. much of european agriculture is still suffering from this without adequate help from the commission, but only with calls from it to find new markets for its products. there are still no concrete measures proposed to help european farmers as a result of the extended embargo and this is unacceptable. i call on the luxembourg presidency to pay urgent attention to this problem and, in close cooperation with parliament, to propose intervention measures, particularly for the stone fruit sector such as cherries, and for the dairy sector. last year, a number of dairy products known on the russian market as bulgarian white salami cheese and cheesecake failed to benefit from the measures proposed by the ec because they did not cover these types of products. european farmers must not be allowed to fall victim to geopolitical decisions without adequate support and compensation.
