mr. chairman, mr. commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, south of our borders, the arab world is facing unprecedented conflicts that have led to the rise of terrorism. from libya to syria, countries are literally disappearing. from paris to sousse, we are all victims of the brutality of terrorists. we are all targets - europeans, arabs, christians and muslims. terrorism is the greatest threat to our security and it must be neutralized. we must mobilize all available resources in the name of restoring stability and security to all conflict points. the challenges facing europe today force it to make an important choice, even a strategic one: to respond to the threat of terrorism. what we need is not self-closure and isolation, but more democracy and tolerance as the safeguards of a truly multicultural society.
madam president, honourable commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the 2014 annual report clearly outlines the many human rights violations. this has been largely a consequence of the conflicts we face. despite the enormous difficulties, the european union remains committed to promoting respect for human rights and international humanitarian law. i call on us to continue to be a major player in promoting freedom of expression and to firmly oppose the death penalty. we need to use the diplomatic tools and international partners available to improve the human rights situation in many of the countries with which the european union cooperates. we must continue to support civil society activists who over the past year have faced increasing pressure from the authorities in many of their countries. these human rights defenders play an important role in promoting democracy.
madam president, commissioner, the wave of migration to europe is a more serious challenge for us than even the current instability in greece. in our immediate neighbourhood there are millions of people who see a way out of violence and misery by storming the european union border. the most important question we have to answer is how we can respond to this challenge and contribute to political stability in the countries of the middle east and north africa. such an answer is not easy, but it is of great interest to us. unfortunately, i believe that air and possibly ground operations against the extremist group "islamic state" must be made. but change has to come from within. of course, i agree with the colleagues who said: a solution must be found to the palestinian question. european countries should also improve cooperation by gathering and exchanging information both among themselves and with some of the governments in the region. religious communities should be encouraged to cooperate with one another. a good example is the 800 christian volunteers who two days ago joined the shiite militia to take part in the liberation of the iraqi city of mosul from the islamic state. the primary responsibility in combating fundamentalism should be on muslim communities and especially on their distinction from the misuse of religion by terrorists. improved coordination between the security services of the member states and those in the region, e.g. turkey, iraq, lebanon, should stop the flow of funds to the islamic state from the sale of illicit energy sources; and internet service providers and social platforms should identify those who want to join the islamic state and extremist propaganda.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, the situation in the middle east and north africa is undoubtedly the most serious challenge to the security of the european nations. the collapse of a number of states in this region under the pressure of the organized arab uprisings poses at least two fatal threats to us. on the one hand, it's the huge wave of migration that's flooding the european continent, threatening to collapse the economies of european countries and forever change our continent demographically and culturally, religiously and otherwise, and in a direction that i don't agree with. on the other hand, the region's statelessness has become fertile ground for the development and rise of extremist salafi islamist organizations such as al shabaab, boko haram, al qaeda, islamic state and others. they all fight against us, against our traditions, customs, culture and values. they want to replace them with a caliphate. today, they're cutting off heads not just in iraq and syria, but in lyon, france. it's high time we took a responsible look at this threat. there is only one adequate response to this threat. it's not a statement and statements. he's a military man.
mr president, mr commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, as you know, the european neighbourhood policy, established in 2004, aims to prevent new dividing lines between the enlarged european union and its neighbouring countries. it is now the right time and place to congratulate the rapporteur, mr cocan, on his efforts and on the work he has done on this report. but unfortunately, we can say that's not what's happening right now. in the east and south of europe, there is a complete breakdown of the states, typical examples of this being libya, egypt, syria, iraq. there are new dividing lines that are being created where values are concerned. the christian values of the european nations, or european values, are being opposed by an increasingly aggressive and growing radical salafist islamism. and we cannot ignore the huge wave of migration that is sweeping greece, spain, italy and bulgaria. this wave of illegal immigrants will hamper economic prosperity and threaten the stability and security of member states. in the north-east we are seeing the same process: the state of ukraine is in a severe political, economic and humanitarian crisis. a new dividing line is being built on its territory, which takes us back to the cold war years. the same is true of georgia, armenia, moldova and the black sea basin in general. in this situation, i believe that the european union should rethink its neighbourhood policy. it is important that the union takes an active part and a role in the arab spring states and thus ensures both peace and prosperity for the member states of the european union and the neighbouring countries.
mr president, commissioner, i would first like to congratulate mr cocan on the good result of his work on the report. the enormous challenges facing both the east and the south force us to rethink our neighbourhood policy. a new policy must take account of the changing threats in the region. unfortunately, not only are frozen conflicts like karabakh and abkhazia not resolved, but new and hot conflicts are being created, like crimea and eastern ukraine. alongside conventional military conflicts and terrorism, we are witnessing hybrid, information and propaganda wars, external funding of anti-european extremist political parties. such factors exploit our inner weaknesses. the european union is made up of democratic states that don't invest in propaganda, but that have the right to respond with the truth. countries like russia are using the economy and energy as a tool for pressure and dependency, not for mutually beneficial cooperation, which is what it should be. for example, the proposed expansion of nord stream and the discussed turkish and greek stream are creating political division within the european union. the aim is to create a conflict between brussels and the member states, as well as between the member states themselves and between companies and governments. unity makes strength, not division, and divide and rule should be our primary goal. we must firmly defend the rights of neighbouring countries to independently choose regional and international partnerships, to strengthen cooperation with governmental organisations, with the non-governmental sector in this region. we need to use all the tools available to support civil society, such as the new european endowment for democracy. i support the idea of informal meetings of foreign ministers from the region and from the european union, but i also think it is good to have meetings of interior ministers as well as of the security services. (the president accepted to answer a question by raising a blue card (rule 162 (8) of the rules of procedure))
answering a question asked by raising a blue card. if i had the recipe, my dear colleague, i would tell you. you know, these are conflicts, some of them have been on the scene for decades, like in nagorno-karabakh. the minsk group is working unsatisfactorily, with no results, and we are trying to put forward our proposals to solve these problems. but they're not just not solved, they're increasing. i said that in addition to these conflicts, we have new ones, for example in crimea and eastern ukraine. there is no recipe. what we're trying to do is help, but it seems like we can't find the right way yet.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, the horrifying events of 20 years ago in srebrenica can be interpreted in many ways, but they should remind us of the most important thing: that the transition from totalitarianism to democratic civil societies in the balkans was marked by bloody ethnic conflicts. against this background, my country, the republic of bulgaria, has managed to carry out a bloodless transition, known in our country as the bulgarian ethnic model - one of the brightest stones on our path to membership in nato and the european union. the active role of the liberal party dps has refuted the expectations of many analysts that the first bloody ethnic war will happen in bulgaria because of the violent change of the names of bulgarian muslims. twenty years after the events in srebrenica and the tragedy of the bosnian muslims, it is clear what a difficult and painful path the balkans have travelled. the memory of the srebrenica tragedy reminds us that europe remains morally indebted to the western balkans. in two decades, the countries of this region have gone from being irreconcilable enemies to partners and allies in the euro-atlantic cause. they have the right to live in a true european multicultural union.
on behalf of the ecr group. madam president, honourable members of the commission, honourable members, in my capacity as shadow rapporteur, i would like to comment on the report by mrs reda on the harmonisation of certain aspects of copyright and related rights in the information society. i would like to thank the colleagues with whom we have worked long and hard to produce what i believe is a well-balanced report. this fragile balance, the result of long talks and contradictions on many issues, which we have managed to achieve, is the result of long and difficult negotiations, and i hope that in today's vote we will be able to maintain what has been achieved so far. it is important to highlight the existing sensitive issues and to send what i believe is the right message to the european commission. i am confident that the report will provide a good basis for the commission's forthcoming review of the existing legal framework on copyright and related rights, which is expected by the end of this year. we need to ensure that the revision and modernisation of the legislation in place today strikes the right balance between the interests of consumers in the member states of the european union, but also protects the rights and legitimate interests of creators and copyright holders.
the biggest challenge to copyright in the 21st century is to strike a balance between the interests of consumers, authors, distributors and copyright holders. changes must be made carefully and after extensive consultation. every step forward, even a small one, reflects entire technological eras. adequate intellectual property rights are one of the factors for the fastest and most seamless transition to the new digital economic model. a model that does not divide countries into small and large, and gives equal opportunity to large companies as well as small and start-up companies. one of the greatest riches we have in europe is the products of our artists, and we all know that talent is not born in a straight line proportion only in the largest countries, but also in the smaller ones. the new rules in the digital single market should therefore give a voice to composers, artists, directors and architects from newer member states, such as bulgaria. i believe that by voting today we will make a strong case for the introduction of working and balanced rules for copyright on the internet.
mr president, i did not support the tpt report for two main reasons: my country is one of the few to be discriminated against by the us in terms of visa policy. the text we voted on calls only for political pressure on the united states on this issue, but without a concrete and clear recommendation for a fair solution and the abolition of visas for all european citizens before the eventual entry into force of the ttip. disproportion in this area means the possibility of constraining economic interests by the united states against my country bulgaria. the mechanism for investor-state dispute settlement is the other aspect that i do not approve of in the final adopted text. my country bulgaria is one of the few member states in the european union with such a mechanism, signed more than twenty years ago. it's time this humiliating instrument was removed. until that is achieved, i will not support a report that does not defend that position. i'm not gonna support a deal with those clauses.
in writing. ‒ i supported the ttip report because: i want a solution that is in the interest of the citizens of the european union and that will strengthen our position globally. two, please. the new global economic challenges require us to support the idea of enhancing cooperation between the eu and the us through a trade agreement. our economies are the most open and democratic in the world, and we share many common values. 3.tip can serve as a model for high standards and for other fast-growing economies, but also to restore lost investor confidence. the number of patients is 4. in addition to its economic dimension, the agreement also has a strong geopolitical dimension. moreover, ttip has the potential to become the most significant project between europe and the us since the creation of nato to date. i think it's very important at this point to focus on the type of agreement.
in writing. ‒ with the adoption of the resolution on the ttip, the ep made its recommendations on the european commission's negotiating mandate, agreed by 14 of its committees. for us meps, the priority in the negotiations is to achieve a new dynamic in the european economy and to guarantee the opening of markets and access to capital for european businesses across the ocean, as well as the preservation of european standards in terms of competition, health, food quality, environmental and labour standards. the ep stands firmly behind the rights and interests of european citizens, as well as the right of states to define public policies at national and local level, to be protected and guaranteed. the european parliament has also expressed its support for the agreement, which is a step towards a more comprehensive and effective agreement on the eu's external borders. without a revision or even a repeal and replacement of the agreement with a new, much more transparent mechanism, the agreement cannot be adopted by the european parliament. i hope that this will be heard, because this resolution was supported by two-thirds of the colleagues who want this agreement to become part of the eu-us relationship. the revision of this mechanism is the only way to give this agreement a chance of achieving a historic common market.
in writing. – i voted in favour of the european parliament’s recommendations to the commission on the negotiations for ttip, which presents a more balanced account of the important advantages and opportunities offered by the agreement. the parliament’s recommendations reflect key arguments in support of ttip and its potential to unlock a billion-dollar market. this is particularly the case for countries like bulgaria, which, as part of the eu, will be able to play a greater role on the global trade market. for such countries, ttip also means enhanced mobility, recognition of professional qualifications on the foreign labour market and access to the us public procurement market, which is the largest in the world. we need to focus on these advantages and avoid equating the debate on ttip to a debate on the negative effects of globalisation. europe must be driver and a main beneficiary of these processes, instead of attempting to circumvent them, and ttip can aid us in this regard.
in writing. ‒ i supported the recommendation for the stabilisation and association agreement with the former yugoslav republic of macedonia because it is a purely technical solution. the reason for the need for such a vote was the accession of croatia to the eu and the need for certain amendments to the eu association documents of macedonia to reflect the change in the number of eu member states.
in writing. ‒ i welcome the accession of the republic of croatia to the protocol to the stabilisation and association agreement between the european communities and their member states and the republic of macedonia, but at the same time i hope that the european union will respond swiftly, effectively and authoritatively to the events in macedonia. i think it is timely, in the european year of development and in the context of the increasingly dynamic changes in the political environment globally, to review the european union's neighbourhood policy, including with regard to the western balkans. in our preparations for it, we have drawn on the general principles laid down in the union treaties, but we have also taken account of the current circumstances in the region. and we should always bear in mind that while enlargement policy and neighbourhood policy are separate policies with different objectives, they overlap to a large extent with those countries that have entered into membership negotiations, and this gives rise to the eu's increased responsibility towards them for maintaining the prospect of and aspiration for membership.
in writing. ‒ i supported the recommendation for the stabilisation and association agreement with serbia because it is a purely technical solution. the reason for the need for such a vote was the accession of croatia to the eu and the need for certain amendments to the eu association documents of serbia to reflect the change in the number of eu member states.
in writing. ‒ i supported the recommendation on the draft council decision on the renewal of the agreement for scientific and technological cooperation between the european community and the government of the republic of india, because the results of the existing cooperation show good results for all the parties involved. that's why i voted to renew the treaty with india. by february 2014, 258 grants had been signed under eu research programmes. one of the major and important partnerships is that between the republic of india and the 'euroatom' agency. other areas of partnership are computer technology, solar energy, water challenges and agriculture. the total budget provided by the eu and india is eur 60 million. with the ep's support for the renewal of the agreement with india, we are continuing to develop scientific and technological cooperation, which is of paramount importance in the 21st century. sharing experiences is a path to further progress.
in writing. ‒ i voted 'yes' on the recommendation for cooperation between the eu and the faroe islands because i support the allocation of funds for scientific and technical programmes in the belief that in the 21st century this is one of the most strategic areas of development and as such is of paramount importance for the future. it is also important to partner and exchange experience with specialists from other countries, with whom we can develop and work on joint projects. through the support of the ep, we support the association of the islands to the framework programme for research and innovation horizon 2020. it is the largest eu framework programme in this field ever, with a seven-year budget of almost €80 billion. the majority of eu research funding is provided on the basis of calls for proposals on a competitive basis. by giving access to the faroe islands programme, we are extending the cooperation and creating the potential for more projects. the strategic location of the islands is a prerequisite for research in the field of water.
in writing. ‒ the commission proposal aims to contribute to the long-term sustainability of companies, create an attractive environment for shareholders and enhance cross-border voting to contribute to growth, job creation and eu competitiveness. however, the report also contains problematic topics such as the mandatory reporting requirement for all large and listed companies in each eu member state in which they are established. that is why i voted with an abstention.
in writing. ‒ i voted against the report on the market stability reserve for the eu ets for a number of reasons. although i am a supporter of the idea of reducing greenhouse gases, this report puts forward concepts for the harmonisation of standards for all member states, without taking into account the specificities of some countries such as bulgaria. as a result of the adoption of this report, we may find ourselves in a situation where our country may become subject to sanctions because of the difficult eu requirements. unlike western european countries, bulgaria, as well as countries like romania, poland and the czech republic, have not had much experience in greenhouse gas trading. the commission notes that the commission has not provided any information on the amount of the financial contribution from the hfsf to the hfsf. however, in the draft put to the vote, countries such as the united kingdom proposed to change the year to 2019 as they already have a ready-to-use. however, with this change in the starting date, bulgaria and a number of other countries will not be able to meet the requirements in time to start the full operation of the reserve. that is why i voted against the report.
in writing. ‒ due to the lack of conformity of the final compromise text with the position of the country i represent, bulgaria, i voted against the change of the date of entry into force of the measure from 2021 to 2017. the date of entry into force of the ppa and the treatment of allowances in backlogging constitute the architecture of the structural change of the este. the commission notes that the measure is not intended to provide a guarantee to the beneficiary of the aid. in its current form, the measure may lead to possible shocks on the greenhouse gas emission allowance auction market, which may also have an impact on the price range.
in writing. ‒ i voted against the adoption of the agreed market stability reserve of the co2 emissions trading scheme before the current period of its operation has expired. the aim of the reform is to increase the price of emission allowances, and therefore directly increase the price of electricity from fossil fuels, from which most of the electricity comes. today, as we struggle to achieve growth and a more competitive economy, higher energy prices will undermine the efforts of economic recovery and the better position of european companies on the world market. i do not think it is justified to over-emphasise climate policies over growth policies when europe is neither the biggest emitter of co2 nor can climate targets be achieved without a global agreement and commitment from the biggest polluting countries. so i think that the european institutions are acting hastily with the reform of the emissions trading scheme, with their desire to generate financial resources from this trade earlier for investments in low-carbon technologies. although the agreement contains safeguards for the industry, it is unlikely that companies will be able to adapt to the new system without significant new costs and loss of competitiveness from the inevitable increase in the price of electricity.
in writing. ‒ in principle, i am always for when it comes to regulations and other documents that protect community producers from unfair competition, whether from companies inside or outside the community. i am particularly interested in the shipbuilding business, not only because bulgaria was a leader in it years ago, but also because europe is a major producer of ships, which is on the one hand an industry with enormous added value and on the other hand one that employs a very large number of workers. our main competitors, the united states and china, are far behind us in shipbuilding, you know, even european shipbuilders had to finish one of the great us cruise ships in the sea of america, and for that reason we have to protect our european manufacturers and help them in any way we can. that is why i support this regulation.
in writing. ‒ the enhanced participation of switzerland in important programmes such as horizon 2020 and other itre activities is a great step forward. switzerland is not only a key contributor to the european economy, it has also proven to be a valuable addition in terms of scientific and technological advances. cooperation will greatly add to improving innovation, skilled labour and other opportunities throughout member states
in writing. ‒ in principle i support the effort to improve employment guidelines by cutting red-tape and easing the burden on the private sector, often carried foremost by smaller- and medium-sized enterprises. nonetheless, i would rather see an improvement of current conditions such as streamlining wages, economic governance and the introduction of guaranteed minimum incomes. what we need are structural reforms that take into account the different realities encountered throughout the union. of special significance are barriers to growth and employment. however, these may not be overcome by over-regulation.
in writing. ‒ i voted against the request that finland has made on the mobilisation of the european globalisation adjustment fund despite the fact that i fully support the measures that put in place policies that create growth and jobs. however, i do not believe that the european globalisation adjustment fund is the right tool for putting in place such policies nor do we believe it is an efficient tool that offers value for money in terms of reintegrating redundant workers back into labour market.
in writing. ‒ in principle i welcome some of the changes introduced to the draft budget for 2016. nonetheless, there are still some considerable issues that remain to be addressed. it is, as the report rightfully reflects, important to recognize past under-budgeting and to note the importance of tackling this issue. however, we cannot simply recognise this fact and move on, so to speak. there are still fundamental structural challenges that require proper analysis and sound financial solutions. another concern is that we need to maintain a clear overview in terms of budgetary expenditure. it is a known issue that the distribution of funds is not always equal or financially justified, especially in the light of recent political activities in and around the union. thinking long-term strategy, in terms of budgetary efficiency, waste management and proper allocation of taxpayers’ money, it is crucial for maintaining a political equilibrium as well as ensuring economic longevity and productivity. without this, we will encounter serious difficulties.
in writing. in rural europe, the trends of depopulation and population ageing continue at an alarming rate. these areas have the potential to be a generator of jobs, of economic growth. i therefore believe that in 2016 the commission should allocate additional resources to programmes and initiatives that increase the attractiveness of rural and remote areas. i draw your attention to paragraph 4 of the opinion of the committee on agriculture and rural development of 28.5.2015, in which we call on the commission to provide additional resources to promote organic farming and the quality schemes for geographical indications and traditionally specific foods. these schemes increase the potential for economic growth of remote regions by emphasising the specific qualities of unique agricultural products and by enhancing the attractiveness and identity of these areas. i therefore strongly believe that the commission should pay particular attention to these schemes and support them even more actively in 2016.
in writing. ‒ i have voted against this initiative as i do not believe the solution lies in simply implementing new reforms and tackling challenges without a profound understanding of the implications these might hold for the labour market. i believe that we should focus primarily on thoroughly assessing the current challenges and potential opportunities and how these may be dealt with best. it is foremost a question of efficiently targeting the challenges the existing workforce is already experiencing and ensuring the correct matching of labour and opportunities.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of the report on tax avoidance and evasion as challenges in developing countries, as the report contains many recommendations to be taken into account at the forthcoming addis ababa conference on financing for development in july 2015. the report also discusses the tax systems of developing countries and the need to address existing problems such as corruption and non-transparent public administration.
in writing. – i voted for the adoption of this report as a stronger commitment by the eu member states to enhance transparency and cooperation would provide vital support for developing countries in the fight against tax dodging. this report urges the commission to support developing countries and regional tax administration frameworks, and urges that information on beneficial ownership of companies, trusts and other institutions be made publicly available in open-data formats. i believe that these are important steps and although this report is not a binding regulation, it is still very encouraging as it shows that it is possible to get cross-party support for tax justice issues.
in writing. ‒ in principle i welcome the resolution. however, there are still numerous insufficiencies. aside from the fact that i consider it to be of the utmost importance that we recognise the relationship between the efficient use of resources and minimizing waste, this report is still rather descriptive and prescriptive. i believe that we need concrete and realistic proposals, such as a focus on efficiency, innovation, and transparency, rather than new regulatory proposals and increased targets, for example. it is the case eu-wide that often the current situation is not properly assessed and an additional burden is imposed by creating higher burdens and challenges. if we want a proper functioning of the european internal market we must first of all understand what the actual context is and improve communication/coordination between the public and the private sectors, before adding additional and unnecessary red-tape, which is only detrimental to the well-being of our citizens.
in writing. – i voted in favour of this report as i fully agree it is crucial for the eu to start using resources more efficiently and reduce our resource dependency. saving natural resources will not only preserve raw materials for future generations but will create multiple benefits such as reducing waste and creating new innovative business opportunities. however, i call on the commission to take a more cautious stand when looking into creating a binding target to increase resource efficiency at eu level, since the principle of subsidiarity must be respected.
in writing. ‒ i voted against the report on the proposal for a council regulation on the establishment of maximum permitted levels of radioactive contamination of food and feed following a nuclear accident or other case of radiological contamination. the main reason for this was that the text was to some extent at odds with the original idea of euratom being a separate and independent organisation that controlled the sector.
in writing. ‒ i supported the motion for a resolution on the construction of a capital markets union. the european commission published a green paper, a call for public debate, on the construction of a capital markets union, which launched a long-term project to integrate capital markets in the european union. the aim is to mobilise funding for the european economy through the capital markets and to boost growth rates in the 28 eu member states. however, the european parliament believes that the commission should develop a better approach to increasing the diversification of sources of finance and investment in enterprises. the commission should also not rely solely on peer reviews with other jurisdictions. finally, by supporting this resolution, we are sending a signal to the commission that differences between individual member states must be taken into account in order to avoid negative consequences. these are just some of the recommendations the ep made to the commission, but they are among the main reasons why i agreed and supported the motion for a resolution.
in writing. ‒ supporting the integration of capital markets in europe is a logical step towards a pan-european financial policy, in line with commission directives and in view of recent developments in the union. due to the over-reliance of business on the banking sector, the eu needs integrated channels and diverse sources of business financing, secured by changes introduced by all member states. the launch of such a long-term project gives a clear signal of the european parliament's commitment to small and medium-sized businesses in europe and to a better investment climate in the eu in general. the unification of capital markets will help to promote small and medium-sized businesses by facilitating access to initial investment and job creation in key and underdeveloped sectors of the economy - a boost that europe needs. the advantage of such a structure would be to stimulate innovation as a highly competitive instrument in the international market and to develop highly specialised sectors of the real economy in europe. for me, the most important thing is that, without a doubt, the measures to introduce unified capital markets will have a favourable effect on foreign investment and the business climate in bulgaria. a pan-european capital markets union is certainly a necessary and right step in further economic integration of the continent.
in writing. – i welcome the commission’s efforts for the creation of a true single market for capital, which would make it easier to channel available savings into the productive economy, enhance the funding sources for smes and break down barriers for investment across borders. the building of a true single market for capital – a capital markets union for all 28 member states – will unlock investment in europe’s companies and infrastructure. a capital markets union will have beneficial effects especially for smaller member states like bulgaria, where businesses remain heavily reliant on banks for funding. i fully agree that the capital markets union will give more financing options first of all to small and medium-sized enterprises (smes) and strengthen investment for the long term. therefore i voted in favour of this motion.
in writing. ‒ by supporting the resolution on the situation in yemen, i express my deep concern about what is happening not only in yemen but in the whole region. the crisis in yemen is an additional threat to the security of the somali peninsula, the red sea region and the middle east as a whole. the first thing to be taken into account is the humanitarian aspect of what is happening in yemen. as a result of fighting between different military factions, as well as bombings, access to basic services for citizens has been disrupted. with the deepening humanitarian crisis, one of the most affected groups in this situation are children. unfortunately, the un's attempts to deal with the situation have been rather unsuccessful. although i support the eu's position on a political solution to the conflict, i remain sceptical that such a solution is possible. yemen's strategic position between africa, the middle east and asia makes it particularly attractive to terrorist groups linked to al-qaeda. i hope that by adopting the resolution on the situation in yemen, we will draw the attention of both the european and the international community to what is happening there and the dangers it poses to the whole world.
in writing. after peace talks in geneva ended without agreement on a ceasefire, yemen is plagued by growing tensions in an already unstable region. that is why i voted in favour of the resolution on the situation in yemen, which calls strongly on all parties to the conflict to put an immediate end to the use of violence. in the resolution we insist that the un-led negotiations be led by yemen in order to reach a political solution that is consensual and inclusive. we also recall the heavy toll paid by the yemeni civilian population, deprived of running water and basic and emergency health care, and we call for a humanitarian truce to allow the delivery, including by sea, of vital humanitarian aid. finally, we strongly condemn the recruitment of children, which, far from being a marginal or temporary phenomenon, is a widespread practice throughout the world and which requires awareness and concerted action.
in writing. i voted in favour of the peillon report because it provides a detailed analysis of the challenges to security and political stability in the countries of the region, and it places particular emphasis on the key to a lasting solution: a comprehensive strategy for building democratic states and for human rights. the deteriorating situation in the middle east and north africa region poses challenges not only for the countries of the region but also for the european union. the report has the merit of emphasising three key elements: freedom of religion and the rights of religious minorities, the importance of developing direct dialogue and cooperation with civil society and, finally, the central and strengthened role that the union should take for the mediterranean. finally, the report also focuses on the building of a shared area of security and stability, which also involves joint energy infrastructure projects and investments in job creation, particularly for young people.
in writing. i voted in favour of the kukan report because it proposes a strategic vision for the revision of the european neighbourhood policy. the enp is an instrument of the union with great potential, but which must now gain political strength and flexibility to meet the challenges facing the european union in its eastern neighbourhood, as well as in the mediterranean. the report has the merit of emphasising a long-term strategic vision, but also a capacity to better anticipate crises and manage emergencies. the european parliament's major contribution to the revision of the enp is arguably its emphasis on democracy and respect for human rights as a priority in differentiated relations with neighbouring countries. the report also gives a strong emphasis to regional integration as a tool for stabilising the neighbourhood, with particular reference to the role of the union for the mediterranean, which, as a forum for dialogue, should allow for a better appropriation of projects under the enp. finally, the visibility and comprehensibility of the enp in the eu and in neighbouring countries is identified as a key objective for the future.
in writing. ‒ i voted for the report because the european neighbourhood policy is of paramount importance to the eu and the riga summit showed that the 28 member states are able to take joint decisions. the eu's neighbourhood is constantly changing, with a growing number of old and emerging challenges, and this requires us to take action. it is now clear that ukraine, georgia and moldova want to engage even more closely with the eu and we need to deepen our cooperation with them in all areas. the report clearly underlines the need to seek a strategic partnership with the other eastern partnership countries and gives direction for a more active eu involvement in resolving frozen conflicts in neighbouring countries. on the other hand, events in the region over the past few years have shown that the european neighbourhood policy is not able to respond adequately and in a timely manner to rapidly changing circumstances. this requires a fair and efficient allocation of resources between the south east partnership. it requires a conceptual separation into two dimensions that can evolve in their own direction. only then will the objectives of the enp be achieved.
eva paunova (ppe),in writing. – as the epp group’s rapporteur for the harmonisation of certain aspects of copyright and related rights in the information society in the internal market and consumer protection committee, i am happy to see that the final text reflects the epp position and provides a more balanced account. this report does not include any compulsory legislative parameters, but is seen as a harbinger for the direction of future legislation. our aim is to strike a balance between the interests of service users, distributors and copyright holders. this would also enable a quick and seamless transition to the new digital economic model. a harmonized approach to copyright in all 28 member states will overcome divisions between large and small, central and peripheral countries, giving artists an equal chance to develop their ideas and promote their products.
in writing. the situation in burundi is extremely worrying and presents very serious human, regional and international risks. that is why i voted in favour of the joint resolution, which clearly sets out the priorities to be implemented in order to achieve a normalisation of the situation. the burundian stakeholders must return to the negotiating table as soon as possible to find a political solution through dialogue and put an end to the violence. nevertheless, a negotiated and consensual solution cannot be too far removed from the constitution and the spirit of the arusha accords. i welcome the clear and constructive positions and the mediation efforts of the eca and the au. i also support the call for the application of article 96 of the cotonou agreement, since the violations of democratic principles and human rights continue. finally, i would like to stress the regional issue of the burundi crisis. beyond the humanitarian situation of the refugee flows, it is crucial that a close dialogue between the countries of the region is maintained to avoid contagion in a politically and security-sensitive region, as the electoral processes are taking place in several of these countries.
mr president, shame on the so-called international community for allowing the 'islamic state' tumour to grow before our eyes and for its various metastases to lead to an unprecedented wave of migration towards europe's borders. these barbarians have lost their right to be part of the human community. the so-called "civilized world" has left syria and the surrounding countries between plague and cholera, between the assad regime and the islamic state. now in europe, we're trying to deal with the symptoms -- millions of refugees. we are reeling between european solidarity and national egoism. i call for immediate coordinated action by all parties, first of all the european union, the united states, russia, iran, the arab countries. this is no time for geopolitical games and propaganda with mutual accusations. we must help, we must restore statehood and security in this region, every delay leads to thousands more refugees coming to our sea and land borders. the time for talk is over. if the civilized world does not act now, we will face even more difficult times in the future.
madam chairman, i believe that the best pr this institution can do is the results it achieves. nothing speaks better than the results we achieve together here and i believe that what mr. sarwama is doing is exactly such a result because the 2007-2013 programming period has taught us something very important and that is that long procedures, many documents, long queues do not help us. if i could sum up my hundreds of meetings with beneficiaries of european funds in a few words, they would be: we need to dramatically reduce the time from filing to actually paying out the funds; we need to base risk controls on those who are doing well being checked less, those who are doing poorly being checked more. we need to get away from paper and use as many electronic services as possible so that people don't have to wait in line and can submit their documents from home with less waiting. i believe that with all this we can only optimise the management and control of european projects.
in writing. europe can become a unique ecosystem of innovative enterprises if we jointly implement successful models to support entrepreneurs in all member states. family businesses represent a long-term view of growth prospects and are a stable factor in job creation. they can play an important role in encouraging more citizens to be active participants in local economies. this is a result of the high degree of social responsibility that these enterprises have towards their employees. unfortunately, family businesses are not developing uniformly in all eu member states. this requires equal opportunities for entrepreneurs across europe. europe needs synchronised rules and policies that create certainty for businesses, encourage innovation and help start-ups to grow nationally and internationally.
madam president, i believe that the key words in this report are three: first, education, because business acumen is a quality that must be built from an early age. the number of young entrepreneurs and business leaders could be multiplied if the education system fosters innovative, creative and critical thinking. the second word is digitalization, because digital skills are essential for the realization of every entrepreneur and young person. they improve their access to sustainable jobs, help them to be self-employed and teach them to better understand the needs of their future employers. and the third word is business, because big corporations can help small companies and new projects with know-how and experience, and we here, our job is to create the most appropriate environment for entrepreneurship to flourish. it is therefore absolutely essential that work experience, entrepreneurship training and programming be part of any training from an early age in all european countries.
in writing.– i would like to express my support for the report on ‘promoting youth entrepreneurship through education and training’. i am convinced that in order to promote youth entrepreneurship, we should start from education. investing in all levels of education, in training and in developing transversal and soft skills is essential, because being entrepreneurial requires acquisition of different skills – not just to start a business, but also to plan it, run it and develop it. being entrepreneurial also helps us achieve sustainable employment and reveal our full potential. entrepreneurial education develops a sense of initiative which will help young people with their own initiatives and their everyday life – not only for their companies and associations. i do realise that youth entrepreneurship cannot completely solve the youth unemployment problem, but it is definitely one of the solutions, especially acknowledging that having an entrepreneurial mind-set is highly beneficial for the development of the creative sector, something that europe very much needs in order to get back to growth and strengthen europe’s competitiveness. to encourage, help and support youth entrepreneurship we need a sustainable approach – we cannot achieve that with a single instrument. it requires sustainable, purposeful, long-term actions. i am happy that in this report we managed to set our clear direction.
in writing. believing in young people and their potential makes me an advocate of the idea of youth employment through entrepreneurship. if you want to fly an airplane, you can't do it in a classroom by reading a textbook. it takes many hours of hard work and hands-on training. similarly, to become an entrepreneur and to go from an idea to a successful product, you have to rely on practical experience and learn from the best entrepreneurs. and since young people value concrete proposals, not just words, i have two ideas: first, why not turn erasmus for young entrepreneurs into a global programme that opens up opportunities beyond the eu's borders. let our young entrepreneurs learn from the best and bring this model here to europe. second, to end unpaid internships in europe. traineeships, internships and training are a shortcut to gaining experience, but around 50% of the traineeships offered in the eu are unpaid. it's discrimination. if you can't afford it, you don't have an internship. often unpaid or low-paid interns have responsibilities equal to employees, and any work must be paid for. let us show young people that europe means acting in their interests and that is what is expected of us in this room.
madam president, i support the proposal by the co-rapporteurs for a total ban on the cloning of all types of animals for agricultural purposes. scientific observations have so far shown that cloning has a disproportionately low success rate and that the whole process carries a number of risks for both the offspring and the surrogate mothers involved. but beyond animal health, we here, directly elected by european citizens, must listen to their voices and attitudes. the majority of europeans are opposed to this kind of reproduction and we are obliged to surrender their will. the effective ban on the import of cloned animals and their products is a step in the right direction in the negotiations for an international agreement such as the ttip. we must not allow products on the european market which undermine the confidence of our citizens in the quality of european agricultural products. the cloning of farm animals and the import of products thereof should be explicitly included as not admissible for import into the european union during these negotiations.
in writing. i welcome the report on the approval of the directive on the cloning of animals for farming purposes, because the ep has once again maintained its strong position that it does not support the technique of cloning farmed animals. the future of european livestock production should be based on traditional breeding and selection methods in order to preserve animal health and to strengthen consumer confidence in the quality of animal products. i support the ban on the cloning of farm animals and the placing on the market of cloned animals and embryos, and the proposal by the two committees of the ep to extend the scope of the ban to include the offspring of cloned animals, germinal products and food derived from cloned animals and their offspring. the ep's position responds to european citizens' concerns about animal welfare and strengthens consumer confidence about the origin of the food they consume. compliance with the ban on cloning should be ensured by introducing a system of traceability and control of imports of animals and animal products from third countries, in particular with regard to countries applying the cloning technique. i support the adoption of special import conditions to ensure that import certificates contain the necessary traceability information.
efficient public administration is a process comparable to open innovation. each stakeholder can contribute to its development with expertise, experience and ideas. the process is endless, but that shouldn't affect the motivation of the participants to succeed. effective administration is also one of the most visible and definitive signals of a stable governance of a country. optimizing systems is a really long and complex process. i am very happy to say that i come from the country i know best - bulgaria. we are boldly continuing to take steps in the right direction towards building electronic governance. it is indeed important that civil society, business and the public sector work together to give business a chance to operate successfully beyond the borders of the union. this really can't happen without a well-functioning administration that provides information about what it's doing for citizens and businesses. to save time and money, we need electronically operated institutions and customs whose successes are tangible. we should not be passive players in the e-governance process, because it is important to be active participants and to benefit from its services. it is important and necessary to be open to the changes that are already happening in order to be better prepared for those that are coming and that are really unpredictable at the moment. innovation is moving our world forward and bringing us closer and closer to the future. the public administration and customs, when they are efficient, can therefore become the engine of the new digital economic model in europe.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on ensuring the correct application of customs and agricultural legislation, as i consider that optimising systems, using innovation and exchanging information and expertise between member states' administrations are the best ways to combat smuggling and customs fraud. new technologies are moving our world forward and we need to maximise the involvement of our public administrations and customs in them, so that they work more efficiently and are more accessible and transparent to european citizens. the bulgarian national administration is already moving in this right direction and i hope that the eu will support and facilitate the completion of this much needed process.
in writing. i supported the report on the seal products trade because it prohibits the commercial killing of seals, thereby protecting their population and reducing the cruelty inflicted on these endangered animals. i appreciate the fact that the european provisions are aligned with those of the wto, which means that the eu is fulfilling its international obligations. i agree with the compromise reached with the inuit community and i find it fair, because i do not believe it is in our power to uproot a thousand-year-old tradition and take away the basic livelihood of the indigenous people when we are absolutely aware that their goals are non-commercial.
in writing. ‒ the report on the state of fundamental rights in the eu in 2013 and 2014 is particularly important as it represents another small step in ensuring the principles of the rule of law, equality, respect for fundamental rights, including the rights of minorities, which unfortunately are not equally protected in all countries.
in writing. ‒ i supported the ep motion for a resolution on the protection of the eu's financial interests in the cap, as i believe that simplifying the cap should go hand in hand with optimising agricultural control policy. a less bureaucratic cap will result in a lower error rate in its implementation and hence in fewer financial penalties for farmers. in addition, payments for projects should not be interrupted when minor or technical errors are detected. digitisation of the application process as part of the simplification of the cap, as well as the creation of a 'single farm file' with crop data, will reduce errors already in the submission of applications. this requires investment in broadband networks in rural areas and consumer training programmes, especially for older farmers. i support the idea of implementing a single audit of the cap within the same year. the integrated approach to carrying out checks will reduce the administrative and financial burden on farmers by reducing the number of on-the-spot visits and better coordinating control activities by audit bodies. this will save the european taxpayer a considerable amount of money, given that the cost of checks amounts to eur 4 billion per year.
in writing. family businesses are the backbone of the european union's economy and are the economic entities that create almost half of private sector jobs. i supported this report because it clearly reflects my priorities and those of my colleagues in the european people's party. measures to improve lending to small businesses, provide staff training, encourage innovation and remove administrative and tax barriers. i believe that the european parliament should be united in taking the measures to support family businesses that the report specifically sets out to create a unique ecosystem of thriving businesses in all eu member states.
in writing. ‒ my colleagues in the european parliament and i are convinced of the potential of the blue economy to generate growth and development, even taking into account its current problems. that is why we voted in favour of the resolution in favour of a single strategy to consolidate the leading role of the blue sector within the union. the blue economy should focus on sustainable economic activities that create jobs and contribute to overcoming the asymmetries between the different european regions. at the same time, however, i am strongly opposed to the possibility that the focus on the blue economy could serve as a pretext for unsustainable exploitation of our water resources. on the other hand, i am convinced that the specific problems that exist can be overcome through more active intergovernmental cooperation, better governance at eu level and a common focus on research and innovation as a way of expanding the opportunities for exploiting the blue potential and overcoming the negative trends towards the deterioration of water resources.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on unlocking the potential of research and development in the blue economy for job creation and growth. the blue economy is a hugely important part of the european economy and we need to make the most of the huge capacity it offers. it is our duty to responsibly manage and protect our natural resources, such as europe's oceans, seas and coastal areas, and to develop sustainably the potential of the many economic sectors they offer. innovation is what drives our world forward, and i believe that public funding of new technology and research is the most appropriate tool to promote growth. the report is important to me because the country i represent has such a huge resource - the black sea border - that it can be used even more efficiently and sustainably to create more jobs for bulgarian citizens and open up new opportunities for development in both our national and international economy.
mr president, i share the view that the gap between education, training and the labour market is one of the main reasons for the high levels of youth unemployment in europe. to combat this anomaly, it is necessary first of all to provide young people with the skills they so badly need, such as entrepreneurship, which will help them to develop fully in a professional and personal way. we must not forget that entrepreneurship is the backbone of the european economy. it has the potential to create jobs and growth and boost competitiveness. this is why it is vital to support young people who want to start their own projects but encounter a number of obstacles along the way. entrepreneurship training should be accessible to all, because the entrepreneurial spirit and attitude can be developed in everyone. it is our duty to provide a suitable learning environment that develops the skills and talents of young people.
in writing. the topics of youth entrepreneurship and education linked to business needs are a priority in my work. in order to have a europe that is prepared and prosperous for the next generation, young people need to be educated from an early age in critical and innovative thinking, entrepreneurship and business acumen, digital and management skills. promoting youth entrepreneurship is an effective way of tackling the worrying situation of youth unemployment in the european union. i voted in favour of the excellent report by mrs šojdrová because it is in line with my vision on the subject and i am convinced that the measures it proposes will help to increase the number of young entrepreneurs, which in turn will inevitably reduce the serious rate of youth unemployment in the european union.
mr president, europe's cultural heritage contains enormous economic and strategic potential. the fact that europe is one of the world's major tourist destinations, attracting millions of tourists every year, is proof of this. i am of the opinion that we should stimulate the potential of the european cultural routes of the council of europe, because this will help to develop sustainable cultural tourism. at the same time, we have to deal with a number of challenges in the sector. one is the gradual disappearance of traditional skills and crafts. they are part of our identity and an essential element of europe's cultural heritage. it is our duty to preserve them and pass them on to future generations. in this spirit, i believe it is extremely important to increase awareness of our tangible and intangible cultural heritage, which is often linked to regional and local culture. in this context, i call on the commission to continue to support the cultural and creative industries and to establish and organise a european year of cultural heritage.
in writing. ‒ the european parliament adopted a resolution endorsing a significant part of the measures set out in the specific report of the committee on culture and reaffirmed the importance of the overall concept of the report. the ep is aware of the importance of cultural heritage as a valuable reference point, a means of individual and collective identification, and as a catalyst for economic progress. i myself consider that the proper use of available resources to support, enhance and promote the preservation of europe's cultural heritage on the basis of an integrated approach is a task of paramount importance. such an approach is not only necessary for achieving sustainable cultural dialogue and mutual understanding, but is also a prerequisite for enhanced social, economic and territorial cohesion. moreover, it can lead to the creation of a single european cultural area without compromising the integrity and specificity of individual national identities. meps strongly support the idea of creating a single eu portal dedicated to tangible and intangible heritage, which would bring together information on the status, funding opportunities and existing policies in the specific area. we also welcome the initiative to designate a european year of cultural heritage, which would undoubtedly contribute to the promotion and more responsible treatment of historic cultural monuments.
in writing. i am proud of the great cultural heritage that bulgaria and europe possess, and i consider it my duty to honour, protect and develop it. the cultural heritage of europe is of fundamental importance. it is at the heart of our european identity. cultural heritage, besides being a testimony to europe's glorious history, is an excellent tool for economic and social development and for creating many new jobs. i voted for the report on the integrated approach to cultural heritage because it covers all aspects affecting cultural heritage. i warmly welcome the proposal to create a common european portal dedicated to tangible and intangible cultural heritage and i believe that funding for cultural heritage should be increased, because investing in the past will create many key and long-term dividends in economic, social and foreign policy terms.
member of the commission.– madam president, today’s debate is very timely. it concerns the positive opinion of parliament on the first emergency relocation mechanism. however, as members know, the commission is adopting a new package on migration which will be officially presented to them tomorrow. as i have repeatedly said, both i personally and this commission are committed to accelerating and enhancing our actions to face the migratory challenge, which is a european one. the proposal for the emergency relocation of persons in clear need of international protection from italy and greece is an important part of the measures we are already putting in place to address this unprecedented situation. therefore, allow me to express my appreciation to our rapporteur, who has led the discussions on this complex file in a very constructive and engaging manner. the aim of this proposal is twofold: to relieve the pressure on italy and greece and to ensure the dignified treatment of the persons concerned. the commission welcomes the approach parliament proposes to take. the interests of the applicants should, as much as possible, match those of the member states. the system has to be implemented in such a way as to create a win-win situation and to prevent secondary movements. this is why, in deciding which member state an applicant should be relocated to, specific account should be taken of the qualifications and characteristics of the applicant, such as language skills and other individual indications based on family, culture or social ties which could facilitate their integration. at the same time, our aim is to have something as simple and efficient as possible in order to ensure that these persons can be relocated as quickly as possible. this will ensure the proper protection of the applicants concerned while alleviating the burden on italy and greece. the commission shares the concerns of parliament when it comes to vulnerable persons. it is foreseen that they should be the priority in the implementation of the scheme. the special needs of asylum applicants, including their health, should be of primary concern, and the best interests of the child should always be a primary consideration. as you are aware, the council reached a general approach in our proposal in july. member states, in general, made considerable efforts to show solidarity and agreed to the relocation of 40 000 persons over two years and, as a first step, to the relocation of 32 256 persons. i am confident that the member states will update their figures by the end of this year so as to reach 40 000, in line with the commitment that they have taken. the discussions in the council confirmed that this remains a very challenging topic, but we should be able to feel proud as soon as the results are achieved. the refugee crisis is a reality and no one can pretend that it does not concern us all. such results would not have been thinkable only a few months ago. indeed, it is the first time that member states have collectively – and not just bilaterally – agreed to relocate persons in clear need of international protection. it is now of utmost importance that we proceed quickly to the implementation of the scheme, including by the establishment of the hotspots. i am personally engaged in assuring an accelerated and effective rollout of the hotspots as soon as possible, and certainly before the decision enters into force. the hotspot approach is not only necessary for the implementation of the relocation decision but is, more importantly, a key measure of solidarity vis—à—vis the most affected member states that rely on the coherent mobilisation of european agencies. the european asylum support office (easo) will provide support in the processing of asylum applications and the registration of migrants. frontex will help with the return process, while europol and eurojust will assist in the investigation of criminal networks. it is crucial that we provide holistic support to member states under pressure and ensure ownership and build trust with other member states. the first hotspots have already been set up in sicily, lampedusa and taranto. one more is going to be set up within the next few days in piraeus in greece. a workshop organised by the commission, together with easo, for the member states on relocation and resettlement will take place on 1 october. the third relocation and resettlement forum will also take place this autumn to continue the discussion on the technical modalities. we can all agree that the recent developments – unprecedented arrivals of migrants and asylum applicants – will require further action. members have clearly called for a permanent relocation mechanism based on a distribution key. parliament will this time be co-legislator on the file and i look forward to your engagement. this proposal will already build on many of the ideas put forward by this house during its debates on relocation. in this respect, i would like to thank you for your valuable contribution. let me underline that, more than ever, we need to work in a comprehensive way as there is no single solution. the commission will continue and speed up the implementation of all the aspects of this common challenge, as set out in the agenda on migration. i wish to thank members very much for their attention and am ready to take note of their remarks and answer their questions.
on behalf of the s&d group. madam president, the events of the past few months have shown that we cannot make a policy for the mediterranean alone. the migratory pressure on land borders is enormous. the measures taken by the commission are no longer up to date. the debate is no longer about the numbers of relocation because they change from day to day. the dublin regulation is no longer working, commissioner. some member states at the external borders have stopped registering migrants because they no longer have the means or resources. the permanent and binding mechanism is more than necessary. the commission must propose it and the council must adopt it in the name of european solidarity. unfortunately, the council seat is currently vacant. it is appropriate to provide some relief to member states at the external borders and to confer on them a special status. they are the ones who welcome the majority of migrants. the hot spots must be made operational as soon as possible. we need a common strategy and measures to dismantle the trafficking networks. none of these measures can be effectively implemented if europe does not actively participate in the resolution of conflicts in third countries. europe must reassess its position as a world leader. (the speaker agrees to answer a question on the 'blue card' (rule 162 (8) of the rules of procedure))
answering a question asked by raising a blue card. in recent days, some european politicians, including mr sarkozy, have proposed that border countries, including my country bulgaria, become a sort of european health zone and accept refugees only there. if you ask me this question, you either agree with this policy, or you don't know european law, because it makes a clear distinction between refugees on the one hand, and economic immigrants on the other. we're talking about refugee policy, and i'm asking you to read the laws carefully.
member of the commission. – mr president, honourable members, let me start by telling you that you are right. as i said in the beginning, the relocation scheme is only one part of the comprehensive approach as described in the agenda of migration. ms sargentini, ms wikström, this house has always had a very strong opinion about solidarity in practice. we agree. i agree with you, and we are working towards the same goal. you have mentioned the council; allow me to be optimistic and confident that the member states will soon take brave decisions. the challenge we are facing requires it. mr díaz de mera, this house – the european parliament – will be co-legislator for the permanent relocation mechanism. even concerning the emergency mechanism, which – as the name itself reveals – had to be adopted urgently, the commission did take the opinions expressed by parliament into account. i am grateful both for the suggestions and for the support of this house. many of you have referred to the need to revise the dublin regulations. i would like to stress that many of the measures that the commission is proposing recognise in practice the need to act above what dublin foresees. regarding its possible revision, as i promised at the very beginning, a thorough assessment will be done in the course of 2016. then we will decide the route to follow, the way ahead. mr deprez, would you agree? indeed, easo and liaison officers from different member states will be fully involved in the selection procedure. let me remind you that migration will be the main point of president juncker’s state of the union speech tomorrow morning, after which we will continue our discussion in detail. allow me now to switch to greek, since i was asked by some greek deputies about the situation in greece. ms spinelli asked me a question about the financing. μέσα σε σύντομο χρονικό διάστημα η κυβέρνηση, δια στόματος πρωθυπουργού, δεσμεύτηκε ότι θα προχωρήσει με γρήγορους ρυθμούς τις απαραίτητες διαδικασίες. με ικανοποίηση σας γνωστοποιώ ότι αυτές ολοκληρώθηκαν. η ελλάδα διόρισε την διαχειριστική αρχή που είναι αρμόδια για τη διαχείριση των κονδυλίων. γνωρίζετε από την εμπειρία σας ότι, όταν διατίθενται χρήματα σε μια χώρα, πρέπει να υπάρχει μία διαχειριστική αρχή. αυτή μας γνωστοποιήθηκε μόλις σήμερα. ζήτησε επίσης η ελλάδα έκτακτη χρηματοδότηση για την κάλυψη άμεσων αναγκών και η χρηματοδότηση αυτή ξεκινά άμεσα. η ελλάδα επισπεύδει επίσης την έναρξη της λειτουργίας του hot-spot στον πειραιά, από όπου οι εμπειρογνώμονες θα σπεύδουν σε εκείνα τα νησιά όπου απαιτείται ενίσχυση. στα δε νησιά που είναι βασικές πύλες εισόδου εγκαθίστανται χώροι υποδοχής. επίσης, ζήτησε η ελλάδα την ενεργοποίηση του μηχανισμού πολιτικής προστασίας. επαναλαμβάνω ότι πρόκειται για προαπαιτούμενα, όχι μόνο για την ελλάδα αλλά για όποια χώρα χρειάζεται τη βοήθεια της ευρωπαϊκής ένωσης. θέλω με την ευκαιρία αυτή να σας πω ότι στο μεγάλο και σημαντικό αυτό ζήτημα, πρωτόγνωρων διαστάσεων, το οποίο αποτελεί ίσως τη μεγαλύτερη κρίση που αντιμετωπίσαμε από τον δεύτερο παγκόσμιο πόλεμο και έπειτα, η ευρώπη δεν έμεινε απαθής. από την πρώτη κιόλας στιγμή, όπως ίσως θυμόσαστε όσοι είστε μέλη των επιτροπών με τις οποίες είχα την τιμή να συνεργαστώ στο ξεκίνημα της θητείας μου, αναλάβαμε δεσμεύσεις και σε χρόνο-ρεκόρ για τα ευρωπαϊκά δεδομένα η επιτροπή ολοκλήρωσε τη δουλειά της και κατέθεσε τη νέα ευρωπαϊκή ατζέντα για τη μετανάστευση. εναπόκειται στα κράτη μέλη να την εφαρμόσουν. σήμερα, κατά τη διάρκεια της συζήτησης, ακούστηκαν πολλά για την ευθύνη των κρατών μελών. η νέα ατζέντα για τη μετανάστευση είναι το πλαίσιο. όπως σας είπα πριν από λίγο, είναι θέμα ωρών για να πληροφορηθείτε σχετικά με ένα νέο πακέτο, πέραν εκείνων που ήδη γνωρίζετε, και αναφέρομαι και στα δέκα σημεία του σχεδίου δράσεως που είχαμε υιοθετήσει και ανακοινώσει πριν από λίγο καιρό. παίρνοντας πρωτοβουλία, κινηθήκαμε γρήγορα μπροστά αλλά μας πρόλαβαν, είναι αλήθεια, αυτές οι δραματικές εξελίξεις που επιτάχυναν τα γεγονότα. τούτη την ώρα όμως, πρέπει όλοι να αποδείξουμε στην πράξη και την αλληλεγγύη και την υπευθυνότητα μας - δύο βασικές αρχές που διέπουν τη λειτουργία της ένωσης. η αλληλεγγύη δεν είναι μόνο μια ηθική έννοια· είναι και νομική έννοια. είναι μια έννοια που καθορίζει από την πρώτη κιόλας στιγμή την εσωτερική μας λειτουργία και υπάρχει κατοχυρωμένη στη συνθήκη. πρέπει να το αποδείξουμε αυτό στην πράξη: αλληλεγγύη μεταξύ μας, υπευθυνότητα των κρατών μελών αλλά και αλληλεγγύη προς εκείνους τους άτυχους ανθρώπους που, στην προσπάθειά τους να ξεφύγουν από τον πόλεμο, την καταστροφή τον εμφύλιο, τους διωγμούς, επέλεξαν ένα πολύ δύσκολο και επικίνδυνο και, σε πολλές περιπτώσεις, θανάσιμο ταξίδι για να προσεγγίσουν τη δική μας μεγάλη χώρα, την ευρώπη. εμείς θα συνεχίσουμε σε μία κατεύθυνση, σε αυτή την πορεία προς την αντιμετώπιση της κρίσης, η οποία δυστυχώς δεν θα λυθεί μέσα σε μία βραδιά, αλλά θα απαιτήσει πολλές προσπάθειες επί πολλά χρόνια ίσως. θα συνεργαστούμε. δεν κρύβω ότι προσωπικά είμαι ευγνώμων στο ευρωπαϊκό κοινοβούλιο διότι από την πρώτη κιόλας στιγμή στήριξε αυτή την πολιτική, αποδεικνύοντας στην πράξη την ευαισθησία του για την ανθρωπιστική διάσταση του όλου ζητήματος αλλά και την υπευθυνότητά του, για να μας οδηγήσει - μέσα από το διάλογο - στο να πάρουμε τις σωστές αποφάσεις. σας ευχαριστώ και πάλι κυρία keller για την
