in writing. mr president, commissioner mogherini, i am strongly opposed to the mandatory nature of quotas and the allocation of refugees on a quota basis. this policy directly infringes the sovereignty of each eu member state to control its own borders and decide which people it can let into its territory and which it cannot. the commission's proposal is hypocritical because by deciding which country to send this refugee to and which to send to that country, they are discriminating against themselves. living standards vary greatly between member states, and i don't think you can force a refugee to stay in bulgaria, hungary or romania. the current approach is wrong, because in practice the eu cannot yet clearly distinguish economic migrants from genuine refugees. last summer we were talking about 40,000 refugees, today another 120,000. what about tomorrow? the quota principle of redistribution of refugees is not a solution to the refugee wave problem. it's a temporary measure that will only encourage more refugees to seek asylum in the eu, and it's about the consequences. the real solution to the problem must be to tackle the sources of immigration, i.e. the eu must focus on normalising the situation in the middle east and neutralising the islamic state.
madam president, mrs westphal, mr deutsch, commissioner, first of all i would like to congratulate everyone on the successful work on these two reports, which concentrate on the main elements of the european union's cohesion policy. they confirm the role of cohesion policy in supporting economic growth and job creation, especially when we are talking about the 6th cohesion report and in a period of economic crisis when cohesion policy is one of the main elements. secondly, the role of cohesion policy in territorial development and, more specifically, in the development of urban areas - a key element of the policy which allows us to look in the future at the role of urban areas in their different dimensions - big cities, capitals, small towns, surrounding regions. but the main theme, which is confirmed by both reports, is the theme of coordination. coordination at all levels, coordination in the thematic area, coordination in the efforts of all levels of government and of all those involved in the implementation of cohesion policy.
madam president, i suggest that the commissioner take on board three central points of the deutsch report. firstly, that cohesion policy has an incontestable role as the principal policy for reducing disparities. secondly, that it should be at the core of the investment policy of the european union in the sense of being an integral and leading part of the overall investment drive that the commissioner is currently trying to generate. thirdly, that there is the need for a new dynamic to the debate on cohesion policy in the longer run, in connection with the termination of the europe 2020 strategy and the movement towards a new multiannual financial framework. you have these three pillars, or points, on which to build a very strong position in the college and we wholly support you in your efforts to that end.

in writing. ‒ i voted against the name change proposal because i think it should be discussed in the committee on foreign affairs and not directly in the chamber. another very good reason not to change the name right now is that such a change at this point could give legitimacy to the terrorist organization (hamas) that is ruling the gaza strip right now. in this context, the eu should not provide legitimacy to a terrorist organisation but should positively promote the peace process by promoting internal palestinian reconciliation. any step that could have a negative impact on the peace process must be avoided. the middle east peace process has a high priority on the eu agenda and it must remain so.
in writing. ‒ i supported the proposal to amend the marrakesh agreement. as the comprehensive analysis has shown, the new agreement will not only benefit our economy but will also significantly improve our trade structures. reducing trade costs is essential in competitive and politically challenging times like these. in addition, effective customs enforcement and compliance are crucial for further improving trade. the distribution of illegal goods and content should be counteracted and public hostility avoided. especially for countries like mine, a healthy and strong trading system is vital. non-ratification is therefore an obstacle to transparency, the elimination of red tape, the simplification of procedures and due diligence in respect of commitments. but above all, we must jointly guarantee the free movement of goods, and this would be hampered if the act in question were not adopted.
in writing. ‒ i strongly welcome the trade facilitation agreement. to maintain a well-functioning, efficient and healthy economic environment, which ensures a more attractive european market and guarantees the preservation of our cultural heritage, less administrative burden is needed. this can be particularly difficult for small and medium-sized enterprises that lack the necessary resources and know-how to cope with the bureaucracy of public administration. the trade facilitation agreement will not only allow for better market access and a wide variety of goods and businesses, but what is even more important is that we will see many new opportunities for innovative technologies, such as electronic payment systems, etc. the agreement will also streamline trade-related procedures, which is very important for small entrepreneurs in terms of simplifying overly complex procedures.
in writing. ‒ accession to the budapest convention (cmni) is a good step forward in streamlining contractual and navigational standards. the use of sea transport routes has long been a problem, particularly for my country, and we often struggle with the non-recognition of judgments and with questions of choice of law for individual countries. in order for everyone to be satisfied with the respective means of transport, we need an integrated legal system of mutually recognised competencies of each other. this will stimulate economic growth, competitiveness and legal stability. and the common judicial area will not only provide greater legal certainty, it will also promote awareness and appreciation of the use of inland waterways in an efficient and mutually beneficial way, without destroying them.
madam president, i did not support the quotas voted on in this report. i have long been convinced that this administrative measure will not work in practice. we should allow asylum seekers to apply in a european country where they want to, where they want to operate, and that european country should decide on their asylum if they meet the international legal conditions. i think this measure is wrong, it is against the interests of asylum seekers and it will not work in practice. these people will go to countries where they want to work, where they have families. and i am convinced that in the end, this sort of administrative division of asylum seekers can lead to a rise in anti-schengen sentiment in europe. and there's a growing desire to re-establish the borders between the states, and that's what i see as the biggest problem. therefore, in line with the czech government, i am in favour of solidarity but against administrative separation of applicants.
my dear mrs. president, ladies and gentlemen, i did not support this report because i am firmly against the mandatory nature of quotas and the allocation of quotas, not least because it infringes the sovereignty of each country to control its own borders and to decide which people it can let into its territory and which not. and secondly, because in some way the standard in half the member states of the european union is different. how will you get some people to stay in, say, hungary, some in bulgaria, some in greece, and some in germany and sweden? how do you decide who stays where, and isn't that discrimination against them? third, there is no clear definition of what constitutes a refugee and what constitutes an illegal immigrant. we all know this in theory, but it's not applied in practice, and until it's specified, until it's clearly and simply said what the capacity is, it doesn't make sense to have these quotas, because they have to be calculated not against 150,000 or 160,000, as is being proposed at the moment, but they have to be calculated against about 100 million, which is the population of africa and the middle east. this is unwise and it can't end well. this also applies to the schengen agreement, not to mention the failure of the dublin regulations. that's why i'm against the quota allocation.
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution because it reflects the urgency of action and the determination of parliament to respond to the migratory challenges facing europe today. in the resolution, we strongly support the establishment of an emergency relocation mechanism which demonstrates our solidarity with refugees, especially the most vulnerable, but also with member states experiencing high migratory pressures. we insist that this temporary mechanism be replaced by a permanent one which can provide a long-term solution to the migration challenge. this mechanism should be mandatory for member states but flexible enough to allow for rapid adaptation given the fluctuating nature of migration. in this resolution we also recall that the western balkans route has become an important route for migration to the union, with a considerable increase in irregular crossings across turkey's borders with greece and bulgaria.
in writing. ‒ more and more people are living in cities in europe and around the world. this development is being accelerated mainly by population growth and new technologies. the aim of this own-initiative report by ms christine westphal is to take a broader look at the challenges facing eu policy and to outline the guidelines and boundaries of a future sustainable urban development policy. in short, the rapporteur believes that eu policy must urgently take action on an effective urban development policy in order to be able to have an impact on the consequences of europe's urbanisation and the speed at which it is taking place. the rapporteur believes that only through active and coordinated action by eu policy will cities be able to continue to fulfil their functions and offer their inhabitants the living conditions they want. however, it is also important to take into account the fact that european cities are extremely diverse and a common eu universal policy would not have the desired effect in all member states. i therefore voted against the proposal. i think it violates the principle of subsidiarity.
in writing. i supported the report on the urban dimension of eu policies because i recognise the importance of the fact that 72% of the european union's population live in urban areas and the trend is for this to increase in the future. this makes urban areas the main engine of our economy, and it is therefore necessary for the european union to create sustainable policy adapted to the needs of these areas in order to stimulate and facilitate their progressive and positive development. i am firmly of the view that the need for administrative relief is extremely cumbersome, not only when we talk about urban areas, but when we talk about eu policy in general.

mr president, i would like to refer you to two points that president juncker particularly stressed this morning regarding employment that are of direct relevance to this debate. firstly, that refugees must be given the chance to work, which – besides letting them earn money – is a matter of basic human dignity. secondly, it is actually full employment that we should be striving to achieve. the report on a competitive labour market has the severe impediment of proceeding from the assumption that people are inclined to remain idle or passive behind rigid protective social and labour barriers, since they supposedly lack the necessary entrepreneurial spirit and impulse to be mobile: a deeply flawed defining proposition, since it is at odds with the basic human preference for a life in dignity and the accompanying desire to engage in productive work given the chance to find meaningful and rewarding jobs. conversely, and quite correctly, the other report highlights the need to enable social- and solidarity-based enterprises to grow, and urges member states to promote the cooperative enterprise model, precisely as contributing factors so that the social- and solidarity-based economy may fulfil its defining mission to provide full, high-quality employment. in this regard, i would like to urge commission vice-president dombrovskis to personally commit himself to ensuring that the commission responds positively to the call, contained in the report, for the commission to come forward with a legal framework codifying a dependable specific status for such enterprises, defined as having other objectives than simply profit for shareholders as to the platform on undeclared work, just a word to assure you – since i am the rapporteur on behalf of parliament – that we shall do everything in our power to enable the inauguration of this platform before the end of the year.
ppe) mr president, mr vice-president of the commission, when we talk about competitiveness, we are talking about high levels of productivity, quality staff and added value, namely the ability to be faster, more successful and more profitable than others. this formula for success is as simple as it is complex, because the foundation of a prosperous europe is in quality human capital. it's really time to invest in our people, in our young generation and especially in our talents. the number of young people out of education or employment is indeed too high. the number of school dropouts is also increasing. even highly educated young people fail to find professional fulfillment. but that doesn't mean that our young generation is incompetent, it just means that the knowledge and skills that are being promoted don't meet the needs of the labour market. unemployment has been a buzzword in europe for too long and this negative trend must change. so we need to steer young people into the careers of the future, those that don't even exist yet, to create fast-thinking, creative people with excellent digital skills and analytical abilities. i really believe very strongly in the need to build an ecosystem of strategies at the european level, including measures like education reform, support for entrepreneurship, improving labour mobility, as many of my colleagues have mentioned, within europe, in order to be really much better prepared for the challenges not only of the 21st century, but also of the 22nd century. by promoting business through quality people and innovative products, we'll finally stop talking about unemployment and mention the word competitiveness much more often.
mr. chairman, what the financial crisis has done is traumatize the labor market. the kind of trauma that young people suffer the most. what the crisis has also done is push these young people into the emergency lane. this strip that provides a perpetual loop of unpaid internships, temporary employment and a gray economy. this is something we have to stop. we need to stop it, and we need to stop it with concrete action, so that we don't let this debate stay here or in some publication. i have a very specific suggestion. the first is to reduce subsidised employment and use the money saved to fund the dreams of europe's brave entrepreneurs. they'll reveal the jobs we're looking for. moreover, erasmus for entrepreneurs only works within the european union, let's expand it. let young european entrepreneurs gain experience in the usa or brazil. i am sure that this will help us to overcome the fact that there is now a labour market for young people and a labour market for everyone else. this is unacceptable.
ppe) mr president, commissioner, i am pleased that we have the opportunity to discuss the sustainability of health systems in europe. despite the european union's limited powers in the field of health, it must become our priority not just in words but in deeds. it is difficult to explain to european citizens why we are more concerned with farm animal health than human health. the challenges of the future will become increasingly complex due to demographic changes, population ageing, the increase in chronic diseases. this reality should be reflected in the drafting of the recommendations to member states in the framework of the european semester. specific commitments are needed to implement structural reforms that ensure not only financial sustainability but also equal access for european citizens to quality healthcare adequate for the 21st century. in europe, there are still large health disparities between the different member states, which hinder economic and social cohesion. i believe that overcoming these problems requires the european institutions to engage even more deeply in assessing the functioning of our health systems, monitoring their performance and - very important to me - measuring access to quality healthcare within the european semester by introducing new health indicators. this will help member states to learn from each other, exchange good practices and identify effective solutions. it will also allow an assessment of the respect of patients' rights in cross-border healthcare. let me ask the commission one last question, commissioner, and it is this: the report published last week states that lack of information is the main reason why patients cannot make the most of their rights under european law. what concrete measures could the commission take to ensure that all european patients are aware of their rights as european citizens?
on behalf of the ecr group. madam president, allow me first of all to congratulate the rapporteur on the comprehensive report he has presented to us. i welcome the fact that his report also highlights a number of challenges to the general application of the law in the european union. despite the positive feedback in terms of the smaller number of infringements recorded, we must not fail to keep our focus on the big picture. we still face numerous challenges, particularly with regard to the general and correct application of european union law in the territory of the member states. for a well-functioning single market, in order to preserve the legal order on the territory of the member states of the european union, the support to national services, which have the main responsibility when it comes to the incorporation and application of new rules and guidelines, needs to be further improved. we also need to further improve eu legislation to prevent overly complex legal structures, which will provide greater clarity and legal stability. this will further stimulate economic growth and employment.
in writing. ladies and gentlemen, the results of the reports show that the late and incorrect transposition of european directives continues to be a major problem in the application of european union law. the active role of citizens and the european institutions in ensuring that member states comply with eu law is also noteworthy. the complaints they file are crucial. however, the overall decrease in the number of formal infringement proceedings over the past five years (from 2 900 to 1 300) shows that a solution has been found. the positive role of the 'eu pilot' pre-procedure cannot be overstated. in this respect, more transparency is needed to ensure that the european parliament and the citizens are properly informed. we know that the primary responsibility for the correct application and enforcement of european union law lies with the member states, but this should not relieve the eu institutions of their obligation to respect primary law when adopting legislation. there is therefore a great need for rules in administrative procedures which are generally applicable to the european institutions. this will reduce red tape, ensure full respect for citizens' right to good administration and make the european union more accessible.

in writing. transport is a highly complex system that is the engine of the fast-paced and mobile world we live in. transport is a key economic sector and vital for european and international business. the european union needs to rethink its policy to meet the near future with a smart, competitive, safe, integrated and modern transport system. i voted for mr van de camp's report because it sets out in depth and detail all the main points, proposes alternatives to deal with the many challenges we face and presents short, medium and long-term measures for the sustainable development of this sector.
in writing. ‒ i voted 'abstention' on the report by mrs wozenberg on the professional careers of women in science and higher education and the existence of so-called glass ceilings. the term 'glass ceiling', coined in 1986 by journalists, describes the invisible barriers that limit women's progress towards higher positions in their professional development. for me and my group, ambiguous messages are being sent through paragraph 48 of the report, which deals with gender balance in boards and specific targets and quotas. i do not support gender discrimination and i think that the rapporteur has produced an excellent report, but i do not think that the introduction of quotas is a solution.
in writing. i voted in favour of the excellent report by my colleague mrs vozemberg, firstly because it precisely identifies the factors behind the glass ceiling faced by women in scientific and academic careers, and in particular the gender stereotypes that are consciously or unconsciously reproduced by men and women. the report then identifies possible measures to break through this glass ceiling. i particularly welcome the emphasis on awareness campaigns for men as well as women, the promotion of women's networking and mentoring schemes and initiatives. the attention paid to institutional change, which requires a strong commitment from member states and relevant ministries, is also highly relevant, as is the full integration of the gender dimension in horizon 2020. finally, i wholeheartedly support the proposal to create the women and science in europe prize.

in writing. we live in a modern world, and in a modern world, everyone is equal, regardless of gender, age, religion and ethnicity. i supported the report on the empowerment of women through education because the situation of discrimination against women is inevitably progressing, but it still exists. we need to take measures to make this process more intensive and to guarantee young women equal opportunities and a chance for economic and social independence. it is unacceptable that young women in the european union should face stereotypical barriers to their career development.
in writing. ‒ i am categorically against the european commission's proposal for mandatory binding quotas for the redistribution of asylum seekers. quotas are not a solution to the refugee problem, they are a temporary measure that will only encourage more refugees to seek asylum in the eu. the solution to the internal migration problem must be based on building cooperation on a voluntary basis and trust between member states, as well as by providing more financial resources in specific areas such as frontex and the european refugee agency. but this is a solution only to the consequences of the wave of immigration, not to the causes. the real measures to deal with the refugee problem are solely linked to the union's external policy. the only way for the eu to deal with the current problem is to establish international cooperation between the us, russia and the arab countries to put down the wars in the middle east and eradicate terrorism in the region. as long as there are wars and terrorist organizations close to the eu, we will see a constant influx of immigrants.
in writing. i voted in favour of the resolution because parliament must send a strong signal of support for a genuine, more coherent and comprehensive migration policy, based on a long-term strategy. if solidarity is to be the principle underlying our action in this area, it cannot exist without the effective implementation of our common standards at union level and effective control of our external borders. the resolution also supports the establishment of a permanent and mandatory relocation mechanism for asylum seekers and also stresses the need to combat the criminal networks of smugglers. finally, there is an urgent need to ensure greater coherence between the union's internal and external policies and to integrate the issue of migration into our external policy, which implies regular dialogue and increased cooperation with third countries.
ppe), in written form. ‒ i supported the resolution on migration because i believe that the measures are beneficial for bulgaria and that a new approach is needed to overcome the migration crisis. first, the european union must set up centres in third countries to determine whether migrants heading to europe are actually fleeing conflict or want to immigrate purely for economic reasons. effectively implemented, this programme will reduce the number of economic migrants who make up a large part of the migration flow to europe. it is extremely important to strengthen border controls to stop illegal immigrants trying to enter bulgaria. only through secure external borders can we guarantee national security and preserve one of the eu's greatest achievements schengen. with its rich experience in successfully protecting the bulgarian-turkish border, bulgaria can only contribute to the schengen area. a more effective return policy for illegal economic migrants entering europe is also needed. the adoption of a pan-european list of safe countries of origin will speed up the examination of their documents and facilitate their return. at the same time, however, the eu must support the economic development of a number of african countries through the proposed trust fund, so that they can transform themselves from a source of immigrants into a source of prosperity.

in writing. ‒ i supported the european parliament's resolution on the situation in belarus, because it deals mainly with the state and level of respect for human rights in the country. progress in the area of freedom of expression and the media, as well as in respecting the political rights of citizens and opposition activists, has led to closer relations between the eu and belarus, and to the first official visit by a european parliament delegation since 2002. but despite these few positive steps in the right direction, the situation remains deeply worrying. there are administrative prosecutions and harassment of independent media for political reasons, as well as criminal prosecutions of young activists. on the eve of the presidential elections in belarus, the country's government must grant the opposition unhindered access to all government-controlled means of communication and allow it to participate in the elections on an equal footing. only then will belarus be able to ensure that presidential elections are held in accordance with internationally recognised standards.
in writing. unfortunately, the social and solidarity economy is overshadowed when talking about the economy as a whole. in fact, there are 2 million enterprises and companies in the european union in the social and solidarity economy. what's remarkable about this type of enterprise is that their primary purpose is not material benefits, but social support and humanitarian aid, and at the same time they employ 14 million people, and among them are the most vulnerable and weakest members of our society. these enterprises need proper attention and concrete assistance, especially in the context of a union based on the principle of solidarity. we need a perfect regulatory framework that promotes social entrepreneurship, stimulates innovation in the sector and ensures sustainability and security. that is why i gave my 'thank you' for the report to my colleague verónica lope fontagné.
in writing. i believe that overcoming the economic crisis and creating a competitive labour market in the eu depends on several key factors. first of all, i believe that to achieve smart, sustainable and inclusive growth, we need to invest in education and skills, in developing talents and creating opportunities for fulfillment in life. however, today we are in a situation where young people are finding it increasingly difficult to make the transition from education to work and are often willing to accept positions that do not correspond to their qualifications. that is why we need to invest in skills that are in line with the needs of the labour market and society. but here i want to make the following clarification. education systems should not be solely geared to stimulating skills and competences that meet business needs. they should focus on building the horizontal skills needed for the personal development and growth of young people, so as to make them proactive and responsible citizens.
in writing. it is extremely worrying that 25 million people are unemployed in europe, but it is equally worrying that there are 2 million jobs that are unfilled due to a lack of qualified people to fill them. it is this paradoxical imbalance that we must combat. it is necessary to prepare young people from an early age for the needs of the market. we need immediate and concrete measures to create a close cooperation between education and business. it is also essential that digital skills become an integral part of the education system in all member states of the european union. i voted in favour of martina dlabajová's report because i support the aspects it sets out.
in writing. ‒ in general, i welcome the idea of a review of the application of the law in the eu. especially when you consider the fact that the complaints have increased compared to previous years. however, we should not be fooled by numbers. although increased awareness has increased public scrutiny, there are more factors to consider. different processes, such as simplification of eu legislation, often occur alongside integration into national systems. particularly countries in the eastern parts of europe, such as bulgaria, have had much greater difficulties, as they often lack the necessary resources, guaranteed stability or know-how. moreover, we must bear in mind that the sectors concerned are among the most complex. violations in transposition are becoming a major problem and the commission has to monitor this process closely because more and more citizens are highlighting these problem areas. however, the report proposes a common european asylum system, which my group and i do not agree with, and that is why i voted against it.

in writing. the conclusions of the justice and home affairs council are a good first step. the agreement reached between the parties, however, is far from what all europeans expect for a unified approach to measures to deal with the migratory pressure and to restore confidence in national authorities and the eu. i am concerned that the conclusions do not take sufficient account of the possibility that the refugee flow may change its route. today, there is enough information that several thousand refugees are likely to try to cross the border between turkey and bulgaria, which is the eu's external border. at this stage, however, it is not mentioned as a place that needs attention and reinforcement. the eu started the debate on the refugee crisis late and with a vague focus, so everything we do today needs to be decisive and right. the huge influx of people is becoming an increasingly serious social problem that may escalate politically. we don't have time to convince our societies that europe can do it. this will be exploited by all kinds of political extremists in our own countries, trying to challenge the european project.
in writing. the current migration crisis requires immediate and decisive action. first, i call on the european commission to draw up a list of transit countries which are in the path of the migration flow to europe. on the one hand, these countries must be helped to combat human trafficking effectively; at the same time, however, we must immediately start negotiations with them on the establishment of distribution centres to stop economic immigrants coming to europe. only in this way can we reduce the flow of migrants before they reach european shores. strengthening external borders is another important measure that will ensure european security while at the same time stopping illegal immigration. the eu has mechanisms in place to allow those who want to enter the member states legally to do so. crossing the green border without a visa or a valid reason, however, is not among them. last but not least, the powers of frontex should be extended immediately so that the agency can organise the return of unsuccessful asylum seekers to their countries of origin. the agency already has experience in similar work on the attica project and common european actions in this regard will make return operations more effective.
ppe), in written form. as president juncker stressed in his state of the union address, when 'the price of a litre of milk is lower than the price of a litre of water', we are talking about a crisis on the milk market. unfortunately, the crisis is gradually spreading to other sectors such as pork, fruit and vegetables. this requires the adoption of appropriate market measures in favour of farmers in all sectors affected by the crisis. the chronic decline in milk market prices has led to significant losses in milk producers' incomes. i therefore welcome the commission's decision to provide a specific package of measures worth eur 500 million to support the sector. a large part of this amount - a total of eur 420 million - will be distributed through national financial packages as direct compensation to dairy farmers, while ensuring maximum flexibility for member states and an accelerated transfer of advances under rural development programmes. all of these measures are a necessary injection to deal with the short-term consequences of the crisis. at the same time, other instruments that can help stabilise the milk market, such as the 'school milk' scheme, should not be neglected. i therefore call for the speedy conclusion of the negotiations on the new school scheme regulation, which will provide a higher level of european funding for the scheme.
alde), in writing. ‒ i support the resolution on the preparation of the european commission work programme for 2016, calling for the inclusion of expiry clauses in the commission's proposed acts so that they can be reviewed periodically, avoiding over-regulation while allowing the necessary flexibility to european legislation to respond to the dynamic challenges of today.
in writing. ‒ the extraordinary vote we held on 17 september is a precedent in the way the european parliament operates, it undermines the democratic principles of the institution and it has once again proved that the social democrats and the christian democrats are pursuing a common policy. the european council has not yet decided on the mandatory quotas for the relocation of asylum seekers residing in greece, italy and hungary, and the president of the ep has decided unanimously to put the commission's proposal to the vote. the european parliament thus took a decision without discussing the proposal in its committee and without being able to make any additions. i strongly disagree with the way this decision was made. i am also strongly against quotas because they are a temporary solution to the problem and if they work, they will continue to be applied. first it was 40,000, now it's 120,000, the next wave will probably be even bigger. under the dictatorship of the social democrats and the christian democrats we will be obliged to accept another quota of refugees. distribution is not a solution to the problem, but rather a clear and loud call for even more refugees and illegal immigrants. the decision taken by the ep is the beginning of the end of the european union.
in writing. ‒ the current european asylum legal framework is extremely damaging to the countries on the union's external land and sea borders. the dublin system is based on the principle that these countries, including bulgaria, must deal with the ever-increasing mixed migration flows of asylum seekers and illegal economic migrants. long before today's crisis, i was among those who called for a profound change and a fundamental overhaul of the very essence of this system. it is now clear to all that a corrective mechanism is needed to ensure that the burden is shared in solidarity between the frontline countries and the rest of the union. the european commission has put forward proposals for mandatory quotas and guarantees that solidarity between member states will not be left to goodwill alone. i stand firmly behind this idea because it is in fact protecting the bulgarian interest and will protect bulgaria for decades to come from the threat of a sudden and massive invasion of our land border with turkey. the european parliament has shown that it can react responsibly and quickly at a critical moment. the commission's proposal to introduce a new system of taxation of profits is to be welcomed by the european parliament. the member states are now in the process of taking the final decision within the council.
the rapporteur. dear mrs president, ladies and gentlemen, today i have the opportunity to present to the european parliament a legislative report on the proposal to amend regulation no 1303/2013 on the provisions of general application as regards the specific measures for greece. first of all, i wanted to start by saying that this is an exceptional situation that needs to be overcome with specific measures. each of us has followed the summit on 12 and 13 july, when the eurozone leaders reached an agreement to support greece, and everyone was aware of the need for urgent action, both the eurozone leaders and the european commission and, of course, the european parliament. in this context, the committees in parliament have decided on a simplified procedure without amendments to this regulation in order to contribute to the general conditions for support to greece and to allow the regulation to enter into force as soon as possible. on 17 july, the european commission presented a legislative proposal, which includes emergency measures and aims to: first, to increase the level of initial pre-financing for the 2014-2020 cohesion policy operational programmes under the investment for growth and jobs goal and for programmes funded by the european maritime and fisheries fund in greece. second, to increase the maximum co-financing rate for the operational programmes for the period 2007-2013 under the 'cohesion' and 'regional competitiveness and employment' objectives in greece to 100% and to remove the ceiling for payments under these programmes. by amending the regulation, the proposed measures aim to overcome a critical liquidity shortage in the greek economy and provide an immediate and necessary stimulus to investment, which will allow a rapid start-up of a larger number of projects. this proposal is budget neutral for the period 2014-2020. it is important that the procedure is completed before the end of 2015 so that the greek authorities can fully use part of the funding available to them for the period 2007-2013. three other committees in parliament have asked for an opinion, namely the committee on budgets, the committee on fisheries and employment and the committee on social affairs. all my colleagues have agreed to apply the simplified procedure. as regards the committee on regional development, no member objected to this procedure. the council, under the luxembourg presidency, has also considered this dossier as an urgent matter and will follow parliament if it accepts the commission proposal as it stands. however, solidarity and exceptional measures should not mean that greece does not bear responsibility. if greece does not accelerate the pace of its payment applications for the programmes concerned before the end of 2016, it risks recovering the additional initial pre-financing. finally, greece should submit a report to the commission on the implementation of these exceptional measures. i would like to emphasize that we are here in this chamber as representatives of the citizens of the european union and can express our concern. we might think that we have more pressing problems now, but we also have to respect our commitment to support greece here and today. for that reason, i urge you to support the motion.
madam president, commissioner, first of all i would like to congratulate the rapporteur on his work on the dossier. i can say that i know the situation in greece well enough to form an opinion on the matter. i am sure that the difficult times the greek people are going through require us to take extraordinary measures. emergencies, of course, require such extraordinary responses on our part. however, these emergencies also require extraordinary control. extraordinary control over every penny of european taxpayer money that goes to greece, because if there's one thing i don't believe in, it's that i trust the government that is currently in greece to the hilt. i don't believe mr. tsipras either, who said he was firmly behind the european union just two days before he went to ask for help from russia. i do not believe that the same mr tsipras who said he would fulfil his commitments to the greek people to the end, would hold his responsibility to the end just one month before he resigned. so i want to call on you, alongside voting on these emergency measures for greece, given that there are 27 other member states in the european union, to have one mind, to keep a close eye on the situation there and the spending of european funds against the plan we are adopting today, so that we can ensure for all the people in europe that their money is spent in the most transparent way possible, so that we can get greece back on its feet.
the rapporteur. madam president, thank you to all the colleagues who took part in the debate, and to the commissioner for his explanations. the debate was very interesting and very focused on the problems of both greece and cohesion policy. let me make a few clarifications and answer some of the lines that have been presented here in the house. first of all, i would like to point out that the committee on regional policy has dealt very responsibly with the commission's proposal at two of its meetings, devoted a special special meeting to it and then held another meeting to discuss the matter. i accept all the concerns of my colleagues who are concerned about the possible lack of control. i would like to stress that this proposal is not a gift to greece, it is a proposal that allows the greek side to use a resource when it needs it, at a time when there is a need for that resource. this proposal is extraordinary, it requires extraordinary behaviour and extraordinary measures, but it does not set a precedent and it does not increase funding for the greek side, but it allows greece to use a resource that has already been agreed to in order to be able to solve problems at the moment. cohesion policy does not become a financial liquidity policy, cohesion policy through this type of exceptional decisions fulfils its role of being applied effectively according to the conditions in each member state. whether the measures will be successful, we will see, we will observe and we will control. the greek government is able to do this through the cohesion policy of such initiatives and measures. whether it will use this opportunity we will be able to control through the reports to the european commission and through all the instruments of control of the european parliament.
madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i should like to begin by congratulating mrs eleni theocharous on this excellent report, for which her experience on the ground gives her every legitimacy to make recommendations. i would like to emphasize three elements. firstly, it is clear that the role of local authorities is becoming indispensable if we are to increase the effectiveness of development aid. the definition, in conjunction with the local authorities, of a limited number of priorities should allow for better adequacy and appropriateness of the projects implemented. it is also a way of sharing responsibility with the state actors who are closest to the citizens. secondly, i believe that it is high time that local authorities cooperate more closely in development, because it is through them that we can promote the emergence of democracy and prevent and heal conflicts, which, as we know, are one of the main obstacles to development in many countries. recognition of the role of local authorities should also improve women's participation in decision-making, both in principle and because the positive developmental impact is known to be significant. finally, the new guidelines in the commission's communication on local authorities must be translated into practice today in the implementation of the 11th edf. there is no time to lose for eu aid to bear fruit: let us not turn a blind eye to the links between today's migration crisis and the perfectibility of our european development policy. i hope that this report will be sent, beyond the council and the commission, to the local authorities in our member states, to the african union for example, to our acp partners, in order to highlight the role of local authorities in development cooperation.
mr. chairman, my city of sofia is facing an extremely big problem and that is the flooding of illegal economic immigrants. all of these people are economic migrants, because according to international norms and treaties, refugees should be housed in the nearest safe country, and according to international law, that is turkey. economic immigrants are taking over the center of sofia and permanently changing the face of this city. the main reason for this is the presence of a camp for illegal immigrants in the very center of the capital of bulgaria. these reception centers create a lot of problems for people who live nearby. migrants who receive refugee status and leave the centers are unable to integrate into society, creating closed religious and ethnic enclaves in the very center of the city. for now, it's a few blocks away from the capital's largest market and the city center. but with each passing day, this enclave expands and takes up more and more of the city center. it's the same in the other two neighborhoods where there are these kind of centers. sofia no longer looks like a european capital and this process must stop. the bulgarian authorities should immediately take measures to remove the refugee camps near the turkish border, where illegal economic migrants pass daily.
mr president, recently all of us became witnesses to unfortunate events in europe: a mass inflow of refugees from the middle east and africa, thousands of dead people and bitter divisions among the eu member states over the migrants crisis. in this regard i would like to draw your attention to the responsibilities of frontex, and those respectively of the commission and the council. in facing the emergency situation in the european union, how the agency fulfils its mission to promote, coordinate and develop european border management in line with eu fundamental rights is of the utmost importance. i would like to call for the urgent implementation of an effective strategy and for the preparation of a new strategy, well before 2017, in relation to the announcement by frontex during the hearing this year. in this context, the following must be answered: how, in the most efficient and effective way, can the agency fulfil its task according to the new strategy developed on the basis of a changed reality in the regions. these issues are becoming more and more important nowadays in view of the worsening security environment and the problems of migrants coming to the eu. there is an urgent need for a common european position and a long-term approach. frontex needs to improve strategy, have a flexible reaction and produce real results in the implementation of the eu’s executive-branch responsibilities.
on 17 september, the mayor of duisburg, mr. søren link, made discriminatory comments against citizens of eastern european countries, in which he said, quote: 'i am inclined to take in twice as many syrian refugees, but at the expense of getting rid of a few eastern europeans.' despite the reactions he provoked, his comments continued in the same vein, this time directed at the citizens of my country, bulgaria. i find such statements to be highly inappropriate, unnecessarily provocative and divisive among the citizens of the union. this comes at a time when the european union is facing a refugee and humanitarian crisis. we are expected to be united to face the challenges that are very likely to change our common future. how long will we be divided into eastern and western europeans? populism in one direction or another is the path to the destruction of everything that has been built. i strongly disagree with mr link's statement and call on my colleagues and other european politicians to refrain from making such statements, which add to the already tense situation. i call on all to engage in constructive dialogue in search of common solutions.
mr. president, i would like to thank you for your service. president, madam commissioner, i strongly support the introduction of a single system for the protection of geographical indications for non-agricultural products at european union level. the benefits of introducing a single protection at european level are many. first, it will stimulate the technological and economic development of certain areas in europe and increase the number of people employed in the production of traditional goods. secondly, the promotion of local and regional traditional products is important not only for ensuring fair remuneration of local producers but also for creating the conditions for the development of sustainable cultural tourism. in conclusion, i would like to say that there is a real risk that traditional crafts and production methods will disappear forever, and we cannot afford that. i therefore call on the commission to develop and propose an appropriate legislative framework in the short term.

in writing. ladies and gentlemen, while the european union is struggling to find a solution, thousands of innocent people continue to suffer. we repeat the need for solidarity every time, but every time we demonstrate how far we are from speaking with one voice. building walls within the eu is a xenophobic measure that goes against the values and policies of the eu. at a time when record numbers of people are fleeing war and oppression, it is unacceptable for full eu members to want to isolate themselves from the problem. we are facing an unprecedented wave of refugees, and the sooner we realize that there is no national solution to this complex problem, but only a common approach, the sooner we can help the thousands of people who are suffering. this is not only a european crisis, but also a regional and global one, and that requires that we include in the solution all the countries neighbouring the eu, which are bearing a large part of the burden of the refugee wave. in this context, i welcome the decision to relocate 160 000 refugees within the eu, as well as the decisions to set up 'hotspots' and to help 2 million refugees in turkey. but that's not enough. the eu needs to take a common position and actively participate in resolving the conflicts in the region that are the root cause of the refugee wave.
in writing. the refugee situation is deteriorating day by day and this requires our timely and rapid response. we cannot stand by and watch as thousands of people risk their lives, leave their homes to escape war. we are currently facing a humanitarian crisis whose scale requires an adequate and timely european solution. we must show solidarity and each member state must shoulder its share of responsibility in tackling this problem. there is another aspect of the refugee crisis that we should not ignore: the fears and worries of the people. yes, we must show solidarity and be sympathetic to the plight of refugees, but at the same time we must respond adequately to these fears, without falling into the trap of populism. i believe that europe can only manage the migration crisis if it is united. instead of building walls and closing borders, let us seek a pan-european solution to the problem and stop blaming each other.
in writing. ‒ the forced labour convention is one of the eight core conventions of the international labour organisation, which constitute the basic international labour standards. when the convention was adopted in 1930, states were called upon to end the use of forced labour within the shortest possible time and to criminalise the act. to date, after more than 80 years, the ilo estimates that at least 20.9 million people worldwide are victims of forced labour. i therefore welcome the protocol signed last year in geneva, which aims to close the gaps in the implementation of the forced labour convention and to make progress in the prevention of trafficking for the purpose of labour exploitation and the protection and compensation of victims of forced labour. i call on member states to ratify those parts of the protocol that fall within eu competence and to make efforts to do so by the end of 2016 in order to ensure effective protection and enforcement of labour laws, awareness raising and international cooperation in the field of migration.
in writing. ‒ mr president, as the report of the european union agency for fundamental rights on labour exploitation in the eu shows, despite the fact that eu legislation contains provisions in this area, workers in europe continue to be at risk of being subject to severe labour exploitation. this is particularly true for those working outside their country of origin. i therefore warmly welcome and support the recommendation to authorise member states to ratify the 2014 protocol to the forced labour convention, as regards judicial cooperation in criminal matters. this is a small but important step in our joint efforts to sanction labour exploitation in europe, to combat trafficking in human beings and to ensure decent working conditions.
in writing. ‒ i welcome the report on the mobilisation of the european union solidarity fund to support bulgaria and greece, which suffered enormous damage as a result of floods earlier this year. this fund was set up in 2002 specifically to provide financial assistance to countries affected by major natural disasters. however, in my view, this mechanism is not working and the compensation it provides is grossly inadequate. it is even an extreme injustice that non-eu countries receive many times more than eu countries. this is the case with serbia. at the beginning of the year, i sent a written question to the commission asking whether the commission intended to propose changes to the regulatory framework for the esf 'solidarity' to make the calculation and distribution of compensation fairer. however, the commission considers that the fund is generally well-functioning, fair and helps affected countries to overcome the financial burden caused by disasters. although the aid will be granted almost a year after the disaster and covers only part of the damage suffered by the state, the commission does not intend to propose any further revision of the instrument in the near future.
in writing. i welcome the release of almost €6.4 million from the eu solidarity fund to help repair the damage caused by the severe disasters in bulgaria earlier this year. heavy rains in january and february caused flooding and landslides that damaged local infrastructure and a number of private homes in the southeastern part of the country. to my great regret, within the last year and a half this is the second devastating disaster to befall my country and this is the third consecutive request for assistance from the fund. yes, the amount we'll get is small compared to the damage, but it will help rebuild public infrastructure. what about the people whose homes were destroyed? the solidarity fund does not reimburse damage to private property, but we have a moral obligation to help affected citizens and to take the necessary measures so that we can prevent disasters of a similar scale in the future. i believe that this is only possible through prevention.
in writing. – in the early 2015, severe winter conditions caused natural disaster classified as "major" in bulgaria and another serious one (classified as "regional") in greece. the calamities resulted in high-cost damages for the less developed south-east region in bulgaria, and evros and central regions in greece. considerable damage occurred to public infrastructure, such as dykes, communication networks and roads, as well as businesses, private homes and immovable assets. the total estimated support from the european solidarity fund was calculated to eur 16.3 million. in fact, the mobilisation would not require an amendment to the 2015 budget, but will provide crucial help for the affected and vulnerable communities. the two cases match the european solidarity fund’s criteria. the nature of the disasters and their vast extent profoundly impacted on the local communities and economies. damage has affected the regions thoroughly - not only public infrastructure (water supplies, sewage and electricity infrastructure) but also private property, businesses, agricultural land and forests.
in writing. – the severe natural disasters in bulgaria necessitated an urgency response and thus the support of the european solidarity fund. i would like to congratulate my colleague andery novakov on the report, which successfully reflects the situation and calls for the mobilisation of the eusf for a total amount of eur 16.3 million, of which eur 6.4 million for bulgaria, in order to grant the very much needed financial assistance.
in writing. ‒ i supported the report by ms rozier on the possible inclusion of non-agricultural products in the protection of geographical indications by the european union. the eu market is full of products that are part of the cultural, social and economic heritage of a place or region in the eu and that are rooted in traditional know-how and production methods. as regards their legal protection: non-agricultural products are industrial products and craft products which do not enjoy this kind of legal protection, and for this reason i agree with the rapporteur that the european commission should propose an instrument for their protection at european level.
in writing. ‒ the labelling of agricultural products and foodstuffs is subject to common european rules. the european union provides for the possibility of additional, supranational guarantee and protection of geographical indications, designations of origin of agricultural products and traditional specialities foods. examples of wines, cheeses or various meat products protected at european level are already known to the european consumer, who is increasingly looking for guaranteed origin goods. the bulgarian producers are also showing interest in the european protection system, as evidenced by the registration of several products. however, the current european legal framework does not provide for this possibility for non-agricultural products. this was found to be a failure for europe, known for its high-quality craft products. local craftsmanship is a testament to the uniqueness of each european region and an integral part of our cultural heritage. the resolution, adopted by an overwhelming majority, calls on the commission to submit a legislative proposal to establish a european system for the protection of geographical indications for non-agricultural products. i welcome good european ideas like this. the need for a new impetus in the development of traditional production methods and craft training is palpable. i hope that this european initiative will contribute to the preservation of the traditional craft sector and work towards its recognisable place on the european and international market.
in writing. – i support the geographical indications for agricultural products i see possible positive outcomes for the european economy. as one of the youngest members in parliament, but also as person who respects traditions, i believe that the combination between innovative ideas and the experience of production will lead to an economic success. currently, national laws are fragmented and offer different levels of protection within national and local transversal rules, but we must avoid such fragmentation as it undermines the european single market.
in writing. ‒ the principle of equal opportunities in the european community is fundamental. i, like my country, was reluctant to accept this report because if such measures are taken for one country, others must be able to benefit, and you know, bulgaria is in a difficult economic situation, one of the poorest in europe (not to say the poorest), and any help for us is welcome. in the end, i voted for the report, and i sincerely hope that everyone will show the same solidarity when we too need certain projects to be implemented quickly and similar measures should be approved for bulgaria. this report proposes measures to address greece's critical liquidity shortfall and to promote the rapid start of necessary european structural and investment funds projects.

in writing. ‒ the european globalisation adjustment fund (egf) was set up to help workers find new jobs and develop new skills when they have lost their jobs as a result of changing patterns in world trade. however, my group and i do not believe that the egf is the right instrument to introduce such policies, nor do we believe that it is an effective instrument that offers a value for money approach to reintegrating redundant workers back into the labour market. and that's why i'm voting against it. i would stress, however, that i fully support measures to introduce policies that create growth and jobs. this report calls for €6.9 million for the 2 692 workers who lost their jobs at opel.

in writing. – alitalia’s case is a typical example of how external factors could endanger economic activity and employment in our regions. such is the case with 1 249 redundancies in gruppo alitalia were provoked. this was a textbook example case, where the help of the european globalisation adjustment fund was required. the financial contribution equals eur 1 414 848. the latter include active job search and training proposed by the italian authorities, including the re-employment scheme targeting dismissed workers over 50 years of age. regrettably, only 184 redundant workers will be targeted by the measures. in addition to that, preparatory activities, management, information and publicity and control and reporting - represent a relatively high share of the total costs (3.99%). last but not least, combination of various types of eu funding support in our regions is a step forward for a stronger and more competitive european economy.
in writing. ‒ politicians have long been exploring ways to improve the effectiveness of international development assistance. for decades, we have seen this development aid to the poorest countries squandered in corrupt deals and not reaching the people. this report is a step towards improving efficiency precisely because it looks at the role of local authorities in the development process. i support the rapporteur's view that decentralisation is needed in the union's external aid because this way aid will reach those most in need, help the development of small and medium-sized businesses and generally empower people at local level. that is why i strongly support this report by mrs eleni theoharus.
in writing. ‒ the european parliament adopted a resolution aimed at strengthening the role of local authorities in developing countries in defining, organising and achieving development goals. despite the eu's continuing efforts to reduce global poverty and their effectiveness, further action is still needed to achieve sustainable and balanced development at global level. the involvement of local authorities in the process of building overall capacity to address the challenges facing developing countries is of paramount importance. decentralisation of cooperation would help to form a comprehensive and effective approach capable of covering the three main dimensions of development social, economic and environmental. dialogue between the eu, the national states and their local authorities would lead to a much more coherent and achievable vision for the future. i encourage the creation of partnership agreements between local authorities in eu member states and local authorities in developing countries, and the channelling of additional resources to strengthen the capacity of decentralised bodies. i believe that real change can only be achieved when all stakeholders work together in this direction.

on behalf of the ecr group. mr president, i would like to thank my colleague, ms göringer de odenberg, for her report and for the compromise reached, which i and my colleagues from the group of european conservatives and reformists supported in the juri committee. as early as july 2011, a small claims instrument was adopted to improve and simplify civil and commercial litigation procedures. under this instrument, the commission is to present to the european parliament, the council and the european economic and social committee a detailed report reviewing the functioning of the european procedure which necessitated the review of the existing regulation. the objective of this review was to make the existing instrument more attractive and useful for european citizens, who are increasingly entering into financial relationships with a cross-border nature thanks to the functioning of the european free market. i believe that with our joint efforts we have succeeded in achieving this goal. i welcome the decision to increase the ceiling on the value of claims from eur 2 000 to eur 5 000 for civil and commercial cases of a crossborder nature. this will ensure effective protection of european citizens and small and medium-sized enterprises. i support the other proposals which i believe contribute to improving the instrument and making it more applicable in practice.
madam president, honourable commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to congratulate the rapporteur and the shadow rapporteurs on their work on the report. i believe that the simplified small claims procedure is a step forward towards the introduction of common rules in european civil proceedings. the increase in the thresholds for admissibility of a claim to eur 5 000 is largely in line with economic reality. i am confident that this will help to improve the practical application of the regulation and that the maximum number of smes and citizens will benefit from it. the future inclusion of labour law claims in the scope of the regulation would give many citizens working in other member states a chance to have faster and cheaper access to justice. in this way, they will be able to make the most of the free movement of people in the european union, while having guaranteed legal protection. the possibilities for remote payment of court fees, for remote hearing of parties and for a simplified written procedure are very attractive and advantageous for small and medium-sized enterprises. in this way they will save time and money both on travel to court and on legal advice. i believe that this regulation will make it easier for our citizens to access justice and will help to resolve crossborder disputes more easily. i hope that small claims procedures will find wide application in the member states.
mr. chairman, what hasn't been said about the conflict in syria? it seems to me that we need to continue to concentrate on the new elements today so that we can participate constructively in this debate. i'll just stop at three. yes, first of all, two new countries were positioned. but it is very important for us that the international community, without setting conditions, but in constant dialogue and partnership, continues to seek a solution to the conflict. in our efforts, let us also not lose sight of the issue of the territorial integrity of syria. next up, yes, the islamic state is a common problem. but most importantly, isis is a serious threat to the region's disintegration and radicalization. a clear and concrete commitment by the countries of the region would therefore help to improve coordination and provide a perspective for overcoming the crisis. finally, the role of the european union. our role is to use our diplomacy to be a balancer in the difficult and probably long process of redistributing influence in the region. that's our strength in this conflict, given the realities.
in writing. dear colleagues, islamic state has been operating in syria for two and a half three years without any russian military strikes. so these countries that have been waging their geopolitical battle in syria, invested in the creation of the so-called "moderate opposition", militarized their military wings and provoked the civil war against assad, bear full responsibility for what has happened so far, and for the refugee crisis that has hit the eu. the islamic state in syria emerged from the chaos created by the country's civil war. russia has its geopolitical interests, and they are very clear: to keep the naval base in tartus and the airport in latakia. it's probably in russia's interest to move towards some sort of federalization of syria, but that doesn't negate the interference, the responsibility and the geopolitical interests of the other major powers. only military intervention in syria will solve the problem of the "islamic state" and normalize the situation in the country. any diplomatic solution to the syrian crisis is doomed to failure as long as the islamic state is in existence. this means only one thing: the eu, the us and russia must unite against the islamic state and agree on the future of assad and syria as a whole.
in writing. it is high time that international actors coordinate their actions to bring about genuine peace in syria and the region, allowing people to stay in their homes and return there. uncoordinated interventions lead to increased chaos and the danger of an even greater wave of migration, which begs the question of whether this is one of the goals of the russian intervention. we need an international conference with all the stakeholders as soon as possible. we in the eu must insist on this every day. on the one hand, in the short term, we need to preserve and restore statehood in syria on its entire territory, but in the medium term - without assad. the factors that create tension - war and conflict, human rights violations and economic deprivation - that motivate people to undertake dangerous transitions in search of a better life, must be removed. to remove these factors, military action is needed, but to find a lasting solution to the crisis, we need a political process. representatives of the syrian opposition, as well as assad, should be involved in such negotiations if they are to have any real success. in the context of the collective efforts to counter the crisis, the priority measure should be the establishment of a 'safe zone' which can be established in the northern part of syria. such a zone would allow the return of refugees.
madam president, a huge wave of refugees, mainly from war-torn syria, has flooded europe, and the member states are having problems with shelter capabilities. the schengen system is cracking under the weight of the refugees, and there is great divergence among the eu member states on how to react to the humanitarian crisis. there are two indispensable players in any solution to this crisis: the eu and turkey. in europe, understanding has grown that without turkey’s support no solution to the refugee crisis is possible. there is currently little that the eu can bring to the table without its strategic partner, despite some of the good intentions in the report on turkey. turkey has harboured 2.5 million refugees, providing all their immediate requirements and needs. the eu is only now facing these problems. the worsening security environment and the migrants’ problems are a wake-up call for action. this time, the eu cannot simply pour in money to make this go away. it has to act swiftly if waves of refugees are to be prevented. we urgently need a united european position, a long-term and improved strategy – which includes turkey – and strong action and solidarity to achieve real results. turkey has already shown responsibility: now it is the eu’s turn.
mr president, turkey has a key role to play in north-mediterranean relations. this is why we need to strengthen our cooperation and common mechanisms to resolve conflicts in the region. i'll focus on three aspects. first of all, turkey has done and is doing a lot for the refugees who are on its territory. it is important that we continue to act together within the european union to strengthen border controls and the fight against organised crime. what is happening on the land border with bulgaria is a good example of successful cooperation in these two areas and it should be welcomed. second, the proposal for a buffer zone in syria. the idea makes sense, subject to discussion. but before that, let's be clear: this has to be done by giving a broad international mandate and we have to answer questions like 'who is providing security?', 'how is the security of the people guaranteed?', 'how do we make sure that the people who are in this territory do not become the target of radicalized groups?' finally, visa liberalisation for turkish citizens will undoubtedly send a strong signal to turkish businesses, and the positive consequences for the european union will also be imminent. in conclusion, i welcome the deepening of the dialogue between the european union and turkey. we know the challenges they face, but it's important now to focus on the important things: the crisis in the region and managing the migration flows very intelligently. this means cooperation between all stakeholders.
