in writing. ‒ i voted in favour because i strongly support the shift towards improving air quality in europe. of course, fuel plants and so on are important to the economy, but technological advances have given us new tools to limit the effects of such air pollutants. my country is particularly affected by this problem and it is important to establish mandatory limits. so far, there has been no proper regulation of this issue in bulgaria, and therefore we now rank as one of the most affected countries in terms of air quality. we must ensure health and safety measures at all levels and for all citizens by avoiding the misuse of our beautiful natural heritage. the creation of mandatory limits on air pollutants will help to achieve this goal.
in writing. ‒ the european parliament, aware of the seriousness of the problem of high levels of environmental pollution, adopted a legislative resolution on the proposal for a directive on the limitation of harmful emissions into the air. despite significant reductions in harmful emissions in recent decades, pollution levels in some parts of europe are still alarmingly high. this requires immediate measures to put an end to such practices once and for all. the directive focuses in particular on medium combustion plants, because they are the only ones not covered by european regulation and because of their growing influence as a factor responsible for pollution. medium combustion plants should be designed and operated in a way that promotes energy efficiency and their operation should be subject to control by the member state. any medium combustion plant should only be operational if it is licensed or registered by the competent authority. i am convinced that this directive is the right step towards the overall objective of the safety and well-being of european citizens and the careful balance between consumer health, environmental protection and economic activity in europe.
in writing. ‒ ladies and gentlemen, i voted in favour of legislation to limit emissions from medium-sized combustion plants (from 1 to 50 mw), for which there were no environmental limits until now. over 143 000 such installations are in operation in the european union and make a significant contribution to air pollution by nitrogen and sulphur oxides and dust. the most important aspect of this legislation is to take adequate account of the small capacity of small and medium-sized enterprises operating such heating installations for heating schools, hospitals and public and residential buildings to adapt to the new requirements. the new installation restrictions will take effect in three years. for existing installations with a heat capacity of up to 5 mw, the maximum emissions of sulphur dioxide, nitrogen dioxide and dust will be effective from 2025. the smallest installations, those with a capacity of 1 to 5 mw, most of which belong to small enterprises, will have to start complying with the limits from 2030. this provides sufficient time to plan and find the means to modernise the plants. taking into account the statistics on air pollution in a number of cities in our country, the introduction of these restrictions will undoubtedly contribute to improving air quality, to healthier living conditions for citizens and especially children.
in writing. ‒ i strongly support this report, as i am all for fighting fraud and corruption. this is also one of the main problems that bulgaria still faces on a daily basis. if we want to counter these problems, we need to be more rigorous and more effective in monitoring the processes involved, as well as raising awareness of the more technical side of such problems.
in writing. – i congratulate mrs ingeborg gräßle for her report showcasing professionalism. 80% of the eu budget is managed by the member states under the cohesion policy. therefore, the union’s budget is a long-term eu growth engine. key instruments for the achievement of this goal are the european structural and investment funds (esif). in this regard, the european parliament should be the watchdog guaranteeing that the eu budget is used in the best possible way. we should remain vocal on ensuring financial prudency and result orientation of the eu budget. regarding performance, we should measure project outcomes (useful results for citizens and businesses). for example, measuring the economic benefits of a road, or a bridge, not their length and features. improving financial management and performance is part of a larger effort – to cut the red tape in eu funding and to fight fraud.
mr president, commissioner, according to steve jobs success is about connecting the dots. i hope that you forgive me by starting off with a quote from an american and not a european entrepreneur, but actually this quote comes from a place where smes get about five times more funding from the capital markets than european companies do. the success of the european economy really does depend on connecting the dots between the individual markets and on unleashing the free movement of capital. this is how i personally see the capital markets union (cmu), as the tool that connects the dots between the capital markets of 28 individual states. i would also like to emphasise three points, out of the six proposed immediate actions, that are particularly important in my view. the first is connecting the cmu with the juncker plan. i am happy to see that support for the juncker plan is priority number one. only through the combination of both for europe can we unlock and diversify financing quickly and as soon as possible. my second point is about connecting achievements and aspirations or, to put it differently, what we have done so far and what we aim at in the future. the call for evidence on the impact of existing reforms is, i believe, also a very important step. it will definitely create an overall regulatory environment that strikes the right balance between managing risk and supporting growth. my last point is connecting and analysing success stories. the commission has proposed gathering best practices in non-bank sme funding, an exercise that i personally have been endorsing for a long time. that is why i would be curious to receive more information on the process and the timeline of the proposed research: it would be very much appreciated.
in writing.– without an integrated payments market on the european level, we cannot have a functioning internal market for goods and services, nor a digital single market or a truly competitive eu economy. in the new economic model and the modern european society, an increasing number of citizens prefer to shop online, yet only 1 in 7 completes purchases across borders. this is a consequence of the fragmentation along national lines we see in important areas of the payments market, such as card, internet and mobile payments. the revision of the payment services directive will change that. it will also bolster e-commerce and provide significant advantages for the european consumers, whose interest is at the heart of every piece of legislation we in the eu institutions produce. i therefore welcome the new measures contained in psd ii, such as enhanced legal protection, information on transactions and unconditional refund in case of unauthorised payments and fraud. by creating a competitive level playing field we will provide consumers with greater choice, transparency and security when making retail payments. on the business side, this certainty and stability will allow for competition and innovation, and thus more opportunities for europeans to reap the benefits of the digital single market.
in writing. ladies and gentlemen, the russian side is pressing ahead with its stated intentions to build a gas pipeline bypassing ukraine, which would allow the termination of gas transit through that country. the agreement between gazprom and five european companies to double the capacity of nord stream is not only a step in this direction, but also poses a significant risk to the security of gas supply for all of south-east europe. the unilateral termination of the south stream project and the serious difficulties encountered in negotiating the turkish stream, not least due to the different positions of russia and turkey on syria, the lack of gas connections between the countries in this region and the weak progress in the implementation of the southern gas corridor, make the transit of russian gas through ukraine the only alternative at the moment. at a time when we are building a european energy union, we are demanding a united voice on energy security, we have clear priorities to follow in relation to the vulnerable region of south east europe, the corporate bargaining for nord stream 2 is putting the pan-european energy policy to the test. 'nord stream 2' clearly demonstrates that much greater coordination is needed between member states in energy relations with third-country suppliers, while avoiding the reduction of energy security in some regions of the european union at the expense of corporate interests of companies in other regions.
mr president, in order to improve the european labour market, it is important that gender equality policy is not only on paper but also in practice. it is a market need that europe's women be economically independent, be paid fairly, have a work-life balance and know that they have no ceiling to running companies. to this we must add a real sectoral balance. it requires improving women's access to typically male occupations and increasing men's access to feminized occupations, such as in education. in bulgaria, low pay acts as a barrier to young people, and mainly men, to pursue the teaching profession. the opportunities for promoting gender balance in professions like this are real. and we should take advantage of them because the two genders contribute differently to the system, to the education and upbringing of the students, including gender equality.

mr president, i am addressing mr schmidt on the eve of the council meeting which must resolutely decide to amend the dublin agreement. in its current form, it is in complete contradiction to the temporary and permanent mechanism of relocation of refugees. there is no point, for example, in a country like bulgaria relocating 800 refugees for the next two years, when 1,000 people are coming in every week, who, under dublin, have to stay in the country. there is confusion and misunderstanding in society about the nature and functioning of hotspots. citizens in the eu's external border states fear that their countries will become a sort of ghetto for migrants who are detained there even though they do not want to stay. europeans need an information and awareness campaign. i would also like to address the commission, even though mr juncker is no longer with us. we must have a proposal for common border controls at the external borders of the european union, not just the schengen area. the additional budget and human resources for the european agencies frontex, europol and easo are a first step, but not enough. we must all tackle the migration crisis together, and european citizens will be our allies if they feel safe.

in writing. as a representative of a nationalist and conservative party, i strongly oppose any claim that political parties like mine incite islamophobic and xenophobic attitudes. neoliberals denounce us as intolerant just because we oppose the division of citizens into equals and the marginalized, with the latter having more rights than the former. the law should treat everyone equally and not favor one over the other, which actually discriminates and incites intolerance among citizens. european citizens do not want to be treated as second-class citizens. the union's policy of prioritising and protecting certain groups of people to the detriment of others further contributes to the spread of islamophobia, anti-semitism and intolerance. on the other hand, there is very little talk of the unfettered distribution of hate-inspired books. go to the frankfurt international book fair and you will find a lot of books by arab publishers that incite anti-semitism. are these the european values we are striving for? is this freedom of speech and opinion? i, as a european citizen, am firmly against the radicalisation of societies in one direction or another. but radicalization is not the cause of the rise of islamophobia and anti-semitism, it's the consequence. the primary cause is inequality before the law. we live in a union where the law and the law is the same for all.
in writing. freedom of expression, freedom to hold different ideas or beliefs, freedom to be different are all expressions of the principles on which the european project is built. and while fear of difference is a normal human reaction, we must be able to understand our differences more and rely on them, rather than on primary reactions like fear and hatred, if we are to continue to be a mature european society, based on the traditional human values that we all aspire to.
in writing. ‒ i supported the report by ms kirkhope on access to the visa information system (vis) for enquiries by the competent authorities of the member states and europol for the purposes of preventing, detecting and investigating terrorist acts and other serious crimes, as the new council decision updates the existing legal framework. i also support the objectives of this decision to ensure an appropriate and effective exchange of information between member states and europol in order to combat cross-border crime. in the context of cross-border crime, and in particular the growing problem of illegal immigration, it is important to work for greater security in the member states and to protect our citizens.
in writing. ‒ mr president, i support the report on the draft council implementing decision on access to the visa information system for enquiries by the competent authorities of the member states and europol for the purposes of preventing, detecting and investigating terrorist offences and other serious criminal offences. i believe that the european authorities and the authorities of the member states are called upon to cooperate effectively and to exchange information in a coordinated manner in order to guarantee the highest possible level of security for all people in the territory of the european union. in addition, respect for fundamental human rights is equally important, in particular with regard to personal freedoms, the protection of personal data and the need for all security measures to be implemented in accordance with the requirements and practice of european law.
in writing. ‒ although i am against the quota redistribution of refugees and the designation of these people as refugees in general, i supported the mobilisation of the flexibility instrument because it was created specifically to be used in such special cases and to respond quickly when needed. as the quota redistribution is not a permanent solution to the problem, the current funds mobilised will only cover part of the costs that await us. the real financial burden will fall on the member states themselves and their budgets, because the process of integrating these people will be very long, we will have to pay for their training for years and we will have to pay incredible amounts for social benefits. who's gonna cover these expenses? the amount the eu will provide for each relocated refugee is symbolic of the cost we will all have to pay. poor bulgaria can't afford to have illegal immigrants and refugees.
in writing. immigration and terrorism are among the biggest challenges facing the eu for europeans. this was also indicated by the most recent eurobarometer data, which showed that concerns about these issues had increased by one third compared to last year. our citizens are giving us a clear signal that we must take timely and concrete measures to deal with the refugee crisis. the allocation of additional funds of eur 401.3 million is a clear step in the right direction and i was happy to support the report. i believe that this financial support should be followed by long-term budgetary measures to tackle the problem, which may also require a review of the seven-year eu budgetary framework.
in writing. ‒ i welcome the proposal to adjust the eu budget for 2015 because the european agencies frontex, europol and the european asylum support office play a key role in resolving the refugee crisis and urgently need additional funds. the additional funds allocated under the asylum, migration and integration fund and the internal security fund are also of great importance and will support member states. the first fund will improve the capacity to receive refugees, and the second will improve border management. for bulgaria, these funds are of great importance because we need additional support for our reception centres and for the construction of the eu's external border. as regards the funds allocated to the regional fund for syria and to the un agencies, i believe that the eu cannot meet the needs of these agencies alone, nor should it be solely responsible for what is happening. the whole international community must come together and provide funds, because as the commission's plan for the quota redistribution of refugees is a temporary measure and will not solve the problem, so the present funds are only the beginning of the financial burden that we will all bear as a result of the policy we are pursuing.
in writing. ‒ i supported the resolution because we are facing an unprecedented wave of refugees and the sooner we realise that there is no national solution to this complex problem, but only a common approach, the sooner we can help thousands of children and mothers. this is not only a european crisis, but also a regional and global one, and that requires that we include in the solution all the countries neighbouring the eu, which are bearing a large part of the burden of the refugee wave. in this context, i welcome the decision to relocate 120 000 refugees within the eu and the decision to provide assistance to 2 million refugees in turkey. we also need to increase resources to deal with the refugee flow and to review the seven-year eu budgetary framework to implement long-term budgetary measures to deal with the problem. of course, these measures must be combined with initiatives aimed at addressing the root causes of the migration and refugee crisis.
in writing. ‒ as for this report, i have decided to abstain from voting on the resolution as a whole. at the same time, i support some of the compromises that have been made. in my view, the various aspects of the unrealistically high 2030 targets and the mortgaging of public money for the green climate fund (gcf) are not only unattainable but also harmful to the economies of the member states. the paris protocol is not a problem here. it is about setting realistic and achievable targets for all eu countries. i don't see the serious economic and industrial issues that countries like poland and bulgaria are struggling with on a daily basis reflected in this report, and in order to achieve a good report, they are key.
on behalf of the alde group.– mr president, in april we postponed the decision on granting discharge to the director of the european institute of innovation and technology (eit) to the financial year 2013. however, after receiving at the end of june a report from eit providing information on the progress since the previous one, we can draw the following conclusions about the institute in several different areas. in the domain of legality and regularity of transactions the institute has improved the quality of audit certificates. the use of a new methodology has further improved the quality of ex—ante and ex—post verifications. the internal procedures of the institute already fully comply with the respective public procurement rules. further to the consultancy performed by the institute’s internal audit capability, a number of activities have been implemented. in the area of budget and financial management there are positive changes related to improved planning and monitoring procedures, significantly improved absorption capacity of the beneficiaries and actions taken to alleviate the high staff turnover. as a result of the activities of the commission’s internal audit service we also observe positive developments, especially in the implementation of the elements of the institute’s action plan. by the end of 2015 it is expected the bulk of these actions will be completed. in the coming months we envisage the publication of a special report of the european court of auditors which will shed light on the ability of the institute to provide innovation in the eu, where i would like to discuss the distribution within the european union of the different sectors of knowledge and innovation communities. having regard to the positive changes, we support granting the director of the institute discharge in respect of the implementation of the institute’s budget and approve the closure of the accounts for the financial year 2013.
ecr), in writing. ladies and gentlemen, the extraordinary meeting called by juncker on 25 october finally took decisions to the benefit of the member states, namely a plan of 17 operational measures. one of the most important is that it 'reaffirms the principle of refusing entry to third-country nationals who do not agree to apply for international protection'. it is well known that migrants do not register in the peripheral eu countries because they want to reach the highly developed northern eu member states. i hope this measure will discourage migrants and traffickers and reduce the flow of illegal migrants. the other very important measure, which unfortunately does not bind turkey because it was not invited to the meeting, is to "discourage the movement of refugees and migrants to the borders of other countries or regions without informing those countries". we all know very well that turkey is the country that is organising the trafficking and the flow of migrants to the european union on a massive scale. the european union must urge the turkish authorities to limit the activities of traffickers to the maximum, to cooperate in the fight against them and to properly implement the readmission agreement signed with the union. only then will turkey prove that it deserves to be a partner of the european union.
ecr), in writing. ‒ i support the proposal to repeal the two acts in question. in order to achieve better lawmaking and greater transparency in european legislation, it is important to keep a constant review of european law. as both acts were taken into account in new commission and council regulations, and due to the fact that the envisaged temporary scheme ended in 2010, their practical application is useless. for that reason, i agree that the only logical next step is to cancel them.
ecr), in writing. ‒ making the existing services agreement with switzerland compatible with existing eu legislation and oecd global standards is an important and welcome step. we need to be very careful when it comes to privacy, information sharing and democratic control, especially when we consider the current issues surrounding digital infrastructures and increasingly digitised economies. it is necessary to ensure that the exchange of information takes place in the most secure and efficient way possible, ensuring transparency and legal clarity, in particular for citizens who rely on the protection and preservation of their personal data. in addition, given the fact that switzerland has long been known for its strict privacy rules involving a very complex set of rules, it is necessary to adjust the existing rules in order to make them compatible with each other and to avoid inadvertently creating loopholes.
ecr), in writing. ‒ following successful negotiations, the report shows a balanced approach which i believe is vital for mutual cooperation on issues such as taxation. the existence of a single applicable standard for the automatic exchange of financial information within the eu will ensure greater efficiency and transparency. when it comes to non-eu territories, i welcome the compromise reached on the scope of the commission's negotiating mandate. in my view, the mandate should be subject to the explicit agreement of the member states. like fiscal policy, it is a matter of national governance. a mandate for the ec in this area would affect the sovereignty of the member states on this key issue. i therefore strongly welcome the final report, as i believe it reflects a balanced approach.
ecr), in writing. ‒ as regards the fight against terrorism and organised crime, crossborder cooperation and the necessary database are a key element in tackling them. i welcome the council report which states that sweden has complied with all the requirements regarding the protection of personal data and in particular fingerprints. i welcome sweden's accession to the cooperation system and consider it an important step for the security of the union, especially in a situation such as the present one, where the eu is facing one of the greatest security challenges it has ever faced since its creation.
ecr), in writing. ‒ as regards the fight against terrorism and organised crime, crossborder cooperation and the necessary database are a key element in tackling them. i welcome the council report which states that belgium has complied with all the requirements concerning the protection of personal data and in particular of dactyloscopic data (fingerprints). i welcome the accession of belgium to the cooperation system and consider it an important step for the security of the union, especially in a situation such as the present one, where the eu is facing one of the greatest challenges to its security since its creation.

ecr), in writing. ‒ as regards the fight against terrorism and organised crime, crossborder cooperation and the necessary database are a key element in tackling them. i welcome the council report which states that poland has complied with all the requirements concerning the protection of personal data and in particular of dactyloscopic data (fingerprints). i welcome poland's accession to the cooperation system and consider it an important step for the security of the union, especially in a situation such as the present one, where the eu is facing one of the greatest challenges to its security since its creation.
ecr), in writing. - i am absolutely in favour of this report. this reform clarifies and improves the situation of consumers, while reducing barriers to information for tour operators and proposing improvements to national insolvency protection schemes. what has not existed so far in the package travel directive and had to be adapted to developments and changes in the market is the lack of consumer protection when buying a 'package service'. the commission notes that the commission has not provided any evidence that the conditions for the granting of the aid are met.
in writing. ‒ mr president, since the adoption of the package travel directive in 1990, the market for travel services has developed impressively as a result of the opportunities offered to consumers by the widespread use of the internet. however, many of the tourist services provided were in the 'grey area'. the need for a review of legislation in this area is evident. i believe that the proposal to update the current european union rules on package holidays meets the needs of both consumers and businesses in this fast-moving sector by adapting the current regulation to the current market specificities and by addressing legislative deficiencies. from a consumer perspective, the proposed amendment provides clearer protection for the rights of european consumers. furthermore, the proposed amendment establishes a level playing field for operators by removing obstacles to cross-border trade. i therefore support the amendment to the package travel directive and believe that it will improve our economy by allowing the tourism business to expand its reach while improving consumer protection through clearer and harmonised rules.
ecr), in writing. ‒ end of roaming from june 2017! it's about time! i support this proposal, but more importantly, in the meantime, we need to monitor the prices of international calls with the different operators, and will they not increase to compensate? i think that's a topic that will continue to be discussed. the report also included a section on the 'open internet' debate. the new rules ensure that when service providers manage web traffic, they do so on a non-competitive basis to ensure the openness of the internet.
ppe), in written form. ‒ my colleagues in the european parliament and i overwhelmingly approved the legislative package, which includes the final abolition of roaming charges and the guarantee of net neutrality. i am convinced that this is an important step towards the removal of the barriers to the digital single market and a more connected europe. work on the phasing-out of call, text and mobile internet charges started in 2007. from april 2016, prices will be reduced by almost 75% and from 15 june 2017 there will be no more roaming in the union. decisions such as these illustrate the direct benefits of well-designed european supranational solutions, which have the real potential to facilitate and improve the daily lives of millions of european citizens. last but not least, this is the union at its finest.
ppe), in written form. ‒ i was very pleased to vote for the adoption of the report by my colleague pilar del castillo and the historic compromise reached with the council on the abolition of roaming charges in the european union from mid-2017. although delayed, as it was planned to happen as early as this year, eu citizens will finally be free of a discriminatory practice and be able to use their mobile phones in other member states as well as in their own countries without fear of excessive bills from mobile operators. starting next april, we'll be able to talk from abroad for no more than 10 cents, send text messages for no more than 4 cents, and exchange data for no more than 10 cents per megabyte. the loss of net neutrality, which has been a major concern for many citizens and businesses, has been regulated in a very acceptable way by requiring service providers to treat all internet traffic equally. the abolition of roaming charges is also a decisive step towards the creation of the digital single market, which is one of the eu's grand projects, alongside the creation of a european energy union. such projects are essential to maintain europe's unity and attractiveness and to ensure its prosperity.
ecr), in writing. ‒ as rapporteur for the legal affairs committee's opinion on this dossier, my colleagues and i have made efforts to limit the scope of the directive, so as not to create an information exchange system which, instead of facilitating public administrations, would overwhelm them with processing documentation. unfortunately, the amendments we adopted were not reflected in the report adopted by our colleagues in the committee on economic and monetary affairs. for this reason, and because i do not believe that the final compromise will achieve its objectives, i voted against the report.
in writing. – the two dossiers (eniac and artemis) for discharge 2013 were considered jointly in the committee on budgetary control since in 2014 artemis and eniac have been merged into one agency- ecsel ju. however, the court of auditors (coa) has risen up particular shortcomings of the two. first of all, the coa issued a qualified opinion for the third consecutive year questioning the ex post audit strategy with respect to the legality and regularity of the transactions of the eniac ju. furthermore, it criticized artemis ju on failing to respect the 2014 recommendations of the discharge authority and did not adopt a comprehensive policy for the prevention and management of conflicts of interests. considering all that, the european parliament postponed the grant of discharge 2013 of the agencies on the 29th april 2015 and required a new more detailed report. after measuring the progress in the latter, including the new methodology for residual error rate estimation, a discharge was finally given. however, in my opinion, we need to finance fewer but bigger priorities keeping in mind efficiency and results. europe does not like useless projects. this should be taken into account by ms, but by institutions as well.
ecr), in writing. ‒ my position in principle on the discharge of any part of the budget is shared by that of my ecr group, namely that it should not be granted until the budget as a whole has received a positive assessment from the court of auditors. in this case, the court of auditors, in its report on the annual accounts of the european institute of innovation and technology for the financial year 2013, found for the second consecutive year a lack of assurance as to the legality and regularity of the operations relating to grants.
in writing. – on the 29 april, the european parliament decided to postpone his grant for discharge for the european institute of innovation and technology (eit) for the year 2013 due to numerous shortcomings in the reporting and fulfilment of budget obligations. among the most serious shortcomings the rapporteur highlights the combined error rate regarding the audit issues and material procurement errors (between 2% and 3% of total 2013 expenditure, which is above the materiality threshold of 2%), the insufficient quality of ex- ante and ex-post verifications, irregular public procurement procedures etc. consequently, the european parliament required a more detailed report considering the problematic issues. the latter was provided to the committee on budgetary control (cont) on the 29th june. the newly introduced complementary report already included serious progress on the ex-ante verifications for grant expenditure, redesigned procurement procedures since 2013 with a proactive approach towards the detection of errors etc. the collaboration of the eit agency and willingness to improve were taken into account by cont and the second report already supported the grant of discharge. this is a clear sign how work between institutions should be simplified and intensified. europe should use its budget focusing on results, integrity and producing higher returns.
ecr), in writing. ‒ here too i voted against the discharge of the eniac joint undertaking. my position in principle on the discharge of any part of the budget is shared by that of my ecr group, namely that it should not be granted until the budget as a whole has received a positive assessment from the court of auditors. the previous court of auditors report contains potential conflicts of interest and should be cleared.
ecr), in writing. ‒ the rapporteur's proposal not to discharge the european council and the council for the implementation of the 2015 budget was supported by me. my position in principle on the discharge of any part of the budget is shared by that of my ecr group, namely that it should not be granted until the budget as a whole has received a positive assessment from the court of auditors. but there are additional complications here: the council did not cooperate with parliament and did not have the necessary dialogue.
in writing. – for the seventh year the european parliament postpones its decision for grating discharge for the council. for the seventh year same questions have been raised and again more detailed information has been required. many questions are left unanswered. with the lisbon treaty, the european parliament gained co-decision budget power and the other institutions should refer to that. my appeal is that rules should be applicable to everyone. we, the institutions should be an example for the ms. how can we expect integrity, accountability and fast reactions from them, if we don't fulfil the criteria ourselves? our role is not only to talk the talk but to walk the walk to a better eu budget and results for the people in europe.
ecr), in writing. ‒ i fully agree with the rapporteur that the international response to the humanitarian crisis has been ineffective and too slow. the eu must strengthen its role as a major player in development not only for humanitarian reasons but also because this is a european neighbourhood policy issue. the continuing state of structural underfunding and lack of investment in public health is detrimental not only to local communities but also to society and economies as a whole. by failing to respond adequately to a so-called 'local problem', international actors are not only undermining humanitarian principles but are also causing additional problems.
ecr), in writing. ‒ i voted against this resolution because the volkswagen scandal affects germany first and foremost and should be investigated at national level. i agree that the systematic use of illegal devices has a serious impact on the economy and the internal market. i also strongly support that there should be an investigation into this matter and that volkswagen should be held accountable for what happened. however, i do not think it is appropriate for the european commission to conduct this investigation, despite the violations of the european directives on maximum emissions from cars. an investigation directly by the european commission would infringe national sovereignty and interfere with a scandal falling within the national jurisdiction of the states. the right and most effective way to deal with this problem in the automotive industry is through investigations and cooperation programmes at national level.
alde), in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of the resolution because the european union is a guarantor of high environmental standards and if decisive action is not taken, the current scandal risks damaging the reputation and competitiveness of the entire sector. the automotive industry is one of the key drivers of growth and innovation in europe and the commission and member states need to restore consumer confidence quickly through concrete action. it is necessary to review the current regime and to propose new measures to ensure that the application of eu regulations can be carried out effectively.
mr president, the budget for 2016 has been discussed several times regarding its priorities and the need to increase financing in key areas, and all in the context of the existing economic situation of an unprecedented migration and refugee crisis. unfortunately, the council decision to make some cuts both in commitment and payment appropriations does not correspond to the political priorities nor to the unfavourable environment. that is why we are reversing the cuts made by the council. furthermore we propose that commitment and payment appropriations be raised in several key areas, supporting the full use of all flexibility tools available in the multiannual financial framework 2014-2020. in order to provide an initial response to the immigration crisis we need to increase by almost eur 1.2 billion the commitments under the headings security and citizenship and global europe, in comparison with the commission’s original proposal. in order to overcome the weakness of the eu’s economy almost eur 1.9 billion in commitment appropriations are envisaged for the smart and inclusive growth heading. this financing supports continuation of the youth employment initiative, restores the original pre-efsi profile of horizon 2020 and the connecting europe facility in line with the commitment the committee on budgets made during the efsi negotiations, as well as additional allocations to small and medium-enterprises. in relation to the ongoing crisis affecting european farmers and in order to support the dairy sector, we have proposed increasing funding by eur 500 million under the heading sustainable growth: natural resources, in line with the measures announced by the council and the commission in september, thus supporting the main goals of the budget – to boost innovation, create jobs, deal more effectively with migration and strengthening the global role of the european union. we should not forget the commitments in the implementation of the payment plan agreed by parliament, the council and the commission back in may 2015.
our role as institutions should be to turn this 150 billion budget not just into accounting balances, but into visible and tangible investments. comparing this budget project to some of the previous ones, i have to say that it's significantly better for several reasons. one is that we have fewer priorities, but we have bigger priorities. the second is that we base the budget not on some imaginary achievements, but on concrete results, so that we have something to show people outside. last but not least, we need very strong communication with the people outside, so that they understand that we are not just here to show some numbers, but to report as many projects as possible with as many visible results.
mariya gabriel, on behalf of the group of the european people's party. mr president, madam high representative, i should like to begin by congratulating you on your personal commitment to this matter. your messages today are very clear, although we would like to hear more good news. however, the strength of your messages is their pragmatism and realism. as you have pointed out, the recent resurgence of violence and tension should be seen in a different light, and perhaps not just as an obstacle to the resumption of negotiations, but rather as an opportunity to further engage both sides in the interests of the people. similarly, internal divisions and extremist views in both camps cannot be invoked as obstacles to concrete progress. but i think today it is equally important to stress that the leaders of each side must demonstrate their will for peace, not only to the international community, but also in their internal speeches by engaging citizens towards a peaceful two-state solution. in addition, you warned us that if the internal israeli or palestinian context does not seem favourable, we must look very carefully at what is happening in the regional context. but again, maybe there are opportunities here. on the one hand, we have the commitment of the arab countries to the fight against terrorism, and on the other hand, we have recently had the nuclear agreement with iran. perhaps we should ask ourselves whether there are any possible levers for the resumption of peace negotiations. moreover, the quartet has recently stressed the role of jordan, as you have also said. but let us also think about the arab peace initiative, launched in 2002, and what might follow. i would therefore like to thank you again and to conclude by assuring you of the full support of this house for all your work on this matter.
mr president, madam high representative, yes, the last few months have shown that the international community must be vigilant and constantly mobilized if there is to be real progress towards peace in south sudan. i would like to emphasize four elements. firstly, the parties to the conflict must make a real commitment, both in the context of the peace agreement of 26 august and in that of the document signed on monday under the auspices of the african union. the security aspect requires that both sides refrain from taking measures which are contrary to the spirit of the peace process. secondly, the role of south sudan's regional partners and the international community remains key. on the one hand, for igad and the african union to monitor closely the implementation of the agreement, and on the other hand, to deal with the humanitarian horror. the international community must continue to help the displaced and those threatened by famine. child protection must also remain a priority of international aid for this country today and tomorrow. thirdly, the fight against impunity. it is a decisive element in the construction of south sudan, including the demobilization of the armed forces and the creation of a single army. those who have committed serious human rights violations must not be allowed to re-enter the army or to be part of a transitional government, especially when we know the scale of sexual violence in this conflict. finally, the union must continue to keep open the possibility for south sudan to ratify the cotonou agreement, which will be another tool for the democratic construction of that state.
madam chairman, i have always believed that one should speak when one has something to say. that is why i initiated this oral question back in june and i am glad that we are debating it today. ask any engineer out there, and they'll tell you that the simpler something is, the better it works. that is why i want simpler rules for european funds, simpler rules for the management and control of european projects. i'm sure that's what the business wants, and i'm sure that's what the people outside want. i want to believe that the reports, resolutions and questions that we adopt in this chamber have a real impact on the people of europe, on business in europe. i would like to congratulate commissioner kreutz on the efforts he is making to reduce the administrative burden, to reduce the bureaucracy. unfortunately, we need results as soon as possible, not at the end of the programming period. let's adopt standardised public procurement across the eu! let's apply electronic cohesion to reduce queues, to reduce red tape, to ask for fewer documents! we can do this. let us adopt simpler procedures for crossborder cooperation so that schengen does not impede member states. europe needs this. we need this. more europe does not mean more bureaucracy.
s&d), in writing. the revision of the europe 2020 strategy comes at a key moment for europe. the last few years have been marked by policies of strict economic discipline because of the financial and economic crisis from which the eu is still trying to recover. unfortunately, in times of crisis, national governments cut back by cutting education and culture budgets. the truth is that investing in education is often seen as a burden, rather than a long-term investment in human capital. this is particularly the case for young people, who have the highest unemployment rates. we are still a long way from achieving the objectives set out in the strategy. achieving smart, sustainable and inclusive growth goes through a clear commitment to invest in quality and accessible education for all, and through providing opportunities for young people in europe to achieve their potential. it's the only way to prevent the brain drain process.
in writing. eu cohesion policy and the europe 2020 strategy have until recently been the main tools for achieving smart, sustainable and inclusive growth in the countries of the union. today, however, we have another strong partner in this endeavour - the european fund for strategic investments. i am pleased that the role of efsi is reflected in the report by mrs ruas. i would like to counter the concerns of some of my colleagues that the differences between the european structural and investment funds and the esi funds could hamper cohesion policy. on the contrary, the efsi complements these funds by creating a higher degree of synergy and added value. the commission shall be empowered to adopt delegated acts in accordance with article 290 concerning the conditions for the participation of the efta states in union programmes. i would like to encourage all competent european and national authorities to place the interaction between the european structural and investment funds and the efsi at the heart of future cohesion policy.
ppe), in written form. the european parliament has consistently rejected the commission's proposal for new rules allowing member states to restrict or prohibit the use of gm food and feed authorised by the commission on their territory. in this way, parliament protects the public interest of all european citizens, including the bulgarian interest. the existing rules allow member states to prohibit the cultivation of authorised gmos on their territory. but when gmos are used (traded), they go beyond national borders because of the free movement of goods. the introduction of a ban on gmos in a country means the introduction of border controls on food and feed imports, which is contrary to the rules of the pan-european market. there is therefore no guarantee that the proposal will work in a single europe that seeks to remove restrictions and barriers to trade. it is clear that the national approach to restricting gmos does not provide solutions that avoid negative consequences for the single market and for the competitiveness of agricultural producers. i am of the opinion that a comprehensive new pan-european approach to the management of gmos from the authorisation process to their end-use is needed, based on a comprehensive assessment of all impacts and taking into account the restrictive policy that many countries (including bulgaria) have towards gmos.
ecr), in writing. ‒ i voted against the report by mr fernandes and mr döpre on the general budget of the european union for the financial year 2016 all sections. in this report, the european parliament's committee on budgets amended the budget proposed by the council and adopted increases of eur 4159 billion for commitment appropriations and eur 4,34 billion for payment appropriations. in this decision, the committee on budgets opposed the budget cuts proposed by the council, and that is why i am against the proposal. yes, more funds must be allocated to deal with the flow of illegal immigrants, yes, more investment must be made to develop the private sector and create jobs. but we also need to find ways to save money, especially from the huge, overcrowded and inefficient administration of the european institutions and agencies, rather than constantly increasing the budget at the expense of member states' budgets.
ecr), in writing. ‒ i do not consider it necessary to increase the number of judges in the general court. it is true that the number of cases the court has to deal with is increasing, but i do not think that appointing more judges is the solution. furthermore, to my knowledge, there is also no assessment of the proposal as to how the reform would affect and what the consequences of it would be. moreover, their appointment would mean a loss to the european budget of approximately eur 20 million per year. and in the current difficult situation that the european union is facing, i think that these funds would be more useful to invest elsewhere.
ecr), in writing. ‒ the commission should not authorise new gmos intended for cultivation or import into europe, i am adamant. the commission's college, wishing to allow member states to decide for themselves whether to authorise gmo products, has put forward this proposal. one of my main battles here in the european parliament is to defend the sovereignty of the member states and to monitor closely the application of the subsidiarity rules. in many areas, the eu cannot interfere in national legislation and the idea of member states deciding for themselves whether to ban the import and use of gm food or feed could be appealing. it is important, however, to note that in practice the idea is not applicable in the existence of a common market. that's why i turned her down.
ecr), in writing. ‒ for those who are not sure what exactly 'novel foods' mean, those that were not consumed on a mass scale in the european union before 1997, when the first regulation on the subject came into force. this proposal simplifies the rules for the whole union and adapts them to technical and scientific progress. the new rules will subject novel foods to safety assessment and authorisation at eu level. if there is a possibility that a new food could affect human health, the european food safety authority will have to carry out an assessment. when decision-making at eu level can make it easier for traders and more secure for consumers, so that everyone wins, i can only support such a proposal.
in writing. ‒ ladies and gentlemen, i supported the report by mrs herling on the introduction of national ceilings for emissions of six major pollutants - sulphur dioxide, nitrogen oxides, non-methane volatile organic compounds, ammonia, methane, and dust particles (less than 2.5 micrometres) - to be achieved between 2020 and 2030. i would stress, however, that i voted against the texts on the inclusion of methane and ammonia in this legislation, in order to protect the interests of farmers who have recently been exposed to too many risks. however, the european parliament eventually succeeded in excluding the limits for enteric methane, which is caused by the degressive processes in farm animals. but methane is essentially a greenhouse gas, and there are already limits on it in climate legislation, and any duplication of standards causes chaos and new difficulties for the people and businesses affected. my support for the new legislation is also due to the fact that the european parliament did not accept an increase in member states' obligations over the european commission's proposal, which would have made it difficult to achieve the objectives and would have cost many billions of euros in additional costs. despite the acknowledged need to limit harmful emissions, which kill hundreds of thousands of people every year, legislation must be commensurate with the real possibilities and costs of implementing it.
ppe), in written form. ‒ the european citizens' initiative is the first tool in the hands of eu citizens to give them the opportunity to address the european commission directly with a request for a proposal for european legislation. the conditions for this are the collection of at least one million signatures in support from at least seven member states. the commission has not received any comments from interested parties. since then, only three initiatives - 'right2water', 'one of us' and 'stop vivisection' - have been considered eligible, managed to collect the necessary number of signatures and received a formal response from the european commission. although these initiatives were declared successful, the citizens behind them were disappointed that they did not ultimately achieve the desired impact on european legislation. as a result, european citizens seem to have lost their initial interest and faith, and now, unfortunately, we are seeing a declining trend in the number of new initiatives. i support the european parliament's resolution on the comprehensive assessment of shortcomings and the search for solutions. the goal is a simple, clear and easy-to-use mechanism. let us not discourage this form of active democratic participation in the political life of the european union. i find it a commitment on the part of every mep to work for the maximum effectiveness of the european citizens' initiative.
in writing. ‒ i welcome the review of the europe 2020 strategy, as the situation in the eu in recent years and the analysis of its implementation clearly demonstrate the need for it. i'm not a fan of "reforming it", which is what he's suggesting. i believe that the european union needs to concentrate its efforts on growth. the europe 2020 strategy makes this possible. it is encouraging that some of the objectives in it seem achievable at this stage. however, it is necessary to focus on the remaining objectives by reviewing the seven flagship initiatives to stimulate progress on each of the priority themes. we must intensify our efforts to avoid repeating the mistakes of the lisbon strategy and to strengthen citizens' confidence in the path we are taking.
mr president, commissioner, the union - as you have said - must ensure a more efficient, modern and rapid management of its borders, while at the same time strengthening the checks at the crossing points. thirteen member states already operate their own entry/exit system, but structured and systematic cooperation within the union is lacking; the status quo is no longer a viable solution, we must move forward. i will raise four points. firstly, the introduction of a smart border system will enable us to exchange data effectively, not only between the different national authorities but also with europol or interpol, to detect, for example, the use of stolen passports through the automatic and automated consultation of their files. it is therefore clear that we must change the way we operate, making cooperation the rule rather than the exception. secondly, the full potential of this system must be exploited by allowing law enforcement access and by providing for a sufficient period of detention for this system to have added value. the sis and vis are already accessible to national authorities and europol. so the question i ask myself is: why not smart borders? thirdly, this system must be adapted to all types of borders: sea, land and air. the current situation shows that we cannot give priority to the protection of certain frontiers at the expense of other types of frontiers. finally, the union's credibility on the international scene also depends on its ability to ensure full security on its territory. so yes to mobility, but yes also to safety. answer is "blue cardboard". thank you for asking. i think mr etheridge really must draw the main conclusion from what has been happening for months, namely that if the union had reacted in common with joint action, we could have dealt better with the current crisis and above all we would have had a better management. so we now see how the member states alone are managing this crisis. so i am going to go with the option of 'european union together through a common approach'.
mr president, i supported the proposal to reject this commission proposal on the use of genetically modified organisms because i believe that in its present form it will lead to malformations and problems in the functioning of the single market and may lead to the presence of gmos where they are not wanted. the commission's model will now allow the use of gm products in part of the european single market. however, within this free market, and the absence of border controls on imports and exports between the individual member states of the european union, the proposal put at risk those member states, including my own country, bulgaria, which do not want gmos and products derived from them to be used on their territory. i support the call for the european commission to prepare a comprehensive analysis of the impact of introducing such a measure and to make a detailed proposal. it must be in the interests of the majority of european union citizens who are against the use of genetically modified food and feed in their countries.
in writing. europe is one of the most sought-after tourist destinations in the world, with millions of tourists coming here every year. the tourism sector generates 2.9% of eu gdp and employs 3.3% of the union's workforce. it's no secret that what attracts them most is our rich cultural heritage. that's why we have an obligation to preserve and promote it. i share the view that europe's cultural heritage has enormous strategic potential which we must develop and make the most of. in order to create the conditions for sustainable growth in europe, it is necessary to promote cultural tourism as a driver for sustainable economic and social development at local and regional level. in addition, i believe that in addition to the most popular tourist destinations, preference should be given to smaller and more unusual destinations, including my home country, bulgaria.
mr president, as i see it, this discussion is about jobs and growth. it is about how to make our economy more competitive and able to meet the new challenges ahead of us. some say we need to turn challenges into opportunities; for instance that the migration flow can increase regional growth and help us address labour market productivity and demographic challenges. i am actually unsure how straightforward this would be: without the skills and competences, asylum seekers could fall into irregular employment, with disruptive effects on the labour market. we must remember that challenges are not always opportunities and that they must always be carefully addressed at the same time. what we need to do is make europe’s economy strong enough to respond to difficulties if they arise instead of simply looking at the best-case scenario for the moment. this means serious structural reforms in places where they are most needed, and investing in europe’s growth. one point i would very much like to emphasise: making the investment plan truly european is also key to the european semester. it must support the member states and regions that were hardest hit by the crisis and those with fewer internal resources. it must also invest in value-added areas, such as technology, innovation, digitalisation and small businesses. this, in turn, means that the investment plan for europe must absolutely go hand in hand with the capital markets union, the european single market and the development of the digital single market. therefore, i call on the commission not only to ensure the continuity of its policies, but also to enhance the link between its separate initiatives.
in writing.– trust is central to the success of one of the eu’s most ambitious projects – the digital single market (dsm). it is the cornerstone of the information society, allowing citizens and businesses alike to make use of technology and expand their activity online. the lack of trust and understanding is the main obstacle to the digitalisation of europe and one we need to address. trust is also instrumental so that practices such as zero-rating are beneficial for europe by providing businesses with more freedom to innovate but at the same time obliging them to use this freedom cautiously. for the first time ever net neutrality is recognised as a principle in europe. providers across the continent need to respect this and manage the future development of their services accordingly. now that we are about to abolish roaming charges in europe, we should put more effort into providing consumers with a broader choice of high-quality services. only then will we create the right digital environment that encourages innovative business thinking and delivers the best services to our customers. furthermore, we should explore the possibilities of using zero-rating to improve digital skills in europe – the main prerequisite for the success of the dsm.
madam president, mr schmit, madam commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the setting up of the trust fund for africa is still relatively new, but it should be remembered that there have been 11 european development funds before it. these programmes failed to anticipate and prevent the crisis we are experiencing today. so let's not stop asking ourselves why? i would like to stress three fundamental aspects: yes to smart financing, yes to anticipation and yes to results. firstly, the need for adequate but above all thoughtful and intelligent funding. caution is in order. let's make this a true multi-donor fund with our public and private donors and let's think together about how we can give this fund a truly global reach. the second is anticipation. these funds should enable us to build up the capacity of these countries, beyond the emergency, to anticipate the next crises as much as possible. in this respect, what is the added value of these funds compared with the old development funds? how is consistency of objectives ensured? thirdly, the results. if we are to increase the effectiveness of aid, perhaps it is time to say that one of the possibilities would be to narrow down our priorities to better target them. it is necessary to strengthen the mutual obligations of donors and partner countries with regard to the results achieved by defining and systematically referring to performance indicators. finally, let us apply the principle of giving more so as to receive more. finally, the efforts of the member states to participate in the fund must be strengthened and the commitments must be kept, but we cannot ask our african partners in valletta to do more if our actions do not reflect our words in the framework of the tools we create ourselves. it is time to redouble our efforts, and while learning from the past, migration can no longer remain a black box in eu-africa cooperation. answer is "blue cardboard". madam president, thank you for the question. i have two pieces of the answer. first, i think we have missed the big lesson of this crisis. if we had acted together as a union many years ago, this crisis would not have had the scale we face today. so, clearly, no, i don't agree with the approach that's being taken. secondly, just look at the figures which show how much some indicators have improved thanks to the european union's funding. we have less poverty, fewer malnourished children, women are better cared for in health care. for that reason, we should continue. yes, it is not easy, but we must continue because, in a few years, we get results. whether we like it or not, the union is stronger and more powerful than any single state.
in writing. i generally support the idea of funding development projects for countries affected by conflict, poverty and misery. improving living standards in these countries may stem the tide of economic migrants coming to europe. this is what the european commission hopes to achieve with the new trust funds for syria and africa. the problem is that for decades the european union has been helping developing countries, but the result is a steadily increasing number of illegal immigrants. it means that by pouring more money, the problems will not be solved, especially in syria. before any funds can be allocated, the problem of the islamic state must be solved once and for all and peace must be established in the country. as regards the trust funds themselves, i believe that they should be aimed at establishing cooperation with countries of origin and transit, which would ensure the effective implementation of readmission agreements, building the capacity of these countries to cooperate in the fight against migrant smugglers and, last but not least, supporting refugee camps. the situation in these countries is getting worse and worse, and that's what's driving people to flee to the european union.
in writing. the european budget is an investment engine for the eu, but also an instrument to support third countries. in both cases, a focus on performance, clear priorities and measurable results are required. the trust funds for syria and africa are one of the instruments to support third countries. the two funds already have €2.3 billion of the budget. the european commission expects member states to contribute an equivalent amount to the funds. these instruments come with their own shortcomings, such as the voluntary principle of contributions and the lack of a timeframe for transactions. the lack of clarity around the expected results is also a serious problem. bulgaria has approved eur 100 000 to be transferred to the 'syria' fund, but a clear vision is needed for investing these funds in order to achieve the desired result. the flexibility of the eu budget, rather than a fresh resource, is needed to finance the two funds. draft amending budget no 8/2015 provides for the release of a resource to be reimbursed to the member states in accordance with the own resources decision and regulation (ec) no 1150/2000 for its implementation. member states should decide on the amount of their contribution to the two funds on the basis of the trust they have earned and a clear understanding of the priorities and expected results.

ppe), in written form. ‒ isa2 is the programme the european union needs to overcome the electronic barriers between member states. the programme is expected to start operations in january 2016 and will aim to support the development of operational digital solutions to be made available to public administrations in europe. the main beneficiaries will be the national, regional and local administrations in the eu countries. they will use digital systems to ensure the interoperability of their it systems and to provide electronic services that will allow easier application and implementation of cross-border projects and programmes, exchange of experience, etc. the work on the ec proposal in the european parliament was divided between the itre, imco and libe committees. i hope and believe that, in addition to cutting red tape, this programme will also be a step towards a common procurement portal for european funding, a more efficient and transparent european union. it will also support citizens and businesses. at the same time, the real output of the programme must meet the real needs of the administration in order to ensure technical assistance and maximum impact from the use of european taxpayers' funds.
in writing. egovernment is an important element of the future development of a united europe. not only does it save us money and time, but it also brings with it greater transparency. i supported isa2 because, for the first time, the programme identifies not only public administrations at european, national, regional and local level as beneficiaries of isa2, but also citizens and businesses in europe. in the amendments to the text, we have focused on improving access to public information for companies, and especially smes, which are the biggest victims of clumsy and outdated administrations. for businesses, this also means opening up easier access to new markets.

ppe), in written form. ‒ to change eu legislation, proposals need to be approved, first, by the council unanimously and, second, by the member states, in accordance with their respective constitutional provisions. it's a slow and difficult process, but sometimes the cause is worth fighting for. in the proposal voted in the european parliament, the new rules require that the ep elections be contested by officially approved leading candidates for president of the commission. these candidates are to stand in the european elections and be formally nominated within 12 weeks of the vote. also, all eu citizens living abroad should be able to vote online or by post.
