mr president, according to eurostat data, the percentage of children living in poverty and social exclusion for countries such as bulgaria, romania and hungary is as high as 40%. child poverty leads to lack of access to quality food, health care, drop-out. limited opportunities form a vicious circle of social isolation. that is why i support the report on reducing inequality and poverty among children and i believe that our policies should be directed towards addressing the causes of child poverty and towards supporting children and their development, unemployed parents, single mothers and parents of disadvantaged children. a specific strategy for investing in children under the european social fund and the fund for the support of the most deprived is also needed, as well as the inclusion of the eradication of child poverty among the objectives of the europe 2020 strategy.
in writing. ‒ i agree that there is a lot of data and statistics that show that child poverty is increasing. bulgaria continues to have the highest child poverty rate in the european union. data from the national children's network shows that about 637,000 children in the country are at risk of poverty and social exclusion. this is almost twice the eu average. however, to solve such a problem, measures are needed, but i do not find that this report proposes such specific measures. it's just a way of exposing the issues in the sector. the proposal for a special guarantee fund for poor children, who would receive free education, health care, clothing and so on, may be a wonderful idea, but it's utopian. who do you think will fill this fund and how can i ask bulgaria to offer such free services when our gdp per capita in 2014 was 5,500 euros and that of belgium for the same period was 33,800 euros? children are our future and to offer them health and education is our moral duty! we have all signed the un convention on the rights of the child, and it imposes legal obligations on states to provide the necessary conditions for children's rights to be exercised effectively.
in writing. ‒ my colleagues in the european parliament and i overwhelmingly approved a resolution calling for tackling child poverty in europe. statistics show that in 2014, the average level of child poverty in the eu member states was 27,7%, or 26 million children. in other words, approximately one in four children is at risk of living in poverty. children are most vulnerable to the consequences of lack of food, clothing, safe housing, limited access to health care and education. unfortunately, bulgaria is second in this statistic, with a high proportion of children at risk, along with romania and hungary. the european parliament therefore puts the issue on the agenda and calls for greater commitment and adequate policies to combat child poverty and its causes. the main role in tackling these difficulties is undoubtedly that of the member states through their social policies. europe also needs to invest more in its future through concrete measures targeting children in unemployed, single-parent and special needs families.
in writing. for over two years, the group of the progressive alliance of socialists and democrats has been calling for the creation of a child guarantee. we need this mechanism now more than ever. the challenges facing the eu - the aftermath of the terrorist attacks in paris and the refugee crisis - require action in several directions. education and environment are the factors that shape an individual's views and attitudes toward others and the world. poverty and social exclusion are among the reasons for the encapsulation and marginalisation of some communities in the eu. for these reasons, i believe that a key priority in the fight against child poverty should be to reduce early school leaving rates. i believe that the establishment of the 'child guarantee' will give impetus to measures to ensure that every child has free access to education and healthcare, a decent place to live and food that meets their needs. it is this potential that makes me feel confident and committed to supporting this report.
in writing. – i supported the resolution, which urges member states to reduce inequality and tackle the pressing situation of child poverty across the eu. as a bulgarian, whose country ranks 2nd in child poverty level in the eu, i believe it is vital for the country’s future to ensure access to education, health and welfare of all children and their families. there is no doubt that reducing, and even abolishing, child poverty should be on top of our agendas. in september 2015, world leaders adopted a new set of sustainable development goals, which will be followed by all countries, including the 28 eu member states. we cannot achieve true sustainable development without investing in those who will actually support it – today’s children, everywhere.
in writing. ‒ i cannot support and have never supported policies based on the presumption of granting privileges. this report calls for just that - give more to the marginalized. and what's the effect? none. practically proven for so many years. the cohesion policy cannot be achieved if it is constantly repeated that racism, xenophobia and sexism are to blame. it's mantras and spells. why does no one say that the marginal communities themselves are resisting en masse and insisting on receiving generous sums but living as they know how? when will we admit that this model of integration is impossible? when are we going to admit that what we've been taught for so many years has failed? i urge you to stop this hypocrisy. pouring hundreds of millions into a bunch of useless programs hasn't changed anything. this policy of tolerance and conciliation is untenable. i don't support policies like this that encourage social parasitism. this report suggests exactly that. no, thank you.
in writing. – cohesion policy is probably the strongest eu instrument to fight inequality and support sustainable territorial development in its regions. i support the report and the current 2014-2020 cohesion framework that puts forward new measures for improving the situation of marginalised communities, such as the investment priority. i believe it could have a positive effect on the situation with the marginalised roma communities in my home country, bulgaria.
in writing. ‒ i did not support this report because i do not think it is aimed in the right direction, namely international cooperation and coordination. the question of whether the eu should have a seat in an enlarged un body is the question that is being raised here and the rapporteur believes that such a seat should be given to our union. but i think it is a union that should cooperate, based on the idea of peace, but not become a federal state with its own government. the sovereignty of the member states must be preserved, which means that the eu is not and cannot be a state and cannot be represented as a full member of the un security council, but it can certainly work for coordination between member states and international cooperation.
in writing. ‒ i supported the report because the european union is a global actor and must play a leading role in safeguarding world peace, security, development and human rights. the european union, with its unique structure, has a strong set of tools that can be implemented within the un to set the global agenda. i believe we must continue to be a leading global actor in the fight against poverty, in promoting sustainable economic, social and environmental progress in developing countries, because that is the guarantee of lasting peace and stability. it is necessary to continue to lead in supporting un operations in areas of mutual interest, in particular in the protection of civilians, and in particular women and children affected by armed conflict. all of our objectives can only be achieved when the eu has solid partners on the world stage with whom to work closely together to tackle global challenges.
in writing. culture is an important element in the eu's external diplomatic relations because it has the merit of stimulating dialogue and mutual understanding. i am of the opinion that it should be an essential component of political dialogue with third countries and be systematically integrated into development projects and programmes. the refugee situation and the paris attacks make the need for increased cooperation between the eu and the un even more apparent. that is why i am advocating that culture should be given a more visible and prominent place in the post-2015 millennium development goals. i share the view that to build a stronger relationship between the eu and unesco, we need to think beyond financial aid and joint project management. i believe that we need to lay the foundations for a permanent dialogue and deepen cooperation in the fields of education and culture in the long term. that is why i called for the establishment of an annual structured high-level dialogue to enable us to meet common challenges in a more sustainable way.
in writing. – i voted in favour of the role of the eu within the un report, because it is an important step towards enhancing the ongoing cooperation between the two bodies. the eu and its member states play an essential role and contribute to the work of the un system in various ways and formats and this role needs to be strengthened further.
on behalf of the alde group.– mr president, first of all i would like to congratulate rapporteurs fernandes and deprez and commissioner georgieva for their contribution to achieving the political priorities of the 2016 budget. the eu budget for next year started with political guidance based on the three highest priorities: employment, enterprises and entrepreneurship. it is time to make the final assessment on whether the priorities have being followed correctly regarding the entire job done in the respective committees, plenary sessions and interinstitutional negotiations in the context of the existing economic situation and refugee crisis. in the 2016 budget, the overall level of commitment appropriations is set at eur 155 billion, and payment appropriations at almost eur 143.9 billion. this does not completely match parliament’s position on the budget. nevertheless, it is a step in the right direction, because we reversed some cuts made by the council. furthermore, in implementation of the political priorities, we have achieved a budget that is even better in comparison with the draft proposed by the commission. the increased appropriations and the mobilised eur 1.5 billion within the flexibility instrument ensure more funding for migration, humanitarian aid, horizon 2020, small and medium enterprises and erasmus+. the youth employment initiative will be kept on as a high and shared political priority. we should continue to work in order to phase out the backlog of outstanding payment claims, and on the upcoming mid-term review of the mff. a serious debate for the full introduction of performance-based budgeting should be linked to the prevention of the subjective financial programme cuts in the member states.
in writing. 150 billion euros, hundreds of pages of numbers and text, three institutions, months of negotiations. we know these things, the people we've been involved with. but tonight i want to talk about values. the budget equals priorities and investments in service of each of the more than 500 million citizens. it puts people, business and their needs at the center. the 150 billion is a bridge to our goals for a better tomorrow. young people, horizon 2020, erasmus+, cosme, infrastructure, entrepreneurship, innovation and education - these are our values as the european parliament. these are also european values, measurable with tangible results. despite the different positions and challenges, the institutions sat around the table and achieved results because we had one thing in mind: a stronger european union tomorrow. the budget is europe.
member of the commission.– madam president, in the aftermath of the tragic events in paris and the developments in brussels, we are all more than concerned. our society is appalled by the terror that extremists are trying to poison us with. while we are still grieving for the innocent people who lost their lives in paris, we cannot allow this to turn into a general psychosis. we cannot allow fear to prevail, because fear is also a threat. we cannot allow the terrorists to demoralise us and win, because terrorists target innocent people, but they also target our values and our way of life. we have been through this before, and we know that our strength is our unity and our resilience. our only way forward is to act to prevent these actions from happening and to ensure the security of our citizens and our democracies. this report could not be more timely for our collective response to fighting terrorism and radicalisation. in these challenging times for europe it is of crucial importance that the institutions of our union see eye to eye on the way ahead. the european agenda on security, adopted in april this year, set out the principles and key actions in this area. this agenda is the reason why we are not just reacting to events now. we are implementing and accelerating the actions we already had in the pipeline to strengthen our resilience to terror. an important example of one such action is the crucial new rules to control firearms that we proposed just last week. i will very soon come forward with an action plan on their trafficking as well. however, let me start with our work on prevention. our strongest partner and guarantor for the delivery of effective preventive measures is the radicalisation awareness network (ran). more than 2 000 local practitioners with different backgrounds from all over europe are working on addressing the root causes of radicalisation. since the year 2011, the ran has produced policy recommendations as well as practical guidance and training material to better prevent and address radicalisation, on schools and the prison and probation environment, on the foreign terrorist fighter phenomenon, and on the development of counter-narratives. the ran has now been reinforced with a centre of excellence, which has been up and running since 1 october 2015 and which will support the ran and help it expand its reach further, even to priority third countries such as the middle east, north africa and the western balkans. many of the perpetrators of these vicious acts are eu citizens, born and bred in our societies. this means that our work has to start right here in our countries, in our societies and in our neighbourhoods. let me now refer to the abuse of the internet and social media for terrorist purposes. we have started our engagement with the industry, and i will formally launch the eu internet forum on 3 december. i can inform you today that i will personally invite the chairman of the committee on civil liberties, justice and home affairs (libe) to attend. i expect this partnership with the industry to lead to concrete outcomes in preventing and countering radicalisation leading to violent extremism. one of the key goals of this forum is to improve our cooperation in removing terrorist material that may push our youth towards radicalisation. the eu internet referral unit, established within europol, is instrumental and very helpful in this regard. it can build on the expertise of the european cybercrime centre within europol, which has many years of experience in dealing with illegal content. so we are by no means starting from zero. in this context, i welcome the call to reinforce the mandate and resources of the european cybercrime centre to enable it to face these new challenges. our inter-institutional discussions to finalise the europol reform will also touch on this. the second priority is to support the development of effective counter-narratives. there is progress in this area, through initiatives such as the syria strategic communication task force, which supports eu countries in developing messages and carrying out targeted communication campaigns. the commission is both financially and politically supporting the continuation of this project. today’s report touches on several other important elements that we are working on. we are working to improve evidence and intelligence-sharing across the european union, in full respect – as is obvious – of fundamental rights. as a matter of priority, we will look into all available options to strengthen our tools against terrorist financing. this includes measures for freezing terrorist assets. i will very soon propose a directive to strengthen our framework on the criminalisation of terrorist offences, particularly travel with a terrorist purpose and terrorist financing. this is how we will tackle the problem of foreign terrorist fighters. i will also soon report on the implementation of the commission’s communication on the prevention of radicalisation. i hope that we will continue our constructive dialogue on matters of security and, more specifically, the pending proposals and envisaged initiatives. we will need your full support and engagement on the difficult way ahead. europe needs to be united and to defend its values with the necessary rigour. you are well aware of the ongoing work on different counter-terrorism measures. we will discuss this in detail tomorrow and will have other opportunities to do so. today, our discussion has focused on counter—radicalisation. it has focused specifically on one of the root causes of the problem, and this is where the solution should come from as well. this report will serve as a basis for a continued exchange of views about our prevention policy. i would like to express my thanks to the rapporteur, ms dati, and i look forward to a very useful and constructive discussion tonight.
the rapporteur on the opinion of the committee on culture and education. ladies and gentlemen, much can and will be said on this undoubtedly most topical subject, but without urgent measures, we will not achieve any visible results. yes, we have certainly produced a report on the subject, in which we have suggested to the commission how to work on this problem. however, immediate action is needed. monitoring of all is-related organisations and suspicious activities, detailed analyses of their activities, banning them where necessary, checks of the educational materials and content of books and information available on global networks are needed. in the last few weeks, all of us european citizens have witnessed the consequences of the radicalisation of european citizens who do not fit into european communities and their recruitment by terrorist groups and organisations. i have been warning for a long time, and it is no secret, about this problem and its dangerous spread throughout the european union. we must immediately, ladies and gentlemen, open our eyes to the fact that this problem has now far exceeded its regional character and is not regional. europe is facing war, and i fear that if we continue to turn a blind eye to the problem, it will be too late.
madam president, i am sure that all of us in this house would like this discussion to be less painfully topical. the report must urgently become the basis for a legislative initiative by the commission. the external borders of the union and, i stress, of the european union and not just of the schengen area, must be strengthened in terms of finances and resources. this measure does not deprive citizens of their rights, but on the contrary, it guarantees their security. secondly, we need controls on the routes of foreign fighters travelling to and from the middle east, including concrete commitments for joint action with third countries and especially turkey. the emergency measures do not abolish the prevention of radicalisation. we can no longer just see that in our own countries, in our own societies, there are lost generations, isolated ghettos, poor people without prospects who easily become victims of radical movements. do we really have the courage to break stereotypes and have real integration? we need to give these young people a future and the confidence that they belong in our world. that's how we get them on our side. and hopefully, the efforts of the last few days to form a truly great international coalition against daesh will finally destroy the source of evil as quickly as possible.
in writing. ladies and gentlemen, the steel sector, which marked the beginning of european integration, is today experiencing the worst crisis in its peaceful history. over the last five to eight years, over 40 million tons of steel capacity has been shut down and hundreds of thousands of jobs have been lost. the crisis in the nonferrous metals and metal alloys sector is similar, facing strong competition from countries that do not have europe's strict standards. at the same time, policies to decarbonise the economy impose a burden of additional carbon costs and high energy prices, which undermines the competitiveness of this industry. given the huge overcapacity in third countries and dumping by foreign players, the trends become even more gloomy. decisive action is needed to prevent a new external dependence of the eu on steel and non-ferrous metals. the fight against climate change must culminate in paris with the creation of a common environmental regime to reduce emissions, in order to prevent the phenomenon of carbon leakage outside the eu. free allowances for emissions from sectors at risk of loss of competitiveness are maintained, innovation and efficiency in industry are encouraged. the instruments for trade defence against unfair competition, including caution in the recognition of market economy status, need to be applied systematically.
i've been listening to this empty debate for over an hour with great disappointment, colleagues. apparently most either don't understand or don't want to understand the reasons for the latest attack on european citizens and civilization. islamic radicalism is not just terrorism, it is a real military attack on europe. multiculturalism and integration don't work and won't work in the islamist ghettos of europe. the real enemy is in power in saudi arabia and turkey, who are funding and organizing the islamic state. with the wave of illegal immigrants in europe, european jihadists are returning to sow death, chaos and destruction. while we welcome these people with teddy bears at the train stations, we will send our loved ones with candles in the squares, esteemed gentlemen. mr. juncker, the fact that you're talking the same way after charlie hebdo and today, tells me you haven't tried to learn anything new. the commission's proposal is partly ridiculous, irrelevant and not working. the immediate closure of external borders, the cessation of the flow of migrants, the extradition of illegal immigrants, the smashing of islamist ghettos in europe, the physical destruction of the 'islamic state' are necessary. all the rest, ladies and gentlemen, is just empty talk.
mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, we will not tire of repeating that our first reaction is to show solidarity with the parisians and the french. it is not france that is under attack, but europe, our freedoms and our values. however, being supportive does not mean that we should ignore our own shortcomings. i have three points to make. the first concerns the management of our external borders and police, judicial and intelligence cooperation between member states. the results are still largely inadequate. there are also important gaps in the linking of the different databases. the second point concerns external action. on the one hand, it is better to cooperate with third countries, particularly in order to stop the financing of terrorism, and on the other hand, we must anticipate the consequences and the follow-up of our actions on the ground. the weakening or destruction of the islamic state must not leave the door open to other security threats. thirdly, it is important to strengthen our work on the prevention of radicalisation. that's the big lesson of these attacks. an analysis, including sociological, of the various individuals and their profiles is necessary. the security response is not enough.
in writing. eleven months after the attacks on charlie hebdo and the cashmere shop in paris, we continue to speak of the need to step up the fight against terrorism at european level. the same measures we discussed in january, we are discussing now - the adoption of the passenger data directive, tightening controls on the eu arms trade, better cooperation between the different security services, preventing radicalisation among european citizens. that is why i call for the swift reinforcement of controls at all eu external borders following the attacks of 13 november in paris my country bulgaria has introduced 100% checks on all those crossing the bulgarian-turkish border. the security of europe's external borders must be guaranteed so as not to jeopardise the free movement of people within the eu and schengen. to this end, the passenger data exchange directive needs to be adopted by the end of 2015. it is unacceptable that european services cannot currently trace when foreign fighters leave europe and, more importantly, when they return to commit a terrorist act. we don't need to wait for more casualties to bring these urgently needed measures to a conclusion. european citizens expect us to act quickly to restore a sense of security in the eu.
in writing. ‒ i have decided to support this draft amending budget because it is a regular annual adjustment exercise. after an initial attempt to link this annual adjustment to the migrant and refugee crisis, the council has been able to successfully refuse this as this would have clearly been without a legal basis, and nor have the member states ever committed to match the commission’s expenditure. nonetheless, with these changes, the procedure was de-politicised and in fact now represents a decrease in member states’ gni—based contributions, which i fully support.
in writing. ‒ i support the idea that more money is needed to tackle the migration crisis, but i voted nonetheless abstained on the report mobilising the flexibility instrument for immediate budgetary measures under the european migration programme . i have taken this position because i am concerned that the mobilisation of these funds exceeds the agreed budget ceilings and spends all the money earmarked for dealing with so-called 'unforeseen circumstances'. exceeding the budgetary ceilings could call into question the sustainability of the union budget, and spending all available funds provided for in the flexibility instrument would hamper the union's ability to respond to other instances of 'unforeseen circumstances' in the coming years. instead of allocating more resources and burdening the budget, the commission should review budget lines with a poor implementation rate and make savings to finance measures to deal with the immigration crisis.
in writing. ‒ i strongly support this report. finally, adjustments are made to do something about the grave natural disasters that occur in europe. in bulgaria we often experience first-hand how devastating these can be. people lose their belongings, houses, life and even suffer secondary losses as well through a lack of clients, etc. we need to be capable of responding a lot of faster in supporting them as, unfortunately, often the smaller states or the ones struggling economically are affected. therefore, often the state itself may not be able to step in immediately. thus, we need the mobilisation of additional sums for the eu solidarity fund.
in writing. ‒ i have voted not to support this agreement. in my opinion, there is a need for additional support but not at the overall expense of member states, which are already under a lot of pressure. in fact, there should be more funding available to handle the crisis but still too much money is distributed in an inefficient way. just a simple sending of large amounts of money does not solve the problem. we need targeted support. currently, as announced under this budgetary procedure, there is a lack of available margins under a number of headings and a lack of redeployment from wasteful budget lines to priority areas.
in writing. – i voted in favour of the eu budget for 2016. in my work and activities as a member of the european parliament, among other initiatives, i fully support the efforts in the spheres of education, combating youth unemployment, development of entrepreneurship and smes and research and innovation. i welcome the decision for erasmus + to receive a 30 percent increase over 2016, reaching an eur 1.8 billion budget for next year, which will tremendously boost education exchanges. moreover, the escalating migration crisis urgently requires further investment. therefore, i am in favour of the budget and, in particular, the increase of funding in these fields.
in writing. ‒ our desire to curb tax and other financial fraud is commendable. what concerns me, on the one hand, is that a number of member states have refused to answer the commission's questions, and that almost all multinational corporations have refused to participate in the commission's meetings. this leads me to believe that these companies have a clear reluctance to participate in the adoption of new rules. the other thing that makes a bad impression on me is that the amendments proposed by our group, and those we supported, put forward by the epp and alde colleagues, were not accepted. i am also concerned that all the proposed measures will either not be complied with by certain member states of the union, or if they are complied with, they will only affect smes, which are the main taxpayers, and will not affect mncs, which are to a large extent those that have armies of lawyers, financiers and accountants who are constantly looking for loopholes in the laws to save their employers taxes. i'm also concerned that if we tighten the measures too much, a number of the current big taxpayers in the eu will take their companies offshore, which instead of helping to increase collection, will reduce the union's money. that's why i don't support the report.
in writing. ‒ the the report gives the example of the luxscs scandal, which brought the issues of corporate tax base reduction and aggressive tax planning to the attention of the public and the media by exposing dubious tax practices. stresses that tax avoidance by some mfis may lead to near zero effective tax rates on profits; acknowledges that in the wake of the economic crisis and the luxscs scandal, attempts to tackle aggressive tax planning by mncs have been high on the political agenda of the member states; the report calls on the commission to fulfil its duty as guardian of the treaties by ensuring that eu law and the principle of sincere cooperation between member states are fully respected. calls also for an investigation into the role of financial institutions in supporting harmful tax practices; i support the resolution.
in writing. – eur 1 trillion a year: that is how much tax evasion and tax avoidance is costing european tax payers every year. i support the report that urges for better tax ruling measures among eu and, in particular, the special focus that is made on multinationals’ preferential tax treatment. if multinationals are treated in the same way as domestic firms, this would help entrepreneurship activities, which i am promoting and fully supporting as a mep.
mr president, the recent attacks in paris and mali have clearly shown that radicalisation and terrorist threats have no national borders and require adequate measures at european and global level. to avoid tragedies of this scale in the future, we must focus our efforts on providing prevention. in this context, i warmly welcome the paris convention of ministers of education, which aims to instill common european values such as freedom, tolerance and the fight against discrimination in young people through education from an early age. europe is going through difficult times and it is time to realise that we need to invest in inclusive education, which creates responsible, tolerant and open-minded citizens who are able to distinguish fact from opinion. i am of the opinion that educational institutions should cultivate critical thinking among young people, which helps them to understand and evaluate information correctly in the context of the internet and social networks and thus be less vulnerable to manipulation and propaganda.

in writing. ‒ i voted against the eu strategic framework on health and safety at work 2014-2020 because i and my colleagues from the eesc are convinced that there is no need for such a framework and for european legislation on osh. i think that the regulatory framework at national level is sufficiently comprehensive and clear, but i must point out that it is often ignored. we must therefore focus on the implementation and enforcement of safety rules by workers and employers, rather than creating new and new frameworks that remain on paper. my position is that this is entirely a matter for the national states, where there are already structures in place and functioning to ensure that health and safety at work is respected.
in writing. the international day for the elimination of violence against women is an occasion to draw the attention of the commission to this problem once again. in each member state there are national or ngo-organised 'hotlines' for women, adolescents and children who have experienced violence. each of these organizations has tried to use its expertise and resources to help those in need. this fragmentation and lack of coordination of efforts cannot lead to a unified policy, accurate statistics and effective measures to overcome women's problems. i find that the rights, equality and citizenship programme could finance the creation of a common european platform for quick and easy access by all women who have suffered violence. to this end, there should be both an eu-level body and bodies in the member states that jointly pursue a common policy to prevent and support the fight against violence against women.

mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, since the last mandate i have spoken of the need for the european union to set up a genuine diplomacy on migration. now we're here. the valletta summit clearly set a milestone in international cooperation on migration, for the establishment of joint and multidimensional policies. this must be a point of no return in a more regular and balanced dialogue with our african partners, but also with other key partners in the mediterranean and the middle east, to face our shared responsibilities. the review of the european neighbourhood policy is also a good opportunity to do so. with this action plan, the challenge of adopting a multidimensional approach has been met. it is a very comprehensive document, but most of the commitments reflect objectives which have been the objectives of european development policy for years. eleven edfs and our results have not been enough to stem the mass exodus. our development policy can become more economically efficient. i would also remind you that this is an imperative in view of the revision of the cotonou agreement. i would like to stress three points. first, regional integration and capacity building of partner organisations such as the african union and the regional economic communities of africa. the european union must support regional mediation efforts and do more in conflict prevention, including through the african peace facility, an instrument we are not sufficiently familiar with. second, the potential of links between migrants and their home communities. as emphasised in the action plan, it is necessary to ensure that transfers of funds actually serve the development of the countries of origin. which brings me to my final point: circular migration. we cannot prevent people from leaving situations of conflict or poverty, but we must think in the medium and long term about incentives to return, because the development of the african continent also depends on the development of human potential, which is the continent's main economic resource. answer is "blue cardboard". mr aker, i am not sure i have understood the question correctly because it seems to me that it raises two completely contradictory aspects as a question. in any case, my position is very clear: no member state can face alone the migration challenge we are trying to face today. only the united is strong. so today, perhaps, it is high time to learn from what has just happened to us, precisely because we have not been united and united enough. let us move forward and stop declarations or contradictions and amalgamations. now let us take action and above all, let us take common action.
mr. chairman, we had high hopes for the meeting in la valletta, months before we talked about the expected results, but the news from there is not enough. the only news from the summit was the emerging 1.8 billion euro fund, the unwillingness of member states to participate in it and the disapproval of african leaders over the lack of funds. we expected agreements to establish 'hotspots' with the countries of origin of migrants, which would be the first crossing point. we expected serious commitments to create working legal avenues. europe can make no good decisions without its partners in third countries. legal channels are the best way to fight human trafficking. however, it was not clear what specific general solutions could be taken to break up the criminal networks of the canal-keepers, who send people to certain deaths either at sea or on the balkan roads. there was also no answer to the most important question how will joint operations be carried out to return illegal immigrants who cannot obtain refugee status. this dialogue cannot be fully effective without the participation of the transit countries turkey, lebanon and jordan.
mr. president, i would like to the chairman, mr the commissioner, mr minister, how long are we going to stand by and watch medieval barbarians cut off heads, crucify, drown and burn people alive, beat women with stones, blow us up even in our own cities? until evil has settled permanently on our continent? it is time for decisive action. we have an economy, we have potential, europe is an economic giant! but you know very well that we are not taken seriously in the political and military aspect. we lack the determination, the speed and the efficiency to eliminate the dangers. yes, our development policy should help the countries of africa and the middle east so that people there have perspective and are not motivated to go to our borders. the countries we help and from which the migrants who are subject to return must take back their own citizens unconditionally. it is time to create a pan-european security service with a common command, a pan-european intelligence and counter-intelligence agency. no country can better deal with today's global challenges on its own. it's clear that air strikes are necessary but not sufficient for ultimate success in syria. i hope that the light from the vienna talks and the g20 will not dim or go out even after the events on the turkish-syrian border. we need coordinated action by the international community now to eliminate the barbarians and cut off the funding of any terrorist movement, no matter where it comes from. enough of this talk! anyone who directly or indirectly funds and deals with terrorists is a terrorist, anyone who deals in human trafficking is a criminal. and it should be clear that all such assets in the european union and other democratic countries will be confiscated immediately and those individuals, companies, organizations or governments that engage in such activity must be held accountable.
mr president, mr commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, georgia is a country with a rich history and a distinct national culture. the country is undoubtedly part of european history and it was rightly celebrating the signing of the association agreement with the european union just a year ago. particularly impressive is the fact that more than 69% of the population support the agreement, and that there is a nationwide consensus for the country's membership in nato. for many years georgia was part of the soviet union and today it is seeking its european identity. as a person coming from a former communist country, i am clearly aware of the challenges facing the young georgian democracy. the disturbing events of the past few months are proof of this and we cannot but tolerate them. however, we must insist that all parties respect the rule of law, the independence of the judiciary and the freedom of the media. georgia needs our continued support to cement its european path.
member of the commission, on behalf of the vice—president of the commission/high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy.– mr president, dear colleagues, i would like to thank you for your interest, your support and your determination on this very difficult and controversial issue in our neighbourhood, with a lot of repercussions in many aspects. our discussion today has confirmed that we share the same concerns and that we agree on the need to continue support to moldova on its path towards political association and economic integration with the european union, as well as in its efforts to build a democratic society and to overcome the interest of individuals for the sake of the country. moldovan society expects its leaders to put an end to state capture; to fight corruption, carry out real reforms and recover the funds stolen from the banks. we remain ready to support moldova in all of this, but it is only moldovan political leaders who can make it happen. together with the implementation of the association agreement, this is key to enhancing the resilience and sustainability of the country. thank you for this very rich discussion and for your determination.
in writing. madam commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, with the signing of the association and stabilisation agreement, moldova has embarked on the path towards deeper european integration. but this process is seriously threatened by the lack of political will to adopt the necessary reforms in the judiciary, the banking sector and the fight against corruption. moldova is in an extremely difficult political situation - 3 governments in one year! the stagnation of reforms and european integration is causing the population to lose faith in the institutions and the state is fragmenting. this is a serious problem because in moldova there are many ethnic communities along with moldovans, romanians and russians there are also very large bulgarian and gagauz communities. only by protecting the democratic, social, political and economic rights of all minorities will moldova be able to ensure its territorial integrity and the country will be able to move forward towards deeper european integration. moldova is a european country and we are all connected to it, historically and culturally, and i hope that one day we will accept it as a full member of the european union.
in writing. ladies and gentlemen, like georgia and ukraine, moldova signed an association agreement with the eu a year ago and has been enjoying its deep european integration. the european family is the future of moldova and its people, despite the country's difficult historical past. unfortunately, in recent months we have witnessed a number of violations and deviations from european values in the country. the widespread corruption in the upper echelons of power and the lack of the rule of law are calling into question the country's european path. the demands of civil society for the depoliticization of the judiciary and the fight against corruption are not heard, and this benefits the pro-russian parties in the country. i therefore believe that the eu should support a new form of government that works to meet the demands of civil society and restore moldova's pro-european course. we must help ensure a free electoral process that will be conducted in accordance with the highest european standards, so that we can give the country a realistic european perspective.
madam president, we welcome the appeal for a wholly new approach to the management of eu investment and spending. certainly the eu budget should be better aligned to the long-term strategic priorities. at the same time, it should be more agile and allow a higher degree of responsiveness to the changing parameters of the dynamic environment. in order to achieve the optimal outcome from the introduction of performance-based budgeting, not only should the intended results be clear, but the financial resources should also be spent lawfully and the intended result accomplished. in order to reduce the level of errors, it is of the utmost importance to undertake massive simplification of the procedures. despite the positive steps taken by the commission in introducing the ‘budget focused on results’, we should be aware of several issues. firstly, this new approach should be introduced carefully and should take account of the impact of irregularities on added value. secondly, there should be a precise timetable for its introduction, and lastly, we should achieve a common understanding among the eu institutions about the indicators that we will use to measure results.
madam president, with this year’s 38th annual report, the court is inviting us to start applying an updated approach to our discharge procedure for determining whether the commission has satisfactorily carried out its responsibilities for implementing the budget. thus the report represents a most timely and valuable input to discharge: designing and putting into practice results-oriented budgeting is an urgent necessity so that citizens recognise that their money is being put to the best possible use. in this regard, three features of the report deserve particular notice. the structuring of chapters to reflect the headings of the multiannual financial framework (mff) which came into force in 2014 is a most valuable input towards the mid-term review of the mff. chapter 3 focuses on the extent to which the performance framework enables the commission to monitor and steer progress towards europe 2020. the stated purpose is that checking the legality and reliability of accounts is intended to assist in identifying risks affecting economy, efficiency and effectiveness. this is precisely the right focus for ever-better results-oriented budgeting, and it is here, i believe, that the court deserves a particular word of thanks. (the speaker agreed to take a blue-card question under rule 162(8))
blue-card answer.– in the briefest possible terms, i believe that the focus over the years has been a bit one-sided on error rates and observance of rules. this, of course, has to continue to be of particular concern. however, it should be used as an indicator to find sectors where programmes are not performing and there is an excessively high rate, and to reorientate the whole exercise of discharge towards results.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, the european union has been a consistent supporter and participant in the reconstruction process in afghanistan since 2001. as we did 15 years ago, we stand today for a peaceful, democratic and stable afghanistan. there have been some positive developments in the country, but unfortunately it continues to be a hotbed of terrorism and extremism, which has led to thousands of innocent victims. recently, we have witnessed ethnic conflict, and large minority groups have been raped and attacked. after the united states and other nato members withdrew their troops from afghanistan, the situation in the country has obviously deteriorated. we can't leave her alone because there are many disturbing processes going on. the taliban are becoming more active, and we're also seeing a significant presence of isis in most provinces. we need to support the authorities to ensure the safety of the innocent population. we need to work together to root out terrorism and extremism from the country, because it's a threat to all global security.
in writing. ‒ i supported the foreign trade committee resolution to the european parliament because it set out the objectives of working towards the ratification and adoption of the trade facilitation agreement at the forthcoming tenth wto ministerial conference in nairobi, kenya, as a step forward in the development of trade. the chances of this agreement being adopted are high, as it is being considered separately from the doha development agenda, which has been stalled for almost 15 years. it is well known that the differences in the positions of the doha development agenda negotiating parties are profound and the chances of making progress in this regard are minimal. this resolution recognises this and proposes that efforts be directed towards the adoption of sectoral international agreements such as the trade facilitation agreement, the information technology agreement and the natural resources agreement. only in this way will the international community be able to overcome the stagnation in the development of trade relations caused by the long-standing negotiations on the doha development agenda.
in writing. ‒ i supported the resolution on the accession of ecuador to the trade agreement concluded between the eu and its member states and colombia and peru. the resolution is comprehensive on the one hand it draws attention to the current economic and social situation in the country and in particular to the strong economic growth in recent years and the significant reduction in poverty, and on the other hand it criticizes environmental problems such as deforestation and issues related to indigenous communities. the european union needs to develop its trade relations with fast-developing regions such as latin america, as this will provide many opportunities for mutual investment and overall contribute to the economic growth of both regions.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of the new animal welfare strategy for 2016-2020 because i believe that animals should be protected from abuse and cruelty. according to various studies, violence against animals has its reflection and continuation in violence against humans. an example of inhumane treatment in bulgaria is the use of horse-drawn carts or donkeys on the road. this is happening in a european capital like sofia and it's unacceptable. there's also evidence of organized pet fights. i believe that a strategy is needed and that action is needed against those who commit such acts and for that reason i support the joint resolution.
in writing. ‒ today i voted in favour of the european parliament's resolution calling on the european commission to draw up a new and more ambitious animal welfare strategy for the period 2016-2020. the commission should also address, among other issues, a number of important issues relating to the needs and problems of specific animal species, such as pets and problems relating to their registration and identification, the growing need to develop an action plan on trade and trafficking in exotic and wild animals and the welfare of animals kept in captivity and in circuses. in some member states these issues are regulated to a greater or lesser extent, but in the absence of uniform and harmonised european legislation, all issues relating to the welfare of these animals will be subject to different interpretation, which will ultimately lead only to legal uncertainty. this in turn would only bring losses losses to european competitiveness, public health and the health and welfare of the animals themselves. in this connection, i believe that the european commission should give these matters careful thought and, in the time remaining until the end of the year, present us with a concrete and adequate policy taking account of all the problems listed by parliament.
in writing. ‒ i supported the european parliament's joint motion for a resolution on a new animal welfare strategy for the period 2016-2020, as i believe that a single legislative framework at european level is needed to strengthen the common understanding of animal welfare. that framework should cover the different aspects of the legislation, including animal health, zootechnical conditions, organic production and official controls. a pan-european approach will not only strengthen the competitiveness of the internal market but also send a clear signal to third country trading partners to respect equivalent standards for imported animals and products. the high level of european standards is directly linked to the quality of products of animal origin and hence to consumer confidence. the common agricultural policy may provide adequate funding for animal welfare activities in order to prevent the diversion of production and trade to countries with lower animal welfare standards. further incentives under the common agricultural policy are needed for investments in farms with a view to improving animal welfare. this will not only make the final product more competitive on the market, but also improve the position of producers in the supply chain.
in writing. ‒ i support this resolution and believe that there should be a strategy to ensure the education of children in emergency situations. education is a guarantee of development and has an impact on the widest range of areas of our daily lives. it's also a guarantee of a good future. we cannot allow a regression and an increase in illiteracy, that would be detrimental to our society. when we admit migrants fleeing conflict to the eu, all those who have gained status must be educated by us, because their integration into our society is mandatory. otherwise, marginalization can only deepen, and this leads to many subsequent problems and above all radicalization - the most urgent threat of the moment, which has however been rooted in the western world for many years and is deepening due to the lack of a strong and decisive policy of the western countries.
in writing. ‒ the easier access to european funds is, the more likely my country is to be able to absorb a large part of the european funds allocated to it. i hope that will happen after this resolution is adopted, especially since two of my compatriots are involved in drafting it. in many respects, european administration is cumbersome and complex, and that is why i always support such documents that make it easier to apply for european projects. of course, these facilitations must not lead to bad practices, non-compliance with the rules or even abuse. i support this motion for a resolution, but i ask the responsible authorities to follow up and punish all offenders with the utmost severity.
in writing. ‒ the main objective of the resolution on cutting red tape in european financing is to reduce the paperwork, time and money lost in applying for, managing and reporting on eu operational programme projects. the document sets as important objectives the switch to fully electronic communication with the beneficiaries of european funds and the reduction of data and information requirements for project promoters. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competent authority' in the context of the directive on the protection of the environment.
- what? the chairman, mr minister, commissioner, firstly, on the domestic front, we have to do our homework together to protect the external borders of the european union as a shared responsibility of all member states, fight against human trafficking, a common intelligence agency, common rules on migration. secondly, in terms of foreign policy, the question we face is what is better with or without turkey, we will deal better with this global challenge. of course the answer is not only with turkey and with lebanon and with jordan and with the african countries we have to work more closely, but we have to say clearly and be frank the negotiations with turkey are different compared to the previous accession negotiations. the negotiations are open ended and no decision has been taken on turkey's full membership of the european union. such membership is not possible at this time. it is of course important for us to negotiate with turkey, to have a predictable partner in the south-east, a neighbour who respects european law, respects its commitments, a neighbour who accepts our legislation. through negotiations, we can also support turkish civil society and turkish democracy. all of this has to be done by opening and closing the negotiating chapters. but we have to watch closely whether our outstretched hand will lead to an improvement in the human rights situation and media freedom. we must first of all do everything in our power to ensure that the millions of people on the syrian border with turkey, lebanon and jordan stay there and do not go to the european borders. every euro invested in this is many times more euros saved by the livelihoods of these people if they were in the european union. we must also, of course, monitor the funds we give for this, together with the united nations high commissioner for refugees.
