answering a question asked by raising a blue card. of course we need to cooperate more in europe and a common intelligence service and a common command. you know very well, or you may not know, that unfortunately this cooperation between our special services is not going as well as it should. only a few countries share information, and for me, addressing global challenges comes through our joint efforts, including a common european army, an idea that goes back to the 1950s for a common defense community in the european union, not duplicating nato and our commitments to the euro-atlantic treaty.
first vice-president of the commission.– madam president, when i hear mr de graaff and some of his colleagues refer to the beauty of european civilisation, the way they do it i am reminded of what ghandi said when he was asked what he thought about european civilisation. his answer was: ‘it would be a good idea’. let us not lose track of the reasons for people coming to europe. they are not coming to europe because mrs merkel invited them, they are coming to europe because they want their children not to be victims of barrel bombs thrown from assad’s planes. (applause)they want to come to europe because they do not want to be slaughtered by daesh or al-nusra in other parts of syria. they want to come to europe because after years of an atrocious war, they have nowhere else to go if they want their children to have an education, if they want to have healthcare, if they want a future that every single one of you wants for yourself and your children as well. we are talking about human beings. we are not talking about fortune-seekers invited by mrs merkel. mrs merkel took responsibility to offer international protection when it was necessary. we would have less trouble in europe if every single leader of europe had done the same when it was necessary at the time. (applause)now let me insist on some of the push factors, because it is important for the agreement we have reached with turkey. as i said before, why are some people leaving from turkey or through turkey to europe? talk to them, try it! these are nice people, educated people; these are people who can tell you a story. they will tell you: ‘i have left because after two years i am finished, all my savings are gone and i am not allowed to work in turkey’. so what did we do? we agreed with the turks that they would allow syrians to start working in turkey very soon. that is part of what we have discussed. they say: ‘i am leaving because there is no education for my children’. this is exactly what we are going to do: we are going to use the eur 3 billion to make sure that there are more facilities for education for children where they are now in turkey. they say: ‘we are leaving because our living conditions are horrible and we do not have access to medical care’. these eur 3 billion will be used to improve living conditions and to improve medical care. it is not just the right thing to do, it is the smart thing to do, because keeping people there and offering them help there is much cheaper for the european taxpayer and is much cheaper in moral terms, because it takes the smugglers out of the equation. it takes the danger out of the equation for people having to cross the mediterranean in very difficult conditions. so when we have these agreements on these projects, it is to take away some of the push factors. this, i think, is a smart approach. visa liberalisation. why are we on this trajectory with the turks? because if we aligned our visa policies, it would be easier for the turks to act at their external borders when people come from pakistan, bangladesh or other countries who do not have the right to international protection and should then be stopped at the borders. visa liberalisation also means that we can create the right conditions for turkey to have the right kind of passports: biometric passports. it would make it much easier for us to control our borders. these are positive measures for the european union. do not portray everything as though we are on our knees for turkey and giving things away. then about the chapters. i really do not understand some here, like ms keller, who just now said: ‘we do not want to talk, we have given up on human rights, we accept everything’. benchmarking chapter 23 and 24 is the best possible way of engaging turkey in a debate on human rights. opening chapters 23 and 24 is the best possible way of forcing the turks to account for what they are doing and to try and help them reform the system so that they move away from the mistakes they make today. so do you really think that we will improve the situation in turkey by turning our backs on the turks and saying: ‘oh, let it go’? i do not think so. i think this engaging with them will yield results. and yes, of course, we are engaging with them because we need to stem the flow of those coming to europe who do not have the right to international protection. of course we are doing that – but it is in our interest to do so, it is in the interests of the refugees to do so and it is in the interest of turkey to do so. there is no shame in coming to an agreement based on mutual interest.
in writing. the eu-turkey deal is a continuation of the wrong european policy to deal with the migrant problem. this agreement is a capitulation of europe and of the european leaders who, by signing it, admitted that they cannot defend their borders on their own. the three billion euros that will be given to turkey should be invested in refugee camps, where basic needs such as water, food and medical supplies are lacking. whether that happens depends entirely on turkey itself. why doesn't the eu demand that turkey open its labour market to refugees and send their children to school? because if that happens and the living conditions of the refugees improve, they will not risk their lives to flee to the eu. it is clear that this is not in the interest of turkey, which will continue to facilitate the smuggling of migrants into the eu and will continue to support the 'islamic state'.
in writing.– the circular economy (ce) package is a forward-looking step, an excellent example of the policies we need to adopt in order to tackle challenges like climate change, job creation, economic and social development. it is key to the new business model in europe, which should focus on innovations and efficiency, smart regulation and competitiveness. through the circular economy, we can achieve eur 600 billion for eu businesses, create 580 000 jobs and reduce eu carbon emissions by 450 million tonnes per year. for the circular economy to work and deliver on these ambitious goals, we need to ensure that small and big companies alike are supported in the establishment of a more resource-efficient industry. i thus urge the commission to establish the necessary regulations and incentives in order to ensure that smes are well integrated into the ce. in this regard, the planned establishment of a european resource efficiency excellence centre, the reinforcement of the resource efficiency element of the enterprise europe network and the work stream of clusters are welcome developments we need to build on. another important step would be making small enterprises aware of the funding possibilities for resource efficiency improvements, such as the financial instruments under cosme and the natural capital financing facility (ncff).
in writing.– the eu has called 2015 european year for development and once again proved to be a pillar in world development policy. we are the biggest donor of official assistance and development policy is an important area of our foreign policy. the primary objective of eu development cooperation is to reduce and eradicate poverty which has multiple and interlinked economic, political, and security causes. not only that, our aim is to promote human rights and to spread democracy, good governance and the rule of law. unfortunately, the ongoing conflicts and humanitarian crises in neighbouring regions proved that development policy cannot achieve these objectives as a stand-alone policy, but must be integrated into a broader approach which combines foreign and security policy, trade and any other external action policy instruments. the eu need a comprehensive and coherent strategy for its development aid in order to meet the united nations’ post 2015 development goals. goals which are very, very difficult like goal number 16 – live in peace. it is not only a moral obligation, it is our duty to work for a stable, prosperous and more equitable world.
author. mr president, commissioner, i would first of all like to thank you, commissioner, but also the luxembourg presidency and, in particular, minister mutsch for their efforts to unblock the situation with regard to this draft directive. unfortunately, two years after the european parliament's vote on first reading, i could almost quote word for word from the speech i made in plenary on that occasion. parliament has done its part and it is up to the member states to roll up their sleeves and put into practice the commitments they have made for gender equality in europe. let me first of all recall the facts, because it seems that there are still some unrelenting sceptics about the potential of this directive. on the one hand, experience has shown that a greater presence of women on boards translates into better governance and economic performance. again, what are we waiting for to unlock the potential of women and empower us to achieve economic and social goals? on the other hand, the facts also show that legislative pressure works. between the publication of the proposal for a directive and today, we have gone from 15.8% to more than 20% of women on boards. the trend of progress has been markedly stronger since there has been european legislative pressure. companies anticipate the effects of the directive and select competent women. i would also like to stress once again the snowball effect that we can expect from this directive: positive effects at different levels of the hierarchy, in particular through the related measures that could be put in place. so for those of you who are not convinced, at least be pragmatic. the second general comment is that the legal basis chosen, the instrument chosen, which is the directive, and the proposals made by parliament already offer a great deal of leeway to the member states. in the end, all we have been waiting for, and have been waiting for two years now, is a commitment from the member states and consistent action to achieve real results. in conclusion, three key words: responsibility, flexibility and concrete commitment. and behind each of these words, the member states.
in writing. mr president, the proposal for a directive on women in management boards, which was supported at first reading by the european parliament, is a small legislative step in view of its narrow scope of impact, but it provides an important example of the real implementation of gender equality policy in the european union. without this example, we would not expect that smes would also encourage women to take up managerial positions. the issue of women's career development is not just a nuance in gender equality policy, but also part of an economically sound balance in decision-making. i therefore call on the council, the member states and the forthcoming dutch presidency to make every effort to overcome their differences of opinion and to demonstrate in practice the importance of a horizontal gender equality policy.
mr president, commissioner, colleagues, today we are discussing once again the situation in hungary, as we did about six months ago. in view of all the other undoubtedly worrying issues, i think this debate is completely unnecessary, it is a waste of time, a waste of effort, with one sole purpose - to make another pointless criticism of the government of viktor orbán. i am even less in favour of the idea of preparing a lot of resolutions on this subject. how will these resolutions be different from the one that was proposed on june 10th? on 10 june we asked the commission to carry out a monitoring exercise on the situation of democracy, the rule of law and fundamental rights in hungary. yes, all member states must respect the obligations they have assumed and protect fundamental rights, but yes, we all have the right to an opinion and the right to express and defend that opinion, but we should not interfere in the monitoring procedure, as it does not fall within the competence of this legislature. according to article 17 of the treaty on european union, only the european commission has the competence to conduct such an investigation and only it has the right to determine whether european law and treaties have been infringed. ladies and gentlemen, i have been listening to this discussion for some time and i think the following: i just heard that viktor orbán's behaviour was dangerous. no, i don't think he did. i think the behavior of those who don't understand what's happening in europe is dangerous. the behavior of those of our colleagues who do not understand that the situation has changed radically, that we are currently at war and that this war will not end unless we defend our values, our national states. the rest is a waste of time, the rest is largely, ladies and gentlemen, empty talk and hypocrisy.
in writing. ‒ a special committee on tax rulings in the european parliament already existed. the mandates of the special committees are six months each, and this was even extended until the end of november 2015. now they want to make a new one with the same name and functions, and even the first one had no clear results. i stress that this is a matter for the member states and that tax policy cannot and should not be decided at the level of the european union. i do not understand why there is a need for another special committee of this kind, given that there is a working group on these matters, and it is chaired by my colleagues on the committee on economic affairs. but if such a committee is to be set up, although my vote is against it, i am not against its current composition.
in writing. – i welcome the decision for the work of parliament’s special committee on tax rulings to be continued under a new mandate for six months. harmful corporate tax regimes and practices at european and international level could hinder considerably economic growth. therefore, i voted in favour because i fully support the ‘good governance’ practice in the tax area (i.e. more transparency, exchange of information and fair tax competition).
in writing. ‒ i support the eu-liechtenstein agreement on taxation of savings in the form of interest payments. the creation of safe and enforceable measures for economic operators equivalent to those in the taxation directive is a positive development. we still suffer too often from financial crimes taking advantage of the different levels of security and safeguards. by levelling out, we can tackle financial crime in a more efficient and transparent way. the updating of the amendment protocol is a welcome change, creating oecd global standards that are compatible with eu standards. data protection is now more important than ever, especially in light of recent political developments.
in writing. – i am in favour of the agreement between the eu and liechtenstein concerning the exchange of information on the bank accounts of each other’s residents, because i believe it will allow a more transparent and open flow of information regarding tax fraud. tax evasion siphons away money that could be invested in productive resources needed to diversify the economy and address social and environmental problems.
in writing. ‒ i did not support the report on the european ombudsman's special report on frontex's own-initiative inquiry oi/5/2012/beh-mhz, as i consider frontex to be an agency that is different in nature from other eu agencies and institutions. the current situation is not the time to impose an additional administrative burden on the agency. hundreds of thousands of migrants enter the eu every day, and instead of criticizing frontex, we need to strengthen its capacity to respond adequately to a crisis that could destroy the eu.
in writing. – i support the european ombudsman’s position that frontex set up a complaints mechanism on fundamental rights infringements occurring in the course of its operations. fundamental rights of migrants and asylum seekers should not be undermined in any way and it is the right of every person to be able to file a complaint when he or she believes his rights are being violated.
in writing. ‒ it takes each citizen an average of one and a half hours a day to travel to and from work. in monetary terms, each hour spent on the road is 80% of that employee's hourly wage. on the other hand, the main polluters in urban areas are private cars. we must therefore aim at both reducing greenhouse gas emissions and reducing the travel time of people to and from their workplaces. so far, no other way to reduce greenhouse gas emissions has been found than to switch from conventional fuels to biofuels or electricity. of course, these measures will not be effective unless new ways of managing traffic are introduced. the introduction of the its is a step towards that governance. of course, we must also pay attention to the protection of the health of pedestrians and other road users. i support this report and hope that it will lay the foundations for the creation of sustainable urban mobility, which will lead to less pollution, shorter travel distances and, of course, fewer road deaths.
in writing. ‒ today, the european parliament adopted a resolution calling on member states and the commission to promote sustainable urban mobility. the approach aims to move towards resource-efficient transport systems and to significantly reduce pollution and congestion in cities. the trend towards urban population growth is not likely to change in the foreseeable future. this requires the need to pursue specific policies for urban mobility and work to ensure sufficient parking spaces and accessible, fast and environmentally friendly public transport. urban mobility projects are usually large and expensive undertakings. that is why the european union's co-financing is key.
in writing. – sustainable urban mobility is an important element of improving citizens’ quality of life, strengthening the economy and, thus, achieving a more sustainable development on a regional and european level. in bulgaria, concrete steps are being taken towards the implementation of the green city philosophy but much work is left to be done. that is why i am in favour of the report and the dedication of part of eu funds for sustainable urban mobility projects.
member of the commission.– mr president, firstly i would like to warmly thank the committee on legal affairs and congratulate the rapporteur, cecilia wikström, for the excellent and successful work on the trade mark reform package. both this work and the very constructive spirit that i observed in the negotiations facilitated reaching an agreement after more than two years of complex discussions. regardless of its apparently technical character, the trade mark reform package constitutes a key element for the eu’s competitiveness and innovation strategy. the reform package will foster innovation and growth by making the trade mark registration system all over the eu more accessible and efficient for businesses, whether in terms of lower cost, increased speed, more predictability or legal certainty. the clear benefits of the compromise package are there for all to see. the package marks a significant step in both the modernisation and the further harmonisation of the trade mark system in europe. allow me now to highlight just a few of these benefits. firstly, the agreed fee reductions at european union level will lead to savings of up to almost 40%, in particular for businesses that seek protection of the registered eu trademarks beyond an initial period of ten years. secondly, the reform of the directive will ensure more streamlined, efficient and harmonised registration procedures across all trademark offices in the european union. faster and less burdensome procedures will constitute a great improvement for growing companies, mainly smes, which roll out their business beyond one member state and are seeking trade mark protection. the arrangements for common application requirements and the future availability of administrative opposition and cancellation procedures at national level are a good illustration of this. thirdly, trade mark owners in the eu will have better tools to fight against counterfeit goods, in particular when these illegal imitations are in transit through the eu’s territory. this will help prevent abuse of the union and its transit routes in its role as a central hub for the worldwide distribution of such goods. at the same time there will be appropriate safeguards in the interest of legitimate trade. and fourthly, the reform will bring modernised rules and increased legal certainty by adapting trade mark rules to the modern business environment and clarifying trade mark rights and their limitations. in relation to the regulation regarding the procedure of preselection and appointment of an executive director of the agency in alicante that is responsible for administering the eu trade mark system, it has to be stressed from the commission’s point of view that the final text is not aligned with the principles of the eu’s interinstitutional common approach on decentralised agencies. notwithstanding this shortcoming and also the fact that key elements of the commission’s proposals concerning the budget were not retained, the final text of the regulation will nevertheless bring about major improvements in the governance and accountability of the agency. thus, considering the overall compromise that has been achieved, the commission welcomes very much and endorses the agreed compromise package.
the following table is provided: first of all, congratulations on your victory, and now, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank my colleague mrs vikström and the other shadow rapporteurs for their efforts. i think that after long and difficult negotiations we have managed to achieve a relatively good result. the agreement has many positive aspects and we in the ecr group have also fought for this good result. however, one of our major concerns remains the budget surplus of ohim and the reference to the budget of the european union. yes, it specifies where and how it can be used, promising to be used to fight copyright infringement. but still, in my opinion and in the opinion of a large part of my group, this money should be returned to the national ip offices, because they are the ones who can best allocate how exactly it should be used and be useful to those who have paid to have a community trademark. this money could be invested, for example, in an anti-counterfeiting programme, but only the national offices could determine where and what these funds would best be used for.
in writing. the european microfinance facility progress is an opportunity that all member states should seize to build a positive impact on their local economies, boosting youth employment and providing sustainable jobs for disadvantaged people, immigrants and others. the instrument is also not currently applied in all member states and, where it is applied, there is insufficient awareness and it does not reach potential beneficiaries. unfortunately, the rate of rejection of projects for applications is still too high in the member states. very often, entrepreneurs prefer to apply for expensive loans because the bureaucracy from the time of applying to receiving the loan, and in monitoring afterwards, is too heavy. the instrument should complement, and not overlap, with funding from the european social fund. we have eur 919 469 000 available for the new programming period. maximising efficiency, delivering real added value and measurable results is needed e.g. monitoring the percentage of microfinance entrepreneurs realised progress i recommend: clearer application procedures, more information, less red tape and easier access for micro-enterprises.
i strongly protest against the visit of turkish prime minister davutoglu to bulgaria at a time when the republic of turkey is openly participating in the civil war in syria on the side of the islamist terrorist groups. i assess turkey's military activity as an unacceptable violation of international law and the sovereign state borders of the republic of iraq and the syrian arab republic. it is beyond any doubt that the republic of turkey is the main organiser of the migrant flow that is flooding the european continent. in this connection, i find the turkish authorities' demand for 3 billion euros of our money to be paid to them to meet their obligations, to be blackmail and racketeering. to protect the european union, of which we are a part, it must reject the erdogan-davutoglu racket, close its borders to illegal immigration, isolate islamist turkey, which is aiding the islamic state and sabotaging the international effort against the jihadists. we owe nothing to the republic of turkey, on the contrary, it has outstanding debts to the descendants of thracian refugees in the amount of millions of dollars under the angora treaty it signed. i insist that the question of the payment of these turkish debts be put clearly and loudly to the bulgarian authorities. i insist that no support be expressed for the continuation of turkey's negotiations for membership of the european union, because there is no place for it in the union and davutoglu must understand this in sofia. he also needs to understand that sofia will not support the allocation of billions to the islamist regime in ankara.
blue-card question.– mr lamberts, do you not think that what is really lacking is domestic demand? that is the critical factor which is stymieing growth. mr dijsselbloem has laid the accent on competitiveness, and the first point in the five presidents’ report is these competitiveness councils. now that only means additional pressure on wages so that seemingly things will start growing. do you not think that is nonsense?
today there were a number of concerns, but it is really good to remember that for the third year in a row the european union is actually on the road to economic recovery. we expect european gross domestic product to increase by 2% in 2016. yes, the percentages are still low, but the important thing is that we are moving in the direction of growth. partial consumption is also increasing, financing and employment are improving. perhaps it is time to acknowledge not only the problems and shortcomings but also the successes of european economic governance. for example, the juncker plan is already in place, with over €44 billion of additional funding mobilised for around 100 projects in 18 member states. the commission communication on the european structural and investment funds yesterday underlines that by 2023, €637 billion of european and national co-financing will be invested through over 500 different programmes under the european structural and investment funds. this is the place, and as my colleague nibler mentioned, of national responsibility. member states should increase the use of financial instruments and make full use of synergies between the structural funds and the efsi and other sources of funding, such as horizon 2020 at project level, at financial instrument level and through investment platforms. finally, i would like to stress the importance of a strong currency in the european union. the uk's desire for the european union to be declared a multi-currency union could be exploited by a number of other member states, which would make the european union more uncertain in its fiscal and economic outlook.

in writing. - no, i do not support this report. we all know that there have been no problems with the member states' contributions so far, but on the other hand we want to introduce stricter measures and increase the interest on late payments, even though there are none or almost none. most countries have different accounting systems for budgetary contributions, and we want to see a uniform approach. let's make changes first in this direction and only then demand new measures. yes, there are good things here, like the depoliticization of the budget, but that's not enough for me to support the report. that's why i voted against it.
in writing. ‒ the allocation and adjustment of member states' contributions to the union budget should not be the subject of political bargaining but should be a technical process. the proposed changes to the methods of contribution adjustments are intended to avoid a repeat of the 2014 scenario. the report stresses that these adjustments to contributions should be made as automatically as possible in order to avoid political interference in the agreed ways of financing the union budget. the report also emphasises the need for effective rules on how member states make their contributions available to the union budget in a timely manner in order to enable the commission to manage its cash efficiently.

in writing. ‒ i fully support the objective of harmonising trademark registration systems to ensure compatibility and complementarity. such modernisation is necessary and vital to improve effective enforcement. modernising terminology, streamlining the different applications and registration procedures, and improving cooperation are key to improving the current state of affairs. however, the budgetary issue remains a cause of political division. the return of the considerable budget surplus of ohim back to the eu budget, in whatever form, i consider to be a superfluous, insignificant and hypocritical decision taken solely by the ec. this is not a decision the commission can make on its own. in fact, i think that this money should be put back into the national intellectual property offices to develop and promote the trademark system. in fact, this money will be used for these purposes only. i am therefore deeply concerned why these funds should go to the ec budget at all.
in writing. ‒ the aim of the commission's proposal is to promote innovation and economic growth by making trade mark registration systems more accessible across the eu. in an increasingly competitive environment, the increase in the number of trademark applications both at national and eu level and the increase in the number of trademark users demonstrate their crucial role in terms of market success and commercial value. this development has been accompanied by increasing expectations on the part of stakeholders to improve the quality of trade mark registration systems, which are more consistent, accessible to the public and technologically up-to-date. the european parliament shall approve the council's position at first reading and instruct its president to sign the act after verifying that all procedures have been duly completed.

in writing. - mr. and mrs. president, ladies and gentlemen, i was pleased to vote in favour of the report on achieving 10% interconnection in the eu. although this objective seems too small for the larger goals of creating an integrated energy market, it reflects the current realities in the union's energy sector. twelve member states on the periphery of the eu are still isolated from the internal electricity market. i support the regional approach of working to increase connectivity between neighbouring countries, which can better identify which connections are the most priority and have the greatest impact on their energy security and on the single regional electricity market. therefore, projects of common interest to the community in the field of electricity infrastructure should also be prioritised on a regional basis. central and south-eastern europe needs more cooperation and coordination in building regional grid infrastructure that makes the best use of both the existing large electricity generation capacity and the huge and largely untapped potential for renewable energy. however, i note that the connectivity ratio criterion in relation to installed capacity in the member states is one-sided and does not reflect the objectives of energy security and a single energy market and more parameters should be selected in the future to track energy connectivity in the eu.

madam president, commissioner, i welcome the commission's proposal, which meets the requirements of efficiency, accountability and credibility. the establishment of an integrated european border management system is a matter of course which we have put off for too long. today, i would like to ask you two sets of questions. a first series on the actual operation. firstly, do we have a timetable with specific deadlines for each commitment? to set up a truly european border guard corps, the agency needs to have its own staff and equipment and, in addition, the capacity to react quickly in the event of a crisis. so who will train the guards? how long will it take to deploy a team in case of an emergency? if the agency takes the initiative to carry out an operation, will the member states still have to agree to it or not? my second question concerns the powers of frontex. how will coordination be ensured with regard to the risk analyses produced by frontex and the frontex mandate for cooperation? i would like to conclude by stressing that this package must make it possible to really strengthen cooperation with third countries, with neighbouring countries. we are awaiting further information on this. and i congratulate you again: this is the right direction, the right response, namely a common european response.
in writing. the european commission's proposal for a package of measures for the european border and coast guard has its pros and cons. the proposal aims to increase the funding and capacity of frontex and to restructure the agency into the european border and coast guard in question. this is a good idea, because a major problem of the european union is the lack of well-guarded external borders, through which hundreds of thousands of illegal refugees pass. neither the dublin agreement nor the inadequate measures to redistribute immigrants will work as long as new immigrants are constantly coming to the european union. separately, a large proportion of migrants coming to the eu are not registered by the border services of the member states, which let them cross freely and leave the country. this in itself is a protest against the dublin agreement, and the low number of relocated immigrants also shows the reluctance of the member states to implement the arrangements. it is not right to impose by force measures which are incompatible with the interests of the member states. another is that member states will have to give up their sovereign right and duty to defend their borders on their own. the new european border and coast guard should not take away sovereignty from states but should support and complement the capacity of member states' border services.
in writing. europe has taken the first strong step towards greater security. the commission presented its proposals for changes to the protection of the eu's external borders, which will see the creation of a european border and coast guard. it will be able to take effective responsibility, together with the national authorities, for the protection of the union's land and sea borders. the more important proposals are to provide a more serious budget and staff, but also more rights. the mandate of the agency will allow it to carry out its own border operations and a standard by which it can be deployed for less than 3 days in the affected area in the event of a crisis. with 1.5 million illegal immigrants entering the eu in the first 10 months of 2015 alone, these measures were necessary. it is the only way to preserve the unity of the eu and to stop the return of internal borders. without secure external borders, both schengen and free movement within the union are in serious jeopardy. for bulgaria, the proposal is important because it means assessing the state of our borders at any given moment and providing concrete assistance with people and equipment in the event of a crisis. the proposal is one of the solutions to the migration problem. the ep should discuss and adopt it as soon as possible.
in writing. the current migration crisis and the terrorist threat to europe show that the external borders of the european union need to be further strengthened to guarantee the security of our citizens. i welcome the european commission's proposal for a european border guard as a necessary first step in this direction, but it will not solve the problems on its own. the introduction of systematic checks on all european citizens crossing the external schengen and european borders is of paramount importance. to be effective, it requires the linking of document and people information systems so that one check will cover the full spectrum of threats. this also means that member states exchange information in a timely manner and share it through these systems, something that unfortunately has not been done until recently. europe also needs to cooperate with its neighbours. our borders can only be protected when we have good operational cooperation with countries like turkey, so that we can fight together against the dangers that exist. last but not least, the european border guard service must ensure security not only in the schengen area but at all external borders, including the bulgarian one. at the same time, however, this should not be at the expense of national sovereignty.
madam president, all of us – and the commissioner most of all – are very well aware that the whole range of issues connected with labour mobility have taken centre stage among all the other problems facing the european union today. given the acuteness of these problems, this merits not a discussion at 22.40 but a key discussion in this parliament with a full house. we had a discussion of that sort this morning. commissioner, if we are serious about building a balanced european union, you cannot afford to pay less attention to the severe problems of labour mobility than to market competitiveness. you have said that you want a balanced package, but the situation now is highly unbalanced with a severe imbalance to the detriment of workers. if you do not fix that, problems will arise.
in writing. mr president, the free movement and freedom of establishment of european citizens in any eu country is one of the greatest achievements of the european project and a cornerstone of european integration. however, european citizens still face unjustified obstacles to the exercise of these rights, in particular with regard to equal treatment of national workers and those who, by exercising their right, have sought employment in another country. i believe that further measures are needed in this regard. such measures may include, for example, the establishment of a european platform to provide information and support in cases of discrimination in relation to freedom of movement and employment. this is necessary because in many cases workers in another country are particularly vulnerable and do not know the legal options or the competent authorities to which they can turn to protect their labour and social rights in the host country.
blue-card question.– mr lamberts, one of the issues which are continuously being swept under the carpet is the labour mobility package. you have pointed to the danger of avoiding problems that then come up into our face. would you support the proposal for a key debate on the whole range of such issues? it is obvious that, if we are talking about a properly-functioning internal market, if we are talking about avoiding the dire problems of social injustice, we need a very serious and involved debate with the full participation of the president of the commission and all of the responsible officials.
mr president, yes, this council meeting is an opportunity to take stock of a year in which much has been done, but also to look realistically at the work that still needs to be done. our area of freedom, security and justice is being tested on both fronts of migration and terrorism. we must strengthen it and defend it against those who use fear as a political argument for simplistic solutions. the union has reached major milestones in migration in the past year. i am thinking here of the frontex budget, operation sophia, the valletta summit, the western balkans route, the proposal for the new european border and coast guard agency. the implementation of all these different elements according to a precise and public timetable requires constant work. the citizens of europe are still waiting for concrete measures to be taken in the fight against terrorism. it's completely anachronistic to think in terms of national security. what member state today can claim to be the sole guarantor of its security? i don't know. at the same time, european cooperation does not imply the absence of responsibility on the part of each member state. four points to be taken into account immediately: automated cross-checking of the various european and national databases, better use by the member states of the europol file on foreign fighters, more aggressive work in the production of counter-narrative and rapid implementation of the pnr. finally, four aspects must be kept in mind in our cooperation with third countries: no blank cheques, increased support for lebanon and jordan, extreme vigilance for what is happening in the sahel and north africa and a little more concrete action in the fight against the smuggling of migrants. (the speaker agrees to answer a question on the 'blue card' (rule 162 (8) of the rules of procedure))
answer is "blue cardboard". i think we must distinguish between the two logics: on the one hand, the intergovernmental logic, with the declarations made by the council, which are the responsibility of the member states, and on the other hand, the community logic of the message we are sending here to the european parliament, the pro-european message, the need to have a common response, to defend our values. i think that, in the light of criticisms such as those we have just heard, the limits of the intergovernmental approach should be clearly defined if we are to continue to move forward in a constructive and more concrete way.
mr president, it is important to ask ourselves today: have we succeeded in creating a holistic european policy to deal with the migration crisis in 2015? eight council meetings were required to adopt the 'urgent measures' proposed by the commission and approved by parliament. the crisis today would not have been so deep if these measures had been applied in time. in fact, 2015 was only the preparatory year for the holistic migration policy. from now on, we must act. the commission's proposal to strengthen border controls has been long awaited. the protection of the schengen area is a top priority, as the president of the european commission told us this morning. but why, mr timmermans, are bulgaria, romania and croatia excluded from the new agency when their borders are also the external borders of the union? they are thus transformed into a 'buffer zone' which will have to take on all the migrants who cannot enter the schengen states, but who cannot return from where they come either. applying the principle of 'double standards' risks once again leading to an even greater migration crisis in the balkans, with consequences for the whole of europe.
in writing. ‒ i supported the report by mrs fontana on the conclusion of a cooperation agreement between europol and bosnia and herzegovina, which i believe will be useful for the security of european citizens. the terms of cooperation shall include the exchange of strategic information, information on criminal investigations, the exchange of personal data, and methods for preventing crime. in today's situation, where the security and integrity of the eu are threatened, we must use all possible instruments of cooperation with neighbouring countries to strengthen the fight against cross-border and organised crime.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report because the conclusion of an agreement between bosnia and herzegovina and europol is key to deepening cooperation between the country and the european union. i consider that the agreement on operational and strategic cooperation has added value as it aims at enhancing cooperation between the eu and third countries to prevent and combat serious transnational crime through the exchange of operational, strategic and technical information, including personal data and classified information. it will allow for effective counter-terrorism measures to be taken by regulating the relations of the european police office with a strategic partner in the western balkans such as bosnia and herzegovina. in conclusion, i welcome this agreement because it highlights the progress made by bosnia and herzegovina in some areas in terms of its integration into european values.
in writing. ‒ i support the report on the draft council decision approving the conclusion of an operational and strategic cooperation agreement between bosnia and herzegovina and europol, as i believe that the only way to effectively prevent and combat organised crime is through the sharing of available information. the events of recent months have clearly demonstrated that cooperation between services and the exchange of operational and strategic information are of crucial importance for the timely response to transnational criminal networks. i therefore believe that the sharing of information between europol and the candidate countries neighbouring the eu should be supported in the name of our common objective of building an area of justice, freedom and security.
in writing. ‒ in this case, half a million euro from the european globalisation adjustment fund is being used to reintegrate 600 workers into the labour market in ireland. the european union must support growth and job creation, but proactively, investment is not matching results. so i'm voting against this fund in principle, as i did now.
in writing. ‒ the application covers 108 people who lost their jobs. the commission's contribution is expected to be eur 442 000. according to the legislation related to the european globalisation adjustment fund (article 4 (1) (a) of the egf regulation), the number of redundancies must be at least 500 and the reasons for this must be directly linked to the economic and financial crisis and globalisation. we see that these are people of working age (80% of them between 30 and 54 years old), who are highly qualified and live near dublin - the largest economic and industrial hub. europe is once again showing solidarity and support for business. thank you for supporting this app. however, i urge that the conditions be met so that the aid is valued, needed and timely.
in writing. ‒ i believe that the draft resolution on invasive alien species is a sensible proposal that should be supported. that's why i supported her project. the arguments presented have their iron and, dare i say, irrefutable logic. of course, we must protect biodiversity, adopt a set of measures that do not allow invasive alien species to disrupt the balance of ecosystems. in addition, extremely strict control is required both in the implementation of the measures and in relation to new challenges. the protection of the environment and of biological diversity cannot be underestimated or derogated from by economic, political or any other interests. on the contrary, this should be the absolute priority of every member of parliament. i would stress that the commission should make more of an effort in this direction and not get away with half-assed proposals. europe's nature and biodiversity must be preserved for future generations, and the spread of invasive alien species must be strictly controlled and specific measures taken to maintain the balance.
in writing. ‒ i supported the motion for a resolution because i believe it is my political conviction and i have made a commitment to my constituents that european countries should not allow food and feed containing gmos to enter their markets. i understand that the i.t.c., including monsanto, is putting a lot of pressure on us to open our doors wide to gmo products. but we must not succumb to the yoke of this pressure, because there are no guarantees about the effects of these gm foods and feed on the environment, human health and lives. the latest research and studies in this area clearly prove this. i understand that the admission procedure itself, which is carried out by the commission, suffers from a number of weaknesses. we can't play god with people's lives and health. we can't open pandora's box for the sake of someone's economic interests. i therefore supported the resolution calling for the suspension of the implementation of an admission decision already taken and for the suspension of all admission procedures pending a change of procedure.
in writing. - the idea of this document is good, but the implementation does not allow me to support it. the fight against tax evasion in the eu must be led, on the one hand, by all member states, and on the other hand, it must be coordinated, because if it doesn't, we will see what we are seeing now. multinational companies will shift their accounting profits from high-interest countries to lower-interest countries, which will be a drain on the local budget on the one hand and the community budget on the other. it could get even more nasty if we step up our measures against base-shrinking and profit shifting. not all of these revenues are transferred to offshore zones and/or non-eu countries. we have a lot of work to do to combat these abuses.
in writing. – the extent of the cost of aggressive tax planning in the eu is estimated of annual revenue losses ranging from eur 50-70 to eur 160-190 billion. fighting tax evasion and tax fraud, and the related opacity and secrecy, requires actions at eu and international level. tackling aggressive tax planning is a major aim of cooperation at eu level, in particular in corporate taxation, where the objective is to reduce the number of loopholes stemming from the complex array of rules, assessment bases and rates which may apply to a single firm. i support the report that urges for bringing transparency, coordination and convergence to corporate tax policies in the union.
in writing. ‒ i supported the report by ms belder on eu-china relations because i found it to be extremely good and comprehensive. the report focuses on five areas: the eu-china strategic partnership and cooperation, the country's internal affairs, its foreign policy, the human rights situation, and relations with taiwan and tibet. the report's recommendations are of particular importance, the most important of which is that reform of the human rights system should not be neglected at the expense of the country's economic growth. economic reforms and those in the field of democracy and human rights go hand in hand with the aim of improving the overall prosperity and quality of life in the country.
in writing. ‒ i supported the report because the eu-china strategic partnership is essential to finding common responses to a range of global challenges and to identifying common interests such as global and regional security. the country is a major international power and one of the eu's most important partners. in 2015, we mark the 40th anniversary of diplomatic relations between the eu and china, but global challenges require us to seek to deepen our relations in the long term. china is a major trading partner for the eu and the conclusion of an investment agreement could have a strong impact on our economies. of course, the eu must build its partnerships on the democratic principles and high standards it stands for. it is necessary, above all, to seek constructive dialogue with the authorities to improve the human rights situation in the country, as well as to address the deterioration of the environment.
in writing. – as a member of the european parliament’s delegation for relations with china, i am following closely the relationship between the european union and china. the eu is china’s largest trade partner and china is the eu’s second largest partner. the chinese economy has been growing vigorously in the last twenty years and eu member states should take greater advantage of the opportunities offered by this economic development. that is why i am fully supporting the report on eu-china relations.
in writing. ‒ the report presented by my colleague, enrique guerrero salom, is substantial in scope and attempts to provide guidance in addressing a difficult issue - that of humanitarian action as a response to human suffering caused by natural events and human activity. it is worth noting here that the eu is one of the largest donors of humanitarian aid in the world, with a number of european countries (member states) also providing substantial humanitarian aid outside of the union's instruments. in this connection, i would like to stress that we are fulfilling our public duty as states, but we cannot solve the world's humanitarian crises alone. moreover, here in europe, right on our borders, there are humanitarian crises. in countries like mine, in bulgaria, there are people who need humanitarian aid to survive. yes, it sounds far-fetched, far-fetched to some, but it's true.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the preparation of the world humanitarian summit because it is the first one, initiated by the un secretary-general, and because the european union and its member states, as the main funder, must prepare and ensure a major influence on the conclusions of this summit. i welcome the request that a five-year road map for the development and implementation of the concrete political commitments that have been agreed be included in the outcome document of the world humanitarian summit. i also welcome the emphasis on coordination between eu and international policies and instruments, on the one hand, and on the close link between humanitarian and development, and international justice, on the other. however, i regret that the report does not propose a more in-depth reflection on the application of international humanitarian law in the context of humanitarian crises such as those linked to terrorist groups, and a reflection on an appropriate status for displaced persons as a result of the effects of climate change.
in writing. – i supported the resolution because for one reason or another, there are ongoing humanitarian crises all over the world. in this context, the upcoming world humanitarian summit is our opportunity to change not only the life of affected people but also to bring back humanity and human rights to the top of our agenda. seen in this light the eu, as the biggest aid donor in the world and human rights champion, must take the leading role in upcoming meeting. perhaps what is more important is the fact that as a leading power at the summit the eu must offer to the summit participants new innovative measures, policies and instruments to tackle ongoing humanitarian crises. leadership is not an easy thing therefore we must prepare for it if we would like to continue to be world’s leading humanitarian power.
in writing. ‒ as one of the major sectors, the steel sector is important to the european economy not only as a high value-added sector but also as a sector that employs a large number of european citizens. for this reason, and because of the problems in this area caused by the economic crisis, the reduced demand caused by many different reasons, and the reduced consumption and the increase in china's own production, we are obliged to protect the european economy and therefore the people employed in this business. however, i voted against this document because i believe that the change and liberalization of the quota system will not help the development of this sector, but will only turn these quotas into a bargaining chip and a kind of security. the protection of the environment and the fight against climate change are of paramount importance for the community, but also for the rest of the world. for this reason, and because a number of other countries have access to much cheaper energy resources, i.e. cheaper end-products, we need to focus this industry on the use of energy-efficient and innovative production processes. unfortunately, this report seems to me to be lobbying, and for that reason i do not support it.
in writing. – a key goal of the europe 2020 strategy has been to increase the contribution made by manufacturing to european gdp from 16% to 20% by 2020. the reindustrialisation of the european economy, if it is to be a globally competitive reality, faces many challenges. the eu base metals industry plays an important role for the reindustrialisation of europe. the eu needs to help build an efficient frame for the industry of base materials in order to re-establish their economic potential. moreover, base metals have one of the largest shares of bulgaria’s overall exports. that is why i am supporting the report and i vote in favour.
in writing. - mr. and mrs. president, ladies and gentlemen, today we voted on the resolution on the development of a sustainable european base metal industry, in which the steel sector is both the largest producer of base metals and the one most exposed to the risks of losing competitiveness and relocating production to third countries. the aim of the resolution was to protect this industry with the tools that are permitted and enforceable in the eu, to enable a smooth transition without disruption to a sustainable, environmentally friendly and cost-effective industry, with a product that is competitive on the world market and with technological innovation that is in line with the goals and challenges of the 21st century. i am disappointed that parliament has not given adequate support to the use of the border correction mechanism, which could as a temporary and targeted measure protect the industry from external competitors that do not respect the eu's high environmental standards. the paris agreement on climate change does not automatically mean that all the world's metal producers will operate on the same environmental standards from tomorrow. on the contrary, it remains to be seen how serious the commitments of the parties are and how long it will take to ratify the agreement. we should therefore support all protection mechanisms where they do not conflict with our own legislation and wto rules.
in writing. ‒ i voted against the resolution on the 'situation in hungary' because once again the european parliament is being used as a stage for criticism of viktor orbán's government by socialists and those who do not support his policies. this resolution is a direct interference in hungary's internal affairs and a criticism of the legislation adopted by the orbán administration. on 10 june we asked the commission to monitor the situation of democracy, the rule of law and fundamental rights in hungary, in particular as a result of the new legislation. all member states must respect the commitments they have made and protect fundamental rights. on the other hand, all politicians have a right to an opinion. but the european parliament should not interfere in the very monitoring procedure undertaken by the european commission, as this does not fall within its competence. according to article 17 teu, only the european commission has the competence to conduct such an investigation and only it has the right to determine whether european law and treaties have been infringed.
in writing. ‒ i supported the resolution because the european union is founded on the values of respect for freedom, equality, the rule of law, respect for human rights and we should not turn a blind eye but oppose the abandonment of our principles, especially when it is within the union. the recent initiatives and interventions by the hungarian government have led to a systematic deterioration of the rule of law. at a time when record numbers of people are fleeing their homes because of war and oppression, it is unacceptable for hungary, which is a full member of the eu and international organisations, to want to isolate itself and refuse to deal with asylum applications. the country is obliged to comply with european and international laws such as the geneva convention. these acts by the hungarian government in recent months should serve as a red light that freedom and democracy are being fought for every day. i therefore consider it necessary for the council of the european union and the european council to discuss and adopt conclusions on the situation in hungary.
mr president, first of all i would like to congratulate mr preda on his excellent work. i would then like to emphasize six elements which i consider essential. firstly, i would stress the need for coherence between our various external policies and between our internal and external policies. we must avoid double standards and this consistency can only be reinforced by full membership of the member states in the action plan and the strategic framework of the union. my second point concerns the unity and visibility of the union's action, facilitated by the work of the union's special representative for human rights. this mandate must become permanent to complete the process. the third element is the role of election observation missions. again, monitoring of these missions is paramount. and i think this is the case even when the conclusions of the mission are positive, because it also constitutes political support for those who are determined to advance democracy in their own countries. the union delegations can play an even more active role in this monitoring. fourthly, the role of parliamentary cooperation through delegations and joint assemblies, which provide considerable support to countries in democratic transition. the fifth element concerns human rights, democracy and development. i would like to stress the need to define a limited number of priorities per country, which could be a strategic and pragmatic approach to better achieve and quantify our results. finally, the treatment and situation of migrants and refugees, including in europe, are rightly integrated into the report. we must not forget that in addition to terrorists and armed groups, migrant smugglers and criminal networks of human trafficking are deliberately violating human rights. illicit trafficking in migrants is a crime and a violation of human rights. we must do more to dismantle these networks, and we must do it in a spirit of good cooperation.

mr president, bulgaria was quoted. bulgaria has a great responsibility as an external border of the european union to ensure the security of the union. the country works closely with european agencies and international institutions and takes measures in accordance with international and european legislation. in this context, i would like to highlight four factors: first, we should use a common methodology for the preparation of monitoring reports assessing national systems for the reception of persons seeking protection. it is definitely missing from the reports cited. the people who are making allegations of wrongdoing should stand up and be counted. second, the unhcr and the bulgarian helsinki committee monitor the principle of non-refoulement on a daily basis. it was decided to carry out unannounced inspections from february 2015. so far, there have been no reports of physical abuse or humiliation. the presence of a fronttex officer during all procedures with detained third-country nationals shall guarantee compliance with the principle of non-refoulement. it's only right that we also show our respect for our own agencies in one such as frontex. moreover, law enforcement officers receive special training on asylum issues. they conduct daily briefings, are subject to constant monitoring for compliance with the instructions. it's time to take into account the difficult conditions in which they work. third, the formal procedure in the event of an allegation of non-compliance with european legislation by a member state requires that an official document be drawn up requesting an explanation to the relevant national authorities. only then, with full information from the parties concerned, can we be objective. finally, ladies and gentlemen, the key role of nafta in the european parliament is crucial. the libe commission shall send delegations which shall clearly state the conditions of admission. but it is very important for us to keep in mind that the countries that are making the effort should be encouraged and, above all, that this problem should be treated as part of our overall migration management strategy.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, in a number of its reports and assessments, the european commission has stated that bulgaria is successfully guarding europe's external borders. the area along the bulgarian border with turkey, which has been under severe migratory pressure for several years, is characterised by a largely cross-country and difficult-to-access terrain. bulgarian border guards successfully work in difficult conditions and ensure european security every day by breaking up networks of human traffickers, apprehending persons suspected of criminal or terrorist activity and rescuing migrants in trouble at the border. despite the constant efforts of the bulgarian border police, my country is again being wrongfully accused by foreign ngos of mistreating asylum seekers. i would like to emphasise that a number of checks have been carried out on the compliance with european legislation and the work of the border police none of them prove these allegations. the authorisation of illegal crossing of the state border through the places not designated for this purpose creates preconditions for the entry into the territory of the republic of bulgaria and the european union of accomplices and members of islamic terrorist organizations. in view of this, bulgaria is taking and will continue to take the strictest measures permitted by national and international law to ensure a high level of border security and to control the flow of illegal migrants and asylum seekers. so i would like to urge all those who criticize the countries on the european union's external border, which are feeling the pressure of the migrant wave, before you point a finger at us, get acquainted with the conditions under which our staff are trying to cope with the situation and stop interviewing only asylum seekers. talk to those who are facing thousands of migrants every day and then, based on the opinions of both sides, write reports!

answering a question asked by raising a blue card. thank you for asking. as you can see, unlike the rest of our colleagues who throw out unsubstantiated accusations, we don't mind answering. yes, i think these ngos have a specific benefit, they work for specific interests and they're a little embarrassed and afraid to say who's to blame for this. they're afraid to say the name of german chancellor merkel and ask her how she's going to take responsibility for what she's done to europe.
on behalf of the ecr group. ladies and gentlemen, the report on arms exports that has been presented to you seems to me to be extremely hypocritical. i agree that under no circumstances should countries and organisations armed with armed conflict be armed. but the point of this report is not that at all. it is a mixed-up, arbitrary, and unconnected system of legal and illegal arms exports and trade. the control mechanism itself is a good wish and nothing more. i call this report largely hypocritical because the big member states have not stopped and continue to produce and sell weapons to third countries, including in risky war zones. in return, countries like my homeland bulgaria were pressured, our military-defense and industrial complex was destroyed. the big member states are taking over our markets, and now, in this report, they're talking about rules, morality and peace in relation to arms exports. there's a double standard here. i'm not taking it. i also reject the idea that european countries should impose restrictions on themselves and that our competitors in the sector should occupy an empty niche because of our own shortsightedness. it will cost us dearly economically, socially and defensively. i do not support this report because it is a symbol of double standards and poorly targeted objectives.
mr. president, i would like to mr. chairman, ladies and gentlemen, the decision of the european patent office nearly seven months ago has given rise to today's debate. this decision set a precedent. it affects many farmers and the whole european union. for them, it is contrary to existing international conventions and european norms. smes in the sector feel threatened by international giants and feel that their competitiveness is being undermined. it is essential to take a balanced approach in this case. we must be able to encourage the development of innovation on the one hand, but on the other hand we must distinguish between situations where biological characteristics are obtained for the purpose of research and development. and when these derived biological characteristics are used for personal purposes and commercialized. when we talk about this commercialization, i think we fall into a different category than the development of scientific research. it is important to be able to distinguish between these situations and to be able to strike a balance, because the wider the exceptions, the smaller the scope of the rules.
in writing.– the arbitrary detention of irish citizen ibrahim halawa represents a violation of human rights, international and egyptian law. it is totally unacceptable for people to be detained, tortured and treated in an inhuman manner just because they have exercised their core human rights of freedom of expression and assembly. what is more, ibrahim halawa is potentially facing the death penalty for both his ‘crimes’ (freedom of expression and peaceful assembly). the eu is the leading institutional actor and largest donor to the fight against the death penalty and is committed to abolition of the death penalty worldwide. having regard to that, we should condemn not only the application of the death penalty in egypt but also we should call on the country’s authorities for the immediate release of the european citizen ibrahim halawa who has faced unfair trial. last but not least, the eu should engage in a constructive dialogue with the egyptian authorities in order to change its low level human rights record. as our common european history teaches us, better human rights record equals development, peace and prosperity. these are things that our partner egypt needs right now.
