in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of the framework agreement on comprehensive partnership and cooperation between the european union and its member states, of the one part, and the socialist republic of vietnam, of the other part, to take account of the accession of the republic of croatia to the eu, as i consider this to be a way of conducting the union's foreign policy responsibly.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of the resolution on the eu-vietnam framework agreement on comprehensive partnership and cooperation. this is because of the eu-vietnam partnership and cooperation agreement, which was also concluded in 2010.
in writing. ‒ i did not support this motion for a resolution because i do not agree with the main message that the european commission's proposals for a package to strengthen economic and monetary union leave insufficient room for parliamentary scrutiny and debate at european level. it is not a question of democratic control, as proposed by the committee on economic and monetary affairs, but of who should oversee national budgets. there are already more than enough mechanisms to ensure democratic control. furthermore, i also do not support the proposal to make the european fiscal board responsible for the political authority of the ep. this board has a purely advisory function and has no wider mandate than that. transferring this prerogative sovereignty to a european level would not only politicise the issue, but would also be wrong and illegal.
in writing. ‒ i voted abstention on the report by mr preda annual report 2014 on human rights and democracy in the world and eu policy in this area, as the report's focus shifts from basic human rights to secondary rights such as the right to abortion, the rights of lgbti people (lesbian, gay, bisexual, transgender and intersex/intersex). what would people who have been in prison for years and have not been given a fair trial, which is a basic human right, think that in the european parliament's annual report on human rights we discuss social rights, the right of financial transactions and labour rights. of course, these rights are also extremely important, but they are still secondary under international law, and the right to abortion and the rights of lgbt people are even third generation rights that divert our attention from the extremely serious cases of persecution, forcible detention, mistreatment, torture, unfair trials and all the other similar gross violations of basic human rights.
in writing. – as we can see from the 2014 human rights and democracy report the world is going backwards. the dark memories of times when human rights were constantly abused are reflected in today’s world news. in this inhuman and intolerant international environment the eu should step up as a champion of human rights. we should seek a better human rights record from our trading partners, political allies and prospective members. most importantly we should not stop our continuing effort to improve the human rights record within our borders, in particular in our member states. because if we manage to fully deal with lack of freedom of expression, racism, exploitation and gender crimes within our borders then there is no reason why we cannot do so outside of them. i hope that the 2016 human rights and democracy report will show a better human rights record worldwide; if not, then we should bear in mind that we have failed.
in writing. ‒ i welcome the european parliament's resolution on the 20th anniversary of the dayton peace agreement. this agreement ended the bloody war between bosniaks, croats and serbs 20 years ago. unfortunately, 20 years later, bosnia and herzegovina is still a very divided country between the different federations. some even call it a failed state. instead of moving forward towards a well-functioning federal government, bosniaks, serbs and croats are in constant conflict with each other and in a struggle for influence and competence of their national components. all this is holding back democratic reforms at the national level, as well as the overall development of the country and its aspiration to integration with the european union. the dayton peace accords ended one war, but the problems and divisions between the warring nations remain.
in writing. ‒ i supported the resolution because the dayton peace agreement, signed in 1995 in paris, ended the bloodiest war in europe since the second world war and paved the way for democracy in bosnia and herzegovina. however, 20 years later, the complex state structure of bosnia and herzegovina brings to the fore complex and unresolved conflicts in europe's most unstable area. the lack of confidence in the institutions and the gradual weakening of the international presence are moving the country further away from the euro-atlantic path, and this has a destabilising effect. that's why today, the country needs our support more than ever. the european union has proved that it can serve as an example of multicultural and ethnic coexistence and we can provide a new impetus for reform. we need to help the country solve its historical overlay problems. the young people in this country need to recapture the european dream and build a european mentality of values, not turn the region into a grey area of europe.
in writing. ‒ there is a deep misconception in the eu institutions that when we adopt certain policies, the rest of the world will also adopt and abide by them. this is where the report in question comes in. it proposes measures that europe should follow, without taking into account that the rest of the world will not. as regards arms exports, we cannot continue to impose restrictive policies on our own countries and companies indefinitely until third countries meet our criteria and effectively displace us from the markets. and not only that, we create complications that benefit third parties who openly break the rules. so the more restrictions we put on the gun industry, the more we favor those who break the rules. therefore, i cannot accept such a report.
in writing. – due to endless conflicts, the worldwide arms trade is on the rise. the arms industry is a major industry for many eu member states. however, what is disturbing is the basic fact that arms indeed are fuelling conflicts and causing sufferings and human rights violations. in this sense, the eu should take steps to limit the trade, or at least to force member states to provide real data and information about their arms trade. if we possess good information about development of the arms market and trade then we can avoid not only human rights violations but also mass killing, ethnic cleansing and terrorist attacks.
in writing. ‒ i voted with 'disgust' on the resolution on patents and plant breeders' rights, as i believe it is important to take a balanced approach to the case with the precedent set by the epo. we need to be able to encourage development and innovation, but we also need to distinguish between situations where biological traits are obtained for research and development, used for personal purposes or commercialized. when we talk about commercialization of the resulting characteristics, i think we fall into a very different category of research development. it is important to be able to distinguish between these situations and to be able to strike a balance, because the wider the exceptions, the smaller the scope of the rules.
in writing. ‒ i supported another resolution on the situation in burundi, which is deteriorating alarmingly with each passing month. just a few weeks ago, the armed forces once again trampled on the rights of the population, at least 240 people have been killed since the current president, nkurunziza, decided to extend his term, and over 200,000 have fled the country. it is essential to re-establish the democratic process and dialogue between political parties and to put an end to the persecution, killings and impunity of the military. also of concern is the case of richard spyros hagabimana, a police officer in burundi of greek origin, who was imprisoned and tortured because as a police officer he refused to fire on the crowd. the european external action service should do its utmost to protect his rights and to arrange his release.
in writing. - i'm reading with amazement what's happening in virunga national park. i can't explain how european countries offer money to rebels, you know, terrorists, so they can get easier access to the oil fields in this park. we recently witnessed the un climate conference in paris, where for the first time, an agreement was reached to reduce its greenhouse gas emissions. and at the same time, two oil companies are helping to make a national park disappear. i can't let that happen. that is why i support this motion for a resolution and i sincerely hope that it will not remain on paper but that real action will be taken and that these barbaric acts in virunga will cease. we have nothing stronger than nature and i hope she doesn't take revenge one day.
